# SPEC-002: Generators & registry

**Crate**: `crates/sciart-generators` · **Milestones**: M1 (registry, starfield), M4–M7 (batches), M10 (Batch E), M11 (titles, taxonomy) · **Status**: M1, M4–M7 (Batches A–D) and M10 (Batch E, `gray_scott` upgrade) implemented; GEN-R12..R15 specified for M11

Ports the Python stability contract (`docs/archive/issue-01-simulator-stability.md`, R1) to Rust
and extends it for real-time animation.

## Registry
- **GEN-R1** `registry() -> &'static [Entry]` lists every generator; `Entry { meta: &'static Meta, build: fn() -> Box<dyn Generator> }`. Names are unique, `snake_case`, and sorted.
- **GEN-R2** `find(name) -> Option<&'static Entry>` is exact-match on `meta.name`, or on a retired name (GEN-R14).
- **GEN-R3** Adding a generator requires exactly: one module file + one line in the registry macro. The contract suite picks it up automatically.

## Stability contract (every registered generator)
- **GEN-R4** Never panics for any `Size` with `w, h >= 1` across any sequence of `resize`, `update(dt)` (`0 <= dt <= 0.25`), and `render` calls — including resizes between any two calls.
- **GEN-R5** Dimension-dependent state is rebuilt in `resize`; `update`/`render` must tolerate the smallest size (1×1).
- **GEN-R6** Determinism: same seed + same call sequence ⇒ byte-identical `Canvas::to_lines()`, in every build profile: a frame must not depend on the optimiser. Rust never contracts to FMA or reorders float operations, but LLVM does fuse `sin`/`cos` pairs by inlining context, so all trigonometry goes through `sciart_core::trig` (CORE-R90), enforced by `clippy.toml` `disallowed-methods`; the snapshot suite runs in both dev and release builds (TEST-R9).
- **GEN-R7** No wall-clock reads (`Instant`, `SystemTime`) and no global RNG (`rand::rng()`/`thread_rng`) inside generators; time comes from `dt`/`ctx.time`, randomness from `ctx.rng`. Enforced by `clippy.toml` `disallowed-methods`.
- **GEN-R8** Performance: `update(1/60)` + `render` < 5 ms at 200×50 in release (criterion bench per generator). Means hide act hand-overs, so the bench also times the slowest known frames (group `worst`: the slowest of 3 frames around each hand-over or peak) and the resize hitches (group `resize`), and `examples/worst_frame` (`pixi run rs-worst`) replays every scene and reports its worst single frame: keep it < 2 ms at 200×50 and < 5 ms at 500×150. A resize is a one-off and may take longer (black_hole's geodesic trace ≈ 14 ms); precompute there, or amortise over the frames before the result is needed, never in one frame of `update`.
- **GEN-R9** Motion: over 1 simulated second at 200×50 the rendered frame changes (a scene is an animation, not a still). Verified by the contract suite.
- **GEN-R10** Unicode glyphs must have an ASCII fallback (`Glyph::new(u, a)`); ASCII mode output is pure ASCII.
- **GEN-R11** After warm-up (build, resize, ≥ 1 step and one render), `render` performs zero heap allocations, and `update(1/60)` performs zero allocations in steady state unless the scene is listed with a reason in `UPDATE_ALLOCATES` of `crates/sciart-generators/tests/alloc_free.rs` (empty today). Resizing may allocate. Verified at 200×50 and 80×24, in both glyph modes, by that test (ADR-0009).

## Titles, categories and names (M11)
Design: `docs/design/M11-titles-taxonomy.md` §1–§3 · ADR-0013 (taxonomy) · ADR-0014 (titles). Mechanism: CORE-R71..R77.
- **GEN-R12** Every registered scene has a valid science title (ADR-0014). The contract suite checks `meta.title` and every entry of `meta.captions`:
  - the title contains exactly one ` · `, with non-empty, trimmed text on both sides;
  - its width is ≤ 32 cells and the phenomenon's is ≤ 20 cells (`Title::width`, CORE-R72);
  - each caption is non-empty, trimmed and has no ` · `, and `phenomenon · caption` is ≤ 32 cells;
  - every char is in the title charset (`title::ascii_of(c).is_some()`), and none of `= ^ _ * / < >` appears (no formulas or markup);
  - titles are unique across the registry;
  - `caption()` sampled at t = 0, 1, 5 and 20 s (seed 42, 80×24) is `None` or an entry of `meta.captions`, and a scene with empty `captions` always returns `None`.

  A title never claims more than the model does; wording is signed off by the science review.
- **GEN-R13** The bottom-left belongs to the title. With the title hidden (`Stage::set_title_visible(false)`), at 80×24 and 40×12, seed 42, t = 0, 1 and 5 s, in Unicode glyph mode, no cell of row `h − 1` left of where the longest `title::readout_room` readout may start (the span of `Title::of(scene)` plus the 4-cell gap, CORE-R76) holds an alphanumeric char. Only text is excluded: half-block, braille and field art may fill the row. A scene that places a readout on row `h − 1` right-aligns it with `draw::label_right` (CORE-R76), inks it `Text` at `title::READOUT_LEVEL` (checked for every scene at 80×24, 40×12 and 200×50, t = 1 s), and shortens or drops it so that at least 4 blank cells separate it from the title's text: forms are chosen against `title::readout_room` (CORE-R76) with the live caption, whether or not the title is drawn. Other scene text (names, epithets) stays off row `h − 1` while the title is shown, so it never reads as part of the title. The moves below supersede the bottom-left placements written in the scene sections.

  | Scene | M11 change |
  |-------|-----------|
  | action_potential | Readout (`HH squid …`) right-aligned on row `h − 1`; its form follows the room right of the title. |
  | belousov_zhabotinsky | The name `Belousov-Zhabotinsky` is dropped (the title names the phenomenon); the beat word and `b=…` are right-aligned, forms chosen by the room. |
  | boids_flocking | `N 192  k=7` right-aligned. |
  | brownian_motion | MSD readout right-aligned, parts two spaces apart; when it would reach the title, trailing parts are dropped (`t = …s` first, then the true D); hidden when the estimator alone does not fit. |
  | cosmic_microwave_background | `CMB  T=2.725K  dT~1e-5` right-aligned instead of centred. |
  | double_pendulum | Divergence and energy readout right-aligned instead of centred. |
  | doppler_effect | The legend describes sound and is right-aligned, longest form that fits: `<- lower pitch  v = 0.5 c_s  higher pitch ->`, `<- lower  v = 0.5 c_s  higher ->`, `v = 0.5 c_s`. |
  | electron_orbitals | Orbital name and transition readouts right-aligned. |
  | fractal_growth | The phrase `diffusion-limited aggregation` is dropped (the title paraphrases it); `p_s …   N …   D_f …` right-aligned; whole parts are dropped from the left, D_f last. |
  | molecular_dynamics | `T = … heating  U/N …  LJ reduced units` right-aligned, shortened by dropping `LJ reduced units`, then `U/N … N …`, then the target temperature. |
  | physarum | `SA … RA … SO …` right-aligned. |
  | solar_surface | `day …  Teff 5772 K` right-aligned (Teff still dropped first when narrow). |
  | strange_attractors | The attractor name becomes `caption()`; `meta.captions` = `Lorenz`, `Aizawa`, `Thomas`, `Halvorsen`, `Rössler`, `Chen` (`Strange attractor · Chen`, 23 cells), and `meta.title` is `Strange attractor · chaos`. The parameter set, when it fits, is right-aligned. |
  | constellation_map | The α-star label is clamped to rows ≤ `h − 2`; the constellation name and its epithet to rows ≤ `h − 3`, so a blank row separates them from the title. |
  | molecule_viewer | The shown molecule's name is `caption()`; `meta.captions` = `water`, `methane`, `ethanol`, `benzene`, `caffeine`, `buckminsterfullerene`. |
  | moon_landing | `caption()` follows the landing: `powered descent` until the footpads touch, `touchdown` until the flag beat, then `Tranquility Base`. The intro caption names only the craft: `LUNAR MODULE EAGLE`, `LM EAGLE`, `EAGLE`. |
  | moon_phases | The phase name is clamped to rows ≤ `h − 2`. |
  | particle_accelerator | The beam-energy gauge on row `h − 1` starts at least 4 blank cells after the title's text, is shortened to end before the last column, and is dropped below 8 cells. |
  | three_body | The shown orbit's short name is `caption()`; `meta.captions` = `figure-8`, `Lagrange`, `Broucke A1`, `Broucke R4`, `moth I`. |
- **GEN-R14** Renamed scenes keep their old names as aliases: `ALIASES: &[(&str, &str)]` maps `chemical_reaction → gray_scott` and `crystal_growth → fractal_growth`. `find(name)` tries `meta.name` first, then an alias, so `sciart run <old name>` and `disabled = ["<old name>"]` (CFG-R7) keep working. Every alias target is registered, and no alias equals a registered name. `registry()` and `sciart list` show only current names. Acceptance IDs keep their prefixes (`CHEM-A*`, `CRY-A*`).
- **GEN-R15** Each scene section heading in this file reads `### <name>  (<category label>, <milestones>)`. Every registered scene has exactly one such section, and its label equals `meta.category.label()` (CORE-R77), which is how each scene's category (ADR-0013) is written down. Checked by a test that parses this file.

## Per-generator spec template
Each scene gets a short section appended below when its batch starts:

```
### <name>  (category label, batch)
Intent: what the viewer should see/feel.
Model: state, physics/maths, parameters.
Motion: what changes over time and at what rate.
Resize: what is rebuilt.
Inks: roles used.
Acceptance: scene-specific assertions beyond GEN-R4..R10.
```

### starfield  (Deep Cosmos, M1, M12)
- **Intent**: travelling through a star field. Near stars stream past, far ones creep, and the Milky Way stays fixed at infinity: a narrow, soft band of pale starlight with clumped star clouds, one bright cloud per screen and a dark dust lane cutting through its middle. There is no twinkle (there is no air in space).
- **Model**:
  - `N = clamp(0.08·area, 1, 4000)` stars at uniform `(x, y)`, in 4 parallax layers at relative distances 8, 4, 2, 1 (4 = nearest). A layer drifts at `12/d` cells/s (parallax `∝ 1/d`: the farthest creeps at an eighth of the nearest). A star's layer is drawn with probability `∝ d`, so farther layers hold more stars.
  - Brightness bucket (5 buckets) from a steep luminosity function, `P(bucket ≥ k) = 3^{−k}` in the farthest layer, each nearer layer half a bucket brighter (inverse-square dimming). Temperature from a field mix (CORE-R88): M 10 %, K 30 %, G 15 %, F 15 %, A 20 %, B 10 % (3200–25 000 K).
  - **Milky Way** (laid out once per size from `ctx.rng`): a band tilted 0.35 h across the width, Gaussian across with σ = 0.05 h (1.3× wider through the star cloud). The light fades to exactly zero between 1.9σ and 3.2σ (smoothstep), so it never ends on a cliff. Its peak is `Ramp(Starlight)` 100, times `1 + 0.6·clump` (three sinusoids along the band) and `1 + 0.8·cloud`, one bright star cloud per screen (Gaussian, 0.1 w long, at a seeded 0.2–0.8 w). A dust lane (super-Gaussian `e^{−(d/0.018 h)⁴}`, passing 15 %) wanders ±0.02 h about the centre line. The glow is shaded into `Pixels` once per resize; levels below 6 dissolve (CORE-R89).
  - **Band stars**: `min(0.2·area, 8000)` resolved stars scattered by rejection in proportion to the band's light, so they crowd the clouds and thin in the lane; `Star` 60–150 by light.
- **Motion**: stars drift right, wrapping at the width. Every star trails a `Star` 70 braille streak over its last 0.25 s of travel (three cells for the nearest layer, under a dot for the farthest), stopping short of a bright star's glyph cell.
- **Resize**: the stars, the Milky Way layout, its glow and its band stars are regenerated for the new size.
- **Inks**: buckets 0–1 are single braille dots in `Star` 80/150 (faint stars look colourless); buckets 2–4 are `+ ✦ ★` glyphs in `Blackbody` at the star's temperature, over the glow's `cell_bg`. The glow is `Ramp(Starlight)`: it has no ASCII form, so in ASCII the band stars carry the band.
- **Stylisation**: stars trail a 0.25 s camera-exposure streak so the parallax reads in a still frame (an eye sees none); layer populations are `∝` distance, not `∝ d³` as shells of space would hold, so the near layers are not empty; the Milky Way is at infinity, so it is fixed.
- **Acceptance**: (`STAR-A1`) the star count follows the formula; (`STAR-A2`) nearer layers move farther, as `1/d` (the nearest 8× the farthest), farther layers hold more stars, and with one star per layer the farthest trails no dots, the nearest ≥ 2, and trail length never falls with nearness; (`STAR-A3`) stars keep their glyph and ink over 10 s and are drawn with them; (`STAR-A4`) in the farthest layer the bucket counts strictly fall and ≈ 2/3 are bucket 0, and the mean bucket rises with nearness by 1.2–2.1 from farthest to nearest; (`STAR-A5`) the glow and the band stars do not move; ≥ 20× as many band stars lie within σ of the centre line as beyond 3σ, and the mean band light at the band stars is > 1.5× the mean over the band; glow pixels are `Ramp(Starlight)` or empty, and empty 4σ off the centre line; the lane is darker than its shoulder in ≥ 17 of 19 sampled columns; in every column at most a third of the pixels reach level 20, the band peaks ≥ 150 somewhere, and from its outermost pixels at ≥ 20 the light takes ≥ 3 px to reach an empty pixel (no cliff); (`STAR-A6`) faint stars are `Star`, bright ones `Blackbody` in 3100–26 000 K (> 20 coloured at 200×50).

---
## Batch A — analytic / time-driven scenes (M4)
Shared helpers: `sciart_core::draw` (CORE-R22..R27). All lengths below are in **rows**; x is stretched by `ASPECT`. `R = draw::fit_radius(size, frac)`.

### solar_system  (Sun & Planets, M4, M12)
- **Intent**: an orrery seen from 30° above the ecliptic, filling a wide terminal.
  - A white-gold Sun whose reddened limb is its glow; eight coloured planets, each a round lit sphere
    whose phase follows where it is: nearly full beyond the Sun, a crescent in front of it.
  - Short fading trails along faint dotted elliptical orbits, a dusty asteroid belt, and a thin tilted
    double ring round Saturn that passes in front of and behind the ball.
- **Model**:
  - View: orbits in the ecliptic plane seen from 30° above it, orthographic. An orbit of radius `a` is an
    ellipse `a × a·sin 30°`. Far-side planets (orbit angle in (0, π)) are drawn before the Sun, near-side
    ones after it, so the Sun hides planets behind it.
  - Kepler speeds: `ω(a) = (2π/12 s)·(a/a⊕)^-1.5`. **Compressed**: periods obey Kepler's law for the
    *drawn* radii, so period ratios are compressed too (Neptune/Earth 5.5×, real 165×).
  - Orbit fractions `{.16,.24,.32,.42,.60,.74,.87,1.0}` of the outer radius `R`; belt 0.47–0.55.
    **Not to scale**: distances are compressed (real 0.39–30 AU).
  - Layout: the bottom two rows stay empty (title row and a gap). `R = min(0.96·w/2 − 1,
    (0.96·min(cy, 2·rows − cy) − 1.5)/sin 30°)` px, with `rows = h − 2` (h ≥ 6). The Sun's core sits on
    cell `(w/2, rows/2)`. At 200×50 `R` = 87 px (the system spans 87 % of the width).
  - Planet radius `r_px = s·(R/R_J)^0.4`, `s = max(0.6, 3.5·R/87)`, floor 0.5 px. **Not to scale**:
    sizes keep their real order, but Jupiter is 2.6× Earth, not 11×.
  - Discs *(changed)*: a disc of `r ≥ 1.5` px is centred on the nearest pixel centre; a smaller one on
    the nearest pixel corner with `r` raised to 1 px, so it is a round 2 × 2 (then 12-pixel) blot, never
    a plus. Each pixel's cover is `min(1, r + ½ − d)`, `d` its centre's distance; pixels with cover
    ≥ 0.15 are drawn, at `0.35 + 0.65·min(1, cover/0.75)` of their shaded level (an anti-aliased rim).
    Every disc is four-fold symmetric.
  - Lighting: each disc pixel's normal `n` (view frame) is lit by `L = n·ŝ`, ŝ the unit vector to the
    Sun; level = day × `(0.3 + 0.7·smoothstep(L/0.5 + 0.5))` × the rim shade, a soft terminator.
  - Jupiter's dark equatorial belts are the pixels at |latitude| 7°–20° (pole along ecliptic north),
    seen as bands.
  - Saturn's rings: B at 1.74 and A at 2.15 Saturn radii, each one thin braille ellipse (Cassini gap
    between). The ring pole is tilted 26.7° from the orbit normal toward +x and fixed in space, so the
    ring ellipse (axis ratio cos 26.7°·sin 30° = 0.447) is the same wherever Saturn is. Ring points
    nearer the viewer than Saturn's centre are drawn over the ball as ring-coloured pixels.
  - 360 asteroids, each at its own Kepler speed, `Dim` 60–100. Trails cover the last `min(0.45 rad, 30
    dots)` of orbit at 0.8 → 0.35 of the planet's level.
- **Raster**: two `Dots` layers (orbits every other dot and the belt; trails and rings), then `Pixels`
  (far planets, Sun, near planets). All rasterised in `update`/`resize`.
- **Inks**:
  - Sun: `Blackbody` of the 550 nm brightness temperature of `I(μ)^3`, by Wien's law
    `1/T = 1/5772 K − (λ/c₂) ln i` (λ = 550 nm, c₂ = 1.438 776 9 × 10⁷ nm·K, i clamped to [10⁻⁶, 1]),
    with linear limb darkening u = 0.6. **Exaggerated 3×**: the real limb is ≈ 1000 K cooler than the
    centre.
  - No halo *(changed)*: any glow ring round a disc this small reads as an outline, darker than the
    orange limb; the reddened limb is the glow.
  - Orbits `Dim` 40. Planets (false colour where a ramp stands in for the real colour):

    | Planet | Ink |
    |--------|-----|
    | Mercury | `Structure` 170 |
    | Venus | `Ember` 225 (pale cream) |
    | Earth | `Ice` 165 |
    | Mars | `Ember` 120 (rust orange) |
    | Jupiter | `Ember` 190, belts 140 |
    | Saturn | `Ember` 222 (gold) |
    | Uranus | `Ice` 220 (pale cyan) |
    | Neptune | `Ice` 105 (deep blue) |

  - Rings `Ember` 200 (B) and 150 (A). All planet and ring levels ≤ 225, so in `mono` the Sun's core
    is the brightest thing on screen (RND-R11 as amended).
  - Night side *(changed)*: 0.3 of the day level, over a soft terminator; **0.5** for discs under
    1.5 px, so a 2 × 2 blot in crescent (Mars at 80×24) never sinks into the sky.
  - The near halves of Saturn's rings cover the ball where it is drawn (the same snapped centre and
    radius as the disc).
- **ASCII**: planet centre cells show `m v e M J S U N`; other disc pixels `o`; Sun `@`. Orbits and belt
  *(changed)*: a `.` in every other lit cell (a checkerboard), not braille's `:`/`+` fallback, so the
  dotted ellipses stay light and the lettered planets stand out. Trails and rings fall back as in core.
- **Acceptance**: `SOL-A1`: ω strictly decreases outward, Earth's period is 12 s and `T² ∝ a³` (Earth's orbit fraction is 0.32); `SOL-A2` *(changed)*: at 200×50, every second for 40 s, every planet's centre pixel is in its role, at a level between its night and day levels; `SOL-A3` *(changed)*: the Sun's centre cell `(w/2, (h − 2)/2)` is `Blackbody` within 2 % of 5772 K, and `@` in ASCII; the brightness temperature is 5772 K at i = 1, 970 ± 20 K cooler at i = 0.4, and finite, positive and ≤ 5772 K for i ∈ {0, −1, 10⁻⁹, 1.5, 100, ∞}; `SOL-A4`: for outer radii 12, 47.5 and 95 px the planet radii keep the real order (J > S > U > N > E > V > Ma > Me, or tie at the 0.5 px floor), none is under the floor, and Jupiter is < 4× Earth; `SOL-A5` *(changed)*: at eight orbital phases, Jupiter's pixel nearest the Sun is at least as bright as the farthest; strictly brighter on the near side of the orbit. At 80×24, over 16 phases in front of the Sun, Mars's four pixels are `Ember` at ≥ half its day level; `SOL-A6` *(changed)*: the ring ellipse has semi-axes `a` and `0.447·a` for every `a`, half of it lies in front of the planet, and at two phases a quarter orbit apart every drawn ring dot lies within one dot of one of the two ring ellipses; at 80×24, over 32 phases, every ring-inked pixel within 4 px of Saturn is one of its disc's pixels (the rings cross the ball only where it is drawn); `SOL-A7`: the eight planet inks are distinct, neighbours differ in role or by ≥ 30 levels, orbits are `Dim` ≤ 40; with every planet at orbit angle 1 rad, trail dots lie behind the planets (read back through the 30° view) and nothing but orbit and ring dots lies ahead; `SOL-A8` *(new)*: at 80×24, 200×50, 120×40 and 40×12, over 30 s, the scene draws nothing on the last two rows (GEN-R13; no orbit dots trailing the title); `SOL-A9` *(new)*: at 200×50 over 10 s, in `mono`, the Sun's centre pixel is brighter than every planet and ring pixel; `SOL-A10` *(new)*: for radii 0.5–3.5 px at three sub-pixel positions, every disc's pixel set is four-fold symmetric (square bounding box, closed under a quarter turn), and the four corners of its inner 2 × 2 (r < 1.5) or 3 × 3 square are drawn at shade ≥ 0.75: never a plus.

### pulsar  (Deep Cosmos, M4, M12)
- **Intent**: an oblique rotator seen from the side: a tiny hot neutron star whose magnetic axis is tilted from its spin axis, so its two beams sweep cones through space. Closed dipole loops co-rotate inside the light cylinder; open field lines leave through it along the beams. Once per turn the beam brushes our line of sight and the star flares; an inset stacks the pulses we receive, each a little different, like the CP 1919 plot on *Unknown Pleasures*.
- **Model** (world x right, y up, z to the viewer; lengths in `R_LC`; orthographic, `0.55·fit_radius` rows per `R_LC`):
  - `P = 1.3373 s` (CP 1919), played in real time.
  - Spin axis `Ω̂ = (0, sin ζ, cos ζ)`, `ζ = 60°`; magnetic axis `m(φ) = cos α Ω̂ + sin α (cos φ x̂ + sin φ ê₂)`, `ê₂ = Ω̂ × x̂`, `φ = 2πt/P`, `α = 50°`; impact parameter `β = ζ − α = 10°`; beam half-opening `ρ = 16°`. We receive the pulse while `∠(m, ẑ) < ρ`, centred at `φ = −π/2`; the other pole stays ≥ 70° away, so one pulse per turn and no interpulse.
  - Field lines `r = L sin²θ` about `m`, from the star's surface (`r ≥ 0.07`): closed loops at `L = 0.4, 0.8` in 4 magnetic meridians (below 120 columns only `L = 0.4`, so the star is not a tangle), each loop further out dimmer; open lines (`L = 2.5`) in 6 meridians from both polar caps, stopped at the light cylinder (distance 1 from the spin axis). Guides, dashed, `Dim` 90: the spin axis and the light cylinder's silhouette (half-height 1.3).
  - Beams: 2 × 160 rays in a sunflower pattern over each cone, turned by 37 rad/s so frames interleave; 150 samples per ray out past the screen edge, weight `(dt/τ)·w(f)/(1 + 2r)` with `w(f) = (e^{−3f²} − e^{−3})/(1 − e^{−3})` (`f` the fraction of the cone radius), zero at the cone's edge, so the fringe fades before it dissolves. They expose into a `raster::Glow` (CORE-R85) decaying with `τ = 0.06 s` (a short motion blur of the sweep), toned by `Tone::asinh(0.4, 60)` in `Ramp(Plasma)`; levels below 24 dissolve (CORE-R89). A beam turning towards us (`axis·ẑ` 0.3 → 0.9, smoothstep) is dimmed by up to 60 % and shortened by up to 60 %: end-on it would fan its whole length round the star.
  - Star: a point of light, a `Ramp(Plasma)` 255 core pixel disc (radius 0.8 px) in a `Ramp(Plasma)` 205 halo (1.9 px).
  - Flash: while we receive the pulse, four diffraction spikes of horizontal half-length `1.6·template(φ)` that taper from `Blackbody` 255 (inner 30 %) through `Ramp(Plasma)` 210 (to 65 %) to 140.
  - Pulse stack (top-right inset, `min(w/4, 40) × min(h/3, 12)` cells from row 1; only on canvases ≥ 60×16): a ring of 16 pulses, one added per turn, 80 samples over a 70° window about the pulse. A pulse is `template(φ)·(0.35 + Σ₃ aₖ exp(−((φ − cₖ)/5°)²)) + noise`, `template = exp(−γ²/2σ²)`, `γ = ∠(m(φ), ẑ)`, `σ = ρ/2`; three sub-pulses 11° apart drift by 0.24 spacings per pulse (`P₃ ≈ 4.2 P`, drifting sub-pulses were found in CP 1919), amplitudes `aₖ ∈ [0.4, 1)` and noise `±0.04` from `ctx.rng`. Drawn as `min(16, dots/5)` lines with a floating horizon (hidden lines removed), the newest at the front in `Ramp(Plasma)` 240 and revealed up to the current phase, older ones 200 → 110. Under the inset the beam is dimmed to 0.3, feathered over ±2 px across the border (smoothstep of the signed depth), so the traces read over a veil, not a pasted panel.
  - Readout `P 1.337 s` right-aligned on the title row when `readout_room` allows (GEN-R13).
- **ASCII**: beam and star pixels below level 60 are left out, the rest by level (`.:+*@`); lines and the stack via the braille fallback.
- **Stylisation**: not to scale: CP 1919's light cylinder is ~6400 star radii in radius; only `P` and `P₃` are CP 1919's — `α`, `ζ`, the sub-pulse spacing `P₂`, the 70° window and the profile are illustrative, and the beam is widened ~3× (a true `ρ ≈ 5°` would miss us at `β = 10°`); the beam is drawn as if lit by scattering (in space it is seen only when aimed at us), and dimmed and shortened when aimed at us; the flash with diffraction spikes stands for the received radio pulse (CP 1919 has no optical pulses); the spikes' core shows the top of the blackbody scale (a real surface is ~10⁶ K).
- **Acceptance**: `PUL-A1` `m(t)` is a unit vector at angle `α` to `Ω̂` with period `P = 1.3373 s`; `PUL-A2` over 10 turns exactly 10 rising edges of `∠(m, ẑ) < ρ`, `∠(−m, ẑ) ≥ 70°` always, and `∠(m(−π/2), ẑ) = β`; `PUL-A3` field-line points satisfy `r = L sin²θ` with colatitude `θ` about `m`, closed lines stay within 1 of the spin axis, the open lines' apex lies beyond it, and `closed_loops(w)` is `[0.4]` below 120 columns and `[0.4, 0.8]` from 120; `PUL-A4` each of 16 pulses peaks within `2ρ` of the pulse centre, their mean correlates ≥ 0.9 with the template, and two consecutive pulses differ by ≥ 0.05 somewhere; `PUL-A5` at 80×24, 200×50 and 60×16 the inset lies in the right half, in rows `1..h − 1`, with > 10 drawn cells after 0.5 s, and none at 59×30 or 100×15; at 80×24 over one turn the beam reaches the inset and no pixel of its interior (2 px in) exceeds `0.3·255`; under an even beam the level across the inset's border falls monotonically in steps ≤ 60 levels; `PUL-A6` at 200×50 the spike row holds ≥ 25 lit dots within 2 % of a turn after the pulse centre and more than twice as many as anywhere away from the pulse; ≥ 1/2 of the sky is black away from the pulse; pixels are only `Ramp(Plasma)` or `Blackbody` (the spikes' core); at 80×24 and 120×40, while we receive the pulse, ≥ 2/3 of the sky stays black.

### moon_phases  (Earth & Sky, M4, M12)
- **Intent**: the real Moon through a lunation, big and soft-edged:
  - grey maria of varied tone in their true places, rayed Tycho brightening toward full;
  - craters shaded along the terminator, as grey shadows, never black holes or white sparks;
  - a full disc bright to the limb;
  - a crescent whose night side is a dim ashen ghost disc ("the old Moon in the new Moon's arms") with
    the maria faintly visible;
  - a face that nods slowly with libration.
- **Model**:
  - Phase and Sun direction unchanged: synodic month 30 s, `L = (sin 2πp, 0, −cos 2πp)`.
  - Disc: `Pixels` disc of radius `2·fit_radius(w, h − 2; 0.9)` px.
  - **Surface texture**: equirectangular, 192 × 96 texels (1.875°/texel), built once per scene with
    `ctx.rng`: albedo, ray brightening and the height gradient per texel.
    - (a) *(changed)* Fourteen mare shapes at their IAU centres, with half-extents of half their IAU
      diameters in degrees of arc (D/2 over 1737 km): Imbrium 18.9°, Serenitatis 11.1°, Tranquillitatis
      14.4 × 12°, Crisium 12.2 × 9.2°, Fecunditatis 11 × 15°, Nubium 11.8°, Nectaris 5.5°, Humorum 6.4°,
      Vaporum 4°, Cognitum 6.2°, Insularum 8.5°; Frigoris a 40 × 5° band; Procellarum two lobes, 21 × 20°
      at (52° W, 30° N) and 17 × 16° at (50° W, 0°). Each has its own albedo relative to the highlands
      (Serenitatis 0.62, Frigoris 0.64, Imbrium 0.55, Procellarum 0.52/0.5, Tranquillitatis 0.42, Crisium
      0.45, …). Outlines are lobed (2nd and 3rd harmonics, ±15 % and ±10 %) and ragged (value noise,
      ±0.15 half-extents); the basalt is at half cover on the outline and thins out over a shore of
      smoothstep over 0.75–1.25 half-extents. Neighbours overlap and merge, as the
      Serenitatis–Tranquillitatis–Fecunditatis chain and Procellarum–Imbrium do; overlaps take the
      darkest. The maria cover 25–35 % of the near side (31 % on the Moon).
    - (b) *(changed)* Tycho (rays to 40°), Copernicus (16°) and Kepler (9°) ray systems, stored apart from
      the albedo. Each has 11 rays at seeded angles, lengths 0.35–1× its reach and widths 0.05–0.13 rad,
      fading along their length and narrowing toward the tip: irregular streaks, not a spoked wheel.
      Aristarchus is bright (1.45×); Plato and Grimaldi have dark floors.
    - (c) 70 seeded craters of radius 1.5–6° (**exaggerated**: real craters this common would be
      sub-pixel), bowls of depth 0.22·r with raised rims.
    - (d) Three octaves of smooth value noise (lattice 24, 8 and 3 texels; ±7, ±5, ±2.5 %), five times
      stronger inside the maria (lava flows of different ages).
    - The height gradient uses a `cos lat` floor of 0.3 and its length is capped at 0.3.
  - **Libration**: unchanged (7.9° / 27.99 s and 6.7° / 27.64 s, exact rotation, bilinear samples).
  - **Relief**: the normal tilts down the height gradient, faded by `smoothstep(μ/0.6)` toward the limb
    (craters there are a pixel or two) and by `smoothstep(μ0/0.15)` at the terminator, so relief never
    moves the day–night line. A shadowed pixel keeps at least 0.3 of its smooth-sphere exposure.
  - **Rays**: albedo + `(0.5 + 0.9·f²)·ray`, f the illuminated fraction. **Stylised**: rays stand out
    toward full Moon, as they do when shadows vanish.
  - **Reflectance**: Lommel–Seeliger `A·μ0/(μ0 + μ)`, so the full Moon has no limb darkening.
  - **Exposure** *(changed)*: `v = min(1, ln(1 + 8r)/ln(1 + 5.6))`; level `60 + 195·v²`. **Stylised**: a
    camera-like log stretch. The floor of 60 keeps the dimmest sunlit pixel at twice the brightest
    earthshine, so the terminator holds in every theme (`mono` maps `Dim` and `Star` through one grey
    ramp; `astro`'s `Dim` is darker than `Star` at any level).
  - **Terminator**: set by the smooth sphere (MOON-A1). Sunlit pixels with v < 0.03 fall back to
    earthshine (only at the terminator itself, given the shadow floor).
- **Earthshine** *(changed)*: `Dim` level `28·A·E(p)`, held between the visible floor (12) and 30,
  `E(p) = (1 + cos 2πp)/2` the Earth's phase from the Moon, shown while `E ≥ 0.5`. **Boosted**: real
  earthshine is ≈ 10⁻⁴ of the sunlit side. Drawn as a dim slate; `Dim`'s cool tint suits it, as
  earthlight is bluer than sunlight.
- **Phase names** *(changed)*: new, first quarter, full and last quarter are instants and own only ±0.02
  of the lunation (±0.6 d); the crescents and gibbous phases own the rest.
- **Inks**: sunlit `Star`; earthshine `Dim`; label ` {name}  {pct}% ` `Text` 220, centred at row
  ≤ h − 2, built in a stack buffer; stars `Dim`, off the disc.
- **ASCII**: sunlit cells by level over 60–255, `.:+*#@`; earthshine `.`.
- **Acceptance**: `MOON-A1`: new Moon (p = 0) lights no point of the visible hemisphere, full Moon lights every interior point, first quarter exactly the right half; the rendered new Moon has no `Star` pixel, the first quarter none left of centre; `MOON-A2` *(changed)*: the principal names hold at exactly 0, ¼, ½, ¾ and within ±0.02; 0.03 and 0.97 are crescents, 0.225/0.275/0.715 are crescent/gibbous, 0.519 is full; `MOON-A3`: the illuminated fraction is 0, ½, 1, ½ at p = 0, ¼, ½, ¾ and matches the lit share of the disc by quadrature within 0.002; `MOON-A4`: Lommel–Seeliger at zero phase angle gives the same reflectance at the centre and the limb, zero for μ0 ≤ 0, and near quarter the bright limb outshines the terminator side; `MOON-A5` *(changed)*: E(0) = 1, E(½) = 0; `Dim` earthshine pixel counts fall from p = 0.02 to 0.15 to 0.4; its mean level is higher at new Moon than at crescent; none at full Moon; its brightest level is ≤ 80. *(New)*: at 200×50, 120×40 and 80×24, at eight crescent phases (illuminated < 0.35), every sunlit pixel within 3 px of an earthshine pixel is at least twice the brightest earthshine level; `MOON-A6`: libration stays within ±7.9° × ±6.7°, reaches ≥ 95 % of both, and repeats with periods 27.99 s and 27.64 s; `MOON-A7` *(changed)*: albedo at Crisium and Imbrium < 0.7× the highland at (10° E, 50° S); Serenitatis is ≥ 0.08 paler than Tranquillitatis; the mare-covered (cover > ½) share of the near side's area is in [0.25, 0.35]; cover > ½ between Serenitatis and Tranquillitatis (24° E, 18° N), Tranquillitatis and Fecunditatis (42° E, 0°) and Imbrium and Procellarum (35° W, 30° N); position checks unchanged; `MOON-A8`: near the terminator (last quarter), Copernicus's sun-facing wall is ≥ 0.05 brighter than its far wall; `MOON-A9` *(new)*: at 200×50, 80×24 and 40×12, at p = 0.1…0.9 (9 phases) and librations 0 and ±(7.9°, 6.7°), every disc pixel whose smooth-sphere `μ0` is ≥ 0.1 is a sunlit `Star` pixel: relief never punches black, earthshine or empty holes in the day side. Near the limb (μ < 0.3), at p = 0.3…0.7 and the three librations, no sunlit pixel is more than 10 % over the smooth sphere's exposure: foreshortened rims never flash white. (Bright specks near the upper-left limb are Aristarchus, 1.45×, and Kepler: real bright craters.); the GEN-R13 label test is kept.

### exoplanet_transit  (Sun & Planets, M4, M12)
- **Intent**: a hot Jupiter circling an orange K dwarf, seen almost edge-on.
  - Emerging nearly full from behind the star, the deep-blue planet wanes to half at elongation and to a
    thin crescent before it turns its night side to us and crosses the limb-darkened disc as a round,
    soft-edged silhouette.
  - Underneath, a photometer strip scrolls the light curve: noisy points around a clean model, with a
    bright tick marking "now". The dip enters from the right.
- **Model**:
  - Parameters after HD 189733 b: `p = Rp/R* = 0.155`, `b = 0.66`, `T_eff = 5050 K`. The planet's blue
    colour follows Evans et al. (2013).
  - Limb darkening is quadratic, `I(μ) = 1 − u1(1 − μ) − u2(1 − μ)²`, `u1 = 0.62`, `u2 = 0.12` (V band,
    approximate).
  - **Stylised**: the orbit is drawn at `a/R* = 2.4` (real 8.8), and the period is 14 s.
  - Orbit, φ = 2πt/P, φ = 0 at mid-transit: `(x, y, z) = (a sin φ, b cos φ, a sin i cos φ)`, z toward the
    observer, `cos i = b/a`.
  - Flux: `F = 1 − ∫ I(r)·L(r) dr / π(1 − u1/3 − u2/6)`, `L(r)` the arc of radius r inside the planet;
    midpoint rule with 200 radii, tabulated once into 256 entries over `d ∈ [0, 1 + p]`, read with
    linear interpolation. `F = 1` when z ≤ 0 (the secondary eclipse, ≈ 10⁻⁴, is not modelled). Maximum
    depth 2.6 %.
  - Planet phase: the visible-hemisphere normal `n` is lit where `n·(−r̂) > 0`, r̂ the unit star→planet
    vector, drawn in `Ice` at `0.25 + 0.4·cos`. **Boosted**: the planet's reflected light is ~10⁻⁴ of the
    star's; its phases are drawn visible as an illustration. The night side is not drawn. In front of
    the disc the planet is a silhouette; behind it (occultation), hidden.
  - Silhouette edge *(changed)*: a star pixel's cover is the share of a 4 × 4 grid of samples inside
    the planet, so a small silhouette is a round blot, not a plus; a part-covered pixel shows its mean
    value, `v·(1 − cover)`, and is blank from cover 0.95 or once below the visible floor (CORE-R66).
  - Day side edge *(changed)*: off the star, a pixel shows the planet from 0.3 cover, its value scaled
    by `min(1, cover/0.75)` with the normal clamped onto the rim, so a planet 2 px in radius (80×24) is a
    round blot, not a plus or a square.
  - Occultation *(changed)*: behind the star, the planet is drawn only on pixels the star left empty,
    so at ingress and egress it slips under the limb.
- **Star shading** *(changed)*:
  - **False colour**: each disc pixel is `Ramp(Ember)` at `v = 0.2 + 0.8·I(μ)^1.3`, so lightness follows
    the limb-darkening law itself: pale gold at the centre (v = 1), visibly darker from mid-radius, deep
    orange-red at the limb (v ≈ 0.34). The ramp's reddening toward the limb stands in for the real
    brightness-temperature drop (≈ 1000 K); the star's true colour (5050 K, pale peach) is not shown.
  - No glow *(changed)*: the ramp's dark end made any glow past the limb a maroon outline. Instead the
    limb is anti-aliased: a limb pixel takes the limb value times the share of it the disc covers (4 × 4
    samples), and is dropped below the visible floor.
  - The star is static and shaded once per size.
- **Photometer strip**: size `clamp(h/4, 4, 12)` rows ending on h − 2, 2-dot margins, newest sample
  right, σ = 0.07·depth scatter from a stateless Box–Muller of `hash01`, `Dim` 30 baseline,
  ` relative flux ` label, `depth 2.6 %` readout, the newest model point `Ember` 255 on a 7-dot tick.
  *(Changed)*: it spans the last 0.55 period (7.7 s), so the dip fills about a quarter of its width, and
  it is brighter: model `Ramp(Ember)` 230, observations `Structure` 150.
- **Layout**: unchanged (star centred above the strip, `R* = min(0.4·rows_px, 0.94 w / 2(a + p))`,
  faint `Dim` 30 orbit off the disc, 80 background stars `Dim` 30–90).
- **ASCII**: star cells by `Ember` level: `@` ≥ 220, `#` ≥ 180, `*` ≥ 140, `+` ≥ 100, `:` ≥ 60, `.` below
  (the anti-aliased limb). Day side `o`.
- **Acceptance**: `TRAN-A1` *(changed)*: `ld_flux(d, p, u) = 1` for |d| ≥ 1 + p, with and without limb darkening, and the scene's flux is 1 whenever the planet's sky distance is ≥ 1 + p; `TRAN-A2` *(changed)*: without limb darkening, a planet wholly on the disc blocks p² (within 0.2 %); `TRAN-A3` *(changed)*: the test-only exact circle-overlap area (the uniform-disc reference for TRAN-A4) matches the lens area 2π/3 − √3/2, is continuous at contact, symmetric in its radii and non-increasing in d; `TRAN-A4`: the law is 1 at μ = 1 and `1 − u1 − u2` at μ = 0; with u = 0 `ld_flux` matches the uniform reference within 3·10⁻⁴ on d ∈ [0, 1.3]; the central depth is > p² and within 3 % of `p²/(1 − u1/3 − u2/6)`; the scene's deepest flux is `F(b)`; `TRAN-A5` *(changed)*: F = 1 whenever z ≤ 0; at 200×50, 120×40 and 80×24, at every phase with the planet behind (z < 0), every star pixel is unchanged: no planet pixel lands on the star; `TRAN-A6`: at φ = π/4, π/2, 3π/4, 5π/4, 3π/2 the lit share of the planet's pixels at 200×50 is within 0.05 of `(1 − z/a)/2`; `TRAN-A7` *(changed)*: the disc value is `0.2 + 0.8·I(μ)^1.3` (1 at the centre), below 0.85 at r = 0.7 and below 0.4 at the limb; drawn at 200×50, the level along a radius never rises, the lit run ends within a pixel of `R*`, and every star pixel is at or above the visible floor; `TRAN-A8`: at 200×50 the strip ends on row h − 2; spanning 0.55 period, every sample keeps its value and moves one dot left per `0.55·P/W` s; mid-transit, the strip's lowest model dot sits at `F(b)`; `TRAN-A9` *(new)*: Mid-transit at 200×50: the planet's centre pixel is empty and the disc centre is `Ember`; every star pixel whose centre lies within 0.3 px of the planet's rim is `Ember` darker than the bare disc there (anti-aliased), at least 8 of them. At 80×24 (rp ≈ 2 px), over eight positions a pixel of drift apart, every pixel centred within rp − 0.75 of the planet is blank (a round blot, not a plus). Off the star, at 64 phases, every pixel at least half covered by the planet whose rim-clamped normal faces the star is `Ice`, and none under 0.3 cover is. No strip below 24×12. In `mono`, at 16 phases, every `Ice` pixel is darker than the disc centre.

### satellite_orbit  (Voyages, M4, M12)
- **Intent**: the Earth turning beneath the orbits we actually fly. The ISS and a Starlink train skim just above the air, a GPS plane rings the planet in MEO, three geostationary weather satellites hang over their own longitudes as the continents pass under them, and a Molniya satellite loops high over the north. Each satellite is a bright head with a short fading trail, so the fleet reads as motion at a glance.
- **Model**: two-body Kepler orbits in Earth-centred inertial coordinates, with real elements: μ = 398 600.4418 km³/s², Re = 6378.137 km, J2 = 1.08263e-3.
  - ISS: 420 km, i = 51.64°.
  - Starlink: a 16-satellite train at 350 km, i = 53°, 4° apart in mean anomaly.
  - GPS: 4 satellites in one plane at i = 55°, on a half-sidereal-day period.
  - GEO: a sidereal-day period at i = 0, over GOES-East (75.2° W), Meteosat (0°) and Himawari (140.7° E).
  - Molniya: e = 0.74, i = 63.4°, ω = 270°, on a half-sidereal-day period.

  Newton's method solves Kepler's equation. Secular J2 drift is applied to the node (`dΩ/dt = −3/2 n J2 (Re/p)² cos i`) and the perigee (`dω/dt = 3/4 n J2 (Re/p)² (5 cos² i − 1)`). Equatorial orbits get no drift: their node is undefined, and station-keeping absorbs J2's ~2 km shift of the synchronous radius. One sidereal day lasts 60 scene seconds, so the Earth and GEO turn together and the ISS laps in ~3.9 s.

  The globe is a 128 × 64 Natural Earth 1:110m land map (public domain), with ice (Antarctica, Greenland, the Arctic and Antarctic pack), ~90 large cities' lights and coarse desert boxes. The desert boxes are blurred over about two map cells into a desert share, and each land pixel is desert or land by a stable per-pixel threshold, so the deserts fade into the land around them. A small globe cannot show that detail: below ~16 px radius the desert share fades out (none below 6 px), and below ~11 px so do the city lights, so one hot pixel never dominates the disc. The globe is lit by a fixed Sun at declination +12°, shaded per pixel, and has:
  - a soft terminator;
  - an ocean sun glint;
  - city lights on the night side, a mid Ember, dimmer on a smaller globe;
  - a limb glow just outside the lit limb, brightest at the edge and fading out over ~2 px, one band with no dark gap to the ground.

  Deserts show only in full day: toward the terminator the sand gives way to land, so it never tints the twilight or the night.

  The viewer sits 20° above the equator. A satellite is in eclipse inside Earth's cylindrical shadow, and is then drawn unlit.
- **Stylisation**: shading moves down the Blackbody ramp, so the sand reddens as it dims toward the terminator (a palette effect, not a physical one). Drawn radii are log-squashed, `r → Re (1 + 0.8 ln(r/Re))`, which puts GEO at ~2.5 Re; periods, inclinations and eccentricity are true. The Sun is held fixed (no seasons). The train is spaced wider than a real one some days after deployment, so each satellite is its own dot. The deserts are approximate: coarse boxes over the great hot and cold deserts, softened. The colours are false.
- **Motion**: the globe spins (features drift left to right on the near side); satellites move at their Kepler rates, each trailing the last 0.4 s of its orbit (long streaks in LEO, none to speak of in GEO); the ISS and Starlink nodes regress ~5° per day.
- **Resize**: the fit (largest Earth that keeps every orbit on screen between row 1 and the row above the title), the buffers and the stars are rebuilt. The fleet and the Earth's phase are kept.
- **Layers**: the globe is a half-block Pixels image. Stars (only where the globe leaves the sky black), orbits, trails and satellite heads are braille Dots drawn over it; an orbit crossing in front of the disc is drawn dimmer there, and every glyph over the globe takes the globe's colour as its background (CORE-R86), so nothing punches a black box into the Earth. Heads are rounded 4 × 4 dot blocks, except the Starlink train's single-dot pearls. Each class's path and trail are tinted in its own ramp, so the classes read apart without their labels. Both layers are rasterised in `update`. One label per class (ISS, Starlink, GPS, GEO, Molniya) sits right of its satellite's head whenever the satellite is not hidden, never on the top row or the title row; labels are drawn last, on a dark chip with a blank cell either side (`draw::label`, CORE-R67), so a city light or the sunlit sea never swallows or touches a letter.
- **Inks**: oceans, ice and the limb in `Ramp(Ice)`; land in `Ramp(Aurora)`; deserts in `Blackbody` (the tan stretch of the ramp: sand); city lights in `Ramp(Ember)` (at most 0.6); paths (front brighter than back, dimmer across the disc) and sunlit trails (the bright end of the ramp) in their class's ramp: `Ramp(Ice)` for the ISS and Starlink, `Ramp(Aurora)` for GPS, `Ramp(Plasma)` for GEO and Molniya; sunlit heads in `Star`; eclipsed heads and trails in `Dim`; labels in `Text`.
- **Shared code**: `Globe` and `surface_ink` are `pub(crate)` and also paint the Earth in `moon_landing`. A `Globe` can be drawn south up (`south_up`), turned half a turn with its Sun.
- **Acceptance**: `SAT-A1` a point is hidden iff it is behind the globe (`z < 0`) and within its disc, and a hidden ISS is not drawn; `SAT-A2` periods follow Kepler's third law on the scene clock: ISS 3.87 s ± 1 %, GPS and Molniya ½ day, GEO 1 day (60 s), GEO radius 42 164 ± 1 km; `SAT-A3` the Kepler solver's residual is < 1e-12 for e ≤ 0.9, |r| lies within a(1 ∓ e), circular orbits keep |r| = a; `SAT-A4` each GEO satellite's sub-satellite longitude stays within 1e-6 rad over a day, while the Earth turns once; `SAT-A5` the ISS node regresses −5.0 ± 0.15°/day, Molniya's perigee drift is < 0.01°/day at 63.4° and has opposite signs at 55° and 70°; `SAT-A6` a β = 0 LEO orbit spends `asin(Re/a)/π` of its period in shadow, ± 0.002; `SAT-A7` the squash is strictly increasing and the identity at the surface, GEO is drawn at 2.3–2.7 Re, every orbit fits on screen above the title row at 200×50, 80×24 and 40×12; `SAT-A8` the map is Earth (Pacific, Atlantic and Indian Ocean are ocean; the Sahara and the outback are desert; the Congo, the Amazon and Siberia are land; Greenland and Antarctica are ice; Tokyo is lit; longitude wraps), and the desert share is > 0.95 inside the Sahara, < 0.05 over the Congo and the Pacific, and falls through (0.15, 0.85) over at least 1.5° walking south out of the Sahara; `SAT-A9` the sub-solar point is a bright day ink, the anti-solar side is near-black or city lights, cities glow in Ember only at night, the Sahara by day is `Blackbody` sand, a globe with no room for detail shows neither desert nor city lights, and the limb glows outside the disc on the sunward side only, and walking out along the sunward radius the glow just outside the edge is at least as bright as the ground just inside it and never brightens again further out; a mid-sized globe's city lights stay at or below a mid Ember; the Sahara under a Sun 3° up or at night is not `Blackbody`; `SAT-A10` the Starlink train keeps its spacing exactly; `SAT-A11` no orbit is painted into the globe's pixels, and every braille cell over the globe at 200×50 carries a background (more than 10 such cells); `SAT-A12` whenever the ISS and the point 0.2 s behind it are both sunlit and facing the viewer, the head's cell is `Star 255` and the trail's cell is in the ISS's ramp below 255; face-on consecutive Starlink heads are at least 2 dots apart; the ISS is labelled within 4 s at 200×50, with a blank cell either side of the label; LEO, GPS and GEO paths have three different ramps (Starlink shares the ISS's, Molniya GEO's), each on more than 10 braille cells at 200×50.

### doppler_effect  (Forces & Waves, M4, M12)
- **Intent**: one moving source, two kinds of wave, two acts with their own captions. **Sound** (26 s): the source speeds up from Mach 0.5 through the sound barrier to Mach 1.6; fronts bunch up ahead (higher pitch) and stretch behind (lower pitch), pile into a wall at Mach 1, and fold into a Mach cone beyond it. **Light** (22 s): the source moves at β = 0.8; light ahead is blueshifted and beamed forward, light behind reddened and sparse.
- **Model**: wave speed `c = 10` rows/s in both acts (light is slowed for display — a stylisation). Fronts are circles of radius `c(t − t₀)` about fixed emission points (exact for sound in still air, and for light in the observer's frame). Sound act: Mach 0.5 (6 s), eased to 1.0 (5 s), 1.0 (3 s), eased to 1.6 (4 s), 1.6 (8 s); a front every `T = 0.3 s`, back-dated by the overshoot (DOP-A1); a front point at θ from its emission point is heard at `f' = f/(1 − M_e cos θ)` with `M_e` the Mach number at emission; above Mach 1 the fronts envelop the cone of half-angle `asin(1/M)` (DOP-A3). Light act: β = 0.8, γ = 5/3, lab emission period γT = 0.5 s (time dilation); each front is photons emitted evenly in the source frame at rest angle θ′, placed at the aberrated lab angle `cos θ = (cos θ′ + β)/(1 + β cos θ′)`, so they crowd forward (beaming; in 2-D the density per angle is ∝ D); each is coloured by the observed temperature of a 5800 K blackbody, `T′ = D·T` with `D = 1/(γ(1 − β cos θ)) = γ(1 + β cos θ′)` — a Doppler-shifted blackbody is a blackbody (DOP-A4): 17 400 K ahead, 1 900 K behind, 3 480 K sideways (the transverse redshift 1/γ). The camera follows the source (a view, not physics) at 0.40 of the width, easing to 0.72 when supersonic (relaxing with τ = 1.2 s); nothing wraps. A front is dropped once faded out, past 3 screen diagonals, or when its rightmost point is left of `x_s − 0.72·width`, the leftmost the camera can reach (the camera can step left while its anchor rises): only fronts emitted above Mach 1 fall behind that line, which then moves at v ≥ c, so they never return (DOP-A2); a circle that holds the whole screen is kept, since a supersonic source later overtakes it. Each act, like the first frame, opens on its steady state: the fronts a source moving forever at the new speed would have emitted (DOP-A10), while the other act's fronts fade over 2 s and are dropped (narrative stylisation). Once the source breaks through Mach 1 (t = 14 s) the fronts it emitted below Mach 1 fade out over 3 s, so the zone of silence ahead of the cone reads dark (narrative stylisation: those fronts still exist and cross the region outside the cone; only sound emitted while supersonic leaves it silent; DOP-A9). Each front is sampled only along the arc that can meet the screen, and not at all when it passes inside the screen's nearest point or outside its farthest corner; for light the lab arc maps to a rest-frame arc through `rest_angle`, the inverse aberration, which keeps order. Within that arc's sample grid only the samples on the parts of the circle inside the screen (grown by one row for the two-dot stroke) are visited (DOP-A11): a big circle centred on screen used to be walked all the way round, most of it off screen, which cost ~6 ms per frame at 500×150 at an act hand-over.
- **Inks**: braille `Dots`, two-dot-thick round fronts. Sound: `Ramp(Aurora)` (false colour) at `shown·(25 + 230·fade·p)`, where `fade = 1 − r/(3·diag)` (linear with radius to where fronts are dropped: art, not geometric spreading), `shown` is the act and subsonic fade, and `p` is the heard pitch, `f'/f = 1/|1 − M_e cos θ|`, mapped as `p = clamp(0.45 + 1.2 q, 0.1, 1)` with `q = tanh(ln(f'/f)/2) = (1 − s)/(1 + s)`, `s = |1 − M_e cos θ|` (DOP-A8): deep teal behind (×0.67 → 0.21), 0.45 unshifted, pale mint ahead (×2 → 0.85), and the Mach-1 pile-up and the cone (s → 0) saturate at 1. Dots under `ink::MIN_VISIBLE_LEVEL` are skipped, so a fading front thins out rather than vanishing at once. Light: `Blackbody` at `T′`, photon density scaled by `max(fade, 0.3)·shown`. Source `◉` `Accent` (sound) or `✦` `Blackbody` 5800 K (light), with a `━─` wake. The two cells after `title::span(..).end` on row h−1 are blanked, so fronts never run into the caption's tail.
- **Captions**: `pitch up ahead` (M < 0.97), `sound barrier` (0.97 ≤ M ≤ 1.03), `Mach cone` (M > 1.03), `blueshift ahead` (light act).
- **Readout** (bottom-right, `READOUT_LEVEL`, GEN-R13; longest that fits): `M 0.50  pitch x2.00 ahead  x0.67 behind` / `M 0.50  x2.00 ahead` / `M 0.50`; `M 1.60  cone 39 deg` / `M 1.60` (only `M 1.00` near the barrier); `v 0.80c  17400 K ahead  1900 K behind` / `v 0.80c  17400 K ahead` / `v 0.80c`. Written through a stack buffer.
- **Resize**: the timeline runs in simulated seconds, so the act never changes; fronts keep their positions in the medium and their emission rows shift with the centre row.
- **Acceptance**: `DOP-A1` at constant subsonic M the fronts cross the axis `(c − v)T` apart ahead and `(c + v)T` behind, for fine and coarse steps and for the prefilled first frame; `DOP-A2` live fronts never exceed `(3·diag/c)·(1/T + 1/(γT)) + 2` (both acts' fronts are live during a hand-over) over any resize order and dt sequence across the loop; a front pruned while still shown never comes back on screen later in its act (400×60 and 80×24, one loop); `visible_arc` is empty for a circle that holds the whole screen or falls short of it, the whole circle for a small one centred on screen, and non-empty for one centred off screen that reaches it; `DOP-A3` for constant M > 1 every front is tangent (1e-9) to both cone lines of half-angle `asin(1/M)` through the source, and M ≤ 1 has no cone; `DOP-A4` `D(0°) = √((1+β)/(1−β))`, `D(180°) = √((1−β)/(1+β))`, `D(90°) = 1/γ`, `aberrate` is monotone, fixes ±1 and satisfies `γ(1 + β cos θ′) = D(θ)`, `rest_angle` inverts it on both sides of the axis, and the photon ink is `blackbody(D·5800 K)`; `DOP-A5` 15 s into the light act only light fronts remain, lit dots ahead of the source outnumber those behind ≥ 2×, and the Blackbody level ahead exceeds the level behind by > 30; `DOP-A6` over two 48 s loops the captions run `pitch up ahead` → `sound barrier` → `Mach cone` → `blueshift ahead` twice, M is exactly 0.5, 1.0 and 1.6 on its holds, the light act is the last beat, sound-act braille is `Ramp(Aurora)` and light-act braille `Blackbody`; `DOP-A7` at 200×50, 80×24 and 40×12 the source stays within 0.40–0.72 of the width for 50 s and is drawn on its row; `DOP-A8` `pitch_value(0.5, ±1)` is ≥ 0.8 ahead and ≤ 0.3 behind, sideways in [0.35, 0.55), `pitch_value(1, 1) = 1`, it is non-decreasing in cos θ at Mach 0.5, and at t = 3 s (120×40) the mean Aurora level of the braille cells ahead of the source exceeds that behind by > 60; `DOP-A9` at 200×50 and 120×40, from t = 18 s to the end of the sound act only fronts emitted at M ≥ 1 are live and no Aurora braille cell lies right of the source's column + 1; `DOP-A10` 0.3 s into the light act and 0.3 s into the next loop's sound act (120×40) the new act already has > 200 braille cells in its own ink; `DOP-A11` `circle_rect_arcs` returns disjoint arcs whose points lie inside the rectangle and cover every point of the circle inside it (random circles and rectangles, including circles centred inside, around and outside the screen), and drawing only those arcs lights exactly the dots the whole visible arc lights, at 120×40 and 200×50 every 0.5 s over a loop, both hand-overs included; `DOP GEN-R13` the two cells after the title span are blank at every time of the loop at 80×24 and 120×40.

### wave_interference  (Forces & Waves, M4, M12)
- **Intent**: a ripple tank. Two coherent sources send out rings; where crest meets crest they flash bright, along the nodal hyperbolas the water stays dark and still. The sources slowly draw apart and together, so the fan of nodal lines opens and closes, and a detector screen on the right shows the fringes the waves paint there.
- **Model**: sources at `(0.2·w_field, h/2 ± s(t)/2)`, `s(t) = 0.3h·(1 + 0.3 sin(2πt/36 s))` rows; `λ = max(2.5, h/9)` rows; `ω = 3` rad/s; `a = sin(k d₁ − ωt) + sin(k d₂ − ωt)` with envelope `E = |e^{ikd₁} + e^{ikd₂}| = 2|cos(k(d₁ − d₂)/2)|`. No 1/√r geometric spreading, so the nodes are exact, and an art vignette `(1 + r/(0.8h))^−½` about the sources' midpoint (both stylisations). The sweep is quasi-static: the pattern is the steady pattern for the sources' current spacing. Each source moves at up to ≈ 0.008h rows/s against a phase speed ω/k ≈ 0.053h rows/s, ≈ 15 % of it, so a real tank would show a Doppler skew of up to ≈ 15 % along the source axis that is not drawn.
- **Inks**: half-block `Pixels` in `Ramp(Ice)` (false colour) at `v = vignette·(E/2)^1.5·(0.6 + 0.4 a/E)`: 0 on the nodal lines (the 1.5 power widens the dark lanes), 0.2 (trough) to 1 (crest) on an antinodal band; v < 0.05 is left blank. Mirror-symmetric about the middle row, so each row is computed once. Sources: 2×2 `Accent` pixels. Detector screen (w ≥ 24): the right `max(3, w/24)` columns after a 2-column gap show the time-averaged intensity at the field's edge, `I = E²/4`, as a bar growing leftward from the right edge, length ∝ I, `Ice` at `40 + 140·I` (at most 180, well under the brightest crest, so the detector reads as an instrument beside the tank rather than its brightest feature). The old centred top title is dropped. ASCII: `. o O 0 @` by mean brightness (blank below 0.05), sources `*`, screen `#` (both halves) or `-`.
- **Title row**: the 3 cells after `title::span(..).end` on row h−1 stay blank, so the field never reads as continuing the title (GEN-R13).
- **Acceptance**: `WAVE-A1` on the perpendicular bisector the amplitude is `2 sin(kd − ωt)` (also for the fast form, which matches the reference); `WAVE-A2` where the path difference is `λ/2` the amplitude is ≈ 0 at all times; `WAVE-A3` the 3 cells after the title span stay blank at every time, and the field resumes after them; `WAVE-A4` every pixel with `E < 0.02` stays dark through a period and is blank in the raster, and pixels with `E > 1.9` and vignette > 0.8 swing by ≥ 140 levels; `WAVE-A5` `s(0) = 0.3h`, `s(9) = 0.39h`, `s(27) = 0.21h`, period 36 s, and the raster's sources follow `s(t)` symmetrically about the middle row; `WAVE-A6` the screen sits right of the field with a never-lit 2-column gap, no screen pixel is above level 229 (10 % under the brightest crest), bar length is monotone in I, an antinode row's bar is longer than a node row's, and the screen is never in the title span; `WAVE-A7` field pixels are `Ramp(Ice)`, exactly 8 `Accent` source pixels, everything else blank.

### cosmic_microwave_background  (Deep Cosmos, M4, M12)
- **Intent**: the Planck-style oval sky map: smooth hot/cold anisotropies that slowly shimmer (not per-frame noise), with a small inset of the angular power spectrum whose first acoustic peak is the "early sound waves" the title names.
- **Model**: an isotropic Gaussian random field by spectral synthesis, `f = (1/N) Σ sin(kᵢ·(x/ASPECT, y) + φᵢ + ωᵢ t)` with N = 96 equal-power modes; `|kᵢ|` stratified-sampled so their density in `ln ℓ` follows `D_ℓ·B_ℓ²` — `D_ℓ` an approximate Planck 2018 ΛCDM TT spectrum (Sachs–Wolfe plateau, first acoustic peak at ℓ = 220 ≈ 5× plateau, trough, 2nd/3rd peaks, damping), `B_ℓ² = exp(−(ℓ/520)²)` a "pixel beam" (it keeps the first peak at B² ≈ 0.84 and the second at ≈ 0.34, so the spots carry sub-structure), ℓ ∈ [10, 1000] — mapped to the screen as `|k| = (2π/6)·ℓ/220` rad/row (first peak = 6-row wavelength); directions from a jittered golden-ratio sequence over [0, π) along the ℓ-sorted modes; phases uniform; `|ωᵢ| ∈ [0.08, 0.35]` rad/s with random sign (slow shimmer, no drift); display saturates at `|f| = 2.7σ`, `σ = 1/√(2N)` (Planck colour bar ±300 μK ≈ ±2.7 σ_CMB); drawn only inside an ellipse covering 94 % of the sky rows (Mollweide-like). The readout `CMB  T=2.725K  dT~1e-5` is right-aligned on the title row when `h > 6` and `readout_room` allows (GEN-R13); the oval then leaves that row free.
- **Rendering**: the sky is a `Pixels` map (`w × 2h`), shaded every step in `update` from per-resize column and row sin/cos tables (the row table is rotated by `ωᵢt`, so no trig per pixel); the oval mask is per pixel.
- **Inset** (top-right, from row 1): the model's own `D_ℓ` for `ℓ ∈ [10, 1000]` on a log axis as a braille curve in `Ramp(Plasma)` 190 over a `Dim` baseline with decade ticks, the first peak marked at 255 and labelled `ℓ≈220` (`l~220`) on the inset's last row, and a dotted `Dim` cursor sweeping ℓ every 8 s. Size 4 : 1 cells (≤ 32 × 8, ≤ w/5 wide), the largest from 8 (or h/5) down to 5 rows whose bottom-left corner lies outside the oval; none below 80×20 or when nothing fits.
- **Inks**: sky pixels `ink::div(f/2.7σ)`, the theme's diverging map (ADR-0011); the inset in `Ramp(Plasma)` and `Dim`; the readout `Text`.
- **ASCII**: a pixel pair becomes `.:*#` by the contrast `|level − 128|` of the `stronger` pixel (CORE-R87).
- **Stylisation**: the shimmer is artistic: the real anisotropy pattern is fixed on the sky; the oval is a frame, not a true all-sky projection — ℓ is remapped so the ~1° first-peak spots are ~6 rows.
- **Acceptance**: `CMB-A1` `|f| ≤ 1`; `CMB-A2` smooth — horizontally adjacent cells differ by at most `Σ|kₓᵢ| / (ASPECT·N)`, and adjacent-cell correlation is ≥ 0.9 horizontally and ≥ 0.7 vertically; `CMB-A3` for seeds 0–7 a 200×50 frame has > 200 hot pixels (level > 160) and > 200 cold ones (< 96); `CMB-A4` isotropic, not stripy — the direction tensor `Σ k̂k̂ᵀ` has anisotropy ≤ 0.05 and the two-point correlation at equal aspect-corrected lags agrees across directions within 0.1 (mode ensemble at 0°/90°/±45°, 1–6 rows; and sampled on a 200×50 screen, horizontal vs vertical, 1–3 rows); `CMB-A5` CMB-like spectrum — `D_ℓ` peaks at ℓ ∈ [200, 240] with `D_220/D_10 ∈ [4.5, 6]`, a trough then a second peak, and damping; the most populated Δln ℓ = 0.5 band of modes is centred at ℓ ∈ [140, 300]; `CMB-A6` inside the oval each pixel is `div(f/2.7σ)` of the field at its centre, outside it is empty; `CMB-A7` at 120×40, 160×48 and 200×50 the inset exists, lies clear of the title row, its cells' sky pixels are empty, its curve's highest dot is at `ℓ` within 15 % of 220 and the peak's label sits inside it on its last row; the cursor moves the inset within 1 s; 60×18 has none.

### gravitational_lensing  (Deep Cosmos, M4, M12)
- **Intent**: a galaxy-scale strong lens in the style of the SLACS survey: a small golden elliptical in
  a dark field of faint blue background galaxies, a few of them stretched into tangential arcs round a
  faint dashed critical curve; every 14 s a larger spiral passes behind, slows as it lines up and closes
  into a bright Einstein ring for a few seconds.
- **Model**: singular isothermal sphere (SIS), the standard first model of a galaxy lens; in units of the
  Einstein radius `θE = R(0.4)`, `β = θ − θ̂`. Inverse ray-shooting on a lattice capped at 24 000 nodes
  (ADR-0012): each node stores its source-plane `β` (built in `resize`) and every step takes the source
  surface brightness there, which lensing conserves. Sources: a tileable 192 × 192 texture (period 6 θE)
  of 28 seeded exponential ellipses (scale 0.04–0.14 θE, axis ratio 0.35–1, peak `0.03 + 0.5 u³` with `u`
  uniform, so most are faint smudges and a few are bright), drifting at 0.12 θE/s; plus the hero spiral
  (radius 0.6 θE, exponential disk with two logarithmic arms of 20° pitch and contrast 0.55, and a bulge,
  seeded inclination `cos i ∈ [0.45, 0.95]` and position angle) crossing `±2.2 θE` along
  `2.2 (0.4 x + 0.6 x³)`, `x ∈ [−1, 1]`, so it slows near closest approach, at a seeded impact parameter
  `|p| < 0.35 · 0.6 cos i θE` (within 35 % of its minor semi-axis, so the lens axis crosses its bright
  disk on every pass), fading in and out over each pass. Lens light: a round Sérsic n = 2 core,
  `1.5 exp(−3.67 √(r / 0.2 θE))`, plus a faint `0.06 / (1 + (r / 0.2 θE)²)` halo, the sum tapered to
  zero by a smoothstep between 0.3 and 0.6 θE; below the faintest visible level it is halftoned with an
  ordered 4 × 4 Bayer threshold per node (kept at that level where `v / v_min` exceeds the threshold,
  fixed per resize), so the glow thins out into an even dot pattern instead of ending on an edge, and a
  dark gap is left between it and the critical curve. Brightness maps to levels through
  `Tone::asinh(0.08, 1.1)` (CORE-R81); levels below `MIN_VISIBLE_LEVEL` are dropped (CORE-R66). The
  critical curve `|θ| = θE` is drawn as 36 faint dashes where nothing shines. Display pixels sample the
  lattice bilinearly (`SampleMap`; 1:1 at 200 × 50).
- **Stylisation (stated)**: the relative motion is sped up enormously (real lens alignments change over
  millions of years); background galaxies are illustrative ellipses; colours are false (Ice for the blue,
  star-forming sources, Ember for the lens's old stars); the lens light is round like its mass, its faint
  edge is halftoned, and it is cut off inside the critical curve (tapered to zero by 0.6 θE) so the ring
  reads against dark sky — real lens galaxies' light extends well beyond θE (θE is typically about half
  their effective radius); lens and source light are not summed: each pixel shows the brighter, in its own
  colour.
- **Motion**: arcs slide round the ring as the field drifts; the hero forms, holds and breaks the ring
  each pass.
- **Resize**: lattice, `β` map, lens light and sample map; textures and pass state survive.
- **Inks**: source light `Ramp(Ice)`, lens light `Ramp(Ember)`, critical curve `Structure` 40. ASCII
  fallback: brightness ramp (shared with black_hole).
- **Captions**: `meta.captions = ["Einstein ring"]`; see LENS-A6.
- **Acceptance**: `LENS-A1` *(changed)* every SIS image maps back to `β` through `β = θ − θE θ̂`; `LENS-A2` *(changed)* for `|β| < θE` there are two images, on opposite sides, at `|θ| = θE ± |β|`, so both approach `θE` as `β → 0`; `LENS-A3` *(changed)* for `|β| > θE` there is one image, displaced outward by exactly `θE`; `LENS-A4` *(new)* surface brightness is conserved: every node shows the source brightness at its own `β` (recomputed from `θ`), so nothing lensed is brighter than the brightest source; `LENS-A5` *(new; was an unnumbered test)* an aligned hero lights the whole circle `|θ| = θE` and leaves `|θ| < 0.3 θE` free of source light; `LENS-A6` *(new)* `caption()` is `Einstein ring` exactly while the hero's arm-averaged surface brightness on the lens axis (`β = 0`, which the whole critical curve images) is ≥ 0.05, the ring is then closed, and every pass closes it for at least 2 s (checked over 400 seeded passes); `LENS-A7` *(new)* the field is a quiet ground: no field texel exceeds 0.75 (half the hero's peak), and without the hero at least 60 % of the frame is dark; `LENS-A8` *(new)* the lens glow fades out through an ordered halftone and leaves a gap for the ring: at 200 × 50, in 0.025 θE annuli from 0.2 to 0.95 θE, the share of lens-lit pixels starts full, is zero from 0.65 θE out, never rises by more than 0.1 outward, and passes through at least three annuli strictly between 10 % and 90 %; and every 4 × 4 block of a uniform light `k/16` of the faintest visible level keeps exactly `k` dots.

### constellation_map  (Earth & Sky, M4, M12)
- **Intent**: a real star chart.
  - Stars in their true colours (orange Betelgeuse and Antares, blue-white Rigel and Acrux), sized by
    magnitude, the brightest blooming in their own hue.
  - The figure in fine, bright braille strokes that stop short of each star, over a quiet, sparse RA/Dec
    graticule, and a star field that thickens toward the Milky Way, whose band is faint braille dust.
  - The name and epithet on rows of their own, below the figure.
- **Model**:
  - Projection and timing unchanged; a stored `Chart` per figure (tangent point, scale, centre), with
    `sky_of` inverting `screen_of` (inverse gnomonic).
  - Fit *(changed)*: the figure fills 0.8 of the chart area, the frame less its bottom 5 rows (a gap, the
    name, the epithet, a gap, the title), so no star or bloom sits on the name block.
  - B–V → `T_eff = 4600 K·(1/(0.92(B–V) + 1.7) + 1/(0.92(B–V) + 0.62))` (Ballesteros 2012), clamped to
    [2000, 30 000] K. Main-sequence fit; it saturates at blue-white for hot giants.
  - Graticule: steps as before (≤ 6 lines each way), 96 samples per line, `cos c ≤ 0.2` dropped, one dot in
    three along each line. Labels `+50°` (column 1, rows 1..h − 4) and `11h`/`11h30m` (row 0), ≤ 8 per
    axis, found in the same pass that draws the line. **Only from 120 columns**: below that it would
    crowd the figure.
  - Background: `0.12·cells` candidate stars (≤ 4000) with a steep magnitude distribution (level
    `30 + 110·u³`). Each chart keeps a candidate with probability `0.25 + 0.75·exp(−(b/12°)²)`, b the
    galactic latitude of its sky position (NGP α 192.859°, δ 27.128°, J2000), so Cygnus, Crux and
    Scorpius sit in a denser field than Ursa Major.
  - Strokes stop 0.9 row short of both stars and trace in at constant pen speed.
  - Star glyphs *(changed)*: `★` for magnitude ≤ 1.5, `✦` ≤ 2.5, `✧` ≤ 3.3, `·` fainter (ASCII `@ * + .`).
  - Blooms *(new, replacing the glints)*: stars of magnitude ≤ 1.5 get a Gaussian of half-block pixels
    round the glyph, σ = 1.3 px, peak `0.7·(1 − 0.3·mag/1.5)`, cut below 0.12 (about 2.4 px out), while
    fade ≥ 0.5. **False colour**: `Ramp(Ice)` for B−V < 0.4, `Ramp(Ember)` above, the ramp nearest the
    star's hue (a dimmed `Blackbody` would redden). The glyph sits on its bloom's colour. Unicode only.
  - Milky Way dust *(new)*: each braille dot is lit with probability
    `p(l, b) = 0.3·exp(−(b/12°)²)·clump·(1 − 0.85·lane)`, (l, b) the galactic coordinates of its cell's
    sky position (IAU 1958 pole and l of the NCP, 122.932°). `clump ∈ [0.3, 1]` is two octaves of value
    noise on 6° and 2° lattices in (l, b) (star clouds); `lane` is a soft dark lane for the Great Rift
    (b ≈ +2° ± 2.2°, l 25°–88°, ends tapered over 8°) and the Coalsack (centre l 302.8°, b +0.4°, from
    RA 12h50m, Dec −62.5°; radius 3°). Dots are `Dim` from 70 (fringe) to 170 (densest). A density of
    dots, not a fill, so the band thins out instead of ending at an edge. Per chart `p` is kept as one
    byte a cell (built in `resize`); one hash per cell gives its 8 dots a byte each, and a dot shows
    while its byte `< 256·p·fade`, so the dust holds still and fading thins it rather than dimming it.
    None on the title row. Unicode only (in ASCII the denser star field alone marks the band).
    **Stylised**: a picture of the band's surface brightness and its two best-known dark lanes, not a
    map.
  - The α-star name goes 3 cells right of the star (past its bloom), else left; it is dropped if neither
    fits. It is always on a star row, above the name block.
- **Raster**: two `Dots` layers in `update` (the graticule and dust, Unicode only; the strokes) and the
  blooms' `Pixels`; the dust densities in `resize`. Stars, pen and text are drawn in `render`, the sky
  layer first so background stars sit on the dust.
- **Inks**: stars `Blackbody(T_eff)` (glyph by magnitude; `·` while fading); strokes `Structure`
  **210** × fade; graticule `Dim` 34 × fade; graticule labels `Dim` 90 × fade; pen `Accent`; names `Text`;
  background stars `Dim`; blooms `Ramp(Ice)`/`Ramp(Ember)`; dust `Dim` 70–170.
- **Acceptance**: `CON-A1`: every edge references valid stars and every star is on an edge; charts put east left and north up, and the gnomonic projection matches analytic cases; `CON-A3`: the figure index advances every 12 s and wraps; `CON-A2`: a render at t = 0 has no `Structure` cells, non-decreasing after that; `CON-A4`: T_eff strictly decreases in B–V (to the clamp); Betelgeuse and Antares < 4000 K; Rigel, Acrux and Shaula > 9000 K; every figure star's cell is `blackbody(T_eff)`; `CON-A1` *(changed)*: figures fill 0.8 of the chart area (the frame less 5 rows), preserving shape; `CON-A5` *(changed)*: meridians deviate < 0.05 cell from their chord; every braille cell is a stroke (`Structure`) or `Dim` ≤ 171 (graticule and dust); labels on rows < h − 2; every figure has a label at 200×50; `CON-A6`: a stroke stops `STROKE_GAP` short of both stars, is absent at progress 0, reaches half the edge at 0.5, and is not drawn for an edge too short to leave a gap; `CON-A7` *(new)*: at 40×12 … 200×50 (7 sizes), fully typed, every figure's letter-spaced name is intact letter for letter and its epithet stands intact with no letter run into either end; from 20 rows, every figure star is at least two rows above the name row; `CON-A8` *(new)*: no graticule and no dust at 100×30 on Ursa Major's chart; `sky_of(screen_of(α, δ)) = (α, δ)`; the NGP has b = 90°, Deneb is (84.28°, +2.00°) ± 0.05° and Sgr A* (0°, 0°) ± 0.05°; at 200×50 Cygnus keeps more than twice as many background stars as Ursa Major. Dust at 200×50, over every chart: the lit share of dots falls strictly across |b| bands 0–5°, 5–10°, 10–15°, 15–20° (each sampled by > 1000 dots), is non-zero in the 10–15° band and ≤ 0.3 in the first; none on Ursa Major's chart or on the title row; on Cassiopeia, Cygnus and Crux the lit dots at fade 0.1 are non-empty and only grow as fade rises to 1; on Cassiopeia and Crux the dot count per 10 × 5-cell block varies (coefficient of variation > 0.2); `p` at the Coalsack's centre and in the rift (l = 60°) is at most 0.15 of the band's. Rendered: dust braille (`Dim` above the graticule's 34) covers over a quarter of Cygnus's cells and none of Ursa Major's; `CON-A9` *(new)*: at 200×50, every star of magnitude ≤ 1.5 has half-block bloom cells beside its glyph, in `Ice` (B−V < 0.4) or `Ember`, at most `0.7·255`; ASCII draws no ramp inks; the GEN-R13 test is kept. `CON-A10` *(M12 perf)*: `resize` builds only the showing chart's dust and background-star levels (one chart, not six: 72 ms at 500×150 before); `update` builds the others ahead in slices of ⌈(cells + stars)·dt/4 s⌉ per step (a chart per 4 simulated seconds), the showing chart and then the next ones first, so each is complete seconds before it shows, and a chart still incomplete when due (a resize just before a hand-over) is finished at once. Frames are identical to building every chart in `resize`: at 120×40 over a whole cycle of the six charts, with steps of 1/60 s and of 0.25 s, and across a resize.

---
## Batch B — particle systems (M5)
Shared helpers: `sciart_core::particles` (CORE-R28..R32). Pools are sized in `resize` from the area and are
bounded (GEN-R8); particles are respawned rather than reallocated. Positions are in **rows** unless stated
(x on screen = `cx + x·ASPECT`). Physics uses `dt` from `update` only.

### black_hole  (Deep Cosmos, M5, M12)
- **Intent**: a Luminet-style black hole (Luminet 1979; *Interstellar*): a black shadow, the thin
  lensing ring (secondary image) hugging it, and an inclined accretion disk whose far side is lensed up
  over the shadow and, as a lower arc, under it; the approaching (left) side burns white-gold, the
  receding side dull ember; the hot gas visibly streams in sheared spiral clumps; behind it a quiet field
  of steady point stars, crowding along a Milky Way band, lensed round the hole. The disk is the
  brightest thing on screen.
- **Model** (units `G = c = M = 1`): Schwarzschild null geodesics from the Binet equation
  `u'' = −u + 3u²` (`u = 1/r`), integrated once per scene with RK4 into a table `u(b, φ)` over 320
  impact parameters (asinh-spaced about `b_c = 3√3`) × 257 sweep angles `φ ∈ [0, 2π]`, plus each ray's
  end angle (capture at `u = ½` or escape at `u = 0`); beyond `b = 48` the first-order weak-field orbit
  and the second-order deflection are used. A camera ray at impact parameter `b` and screen angle `ψ`
  crosses the disk plane (inclination 80°) at `γ_n = γ₀(ψ) + nπ`, `γ₀ = atan2(cos i, −sin ψ sin i)`; the
  thin, opaque disk (`6 ≤ r ≤ 24`, ISCO to outer edge) is hit by the first crossing that lands on it —
  order 0 (direct image) or order 1 (the secondary image: the lensing ring above the shadow, the
  brightest photon subring, and the lower arc below it). The fainter n ≥ 2 subrings are omitted. Per hit
  the redshift is `g = √(1 − 3/r) / (1 + Ω b cos ψ sin i)`, `Ω = r^{-3/2}`: `√(1 − 3/r)` is the
  gravitational redshift times the transverse (time-dilation) Doppler shift, the denominator the
  line-of-sight Doppler shift. The emitted temperature follows the Newtonian (Shakura–Sunyaev)
  zero-torque thin-disk profile `T ∝ [r⁻³(1 − √(6/r))]^{1/4}` (peak at `r = 49/36 · 6`; the relativistic
  Page–Thorne correction is omitted), the bolometric weight `(g T)⁴`. A pixel sums `F = Σ (gT/T_peak)⁴ ·
  clump` over 2 × 2 supersamples (partial coverage cools and dims the edges) and shows the blackbody
  colour of the display temperature (see Stylisation). Nodes within 0.8 of the shadow's edge trace the
  order-1 image with 6 × 6 samples. Clumps: a log-normal multiplier (mean 1, σ_ln = 0.45, easing to 0
  between 0.8 and 1 × the outer radius so no clump shows past the feathered rim) over 48 `ln r`
  × 256 `φ` bins, made of two layers of seeded noise born 3 s apart and weighted `sin(π age / 6 s)` (so
  `w₀² + w₁² = 1`); each layer is carried by the disk flow — Kepler ω rescaled so the ISCO period is
  2.5 s, inward drift `dr/dt = −0.02 r` — in closed form (gas now at `(r, φ)` came from
  `ln r + 0.02 age`, turned by `ω(r)(1 − e^{−0.03 age})/0.03`), so differential rotation shears it into
  spiral streaks before it is replaced. Escaping rays look up the sky in the direction they came from
  (observer at `D = 60`: screen angle `b / D`, source angle `b / D − α(b)` with `α = φ_end − π`). Stars:
  2400 seeded points (a quarter crowd a tilted great circle, the Milky Way band), one per texel of a
  1024 × 512 equirectangular map, levels 40–150. Of the supersamples that see a texel on one side of
  the hole (`ψ ≥ 0` or `ψ < 0`; a star's direct and lensed images lie on opposite sides), only the one
  nearest the texel's centre carries its star, so each image is a single pixel and, as the sky turns by
  whole texels, never drops out between texels. Tracing runs in `resize` on a lattice capped at 24 000 nodes (ADR-0012;
  200 × 50 is traced 1:1); each step advects the clump texture, sums the hit list and looks up the sky.
  The camera fit uses the disk image's traced extent: the outer edge spans 90 % of the width, with one
  cell clear above and two below (title row).
- **Stylisation (stated)**: colour contrast is exaggerated: the display temperature is
  `T_peak · F^{3/4} = T_peak (gT/T_peak)³` with `T_peak = 6 000 K`; from 600 K to 4 500 K it is mapped
  onto 1 000–4 500 K (the coolest blackbody level up) and lifted in log T by the power 0.8, so the dim
  receding and outer disk glows as a continuous ember envelope round its clumps; above 4 500 K it is
  rolled off in log T as `x/(1 + x)` towards 10 000 K (never clipping), so the disk runs from white
  through gold to ember in visible colours (real disks peak in the UV or X-rays and their colour
  contrast is far smaller). The
  redshift `g` is computed exactly, but the display cubes its colour effect along with the radial
  temperature contrast, so the Doppler colour asymmetry is exaggerated by the same power; the ordering —
  approaching side brighter and bluer, the gravitational redshift towards the ISCO — is physical. Colour
  is false. The lensing ring is drawn at full coverage wherever it touches a pixel, so it reads as a
  one-pixel line (it is thinner than a pixel). Stars are points whose lensed images keep their brightness
  (magnification is not shown); sky rays deflected by more than π (the sky's own photon subrings, within
  ~0.15 M of the shadow's edge) are not traced and are drawn dark. The Milky Way is shown only as a
  crowding of stars, not as diffuse light. The outer 45 % of the disk is feathered to zero emission. The clumps are
  illustrative turbulence, regenerated every 6 s. The star field drifts slowly round the disk axis (once
  per 150 s). The disk is infinitely thin and opaque; plunging gas inside the ISCO is not drawn; the sky
  mixes an observer at infinity (for the disk) with one at `D = 60` (for the sky angles).
- **Motion**: clumps stream and shear with the differential rotation (inner orbit 2.5 s); the lensed
  star field drifts round the hole.
- **Resize**: lattice, hit list, per-node star texels and sample map; the geodesic table, image extent,
  star map and clump noise are size-independent and survive.
- **Inks**: disk `Blackbody` (the display temperature), stars `Star` (levels 40–150, below the disk's
  hottest level in every theme), shadow and empty sky blank. ASCII
  fallback: brightness ramp `.:-=+*#%@` from the brighter half-pixel's level.
- **Acceptance**: `BH-A1` ω strictly decreases with r and the ISCO period is 2.5 s; `BH-A2` *(changed)* the shadow is black: no ray with `b < b_c` receives sky light, and the only disk light inside the shadow is the direct image of the near half (the far half is seen only outside it);
