`BH-A3` *(changed)* the approaching half is bluer and brighter: `g(ψ = π) > g(ψ = 0)` at every radius and the mean Blackbody level of the left half of the image exceeds the right's; `BH-A4` *(changed)* the clumps ride the disk flow: a parcel orbiting at Kepler ω while drifting in at `dr/dt = −0.02 r` maps back to its birth position through the layer's closed-form transport, the two layers' weights satisfy `w₀² + w₁² = 1`, and the clump multiplier averages 1 with at least 5 % of bins below 0.6 and above 1.6; `BH-A5` *(new)* the geodesic table is right: rays are captured iff `b < 3√3` (outside the table's resolution there), for `b ≥ 20` the deflection matches `4/b + 15π/(4b²) + 128/(3b³)` within 1 %, and early on every ray is the straight line `u = sin φ / b` to first order; `BH-A6` *(new)* the order-1 (secondary) image lies outside the shadow; above the hole (`sin ψ ≥ 0`) it is a thin lensing ring, `b < b_c + 0.8`, drawn without gaps (at 120 × 40 and 200 × 50 every pixel it passes through above the shadow, where its gas is hot enough to show, is lit), and below it the wider lower arc; `BH-A7` *(new)* through the poles of the image (`cos ψ = 0`) there is no line-of-sight Doppler shift: `g = √(1 − 3/r)`, gravitational redshift times the transverse (time-dilation) Doppler shift; the emitted temperature peaks at `r = 49/36 · r_isco` and is 0 at the ISCO; `BH-A8` *(new)* the sky is a quiet field of steady point stars: over a full turn of the star field, stars light at most 5 % of the frame's pixels; however strongly lensed, a star shows in at most two nodes (one per image); and at 80 × 24, 120 × 40 and 200 × 50 over 20 s, a star image that vanishes with no star within two pixels has lasted at least 0.55 s on average; `BH-A9` *(new)* the disk is the hero and never clips: the display temperature starts at the coolest blackbody level, rises strictly and continuously (also at 4 500 K), lifts the dim disk (2 000 K shows above 2 600 K) and stays below 10 000 K, fewer than 5 % of the disk's pixels come within 3 % of it, and the disk's hottest level exceeds every star's (at 80 × 24, 120 × 40 and 200 × 50).

### galaxy_collision  (Deep Cosmos, M5, M12)
- **Intent**: two disk galaxies swing past each other and pull out tidal tails and a bridge (Toomre & Toomre 1972), drawn as a deep exposure: smooth glowing disks with turning spiral arms in two host colours, bright nuclei, and pink star-forming knots that bloom after the close approach and outlast it.
- **Model**: restricted three-body: two cores (masses `M1 = 1`, `M2 = 0.8`) on a bound, eccentric, softened (`ε = 0.3` of disk radius) orbit integrated with kick-drift-kick leapfrog at `dt` sub-stepped to ≤ 1/120 s; massless test stars (`clamp(0.3·area, 64, 3000)`, split ∝ mass; the force loop is O(N)) start on circular orbits `v = √(G M r² / (r² + ε²)^{3/2})` in disks of radius `Rd`, radii uniform in `r` from `0.1 Rd` (surface density ∝ 1/r, centrally concentrated) along two trailing log-spiral arms with scatter, one disk prograde and one inclined/retrograde, projected to the screen. Cores start at apocentre of an orbit with `a = 3.5 Rd`, `e = 0.63` (Kepler period mapped to 24 s). A cycle is 40 s (approach, pericentre ≈ 12 s, tails, second close passage ≈ 36 s) then restarts in place from a re-seeded configuration (galaxy 2 spin 40–140°), reusing the star buffer; the camera eases to frame both cores.
- **Rendering**: each host has an `f32` exposure buffer at `Pixels` resolution (`w × 2h`). Every step it decays by `exp(−dt/τ)`, `τ = 0.15 s`, and every star splats weight `1 − exp(−dt/τ)` bilinearly at its projected pixel (a star holding still accumulates ≈ 1), each nucleus a 3 × 3 kernel worth 3 stars.
  - **Spiral arms**: inside its disk a star's light is scaled by `1 + 0.75·cos ψ`, `ψ = 2(φ + 2.7·ln(r/0.1 Rd) − 2πt/30 s)`: a two-armed trailing density-wave pattern (pitch ≈ 20°) turning once per 30 s, fading out between `Rd` and `1.4 Rd`, so the arms persist while the stars' own initial arms wind up.
  - **Tails**: beyond the disk (weight `1 − inside`) a star's light is spread back along its path in 6 bilinear splats over its own exposure span `0.6 s × (0.7 + 0.6·hash)`, with a per-star sample phase, so the sparse streams read as streams, not speckle, and neighbouring streaks do not line up into a comb.
  - A pixel's level is `Tone::asinh(0.1, 14)` of the sum of both buffers times the cycle fade; its ink is the brighter host's ramp, except where their light overlaps: from a 30 % share the minority host stipples through on a hash per 2 × 2-pixel clump, up to 30 % of the pixels at an even share, so crossing discs show small patches of each other rather than a hard edge.
  - **Knots**: 5 % of the stars beyond `0.35 Rd` trace star-forming clouds. When the cores first come within `2.2 Rd`, a burst envelope (rise 1.5 s, e-folding 7 s) starts; a tracer where its host's exposure is high (`min(ρ/1.2, 1)`) and the gas is squeezed (on an arm's crest in the disk, anywhere in the tails and bridge) becomes a knot of strength `b` when `b ≥ 0.12`: a pink `Ramp(HAlpha)` core at `0.6 + 0.4 b` (never dimming a brighter knot) whose eight neighbours are brightened in their own colour, `0.3 b` of white orthogonally and `0.55×` that diagonally. Non-finite tracer positions are skipped.
- **Inks**: host 1 `Ramp(Ice)`, host 2 `Ramp(Ember)`, knots `Ramp(HAlpha)`; 70 faint background stars `·` in `Star` 60. ASCII: a pixel pair becomes `.:+*@` by the level of the `stronger` pixel (CORE-R87).
- **Stylisation**: all three ramps are false colour — real hosts are both blue-white disks with yellow bulges; colour here says which galaxy a star came from. Tail and bridge stars are exposed longer than disk stars (~0.6 s vs 0.15 s) to show the faint streams; the arms are an imposed density-wave pattern (the test stars feel no self-gravity); knots are a star-formation proxy (the model has no gas); the second close passage near 36 s is not a merger — a restricted three-body model cannot merge.
- **Acceptance**: `GAL-A1` the core pair conserves energy within 1 % over a full cycle (leapfrog), with pericentre at 1–2.2 `Rd`, and the centre of mass stays at rest; `GAL-A2` initial star speeds equal the softened circular speed, the split is ∝ mass and radii lie in `[0.1, 1] Rd`; `GAL-A3` after pericentre some stars are farther than `2·Rd` from both cores (tails form), none before; `GAL-A4` the cycle restarts at 40 s, resetting the burst and reusing the star buffer, and the restarted encounter matches a fresh one; `GAL-A5` the bilinear splat conserves weight: the weights at any in-bounds point sum to the deposit, none is negative, and off-buffer or non-finite points deposit nothing; `GAL-A6` at 200×50 (seed 42, t = 2 s) every pixel is empty, `Ramp(Ice)`, `Ramp(Ember)` or `Ramp(HAlpha)`, both host ramps light > 200 pixels and the brightest pixel is above level 200; the minority host shows nowhere below its 30 % share, and at an even share on `MIX_MAX ± 0.05` of the pixels, in whole 2 × 2 clumps; `GAL-A7` a knot's core is `Ramp(HAlpha)`, its 8 neighbours keep the host ramp and are brighter (orthogonal more than diagonal), and pixels two away are untouched; at 200×50 there are no knots before the close approach, ≥ 5 knot pixels at some frame 3–10 s after it and ≥ 30 still 8–10 s after it, and the tracers are 2.5–7.5 % of the stars; `GAL-A8` the arm pattern's crest lies at `φ = −2.7·ln(r/0.1 Rd)` (trailing), a ring's mean light is unchanged by it, it is absent beyond `1.4 Rd`, and it turns forwards at 1/30 turn per second.

### stellar_nursery  (Deep Cosmos, M5, M12)
- **Intent**: a glowing emission nebula seen as a narrowband image, lit by an off-frame cluster that carves dark dust pillars and lights their rims from one side, while gas streams onto protostars that ignite, flare bipolar jets, then shine.
- **Model** (dynamics NUR-A1..A3 unchanged): 3–5 protostars at seeded positions; a gas-particle pool (`≈ 0.03·area`, max 600) spawned in the nebula, accelerating toward the nearest protostar (`a = gm / (d² + ε²)`), absorbed within 0.8 rows, adding mass; a protostar ignites when mass ≥ `M_ign` (brightness ramps over 1 s) and then emits short-lived jet particles along a seeded axis; after 25 s of shining it fades (2.5 s) and is replaced by a new protostar elsewhere. Gas is stored in polar coordinates about its target: only the radial motion follows the softened gravity, the swirl is kinematic (pure rotation), which keeps NUR-A1 exact; new gas targets the nearest *accreting* protostar. Jets are pulsed (Herbig–Haro knots); jet pool `clamp(area/60, 4, 240)`.
  - **Source**: the ionising cluster sits off-frame at a seeded upper corner (side `s = ±1`, direction `d̂ = (s·sin 35°, −cos 35°)`, y down); its light bleeds in from that corner.
  - **Nebula**: a slowly drifting, separably domain-warped sum of 12 sines, log-uniform wavelengths 5–42 rows, weights `U(0.5, 1)·(λ/42)^0.8` (a 1/f-like spectrum: filaments with texture), plus a brightness gradient towards the source; shaded per half-block pixel in `update`. Exposure `v = peak·(1 − e^{−1.6 t})^1.5`, `t = z/2.6`: a shoulder, not a clip, with `peak` 0.85 for [O III] (below white, so its filaments keep texture) and 0.95 for Hα. Levels below 24 are left black.
  - **HOO palette**: `Ramp(Ice)` [O III] where an ionisation proxy (rising towards the source and in the young stars' bubbles, falling in dense gas, which recombines to Hα) exceeds ½, else `Ramp(HAlpha)`; the seam is stippled over `smoothstep((ion − 0.475)/0.05)`.
  - **Pillars** (static per size, at pixel resolution): 2–3 per sky, each with its own height (0.35–0.75 h), lean of the tip towards the source (0.03–0.16 w), base half-width (0.04–0.07 of `max(w, 110)` columns, so narrow terminals keep columns, not sticks), neck (0.45–0.6 of the base) and head (1.9–2.3× the neck, decaying over s ≈ 0.24 below a round dome). The edges carry lobes of `±30 %` with periods 0.3–0.6 of the pillar's length, the two sides out of step (necks and bulges), growing in below the head, plus `±2 %` roughness, and 1–2 round side knobs (0.35–0.6 of the local half-width, half-emerged).
  - **Rims** are ionisation fronts lit by `smoothstep((n̂·d̂ + 0.2)/1.0)`, `n̂` the outward normal of the 7 × 7-smoothed outline: faces edge-on to the source keep a dim rim (the diffuse ionising field and the photoevaporative flow), faces turned away and lobe or knob undersides stay dark. Two pixels deep (the inner at 0.45), `Ramp(HAlpha)` 60–250, brightest at the tip and falling to 0.55 at the base.
  - **Dust**: two-octave value noise (6 and 2.5 px) over `Ramp(HAlpha)` 0–28, plus up to 24 levels of limb brightening within 5 px of a lit face (scaled by the tip weight), so a pillar reads as a lit 3-D column, not a cut-out.
  - **Stars**: an embedded protostar is drawn only while it sits in a pillar: a soft Gaussian `Ramp(Ember)` glow (σ = half its radius, 2.0–3.4 px with mass, peak 0.5–0.75) that only brightens what is under it and stays below the rims. In the open nebula an accreting hub shows only through its infalling gas. A shining star is a `✦` with dot spikes in `Blackbody` 22 000 K over a `Ramp(Ice)` glare `0.85·b·(1 − r/R)²` to `R = 3.5·b` px that only brightens.
  - **Flow**: gas streaks span the last 0.35 s of infall at `Particle` `130 + 125·closeness`; jets are `Accent` streaks of 0.3 s of flight that become knots after 60 % of their life. Gas, jets and spikes are braille dots over the nebula (`blit_over`).
- **Inks**: nebula `Ramp(HAlpha)` / `Ramp(Ice)`; pillars `Ramp(HAlpha)` (dark dust, lit rims); embedded protostars `Ramp(Ember)`; shining stars `Blackbody` and a `Ramp(Ice)` glare; gas `Particle`; jets `Accent`. ASCII: nebula by level (`.:+#`, the `stronger` pixel, CORE-R87); a cell of plain dust is blank, so pillars read as silhouettes inside their lit rims.
- **Stylisation**: a narrowband HOO composite (Hα red, [O III] blue, false colour); each pixel shows its dominant line, channel blending is not modelled; embedded protostars are overlaid as infrared sources (a composite, not HOO); a young star's glare is drawn in the [O III] colour of the small high-ionisation bubble it would carve; pillar shapes are procedural.
- **Acceptance**: `NUR-A1` a gas particle's distance to its target protostar is non-increasing until absorption (for dt ≤ 1/60); `NUR-A2` protostar mass is non-decreasing while it accretes and ignition happens exactly when mass first reaches `M_ign`; `NUR-A3` particle pools never exceed capacity; `NUR-A4` a face turned to the source is fully lit, one turned away or a flat mask is dark, and lighting turns on smoothly with the facing angle; at 160×48 (seeds 3, 8) ≥ 85 % of well-lit rim pixels have open sky on the source side within 3 px, and down a lit side the surface light hardly ever flips between rows (≤ 1/8 of row pairs); for seeds 1, 3, 5, 8, 13 at 160×48, down each pillar's body the source-side surface is lit in ≥ 2/3 of rows and its dark rows come in ≤ 3 runs (shadows under lobes and knobs, not a zipper); `NUR-A5` for seeds 1–6 every pillar tip is offset from its base towards the source by at least 3 % of the width; `NUR-A6` nebula pixels are only `Ramp(HAlpha)` or `Ramp(Ice)` and the [O III] fraction in the source half exceeds the far half's by > 0.2; after 20 s at 120×40 (seeds 8, 21) no [O III] pixel exceeds `0.85·255` and fewer than 5 % sit within 4 levels of the brightest; `NUR-A7` for seeds 1–12 the head is ≥ 1.3× the narrowest neck, the width down the body turns at least once, and each pillar has a knob; at 160×48 (seeds 3, 8) deep dust spans ≥ 12 distinct levels, all ≤ 28, and dust within 2 px of a lit rim averages ≥ 5 levels brighter; over 8 s at 120×40 (5 seeds) an accreting protostar outside the dust has no `Ember` pixels, and one inside has ≥ 3 distinct levels peaking ≤ 0.75·255, below the rims.

### brownian_motion  (Forces & Waves, M5, M12)
- **Intent**: a dark-field view of a micron-sized grain (like the particles Brown saw released from pollen grains; the pollen grains themselves, tens of µm across, barely move). The grain is a lit amber sphere jittering in a field of fine, cold solvent specks, and it leaves a 22 s ember random-walk trail whose newest 5 s glow brightest. Specks that bounce off the grain flash. A faint rms-displacement ring grows from a marked origin, and the grain wanders inside and outside it. The ring's radius is `√⟨r²(t)⟩` of free Langevin (Ornstein–Uhlenbeck) motion, which tends to `√(4Dt)` for `t ≫ 1/γ`. The module doc and `description` read "A micron-sized grain jittering among solvent molecules (Langevin dynamics)".
- **Model**: grain obeys the Langevin/Ornstein–Uhlenbeck equation `dv = −γ v dt + σ √dt ξ` (γ = 2 s⁻¹, σ chosen so the diffusion constant `D = σ²/(2γ²)` gives ~3 rows/√s), exact OU update for the velocity; the joint position–velocity OU transition is sampled exactly; walls are exact specular reflections via folding (method of images). Solvent is `0.12·area` molecules (at most 1600) moving ballistically with Maxwell–Boltzmann velocities; they reflect off the walls and the grain, and a hit sets that molecule's glow to 1, which decays as `e^{−t/0.3 s}`. The trail is sampled at 32 Hz (4 per 0.125 s MSD tick) and keeps 704 samples (22 s). Ring epochs: at each epoch start the ring's centre becomes the grain's current (folded) position; its radius is the exact free-space rms displacement `√msd_ou(age)` = `√(4D[t − (1 − e^{−γt})/γ])`. An epoch lasts `epoch_len(half)`, the age at which that radius reaches 0.8 of the grain box's half short side, capped at 16 s (≈ 8.3 s at 80×24, 16 s at 200×50); the ring fades in and out over 0.8 s at each end. The grain radius is `0.075·min(rows, w/ASPECT)`, clamped to [0.6, 3.5] rows; it is drawn only, and the grain's own dynamics (γ, σ, D) do not depend on it.
- **Inks**: solvent `Ramp(Ice)` at `0.3 + 0.35·speed`, or at its glow when that is higher, capped at 0.68; a glow above 0.35 draws the molecule as a 5-dot bead. The trail is `Ramp(Ember)`: segments under 5 s old at `0.8 − 0.1·age/5 s`, older ones fading from 0.55 to 0.2 at the 22 s tail (`0.2 + 0.35·fresh^1.2`); the newest 3 s are a two-dot stroke (the segment doubled one dot across its direction), older trail a one-dot line. The hot head is brighter than the ring and every flash, so the hero path is never painted over. The grain is a `Pixels` half-block sphere in `Ramp(Ember)`: `0.3 + 0.55·Lambert + 0.15·Lambert^12`, lit from the upper left. The ring is `Ramp(Ice)` at `0.2 + 0.25·fade`, one dot in two, with a 5-dot cross at its centre. Readout `Text` at `title::READOUT_LEVEL`. Palette ownership in the dynamics group: the six named ramps cannot give four scenes eleven unshared colours, so each pair a scene *identifies* things by is kept apart from its neighbours'. `double_pendulum` owns Aurora + Hα (its twins); `three_body` uses Ember + Ice + Plasma (no Aurora); `brownian_motion` uses Ember (grain) + Ice (solvent); `strange_attractors` uses all six, one per attractor, where a palette names a shape, not a body, so its reuse does not confuse identities. Every palette is false colour (ADR-0015).
- **Readout** (GEN-R13): `D 1-path 2.31  true 2.25 rows^2/s  t 42s` (ASCII, right-aligned), naming the estimator as this one grain's time-averaged MSD at a 2 s lag with the exact OU inertial correction (a single grain's `r²/4t` from the start never converges). It shows `D 1-path ...` until 80 lag samples (10 s of lag samples, about 12 s of track, since the first sample lands once the 2 s lag window has filled) have accumulated: before that one path holds under one independent 2 s window and the estimate swings by a factor of four. The estimate accumulates over the whole run, so it converges towards the true D. When it would reach the title trailing parts (t, then the true D) are dropped, never clipped, and the readout is hidden when even `D 1-path …` does not fit.
- **Raster**: solvent, trail and ring are `Dots`; the grain is `Pixels`. Both are rasterised in `update` and `resize`, and `render` blits the dots, then the pixels, then the readout. In ASCII a grain cell is `@` (both halves) or `o`. Nothing is drawn on the readout row.
- **Stylised**: the specks are decorative: they exchange no momentum with the grain, whose kicks are the Langevin noise term, and a flash marks a speck bouncing off the grain, not the kick that moves it. Speeds are scaled for visibility: a micron-sized grain is some 10¹⁰× heavier than a water molecule, so by equipartition the molecules move ~10⁵× faster. The ring is the free-space ensemble rms (walls ignored); an epoch ends before the ring reaches 0.8 of the box's half short side, but a grain near a wall still sees the wall. The grain's velocity memory 1/γ = 0.5 s is stretched ~10⁶× (a real micron grain in water forgets its velocity in ~0.1 µs), so the ballistic start of the ring and of the MSD correction is visible; D itself is a free choice of units. The readout is one path's estimate: over a 60 s window it scatters by about ±30 % around the true D (it is labelled `1-path` and shows `true` beside it). The colours are false colour.
- **Acceptance**: `BRO-A1` the stationary velocity variance per axis converges to `σ²/(2γ)` (seeded statistical test, 5 %); `BRO-A2` the ensemble MSD at time t is `4Dt` within 10 % for t ≫ 1/γ (seeded, many walkers); `BRO-A3` the grain and molecules always stay inside the box; the trail length is ≤ 704; `BRO-A4` a molecule sent into the grain reflects with glow 1, and one second later its glow is `e^{−1/0.3}` (1e-9); `BRO-A5` 4000 free stationary-start OU walkers lie inside `√msd_ou(t)` with probability `1 − e^{−1}` ± 0.031 (4 s.e.) at t = 1, 4 and 12 s; `epoch_len(half) ∈ [0, 16]`, with `√msd_ou(epoch_len) = 0.8·half` when below the cap; in the scene the ring age never exceeds the epoch, and each restart recentres on the grain (≥ 2 restarts in 3 epochs); the fade is 0 at both ends and 1 mid-epoch; `BRO-A6` the trail is 704 samples = 22 s; the grain's lit pixels are within 20 % of `π(2r)²`; the readout row has no dots; the trail value is 0.8 at age 0, 0.2 at the tail and non-increasing with age, and for its newest 5 s it is ≥ 0.7 and above both the ring's brightest (0.45) and the flash cap (0.68); the newest 3 s of trail are a two-dot stroke (two dots per column when shallow, per row when steep) and older trail is a one-dot line; `BRO-A7` (readout) the estimate is this one grain's time-averaged MSD at a 2 s lag, inertia-corrected, within tolerance of the true D over a long run; there is no estimate and the readout reads `D 1-path ...` at 11.5 s (seed 8), and an estimate at 12.5 s.

### molecular_dynamics  (States of Matter, M5, M12)
- **Intent**: a Lennard-Jones fluid, drawn as glowing balls on the terminal's black, is slowly heated and cooled. A crystal melts into a hot gas of streaking atoms, which condenses back into droplets and crystallites. Each atom's colour shows its own kinetic energy on a blackbody ramp: cold atoms glow copper-red, hot ones white-blue. Hot atoms leave faint ice-blue streaks, a long exposure of their paths. On large terminals a small instrument at the top right shows the live speed histogram against the 2-D Maxwell–Boltzmann law; atoms pass behind it.
- **Model**: reduced LJ units, `U(r) = 4(r⁻¹² − r⁻⁶)` shifted at cutoff 2.5, in **2-D**; constant reduced density ρ = 0.25, inside the 2-D LJ vapour–liquid dome (r_c = 2.5 truncated-shifted: T_c ≈ 0.46, ρ_c ≈ 0.33–0.35, triple point ≈ 0.40); per-terminal layout: rows per σ `s = clamp(√(ρ·A₁/240), S_MIN, 2.5)` and `N = clamp(round(ρ·A₁/s²), 8, 240)` with `A₁` the interior area in σ² at one row per σ; above ≈ 250×50 the scale clamps and ρ falls. There is no box border (M12): the box spans the whole canvas above the title row (rows `0..h − 1` when `h ≥ 8`, else all rows), so the walls are the screen edges. `S_MIN = 1/(R_MIN·√3/2) ≈ 1.029` (R_MIN = 2^{1/6}): the start crystal's rows are then exactly one terminal row (two pixels) apart, and the first row sits on a pixel centre, so the single-pixel atoms of 80×24 fall on evenly spaced pixel rows (a scale of 1.1 left a blank pixel row every ≈ 7 crystal rows). 80×24 gets N = 205. Scales between about 1.07 and 1.15 (≈ 90×28) can still show one such gap; snapping the scale would break the fixed density. One simulated second = 6 τ; velocity-Verlet with `dt_md ≤ 0.004` sub-steps per frame; cell list for O(N) forces; reflecting walls; atoms start on a triangular crystal; below `r = 0.8` the potential continues linearly so forces stay finite; a 30 s temperature cycle `T = 0.25 + 0.75·u²`, `u = (1 − cos 2πt/30)/2` (below T_c for about half the cycle, ≈ 2 T_c at the peak), by gentle velocity rescaling (Berendsen, τ = 0.5 s).
- **Stylisation (stated)**: *false colour*. An atom's colour is `Blackbody` level `255·u`, i.e. `K(e) = 1000 K · 30^u`, with `u = min(1, ln(1 + ē/0.08) / ln(1 + 4/0.08))`. ē is the atom's kinetic energy `v²/2` (LJ units) smoothed by an EMA with τ = 0.4 s: one atom's instantaneous energy is exponentially distributed, so without smoothing a crystal at one temperature reads as speckle; the smoothed value is a local kinetic temperature. On every (re)seed each atom's ē starts at the mean kinetic energy per atom (2-D equipartition, ⟨v²/2⟩ = T), so the opening crystal is one colour. Reduced LJ temperature is not kelvin; the blackbody ramp is used only because "hotter = whiter" reads at a glance (ē = 0.25 ≈ 3 400 K orange, ē = 1 ≈ 9 000 K white-blue). Atoms are drawn as balls of radius 0.5 σ, a display choice: LJ atoms have no hard edge. Only a ball's centre follows the colour key exactly: the shading moves a pixel's level by about ±25 and the edge antialiasing dims rim pixels by up to 25 %, so a limb reads somewhat cooler than its atom. Streaks are a long exposure, not a physical field.
- **Draw** (everything is rasterised in `update`/`resize`; `render` only blits): *Streaks*: after every Verlet sub-step each atom deposits `(ē − 0.35)₊² · h` into a `raster::Density` at its display pixel (the density spans `⌈w/2⌉ × ⌈h/2⌉` cells so that its dots are the box's `w × 2h` pixels), so atoms in droplets and crystals leave nothing. The density fades with `exp(−dt/0.12 s)`, is tone-mapped with `Tone::asinh(0.02, 0.25)` and shaded into `raster::Pixels` in `Ramp::Ice`, capped at 0.2 of the ramp (level 51): below the dimmest rim pixel (≈ 64) of any atom that streaks (level ≈ 110), so atoms stay brighter than streaks in every theme, Mono included. *Balls*: atoms are painted over the streaks as half-block discs with Lambert shading `I = 0.35 + 0.65·max(0, n·L)` (light from the upper left) and 2×2-supersampled edge coverage; the level is `(bb + 70·(I − 0.7))·(0.75 + 0.25·coverage)` with `bb = 255·u(ē)`, so the lit side runs hotter and the limb cooler, like a glowing sphere. Balls under 1.25 px in radius (80×24) are single pixels, so neighbours keep a dark gap.
- **Inset** (`MD-A7`, `MD-A8`): shown when `w ≥ 100` and `h ≥ 30`: a 26 × 6-cell panel at the top right (column `w − 27`, row 0) over a blank one-cell margin; below that size it would hide over a tenth of the box and its bars and curve merge into one braille blob. Atoms and streaks pass behind it, and the start crystal is centred in the part of the box below the panel, so the opening frame is clear. Row 0 reads `speeds` in the bars' colour (`Blackbody` at `255·u(1)`) at the left and `Maxwell-Boltzmann` (`Text` 150, the curve's ink) at the right. Rows 1–5 are a braille plot of 26 speed bins over `|v| ∈ [0, 4]`; the last bin also holds every faster atom. Bars are the fraction of atoms per bin, smoothed by an EMA with τ = 0.3 s, each in the blackbody colour of its bin's energy, so the inset doubles as the colour key. The curve is one dot per bin at the exact 2-D MB bin probability `exp(−v₀²/2T) − exp(−v₁²/2T)` (the tail for the last bin) at the *measured* kinetic T, in `Text` 150; a braille cell has one ink, so the curve is drawn first and a bar reaching its cell recolours it (grey marks show where the live bars fall short). The MB peak is scaled to 92 % of the plot height.
- **Captions** (GEN-R12): `meta.captions = ["cool atoms cling", "hot atoms scatter"]`; `caption()` returns the first while the thermostat *target* is below the 2-D critical temperature T_c ≈ 0.46 of the LJ potential truncated and shifted at 2.5 (Smit & Frenkel 1991, J. Chem. Phys. 94, 5663; ≈ 0.53 untruncated), else the second. It follows the thermostat, not a measured phase. `meta.title` stays `Condensation · cool atoms cling`.
- **Label** (GEN-R13): right-aligned on row `h − 1`, the longest form that fits `title::readout_room`: `T 0.40 -> 0.25  U/N -2.63  N 240  LJ units`, `T 0.40 -> 0.25  U/N -2.63  N 240`, `T 0.40 -> 0.25`, `T 0.40`, else none. The caption says whether the thermostat target is below or above T_c (MD-A9); the arrow in the readout shows which way T is being driven.
- **Inks**: atoms `Blackbody`; streaks `Ramp(Ice)`; inset bars and the word `speeds` `Blackbody`; MB curve `Text` 150; readout `Text` at `title::READOUT_LEVEL`. No box, no bond dots.
- **ASCII**: a cell containing a ball pixel is `o`; a streak-only cell is `.`. The inset's braille falls back to dot counts, as everywhere else.
- **Acceptance**: `MD-A1` the force equals `−dU/dr` (finite-difference check) and vanishes beyond the cutoff; `MD-A2` with the thermostat off, total energy drifts < 1 % over 5 simulated seconds; `MD-A3` atoms always stay inside the box; `MD-A4` the thermostat moves temperature toward its target; `MD-A5` the layout (border-free interior) keeps ρ within 5 % of 0.25 wherever `s` is not clamped, 80×24 gets N = 205 and its start crystal's atoms occupy evenly spaced pixel rows exactly two pixels apart, and at 80×24 (seeded) the mean coordination within 1.5 σ in the cold phase (28–33 s) is > 2× that around the peak (14–19 s) and > 3.5; `MD-A6` `u(e)` is non-decreasing in e, 0 at e = 0 (and for NaN or negative e), 1 from e = 4 on (and at +∞); level `255·u` of `Blackbody` is `1000 K · 30^u` within 1 %, `K(0.25) < 4 000 K` and `K(1) > 6 000 K`; at 200×50 near the hot peak every lit pixel is `Blackbody` (ball) or `Ramp(Ice)` (streak), with more ball pixels than atoms and at least one streak pixel; the streak cap `0.2·255` is below the dimmest rim level of the coolest streaking atom; at t = 0 (80×24, 120×40, 200×50) every atom's level `255·u(ē)` is within 10 levels of every other's; `MD-A7` for T ∈ {0.25, 0.5, 1} the MB bin probabilities sum to 1 within 1e-12, and at 200×50 (seed 7), after 6 s from t = 12 s, the smoothed histogram sums to 1 within 1e-9 and its L1 distance to the MB bins at the measured T is < 0.25; `MD-A8` at 200×50, 120×40, 100×30, 99×40, 160×29, 80×24, 40×12 and 30×7, with a title row (h ≥ 8) every cell of row `h − 1` is `Text` or `Background`; the inset exists exactly when `w ≥ 100` and `h ≥ 30`, starts right of the title span, ends above row `h − 1` and before the last column; at 120×40, 100×30 and 200×50 no atom starts in the panel or its margin, and at t = 0, 5 and 60 s every box pixel there is empty; `MD-A9` `caption()` is `cool atoms cling` exactly when `target_temperature(t) < 0.46` (sampled every 0.05 s over a 30 s cycle), and `meta.captions` is the two-caption list.
- **Perf** (200×50, step + render, 120 s run, the four M12 matter scenes in one release run, load ≈ 30 on 14 cores): mean 0.47 ms, heaviest 1-s window median 0.59 ms (at 8 s). Live bytes at 500×150 are 2.03 MiB (TEST-R7 ceiling 4 MiB): the streak buffer is at pixel resolution, a quarter of a dot-resolution one.

### particle_accelerator  (Forces & Waves, M5, M12)
- **Intent**: charged particles made visible by the tracks they leave in a magnetic field, in two
  acts. Act 1 (26 s), *collider event*: the end view of a general-purpose LHC-style detector with
  bunch crossings every 2.2 s from 0.05 s (the first event is on screen within three frames), stiff jets, curling loopers, calorimeter towers, the odd muon pair
  bending back in the return yoke, and a K⁰_S vee; one hero event at a time, the previous one
  fading out. Act 2 (18 s), *bubble chamber*: a 1960s heavy-liquid (propane/freon) chamber
  photograph every 3 s with a dim negative beam from the left, a flashing interaction star, e⁺e⁻
  pairs spiralling inward over several turns, δ rays curling off the beam, fiducial crosses and
  film grain in a round window.
- **Model**: one exact path for both acts, `Spiral`: m = 1, `v̇ = (−k + iqB)v`, so
  `z(t) = z₀ + v₀(1 − e^{−λt})/λ`, `λ = k − iqB`: curvature radius `p(t)/B`, `p(t) = p₀e^{−kt}`,
  turning clockwise on screen for qB > 0 (y down); k = 0 is a circle, B = k = 0 a line.
  Act 1 is to scale in the transverse plane, in metres: `B → 0.3·B` with B = 3.8 T in the solenoid
  and −1.8 T in the return yoke, so `ρ = pT/(0.3B)` (1 GeV ⇒ 0.877 m). Radii (m): tracker layers
  0.16 (Phase-1 pixel layer 4), 0.43, 0.80, 1.08; ECAL 1.29–1.75; HCAL 1.81–2.95; coil 2.98–3.18;
  muon stations (12-sided) 4.0/4.35, 4.9/5.25, 5.9/6.25, 6.95/7.3. A charged track stops at the
  ECAL face at its first crossing of R = 1.29 m, from any start point: about the circle's centre
  Z = z₀ + c (c the pole, ρ = |c|), `cos(θ − arg Z) = (R² − |Z|² − ρ²)/(2|Z|ρ)`, and the swept
  angle to the nearer root in the turning sense over |qB| is the time; if `|Z| + ρ < R` it is a
  looper and draws its whole circle (the transverse projection of a helix). Muons cross to the
  coil's outer radius, then continue with `qB·(−1.8/3.8)` to 7.3 m. Each crossing: 6–12 soft
  tracks, pT = 0.45·u^{−1/2} GeV (≤ 20); with p = 0.7 a back-to-back dijet (4–7 tracks each,
  2–25 GeV, σφ = 0.1, plus 2–4 photon ECAL towers); p = 0.3 a dimuon (20–45 GeV); p = 0.3 an
  isolated electron (8–40 GeV); p = 0.55 a K⁰_S of p_K = 1.5–6 GeV flying `βγ·cτ·(−ln u)`
  (cτ = 2.684 cm, clamped to 0.12–0.45 m) unseen (dashed), then a two-body decay K⁰_S → π⁺π⁻ in
  the transverse plane: p* = 0.206 GeV back to back at a uniform rest-frame angle, boosted along
  the flight, so the pions' momenta sum to p_K along it and their invariant mass is m_K. Towers
  at the exit azimuth, E = pT (η ≈ 0): electrons all in the ECAL, hadrons 25 % ECAL / 75 % HCAL;
  length `depth·ln(1 + E/0.5)/ln(1 + 80/0.5)`, capped at the depth, 0.087 rad wide (one 5° HCAL
  tower; the ECAL tower is the 5 × 5 crystals in front of it). Events stay bright 1.6 s, then fade
  with τ = 0.45 s (about ¼ when the next crossing arrives, < 0.1 half a second later); each
  crossing drops the events ≥ 3.8 s old, so at most two share the screen. Pools: 192 tracks,
  144 towers. The view spans 2.6 m at a fit radius ≥ 20 rows and zooms in to 1.8 m at ≤ 10 rows
  (linear between), so at 80×24 the tracker is ≥ 30 columns across and the outer calorimeters
  are cropped instead.
  Act 2 in chamber units (stage height 1), B = 1: beam of 5–8 π⁻-like tracks from the left edge,
  p = 6·width (nearly straight), bubble density 0.3; one ends at an interaction star on a nucleus
  of the liquid (nuclear break-up allows any prong count and net charge) with 3–6 secondaries
  (p = 0.06 + 0.5u², k = 0.12, bubble density `min(1, 0.45 + 0.05/p)`); 1–2 photons convert
  0.08–0.3 away into e⁺e⁻ (energy 0.15–0.45 shared 25–75 %, opening ±0.04 rad, density 0.55)
  that keep 0.6 of their radius per turn (`k = −ln 0.6·B/2π`) and are drawn for 4 turns; 0–2 δ
  rays per beam track (e⁻, p = 0.04–0.1, k = 1.0, ~70° off the beam). Bubbles are kept per sample
  with the track's density (fixed hashes, no shimmer). Pool: 64. The round window's rim fits
  between the stage's top and bottom rows.
- **Motion**: tracks grow outward from their origin over 0.35 s (δ rays and V⁰ daughters after
  their parent, towers after their track), events fade; the primary vertex flashes for 0.5 s as its
  star appears; each photograph appears, holds and goes dark for 0.25 s as the film advances; each
  act fades in and out over 0.8 s.
- **Resize**: act-1 paths are in metres and act-2 paths in stage heights, so they survive a resize;
  `Dots`, `Pixels` and the glow grid are rebuilt over the stage (the glow restarts dark). The stage
  is every row above the title row while the title shows.
- **Inks** (false colour): act 1 tracks `Ramp::Ember` by pT (dim red soft, pale gold stiff),
  electrons and ECAL towers `Ramp::HAlpha`, HCAL towers `Ramp::Ice`, muons `Ramp::Aurora`, the
  K⁰_S flight dashed at the softest hadron's 0.45, detector outlines `Structure` 48 (below every
  flavour's fresh track in every theme, mono included; the outer two muon wheels at 0.7 of that, so
  a wide terminal's edges stay quiet), and an `Ember` glow (a `raster::Glow`,
  CORE-R85, `Tone::asinh(1.5, 40)`, τ 0.3 s, ≤ 0.7 of the ramp) from each crossing's IP flash (a
  bright pixel and a softer cross) and from fresh deposits for 0.6 s after a tower appears (a soft
  3 × 3 spot, deposited in `update` at a dt-independent rate), carried under dotted cells
  (`Dots::blit_over`, CORE-R84). Act 2 is sepia film on black: bubbles, rim, crosses and grain in
  `Ramp::Ember`, beam bubbles at 0.5 of the secondaries' brightness, δ rays 0.75, pairs 0.85. Readout
  `Text` at `READOUT_LEVEL`: `event N  B 3.8 T` or `B 3.8 T` (from the solenoid constant);
  `film frame N` or `frame N`. An old event recedes under the outlines as it fades (it is at about
  a quarter when the next crossing arrives), so the new one is the only bright thing.
- **Stylisation**: growth over 0.35 s (real tracks form in ns); crossings every 2.2 s (really
  25 ns) with the previous event left fading on screen, where an event display shows one
  triggered crossing; energies and multiplicities are illustrative, not a generator tune; η ≈ 0
  (pT = p) and the V⁰ decays in the transverse plane; the K⁰_S flight is clamped to 0.12–0.45 m so
  the vee is always visible in the tracker (at p_K = 1.5 GeV about 77 % of real decays fall inside
  12 cm); the IP flash, the deposit glow and the chamber's vertex flash are decorative; the
  chamber's units, field and energy losses are illustrative, and the bubble densities of the beam
  and the pairs are set for legibility (both are near minimum ionising in reality, within ~10 %);
  only the slow secondaries' 1/p rise follows dE/dx; colours are false.
- **Perf** (update + render, fastest of 5 replays per frame over 110 s at 1/60 s, seed 42, machine
  load ≈ 14): 200×50 mean 0.14 ms, worst 0.24 ms; 400×100 mean 0.35 ms, worst 0.55 ms.
- **Acceptance** (ACC-A1 and ACC-A3, the bunch ring, are retired): `ACC-A2` a `Spiral`'s curvature radius equals p(t)/B, its turning sign equals the charge, its speed is its momentum, and with k > 0 p decreases; B = k = 0 is a straight line; `ACC-A4` two acts, 26 s + 18 s: `caption()` is `collider event` / `bubble chamber` by act, and each act keeps only its own objects alive; `ACC-A5` exit times put a track exactly on r = 1.29 m from the IP (agreeing with `2 asin(R|qB|/2p)/|qB|`) and from 500 random displaced starts (never beyond r before it), loopers are exactly the circles with |Z| + ρ < 1.29 m, 1 GeV in 3.8 T gives ρ = 0.877 m; in generated crossings every hadron or electron, IP or V⁰ daughter, stops at the ECAL face or is a full looper circle inside it, and every muon leaves through r = 3.18 m and continues from that point with the field ratio −1.8/3.8; `ACC-A6` tower length is monotone in E, 0 at E = 0, capped at its calorimeter's depth, and a tower is 5° wide; every tower sits in its calorimeter; every electron's full pT lands in an ECAL tower at its exit azimuth; `ACC-A7` event brightness is 1 while fresh, then decreasing and positive, 0.15–0.35 when the next crossing arrives, < 0.1 half a second later and < 0.01 when dropped; over act 1 (mixed dt) pools stay bounded, ≤ 2 events share the screen and 2 do at times, and the crossing count is `floor((26 − 0.05)/2.2) + 1`; `ACC-A8` each photograph has 5–8 negative beam tracks from x < 0 that are the stiffest tracks; pairs start at one point with opposite charges, keep 0.5–0.8 of their radius per turn and run ≥ 3 turns; δ rays are negative, p < 0.12, k > 0 and start on a beam track; slower secondaries have denser bubbles; secondaries are brighter than pairs, pairs than the beam; the window's rim fits between the stage's top and bottom rows (80×24, 120×40, 200×50, 60×40); `ACC-A9` at 80×24 the stage is 80×23, and at act-1 and act-2 times row 23 holds only the right-aligned readout within `title::readout_room`; tiny sizes never panic and ASCII is pure; `ACC-A10` the K⁰_S vee conserves momentum (the pions sum to p_K along the flight, 0 across, 1e-12) and has invariant mass m_K (1e-9) for p_K 1.5–6 GeV at any decay angle, p* = 0.206 GeV; generated vees start both pions (π⁺ then π⁻) where the dashed flight ends, 0.12–0.45 m out; `ACC-A11` the view spans 1.8 m at fit radius ≤ 10 rows and 2.6 m from 20 rows, the full 2.6 m at 200×50, and the tracker is ≥ 30 columns across at 80×24; `ACC-A12` in astro and mono the detector outline is ≥ 10 luma below every flavour's fresh track at its softest (hadron, electron, muon and the dashed K⁰_S flight).

### rocket_launch  (Voyages, M5, M12)
- **Intent**: a physically integrated launch profile, from the pad to the edge of space. Deluge steam billows off the pad, the vehicle clears the tower on a white-hot flame and pitches over, and the camera pulls back as the sky darkens from a hazy blue to black and the whole gravity-turn arc hangs in frame under a spreading smoke trail. Max-Q, main-engine cutoff, stage separation and the upper stage's vacuum plume follow, while a Δv bar shows where the engine's work went.
- **Model**: a Falcon 9-class two-stage vehicle, using approximate public figures:
  - stage 1: 411 t propellant, 25.6 t dry, 7607/8227 kN sea-level/vacuum thrust, vacuum Isp 311 s, constant mass flow;
  - stage 2: 107.5 t propellant, 4 t dry, 981 kN, Isp 348 s;
  - payload: 15 t.

  It is a 2-D point mass over a flat Earth with:
  - inverse-square gravity, less the centrifugal relief `vx²/(Re + h)`;
  - an isothermal atmosphere (ρ₀ = 1.225 kg/m³, H = 8.5 km);
  - Mach-dependent drag, with a transonic hump on Cd = 0.26 and A = 10.75 m²;
  - back-pressure thrust loss.

  The flight rises vertically, pitches 3.2° from T+9 s over 4 s, then flies a zero-angle-of-attack gravity turn. Cutoff comes at stage-1 depletion (T+152 s), separation 3 s later, and second-stage ignition 8 s after cutoff. The ascent is integrated once in `build` with RK4 (four substeps per 0.1 s sample), including a Δv ledger:
  - ideal `∫T/m`;
  - gravity loss `∫g sinγ`;
  - drag loss `∫D/m`;
  - steering loss `∫T/m (1 − cos α)`.

  The spent stage coasts ballistically. The 60 s loop replays the ascent on a time-lapse clock: countdown and liftoff at 1×, the climb on a Hermite ease peaking near 8×, staging at 2×, and the upper stage at 4×.
- **Look**:
  - The camera spans `√(240 m² + (h/0.62)²)` of altitude. The rocket drifts right as the pad recedes. The ground curves away as `x²/2Re`, with scrub, a beach and the Atlantic 1.8 km downrange; the coast is dithered over ±3 pixels, and over the sea the limb is anti-aliased (the edge pixel mixes sky and sea by its share of each).
  - Sky brightness is `0.60 √(p/p₀)` in Ice at each pixel's altitude, times a horizon haze `0.62 + 0.7 e^{−u/0.18}` (`u` the pixel's height above the ground line as a share of the frame), so the sky pales toward the horizon and deepens toward the zenith. It falls as one smooth level ramp to the last visible level (12, CORE-R66); over the next 30 % of the fall that level is stippled into the terminal's black with a chance falling from 1 to 0 (a few pixel rows), so the air has no hard top edge; above that is black, where stars appear.
  - The smoke trail and pad steam are re-deposited each frame into a pixel-resolution buffer and tone-mapped (asinh). Smoke laid down `t` s ago at pressure ratio `p` has radius `(8 + 30 t^0.75)/√p` m and is sheared downrange by a wind rising to 25 m/s at 12 km; its weight spreads over its area, so it billows low and thins high. It is a veil: over the sky it lifts the sky's level toward white by its density, over black space it is a faint glow of its density, over the ground it covers a share of pixels. Above ~45 km (`p/p₀` < 1 %) the trail fades out, so no dim bar is left over black space.
  - The plume leaves a dark engine bell, so its white-hot core (the top of the Ember ramp, along the axis over its first quarter) never merges with the white body. It cools through Ember yellow and orange into a deep Ember fringe, at least 3 px across at the nozzle, in a contiguous halo ~1.5 px wide that pales the sky or smoke under it toward white (never below its own level) and fades outward. In air it is 1.5 vehicle lengths long at the pad and 2.5 in thin air, widens as the ambient pressure falls, `1 + 2.6 (1 − p/p₀)^1.5`, and shows blackbody-white shock diamonds low down, spaced as `1/√p`. On the pad the flame stops at the ground (the flame trench is off screen) and runs out sideways along it, white-hot where it lands, cooling and curling up into the steam. Shock diamonds are short lozenges on the axis, at least a pixel inside the flame's edge. The upper stage's vacuum plume is an under-expanded fan 2.5 upper-stage lengths long, 3 nozzle widths across at its end at 40 km opening to 5 by 100 km: a short opaque white-hot and yellow core over its first 30 %, then thin gas drawn as braille Ember dots whose density follows its heat, so the stars show through.
  - The vehicle is drawn to scale near the pad and at a readable minimum size once the camera pulls back; after staging the upper stage stays at least 4 pixels long with its nose cone. The falling first stage is drawn only while it is 3 px clear of any flame pixel or gas dot; closer than that it is lost in the plume. Stars are left out of the gas's cells.
- **Stylisation**: the vehicle is Falcon 9-class, not a specific flight. It is expendable (it burns to depletion, with no landing reserve). There is no throttle bucket, so max-Q comes out a little high and late (39.7 kPa at 15.2 km, T+76 s; a Falcon 9 sees ~30–35 kPa near 12 km), and cutoff low and flat (61.5 km, 2.9 km/s, 16° above horizontal; a Falcon 9 is at ~65–70 km and ~25°, and ~2.2–2.4 km/s on reusable flights; this expendable profile stages faster): the numbers are indicative. The speed of sound is held at 340 m/s. Art-directed rather than computed: the diamond cutoff (`p/p₀ > 0.27`, ~11 km); the flame's colours, length and widening law; its splash along the ground; the vacuum fan and its gas; the stippled top of the air and the trail's fade above 45 km; the sky's brightness law (`√p`, where single scattering goes roughly as `p`, keeping the blue in frame longer) and its horizon haze; the smoke's growth law and the wind profile. The current time-lapse factor is shown on screen (`x2`…`x8`, hidden below 2×).
- **Text**: the countdown (`T-3`…), event callouts (`LIFTOFF`, `MAX-Q`, `MAIN ENGINE CUTOFF`, `STAGE SEPARATION`, `SECOND STAGE IGNITION`) and telemetry on row 0 (`T+01:12  ALT 12.3 km  VEL 452 m/s  q 31.0 kPa  x8`). At ≥ 100 columns, the top-right Δv bar splits the stack's total ideal Δv into speed, gravity, drag and steering, with a legend naming all four. All text sits on dark chips (`draw::label`: the terminal's background, a blank cell either side), legible over any sky in every theme.
- **Captions** (`meta.captions`): `liftoff`, `gravity turn`, `max-Q` (±8 s around it), `stage separation`, `second stage`. The countdown shows none (the title's `stages drop away`).
- **Resize**: the buffers and stars are rebuilt; the ascent does not depend on size.
- **Inks**: sky, smoke, steam, ocean and the plume's halo over them in `Ramp(Ice)`; the plume's core, body and fringe, its splash, its vacuum gas (braille, at least Ember 0.5) and its faint halo over black space, in `Ramp(Ember)`; its shock diamonds in `Blackbody`; the engine bell in `Dim`; scrub in `Ramp(Aurora)`; the beach in `Ramp(Ember)`; the pad and tower in `Structure`; the vehicle body in `Star`, with a `Dim` interstage; the Δv bar in Ember, Plasma, Ice and Aurora; text in `Text`.
- **Acceptance**: `ROC-A1` with drag off, a vertical first-stage burn's ideal Δv lies between the vacuum rocket equation `ve ln(m0/m)` and its sea-level-thrust fraction, speed equals ideal Δv minus gravity loss (±0.1 %), flight stays vertical before the kick, mass is `m0 − ṁt`; `ROC-A2` pad steam appears only from ignition, only while the plume reaches the pad (below 150 m), and the pool never exceeds its capacity; `ROC-A3` altitude never falls before cutoff, after the kick the flight path only turns toward horizontal, cutoff comes at 55–90 km, 10–40° above horizontal, at 1.9–3.2 km/s; `ROC-A4` max-Q comes at T+55–90 s, at 9–16 km, at 20–45 kPa; `ROC-A5` sky brightness strictly decreases with altitude, is > 0.5 at the ground and < 0.03 by 60 km, and on the pad the lowest sky pixel of a column is more than 40 levels paler than the top one, and high in the climb (T+2:40 and T+3:28 at 200×50) up every open-sky column of the right quarter the level never rises and, below level 40, never falls by more than 13 levels per pixel down to black, apart from the faintest level stippled into the black, and that stipple mixes with black over at least 3 pixel rows; `ROC-A6` the plume's width never shrinks as altitude rises and is 1 at sea level, diamonds appear at 1 km and not at 12 km and their spacing grows as pressure falls, the smoke radius strictly grows with age, is wider in thinner air and more than 4× its initial size after 60 s, the plume in air is at least 1.5 vehicle lengths below 10 km and the vacuum fan opens from 3–3.2 nozzle widths at 40 km to 4.8–5 at 100 km, after liftoff the plume holds a pixel at the top of the Ember ramp and on the pad, in the climb and on the upper stage no such pixel touches the body's white, and at 80×24 after second-stage ignition no spent-stage pixel has a flame pixel within 2 px; at 80×24 and 200×50 late in the upper-stage burn the vacuum plume's opaque flame pixels are fewer than twice its braille gas cells (under half its area), there are at least 4 gas cells, and every gas cell is at least Ember 0.5; at T−1 on the pad no flame pixel is below the ground and at least 4 lie more than 4 px off the axis; at T+14 s at 200×50 every shock-diamond pixel has flame on both sides; `ROC-A7` the Δv ledger closes (ideal − losses = speed, ±1 %), losses are positive, gravity loss at cutoff is 0.9–1.7 km/s and drag loss 20–300 m/s, and ideal Δv stays below the stack's budget; `ROC-A8` mission time is strictly increasing in scene time and hits liftoff and cutoff exactly at act boundaries, and the loop restarts, and during the countdown the row-0 clock and the centred `T-n` show the same whole seconds still to go; `ROC-A9` at 160×48 the row-0 telemetry cells keep the terminal's background, and the legend names speed, gravity, drag and steer.

---
## Batch C — grids, automata & stochastic processes (M6)
Shared helpers: `sciart_core::grid` (CORE-R33..R36) plus `particles` and `draw`. Grids are rebuilt (or `resize`d) in `resize`.

### fractal_growth  (States of Matter, M6, M12; renamed M11 from `crystal_growth`)
- **Intent**: diffusion-limited aggregation (Witten–Sander) in two alternating acts. The first is a frost dendrite growing from a seed in the middle of the screen; the second a metal forest electrodeposited on a cathode strip along the bottom, filling the width of wide terminals. Each aggregate is drawn as fine braille filaments on the terminal's black, in the colours of its arrival times (a deep core and pale fresh tips); it then dissolves from the tips inward and hands over to the other act.
- **Model**: DLA on the square **braille sub-cell lattice** (2×4 dots per cell = 1/4 row per side with `ASPECT = 2`), so walks, contact distances and particle cost are all isotropic (weighting steps on the 2:1 cell grid only fixes diffusion and grows vertical needles, D_f ≈ 1.44); 8-neighbour sticking; far-field and near-field jump accelerations (Meakin / Ball–Brady; no measurable effect on D_f); sticking probability `p_s ∈ [0.3, 1]` (seeded per run: lower = denser). Melting removes sites by chemical (8-neighbour BFS) distance from the roots, tips first, so the remainder stays connected. Each crystal site stores its attach time. *Art area*: the lattice covers every row except the title row when `h ≥ 12` (80×24 is a 160×92 lattice). *Lattice cap (ADR-0012)*: the lattice is `grid::lattice_for(art dots, 2^17, exact aspect)`, one site per dot; up to about 250×65 cells (200×50 is 80 000 dots) it is the whole art area. When capped, the frost's window is centred in the art area and the forest's is centred across but sits on the art area's bottom row, so the cathode is the picture's floor on any terminal. A resize reseeds, so nothing is resampled. The sites in attach order form a fixed-capacity pool of `area/4 + 1` (growth ends at a quarter fill at most, and a walker never sticks to a full pool); the melt's breadth-first queue has the same capacity, bounding memory at about 11 bytes per site. *Frost act*: walkers launch on a circle just outside the cluster radius and are relaunched beyond `2×` it; it grows to `R(0.85)` of the art area (within the window). *Forest act*: the cathode is the whole bottom row of the lattice (as much of it as the site pool holds: all of it unless the lattice is under 4 dots tall): attach time 0, the melt's BFS roots, and marked in the free-distance map. Walkers launch uniformly in x on the launch row, the highest row at or below `extent + 2` above the cathode (extent = the highest deposit's height). A walker that steps above that row is put straight back on it at its exact first-passage point: from height a, a Cauchy offset `x + a·tan(π(u − ½))` (the half-plane Poisson kernel), folded into the strip by the reflecting walls (method of images), so there is no kill height and no absorbing lid over the valleys. Near-field jumps use the free-distance map; walls reflect. It grows until its tallest tree reaches 0.8 of the lattice height (≈ 10 s at 200×50). *Why the forest act is honest*: DLA from a line (Meakin 1983, Phys. Rev. A 27, 2616) is DLA's line geometry, and diffusion-limited electrodeposits are DLA fractals (Brady & Ball 1984; Matsushita et al. 1984; Grier et al. 1986; Sawada et al. 1986, mostly in radial cells); the cathode strip is the line-geometry version. Real cells add ion migration and convection; the scene models the diffusion-limited limit only. *Budgets*: the frost act gets `8000·(R/21.25 rows)^2.5` walker steps per second, boosted 4× at the start of the act and easing to 1× over its first 2 s (a small aggregate is cheap), so it opens on a visible crystal: N ≈ 700 after 1 s at 200×50, growth takes 6–7 s there (5.6–8 s at 80×24). The forest gets 40 steps per lattice column per second, unboosted (it opens on its whole cathode). *Timing*: grow (at most 40 s) → hold 5 s → melt 3 s → the other act.
- **Stylisation (stated)**: *false colour*: `Ramp(Ice)` for frost and `Ramp(Ember)` for the metal (copper-like). Frost here is DLA, not a Reiter snow-crystal model.
- **Draw**: `Dots` braille. Each cell's ink is `ramp(act, 0.5 + 0.42·arrival/t_grown)` of its youngest site, where `t_grown` is the growth clock, frozen at the end of growth; a site younger than 0.3 s is at the top of the ramp (a flash). While melting, a site's ramp position rises toward 1 as the front reaches it. Nothing is drawn behind the crystal (CRY-A8): a cell-resolution glow outlines dot-resolution filaments in stair-stepped blocks, so every cell keeps the terminal background, the filaments are the brightest thing on screen and the fjords stay dark. Walkers are `Dim` 110.
- **Captions** (GEN-R12): `meta.captions = ["walkers stick", "ions deposit"]`; `caption()` returns the live act's. `meta.title` stays `Fractal growth · walkers stick`.
- **Label** (GEN-R13): right-aligned on row `h − 1`, `p_s 0.62   N 812   D_f ~1.6` in `Text` at `READOUT_LEVEL`, parts dropped whole from the left to fit the act's `readout_room`. N and D_f are measured during growth and frozen from its end through hold and melt. D_f is a live mass–radius fit, shown for the frost act only and only from N ≥ 600 (a smaller cluster's fit is noise); at `p_s = 1` a grown cluster (N ≈ 1 500 at 200×50) fits 1.45–1.65, the finite-size underestimate of the asymptotic 1.71, and low `p_s` fits higher, since its small scales are denser; hence one decimal and `~`. The forest shows no D_f (its mass–height law is a different fit), and its N counts deposit only, not the cathode.
- **Acceptance**: `CRY-A1` the cluster is always 8-connected to a root (the seed or the cathode) in both acts, and the melt floods from every root; `CRY-A2` during growth cells are only added (count non-decreasing, attach times never change), frost and forest; `CRY-A3` walkers stay within the kill radius (frost) and the lattice, frost and forest; `CRY-A4` the mean mass–radius fractal dimension of a small seeded ensemble is in `[1.45, 1.95]` (single clusters scatter ±0.08; finite-size fits read ≈ 1.63 vs the asymptotic 1.71); `CRY-A5` at every size the lattice holds at most 2^17 sites, is the whole art area when that fits, and with a capped lattice the frost's window is centred (margins differ by at most one dot) and the forest's is centred across with no margin below it; the crystal and its walkers stay inside the window, and 70 s at 500×150 (both acts grow, hold and melt) never reallocate the site pool; `CRY-A6` a seeded 200×120 forest (p_s = 1, grown to height 60) is a sparse, screened deposit: the occupied fraction of rows 5–20 above the cathode is < 0.45, rows 35–50 are sparser than rows 5–20, and every site is 8-connected to the cathode; `CRY-A7` at hold, levels strictly increase with arrival time in 1 s steps; the core is `ramp(Ice, 0.5)`, a tip younger than 0.3 s is `ramp(Ice, 1)`, the forest is Ember, and the melting front brightens the sites it is about to take; the acts alternate `walkers stick → ions deposit → walkers stick` within 90 s at 80×24; `D_f` stays `None` while N < 600 and is `Some` once the frost has grown (200×50), within [1.4, 1.8] at the hold; `CRY-A8` at 200×50 and 80×24, sampled every 0.5 s over 60 s (both acts), in both glyph modes, the frame draws something and no cell has a background ink.
- **Perf** (200×50, same run and load as molecular_dynamics): mean 0.05 ms, heaviest 1-s window median 0.09 ms. Live bytes at 500×150 are 2.73 MiB.

### radioactive_decay  (Forces & Waves, M6, M12)
- **Intent**: a lattice of unstable nuclei flickering through a decay chain while live population curves trace the exponential law.
- **Model**: chain `A → B → C` (C stable) with half-lives `T_A = 8 s`, `T_B = 3 s`. Each step samples every live
  nucleus once from the chain's exact 3×3 transition matrix over `dt` (the single-species decay probability is
  `1 − 2^{−dt/T}`, and `A → B → C` within one step is included), so the populations are exactly binomial with the
  Bateman fractions for any sequence of steps. The per-nucleus draws come from a `SplitMix64` seeded from `ctx.rng`
  once per step (CORE-R57): ChaCha8 per draw was a third of a 500×150 frame (RAD-A9). One nucleus per half-block pixel: the lattice is `lat_w × 2·rows`
  pixels (rows = h − 1 while the title shows, RAD-A4), `lat_w` = panel start − 2 (the full width without a panel).
  The plot panel is the right 30 % of the width, shown only when it gets ≥ 24×10 cells (≈ terminals ≥ 80 columns),
  with measured `N_A, N_B, N_C` and the analytic Bateman curves `N_A = N₀ 2^{−t/T_A}`,
  `N_B = N₀ λ_A/(λ_B − λ_A)(e^{−λ_A t} − e^{−λ_B t})`. Reset to all-A when `N_A + N_B < 2 %` of N₀ (≈ 50.6 s); a
  resize starts a fresh run. Every decay flashes; only a budget of them is tracked (`n0/500` slots in [4, 60]). The
  tracked decays are a uniform sample of each step's decays (reservoir sampling, then a uniform pick for the free
  slots), so tracks start anywhere on the lattice. A tracked decay bursts (a 12-dot braille ring growing from 1.5 to
  3.5 dots over 0.12 s, in the daughter's ramp) and leaves a braille track over the lattice in square pixel units,
  one pixel out from its nucleus and clipped to the lattice, built of straight pieces of 0.02 s of flight: α
  25 px/s for 0.2 s (5 px, ω = 0.6 rad/s, no scattering, drawn two dots thick), gone at 0.35 s; β⁻ 40–90 px/s for
  0.25 s (10–22.5 px, always longer than an α; ω = −5 rad/s, and a fixed random heading kick of up to ±0.35 rad per
  piece for multiple scattering), gone at 0.4 s; an `A → C` within one step emits both. The path depends only on
  the track, so it is the same for any dt. The plot's y axis eases from linear to log (3 decades) over 18–20 s of
  each run by `plot_y(f, m) = lerp(f, max(0, 1 + log10 f / 3), m)`; on the log axis every exponential is a straight
  line of slope `−λ/(3 ln 10)` per second, and N_B's line turns parallel to N_A's (transient equilibrium,
  N_B/N_A → λ_A/(λ_B − λ_A) = 0.6 since T_B < T_A). Measured counts are bright curves, two dots thick, over dim
  analytic curves, braille `Dots`, two samples per cell.
- **Inks** (false colour): the ground is dim and calm, the species differ in texture as well as hue, and only events
  are bright. A is a flat `Ramp::Ember` 0.16 half-block pixel; B a 2×2 cluster of braille dots filling its pixel in
  `Ramp::Aurora` 0.45 (dots light less of a cell than a block, so B sits at ≥ 2× A's level and reads at least as
  strongly as A, in a cool hue against A's warm one); C an empty site. So the lattice hollows out from a warm field
  to black as B comes and goes: the emptying is the story. A decay lights its pixel's four dots at the top of the
  daughter's ramp (Aurora for A → B, Plasma for B → C) for 0.03 s, about one frame, then the site settles at once; a
  restart does not flash A. Tracks in hues no species uses: α `Ramp::HAlpha`, β `Ramp::Ice`, at 0.95 of the ramp
  while moving, fading to half that, one ink per track; fresh tracks are ≥ 3× A's pixel and ≥ 2× B's dots, fading
  ones still above B. Marks, bursts and tracks carry the lattice under their cells as background (`Dots::blit_over`,
  CORE-R84). Plot: measured curves in the species ramps at 0.85 (A solid, B dashed, C dotted, so they read apart in
  mono), analytic curves `Structure` 70, axes `Structure` 110, header and counts `Text` 200, time labels at
  `READOUT_LEVEL`. Legend per species: its lattice look (`█` in A's pixel ink, `⠛` in B's dot ink, a blank for C)
  then its stroke (`─` `╌` `┈`) in the curve's ink, then the count; ASCII `A-` `B=` `C.`. ASCII lattice, per cell
  from its two nuclei: a flash `*`, then B `o`, then A `-` (a light glyph, so the ground stays quiet); C is blank,
  and no nucleus uses a braille fallback glyph (`.` `:` `+` `#`), so tracks never read as nuclei.
- **Stylisation**: a fictional A → B → C chain with half-lives of seconds; tracks drawn as if in a cloud chamber in
  a magnetic field (α short, straight and dense, β⁻ longer, thin, curling the other way and knocked about), with
  display-scale ranges, speeds, curvatures and scattering: the sign and ordering of the curvature are right, but the
  α : β radius ratio is compressed from ~70 : 1 (a 5 MeV α against a 1 MeV β in the same field) to 2–5 : 1 so both
  read on screen. Only a sample of decays is tracked and the burst is decorative; colours are false. In mono the
  species read apart by texture (block, dots, empty) and the curves by stroke, not by grey.
- **Perf** (update + render, fastest of 5 replays per frame over 110 s at 1/60 s, seed 42, machine load ≈ 14):
  200×50 mean 0.14 ms, worst 0.30 ms; 400×100 mean 0.59 ms, worst 1.19 ms; 500×150 mean 1.16 ms, worst 2.5 ms. The
  cost is one uniform draw per live nucleus and one pixel write per nucleus per step, so it grows with the
  terminal's area. The lattice is exempt from ADR-0012's cap: one nucleus per pixel is the picture, and the scene
  restarts on resize, so it has no evolved state to resample.
- **Acceptance**: `RAD-A1` per-step decay probability is `1 − 2^{−dt/T}` and composes exactly (`p(dt₁) ∘ p(dt₂) = p(dt₁ + dt₂)`); `RAD-A2` a seeded 20 000-nucleus run keeps `N_A(t)` within 4σ of `N₀ 2^{−t/T_A}` and `N_B(t)` within 4σ of Bateman; `RAD-A3` `N_A + N_B + N_C = N₀` at all times; `RAD-A4` (M12, GEN-R13) whenever the science title shows (`title::span` is `Some` for the title with its longer caption), the lattice and its tracks fill rows `0..h − 1` only, so the title does not punch through the last row of nuclei; with no title they fill all `h` rows. `RAD-A5` one nucleus per pixel (lattice `lat_w × 2·rows`, n0 its size): every settled A nucleus is an `Ember` half-block pixel, every settled B an empty pixel with all four of its 2×2 dots lit in `Aurora`, every settled C nothing at all; A is ≥ 2× `MIN_VISIBLE_LEVEL` and B's level is ≥ 2× A's, in a different ramp; a B or C flash is its ramp at 255; fresh A does not flash; Unicode draws half blocks and braille, and ASCII is pure ASCII, each lattice cell a flash `*` over B `o` over A `-`, C blank. `RAD-A6` `log_blend` is 0 until 18 s, ½ at 19 s, 1 from 20 s; `plot_y` is the identity at m = 0, one decade is ⅓ at m = 1 and values ≤ 10⁻³, 0 or NaN sit at the bottom; on the log axis N_A's slope is `−λ_A/(3 ln 10)` (1e-9) at 2–40 s and N_B's is within 2 % of it at 36–44 s; `caption()` is `on a log scale` from 19 s and back to `random yet lawful` after a reset. `RAD-A7` every measured sample is lit at `dot_y(plot_y(N/N₀, m))` of its dot column where its stroke is drawn (zeros skipped on a log axis), curves stay inside the plot's data rows and columns, and the measured cells wear species ramps. `RAD-A8` the ground is dim and only events are bright: a flash is at level 255 and settled (< 150) after 0.03 s; every live track's fresh ink is ≥ 3× A's pixel and ≥ 2× B's dots, and its faded ink still above B; every β range (at the slowest β) is > 1.9× the α range (exactly 5 px), and an α runs straight; over a 50 s run at 120×40 (mixed dt) fewer than 5 % of lattice cells (glyph or background) are at level ≥ 150 on average and never more than 10 %, tracks, bursts and flashes included; track ramps are distinct from each other and from every species ramp. Over 20 s at 200×50 (seed 42) the tracks' start rows average within 0.05 of the lattice's mid-height (as a fraction of it) and each third of the lattice holds ≥ 20 % of them. A fresh run is a full field (no empty lattice cell) and after 30 s > 60 % of lattice cells are empty. The legend row reads `█─ ` … `⠛╌ 0` … ` ┈ 0` with the A chip in the lattice's ink and its stroke in the curve's; A's stroke is solid and the three strokes differ; the panel is ≤ 30 % of the width. `RAD-A9` *(M12 perf)*: through `update` itself (SplitMix64 draws, seed 42, 200×50), N_A and N_B stay within 4σ of the Bateman binomial at every whole second to 20 s.

### gray_scott  (Reactions, M6; upgraded M10; renamed M11 from `chemical_reaction`)
- **Intent**: upgrade of the M6 scene; this section replaces the M6 one, and the scene moves to `Category::Chemistry` (CORE-R77). It shows living reaction–diffusion patterns (Pearson's self-replicating spots, not a Turing instability) from the Gray–Scott system `U + 2V → 3V, V → P` (Gray & Scott 1983; Pearson 1993). One culture walks through five regimes of Pearson's (F, k) plane: spots that bud and divide, a short beat of branching coral, then three regimes that never settle (loops, a swarm of spots and short worms, and moving spots). Each regime melts into the next, so the pattern reorganises instead of being replaced. The field is drawn in half-block pixels in `Ramp::Aurora`: dim teal rims, emerald bodies and lime-white spines and cores inside faint teal depletion halos, floating on the terminal's own black, which stays the majority of the screen in every regime a visit opens in. The colour is false colour: u and v are dimensionless concentrations with no hue. The live regime names the title (`caption()`), and the parameters are a title-row readout; nothing else is drawn. At 80×24 there are about forty mitosis spots, each ≈ 5 columns × 2½ rows. At 200×50 there are about sixty, each ≈ 10 columns × 5 rows, round with graded edges. The scene stays distinct from `belousov_zhabotinsky` (travelling spiral fronts) and `liesegang_rings` (fixed concentric bands) because its forms are near-stationary blobs and stripes that divide, branch and reorganise.
- **Captions**: `meta.captions` = `spots divide`, `coral branches`, `loops form`, `spots swarm`, `spots glide`, one per preset in walk order. `caption()` is the hold's preset, and during a morph the source preset until m = ½, then the target, so the title reads e.g. `Spots and worms · coral branches` and its fade restarts on each regime (CORE-R74).
- **Model**: `∂u/∂t = D_u∇²u − uv² + F(1 − u)` and `∂v/∂t = D_v∇²v + uv² − (F + k)v`, with `D_u = 2 D_v` and `D_v = 0.03` nodes² per reaction time unit. Kept from M6: the isotropic 9-point Laplacian (Patra–Karttunen), a periodic lattice, and an explicit Euler sub-step τ = 0.5 inside the positivity bounds `τ(10/3·D_u + 1 + F) ≤ 1` and `τ(10/3·D_v + F + k) ≤ 1`. The bounds are const-asserted for every preset and checked along the whole walk (CHEM-A5); the step is a convex combination plus non-negative sources, so u, v ≥ 0 and u ≤ 1, and decaying diffusive modes stay decaying (τ·D·16/3 ≪ 2). Every preset has its own **reaction rate** (reaction time units per simulated second, see the walk): 400 for mitosis and coral (800 sub-steps/s, ≈ 13.3 per frame), 70 for loops, 110 for swarm and 150 for spots. A morph lerps the rate by the same weight m as F and k, and τ stays 0.5, so the rate only sets how many sub-steps a frame runs (at most 200 per `update`, the 400 rate at dt = 0.25) and the positivity bounds are unaffected. At a single rate of 400 the moving regimes, which evolve far faster in reaction time than mitosis divides, read as a boiling field: loops renewed ≈ 60 % of the ASCII cells in 0.25 s (≈ 13 % per frame, swarm ≈ 11 %). **Lattice (ADR-0012)**: square nodes decoupled from the display, one node per half-block pixel below the cap: `lattice_size(display) = grid::lattice_for(Size(max(w, 32), max(2h, 16)), MAX_NODES = 5 120, BUCKET = 1.12)`, with the 32×16 floor restored inside the cap when the aspect snap undercuts it on extreme displays. Below the cap `lattice_for` returns its input (80×24 → 80×48, 40×12 → 40×24). Above the cap the lattice stops growing (200×50 → 100×50, two pixels per node), its aspect snaps to a power of 1.12 (stretch ≤ ±6 %), and the pattern zooms instead of getting denser. A pixel is square, so the lattice stays isotropic; the pixel scale holds 7–8 pattern wavelengths across an 80×24 screen, which keeps small displays from locking into a torus mode (the M10 draft's 40×24 lattice held 3–4 and settled into diagonal plane-wave stripes; a 20×12 lattice visibly tiled). The 32×16 floor keeps a culture alive through tiny transient sizes. **Preset walk**: a looping 10-beat `Timeline` of five holds, each followed by a smoothstep morph to the next, 119 s in all. Name, (F, k), rate, hold, morph that follows: mitosis (.0367, .0649; Sims), 400, 8 s, 6 s; coral (.0545, .062; Sims), 400, 3 s, 6 s; loops (.018, .051; Márquez-Neila's "spots and loops"), 70, 24 s, 8 s; swarm (.016, .052), 110, 24 s, 8 s; spots (.014, .054; Márquez-Neila's "moving spots"), 150, 24 s, 8 s; then back to mitosis. The morphs into and out of coral take 6 s rather than 8 s so the blown-out labyrinth is brief: at 80×24 the share of time with ≥ 50 % `#`/`@` cells fell from 7.25–8 s to 6.25–6.5 s, and with ≥ 30 % of the half-block pixels at Seq ≥ 192 from ≈ 8 s to 6.5 s (seeds 1, 3, 7, 42). The coral hold is short because coral branches only during the morph into it and the first seconds of the hold; a 12 s hold spent ≈ 10 s on a frozen, blown-out labyrinth (2–8 % of cells changing per 4 s, 57 % `#`/`@`), which the rejection criteria below exclude. From seed bumps, mitosis spots divide for ≈ 12 s, through the 8 s hold and into the morph, then jam once they fill the torus (a 16 s hold was still for its last 4 s, 3 % change). **Passaging**: without it, the return to mitosis inherits a full field of moving spots, which divide until they tile the torus and then jam: the returning hold was still for ≈ 8–10 s (4 s change 0.00–0.02). So the return is passaged, like a culture split into fresh medium. The first `resize` draws a seed from `ctx.rng` and builds a mask on the lattice of blocks of ≈ 6×6 nodes (`round(W/6) × round(H/6)` blocks tiling the torus exactly), each active when a SplitMix64 draw keyed by the seed and the block index is below 0.8; it is rebuilt from the same seed on every lattice change. During the spots → mitosis morph, each sub-step is followed by the sink `v ← v(1 − τκ m² mask)` (Lie splitting, κ = 0.02 per reaction unit, m the morph's smoothstep), a shrinking factor, so positivity holds. About four in five moving spots fade out over the morph's second half, and the survivors divide into the freed room through the mitosis hold. Measured at 80×24: masking half the blocks left ≈ 20 survivors that refilled the torus within ≈ 6 s, and the hold still froze for its last 2–4 s (4 s change 0.07–0.11); with κ·m instead of κ·m² the spots died by mid-morph and its last 4 s were still (0.06–0.12). With 0.8 and m², every 4 s window over 160 s from the mitosis and the spots slot (12 seeds each) changes ≥ 15 %; the returning hold changes 0.20–0.24 per 2 s, as the first one does. Each preset sits within 0.005 of the saddle-node curve `F = 4(F + k)²` (F − 4(F + k)² ∈ [−0.0046, +0.0002]). **Morphs follow the saddle-node curve**: F is interpolated, and k keeps the interpolated signed offset from the curve, `k = k_sn(F) + lerp(k_a − k_sn(F_a), k_b − k_sn(F_b), m)` with `k_sn(F) = √F/2 − F`. A straight chord can bulge across the curve into the region where the uniform v₊ sheet is stable (the M10 draft's worms → holes chord reached F − 4(F + k)² = +0.0028 and reseeded every loop), so following the curve is what lets the culture carry over. Rejected regimes, measured on this lattice: worms (.078, .061) never grows from seed patches (it reseeds for its whole hold); holes, maze and chaos cover 98–100 % of the screen and freeze, and maze and chaos lock into diagonal stripes at 80×24; chaos at Márquez-Neila's .026 is a near-homogeneous Hopf oscillation; solitons (.030, .062) and pulsating solitons (.025, .060) freeze within ≈ 12 s; waves (.014, .045) becomes one pulsing blob. The seeded start is a whole start slot, the start of the mitosis, loops, swarm or spots hold (never coral, whose labyrinth fills the torus). A `sim` visit of 15 s shows one regime develop and keep moving (from mitosis: spots divide, then elongate toward coral), and a long `run` shows the walk. Seeds are Gaussian bumps `v = ¼·exp(−(r/r₀)²)`, `u = 1 − 2v` (Pearson's u = ½, v = ¼ at the centre), r₀ ∈ [1.5, 3] nodes, ±5 % noise per node, 3 + nodes/300 of them; a bump only raises v and lowers u. The first `resize` pre-rolls 200 sub-steps (100 reaction units, ¼ s at the mitosis rate), so the first frame already shows graded bodies. A once-per-second featureless check (max v < 0.02, or max v − min v < 0.05) reseeds and counts it; the spread, unlike σ(v), does not mistake a young sparse culture for a dead one. It never fires in the walk (CHEM-A4, CHEM-A5).
- **Motion**: sub-steps advance the fronts every frame. Mitosis spots swell and divide for ≈ 12 s; loops, swarm and spots jostle, merge and split without ever settling, as organisms rather than a boiling field: at 80×24 and 200×50 about 2–4 % of the ASCII cells change per frame and ≈ 20 % per 0.25 s (loops 2.1–2.8 % and 0.18–0.22, swarm 3.0–3.6 % and 0.19–0.23, measured over seeds 1, 2, 3, 6, 7, 11), while 50–80 % still change per 4 s at the end of each hold. The 6–8 s morphs reorganise the whole field: mitosis spots elongate into coral branches, the coral breaks into loops, loops shrink into a swarm and then into moving spots, and most moving spots fade (passaging) while the rest slow into dividing mitosis spots. The walk repeats every 119 s, but frames never repeat because the culture carries over.
- **Resize**: if `lattice_size` is unchanged (every display at or above the cap in the same bucket), the simulation is untouched. Otherwise u and v are resampled onto the new lattice with `grid::resample_bilinear`, so the pattern is stretched to the new domain rather than cropped. The bilinear edge clamp leaves a seam that diffusion heals within ≈ 1 s. If the node scale changed, spots divide or merge back toward the intrinsic wavelength, which is physically faithful. Nothing is reseeded unless the field is featureless. `Pixels` and the `grid::SampleMap` (lattice → pixels) are rebuilt here. The view (u, v → Pixels) is refreshed at the end of `resize` and of `update`, so `render` only blits and writes the readout.
- **Inks**: `Role::Ramp(Ramp::Aurora)` only (ADR-0011, ADR-0015); the M6 `Hot`, `Cold` and `Accent` inks are retired. u and v are sampled bilinearly into the `Pixels` through a `grid::SampleMap`; v first, and u only where the pixel is not pattern (the halo needs it). A pixel with `v ≥ 0.12` is pattern (the M10 edge, q = 0.3 of 0.40, which keeps dense regimes' thin tails dark, CHEM-A8: at a lower edge the v ≈ 0.05–0.12 tails between loops read as a wall of `:` and `+`). It is shaded against the **exposure** `v_top`: `p = clamp((v − 0.12)/(v_top − 0.12), 0, 1)`, level `255·(0.26 + 0.74·p²)` from a 256-entry table built once, so the pattern edge is a dim teal rim (level 66, above the halo's peak) and the top of the ramp is kept for the spine. `v_top` is the v below which 97 % of the pattern nodes lie (a 64-bin stack histogram over [0.12, 0.45], one pass per `update`), clamped to [0.24, 0.45] (so a young culture of seed bumps is not blown out) and eased towards that target with time constant 1.5 s; the first resize sets it directly. Why: at a fixed full scale (M10: 0.40, exponent 0.8; M12 round 0: exponent 1.3) worms and coral, whose plateaus sit at v ≈ 0.3–0.37, were a flat mid-mint band with no spine; with the exposure every regime shows a bright ridge inside its bodies (5–22 % of body pixels at level ≥ 200 at 80×24) while most of each body stays emerald. Otherwise the pixel gets the halo, which fades in with substrate depletion from zero at `1 − u = 0.2` to its peak at 0.6: `ink::ramp(Aurora, 0.1·clamp((1 − u − 0.2)/0.4, 0, 1))`, and `Ink::BLANK` (the terminal background) where that level rounds to 0, so there are no hard black notches in the valleys. The readout is `Role::Text` at `title::READOUT_LEVEL`.
- **ASCII**: the `Pixels::blit` closure uses the brighter half, so thin features survive. Ramp levels 13–65 give `.` (the stronger half of the halo); fainter halo, which Unicode shows as a fade to black, is left blank. From the body's lowest level (66) up, `:` `+` `#` `@` by quartile of `(level − 66)/189`, keeping that pixel's ink. A cell with both halves empty is left untouched. The look matches M6: `.:+#@` blobs on blank.
- **Readout** (replaces the M10 row-0 label): `draw::label_right` on row `h − 1`, `Text` at `title::READOUT_LEVEL`, the longest form that fits `title::readout_room`: `Pearson F=.037 k=.065` (room ≥ 21), `F=.037 k=.065` (room ≥ 13), otherwise none. F and k are rounded to thousandths with no leading zero, written from integers into a stack buffer, and track the morph continuously (coral's F = .0545 shows as .054 or .055, by f32 rounding). `Pearson` names the (F, k) parameter plane of Pearson (1993); the loops, swarm and moving-spots presets are later explorations of that plane (Márquez-Neila), not Pearson's own pattern classes. The M12 round-0 Pearson-map inset was dropped: an unlabelled braille squiggle in a knocked-out corner box read as a rendering hole over worms and swarms, and the readout and regime caption already carry the science.
- **Perf**: at the 400 rate (mitosis, coral), 80×24 runs 3 840 nodes × 13.3 sub-steps ≈ 51 k cell updates per frame and 200×50 (and above) 5 000 nodes ≈ 67 k (~3 ns each); loops, swarm and spots run 2.3, 3.7 and 5 sub-steps per frame. The passaging sink adds one multiply per node and sub-step during one morph. The view samples v for every pixel and u only off the pattern, plus the ink mapping and the blit. Measured (`cargo bench`, frame = update + render, seed 42, M12 on a machine at load average ≈ 36): 0.30 ms mean at 200×50; worst frame over a whole walk ≈ 0.37 ms (minimum of 5 replays per frame, seeds 1 and 42). The exposure adds one pass over ≤ 5 120 nodes per `update`. The first resize's 200-sub-step pre-roll is a one-off scene-entry cost of ≈ 2 ms. (M10 round 3: 0.26 ms at 200×50, 0.90 ms at 400×100.) The simulation is capped at any size; the view grows with the display (every pixel samples v bilinearly), so 400×100 costs ≈ 3× 200×50 (informational, GEN-R8 is measured at 200×50). `render` and steady `update` stay allocation-free.
- **Acceptance**: `CHEM-A1` u, v stay finite and in `[0, 1]` for any dt ≤ 0.25, and the kernel's clamp is only a guard: at every 4 s frame of the whole-walk runs of CHEM-A8 (seeds 1 and 7), the unclamped reference Euler update of the live fields (core `laplacian9`) is already in `[0, 1]` and its v stays below 0.7 (measured ≤ 0.63, loops colliding); `CHEM-A2` the uniform state `(u, v) = (1, 0)` is a fixed point; `CHEM-A3` with the reaction terms off, diffusion conserves `Σu` and `Σv` (periodic); `CHEM-A4` from each start slot (mitosis, loops, swarm, spots) and seeds 1, 2, 3 and 7, an 80×24 run develops structure within 10 s: var(v) > 3e-3 and at most 90 % of the ASCII cells between row 0 and the readout row are blank or `.`; from the mitosis slot the number of nodes with v > 0.1 at least doubles (the moving regimes are already developed by the pre-roll); and no reseed happens in the first 20 s; `CHEM-A5` the preset table is exactly the five presets above (names, F, k, rates, holds, morphs);
