`preset_at(t)` visits them in order, equals each preset exactly during its hold (its F, k and rate), is between preset i and i + 1 at mid-morph with the rate at the midpoint of the two rates, changes F and k by ≤ 1e-4 and the rate by ≤ 1 per 10 ms across the whole walk, keeps the rate within [70, 400], and wraps at 119 s; every seeded visit opens at the start of a start slot, all four occur over 40 seeds, and coral never does; at every 10 ms of the walk both Euler positivity bounds hold, F, k > 0, and F − 4(F + k)² ∈ [−0.005, +0.0015]; at mid-morph F is the midpoint and k − k_sn(F) is the midpoint of the endpoints' offsets; `k_sn` solves the curve to 1e-12; `uniform_states(F, k)` returns (1, 0) plus, iff F > 4(F + k)², the two non-trivial homogeneous states `v± = [F ± √(F² − 4F(F + k)²)]/(2(F + k))`, `u = (F + k)/v`, each with ODE residual < 1e-9 in f64; one full walk plus 2 s from the mitosis slot at 80×24 for seeds 1, 5, 7 and 11 never reseeds; `CHEM-A6` for displays 1×1 … 1000×300 `lattice_size` gives ≤ 5 120 nodes and at least 32×16, equals `(max(w, 32), max(2h, 16))` whenever that has ≤ 4 500 nodes, gives 80×48 at 80×24, 40×24 at 40×12, 100×50 at 200×50 and 80×63 at 120×50, and gives 200×50, 300×75 and 400×100 one shared lattice (100×50); a resize that keeps the lattice leaves u and v bit-identical; in the mitosis hold, 200×50 → 120×50 → 200×50 resamples with the reseed counter unchanged, keeping mean v within 5 % of before, and 2 s later std(v) is within 30 % of before with still no reseed; `CHEM-A7` for `v_top` ∈ {0.24, 0.33, 0.45}, `v_ink(u, v, v_top)` is `None` iff v < 0.12 and the halo level rounds to 0, and is otherwise `Role::Ramp(Aurora)` with level non-decreasing in v for fixed u; the level at v = 0.12 is in [60, 90], at `v_top` it is 255, and at the midpoint of [0.12, v_top] it is ≤ 140; the halo level rises by at most 1 per 0.01 of depletion from 0 to `ink::ramp(Aurora, 0.1)`; a pattern pixel never reads u; `exposure_target` of a uniform ramp of pattern values is its 97th percentile to within one bin, ignores substrate nodes (v < 0.12), is 0.45 with no pattern and clamps to [0.24, 0.45]; in live 80×24 frames (seed 7: mitosis at 4 s and 16 s, loops, swarm and spots at 10 s) 3–35 % of the body pixels (level ≥ 66) are at level ≥ 200; `ascii_cell` leaves levels < 13 blank, gives `.` for 13–65 and `:` from 66, and uses the brighter half; an 80×24 Unicode frame after 3 s uses only `Ramp(Aurora)`, `Text` and `Background` inks; with the title hidden every row of the 80×24 ASCII frame above the readout row is from `.:+#@` and blank; `CHEM-A8` from each start slot at 80×24 and 200×50 (seeds 1 and 7), 10 s into the hold (3.9 s into the 8 s mitosis hold) at most 50 % of the ASCII cells between row 0 and the readout row are `#` or `@` and at least 20 % are blank or `.`; at least 5 % of cells change in the next 4 s; and in the loops, swarm and spots holds at least 30 % change between 20 s and 24 s. In the loops and swarm holds at 80×24 and 200×50 (seeds 1 and 7), sampled at 2, 5, 8, 15 and 18 s, each sample with the frame 1/30 s later and the frame 0.25 s later: on average over the samples at most 4 % of the ASCII cells change per frame and at most 35 % per 0.25 s, at least 50 % of the half-block pixels below row 0 are near-black (`Ink::BLANK` or the halo, level below 32) and at least 45 % of the ASCII cells are blank or `.`, and no single sample has under 45 % near-black or under 40 % blank or `.`. Over the whole walk and on through the passaged return to mitosis and its hold (to 136 s), from the mitosis slot at 80×24, and from the spots slot through the first return and its hold (to 48 s), seeds 1 and 7: each frame at 4i s has at most 50 % `#`/`@` cells (60 % in the coral beat, from mid-morph into coral to the end of the morph out of it: a labyrinth covers ≈ 55 % of the cells at any exposure, measured 0.51–0.56), and in every 4 s window [4i, 4i + 4] at least 10 % of the cells change, with no exemption. `passage_mask` is a pure function of lattice and seed (different seeds give different masks), holds only 0 and 1, is constant on blocks (at most one change of value per 5 nodes along a row), and marks 55–85 % of the nodes on average over 8 seeds at 80×48, 100×50, 32×16 and 40×24; `CHEM-A9` for each preset i, `caption()` is caption i in mid-hold and at 45 % of morph i → i + 1, and caption i + 1 at 55 %; the readout ends at the last column with a blank cell each side; at 80×24, 60×12, 40×12, 30×12 and 20×8 (loops slot) it is exactly `Pearson F=.018 k=.051` when `readout_room` ≥ 21, exactly `F=.018 k=.051` when 13 ≤ room < 21, and absent below 13; its ink is `Text` at `title::READOUT_LEVEL`.

### spacetime_grid  (Deep Cosmos, M6, M12)
- **Intent**: the rubber-sheet picture of a binary black-hole merger (GW150914): a dim purple braille
  mesh funnelling down into two orbiting black holes — dark cores with hot, glowing rims — with cooling
  orbit trails; quadrupole ripples spiral outward as magenta-to-orange crests and tighten as the orbit
  shrinks; the holes plunge and merge in a flash; the merger's burst sweeps across the sheet as an
  expanding band of light while the single remnant rings down; a strain inset (top-left) draws the chirp
  a distant detector would record, brightening to the merger.
- **Model**: sheet height `z = −K Σ m / √(d² + ε²)` (softened Newtonian wells, `K = 0.16`, `ε = 0.16`)
  plus the quadrupole wave `A f(r) S(t_r) cos 2(φ − Ψ(t_r))` with retarded time `t_r = t − r/c`,
  `c = 0.35` sheet units/s, `A = 0.08` and `f(r) = (1 − e^{−(r/0.5)²}) √(0.5/r)`, the 2-D sheet's
  energy-conserving fall-off (real strain falls as 1/r). The source runs a 20.1 s cycle: *inspiral* on
  the Newtonian Peters chirp `a = a₀(1 − τ/τc)^{1/4}` (τc = 15 s, Ω₀ = 2π/4 s), Kepler Ω and closed-form
  phase, from `a₀` to `0.5 a₀`, strain `S = a₀/a` (`h ∝ a²Ω² ∝ 1/a`), eased in over the first 2 s;
  *merger* (0.5 s): `a = a_m(1 − s²) → 0` while the orbital speed doubles linearly, `S` rises by 2.2×,
  and 5 % of the mass is radiated; then one remnant of `0.95 (m₁ + m₂)` at the centre of mass with an
  `m = 2` *ringdown*, `S ∝ e^{−τ/0.35 s}`, pattern speed 1.2 × the end-of-plunge orbital speed (which is
  `2 Ω_m`), i.e. `2.4 Ω_m`; *quiet* for 5.5 s while the burst runs off the sheet; then a cut (0.5 s dip
  to dark each side) to a new binary at a new angle. Merger flash: `s²` through the plunge, 1 at
  coalescence, then `e^{−τ/0.3 s}`. Mesh: perspective camera (pitch 55°, slow yaw sway), lines every ~7
  columns / ~2.8 rows, 3 samples per edge, rasterised as braille `Dots` segments with the parts inside
  each black hole's moat clipped out, fitted into rows 1 … h − 3 (a clear top row; the title row and a
  gap below). Display low-pass: waves shorter than 2.5 line spacings are faded by `(Ω_max/Ω)²` instead of
  aliasing into moiré. Black holes on their own `Dots` layer (drawn in `update`): a hot two-dot rim of
  radius `0.12 m^{1/3}` (× (1 + flash); at least 2.5 dots) round a dark core, graded by a cooler halo
  ring 2.5 dots further out (in braille cells of its own), in a dark moat to 1.4 × that radius plus a
  dot that the mesh and trails keep out of, and a 1 s orbit trail that cools as it fades. The camera fit
  includes the remnant's lifted well bottom, derived from `K`, `ε`, the radiated fraction and the lift. Inset:
  the source strain, signed-square-root scaled to the merger peak, over the last 4 s, one sample per dot
  column, in a 3-row strip at the top-left, `w/6` columns (12–22) wide (cleared under, labelled `strain`
  above its left end); the mesh slides right into any free margin, and where that is not enough the
  sheet is narrowed (its scale kept) until the inset has its full width; it narrows to stay two columns
  clear of the mesh, and is dropped when narrower than 12 columns or on canvases under 48 × 16.
- **Stylisation (stated)**: the rubber sheet is a metaphor (Newtonian potential depth, not a metric); the
  wells follow the masses instantly (Newtonian), only the ripples travel at c; wave speed, amplitude and
  time scales are artistic (real ripples are vastly weaker and longer than the orbit); merger and
  ringdown are phenomenological, not numerical relativity; the black holes are drawn as dark cores with
  hot Blackbody rims so they read in their funnels — the rims mark them (black holes emit no light), rim
  size is not horizon size, and the merger flash is a visual cue for the energy radiated; the display
  low-pass hides the shortest ringdown ripples on the sheet (the inset shows them), but the mesh's light
  still carries the unfiltered wave energy density (`∝ h²`), so the burst reads as a band of light where
  its ripples are too fine to draw; the loop restarts with a cut.
- **Motion**: black holes orbit and spiral in (4 s → 1.4 s orbits), brighter crests stream outward; a
  merger flash and ringdown every 20 s.
- **Resize**: mesh sample lists, camera fit and sheet width, low-pass limit, the three `Dots` layers and
  the inset.
- **Inks**: mesh `Seq` (a field of sheet energy; inferno-like in astro): resting level 60, + 45 in
  wells, + 110 on full crests (troughs dimmed by up to 30 %), + 220 × the wave's normalised energy
  density `(f(r) S(t_r) / max)²`; nearness shades it by at most 30 % and the foreground edge fades; all
  scaled by the cut and brightened up to 1.6 × by the merger flash; capped at level 210, and at 170
  within 2.5 moat radii of a black hole (easing back to 210 by 3.5), so the rims win in their funnels.
  Rims `Blackbody` (30 000 K and 25 000 K, levels ≥ 240; remnant 30 000 K), halo rings at 0.15 × the rim
  temperature, warming with the flash; trails cooling from 0.35 × to 0.1 × the rim temperature.
  Inset trace `Ramp(Aurora)` from level 110 (quiet) to 255 (merger peak) with a dim axis, label `Text` at
  `title::READOUT_LEVEL`.
- **Captions**: `meta.captions = ["merger", "ringdown"]`; see SPT-A7.
- **Acceptance**: `SPT-A1` single-mass well radially symmetric, deepening toward the mass; `SPT-A2` circular binary: fixed centre of mass and constant separation; `SPT-A3` the wave has period π in azimuth and its phase at radius r equals the source phase at `t − r/c` (inspiral); `SPT-A4` *(new)* the merger is continuous: separation shrinks monotonically to 0, phase and well depth are continuous at both joins, the centre of mass stays fixed, and afterwards one mass `0.95 (m₁ + m₂)` sits at the centre; `SPT-A5` *(new)* the strain envelope peaks at the merger, then decays as `e^{−τ/τ_rd}` while the pattern turns faster than at the end of the inspiral; `SPT-A6` *(new)* the inset draws the (scaled) source strain at every dot column of its window, its rectangle stays inside the canvas and above the title row (none below 48 × 16), no mesh dot ever enters its cleared box, and from 120 columns up it has its full width (at least 20 columns); `SPT-A7` *(new)* `caption()` is `merger` from 0.5 s before the plunge until 0.5 s after coalescence, `ringdown` from then until 2.5 s after coalescence, and `None` otherwise; `SPT-A8` *(new)* the merger is the brightest moment: the flash is 1 at coalescence, 0 through the inspiral and from 3 s after it, and it raises the mesh level; `SPT-A9` *(new)* the black holes sit in deep funnels and outshine the mesh: at 80 × 24, outside the cut, the sheet under the heavier mass lies at least 2 rows below the sheet 0.5 units to either side of it, no mesh dot lies inside a black hole's moat, the brightest rim level is at least 1.5 × every mesh cell's level within two moat radii (about three disc radii) of a black hole, and the camera fit's lowest point equals the remnant's lifted well bottom; `SPT-A10` *(new)* the chirp and the burst light up the sheet: at 120 × 40, the mean on-screen mesh level over the last 3 s of the inspiral exceeds that over its early half, the mean over the 3 s after coalescence is at least 1.4 × the early-inspiral mean, crests reach level 120 during the inspiral and the burst reaches level 180.

---
## Batch D — physics sims & narrative scenes (M7)
Shared helpers: `ode` (RK4), `timeline` + `ease` (CORE-R37..R39) plus `particles`, `grid`, `draw`. Narrative scenes loop forever: every phase has a fixed duration in simulated seconds, so a run is deterministic per seed and resizes never desynchronise the story.

### double_pendulum  (Forces & Waves, M7, M12)
- **Intent**: chaos made visible. Two double pendulums start 10⁻³ rad apart and swing as one white pendulum. Once they are about a braille cell apart they split, in one continuous fade, into an aurora-green twin and an Hα-crimson twin, and each lower bob traces a 3 s glowing path. A fan of ghost pendulums, all started inside the 10⁻³ rad gap between the twins, is drawn as the line joining their lower bobs: chaos stretches and folds that material line across the swing, and it dissolves once it outgrows its samples.
- **Model**: exact Lagrangian equations for point masses `m₁, m₂` on massless rods `L₁, L₂` (rows, aspect-correct on screen), `g` scaled so the upper arm alone has a small-angle period of 2.2 s (the coupled slow mode is ≈ 2.78 s); physics runs in model units (`L₁ = 1`) with a separate screen scale, so motion is identical at any terminal size; both twins share one pivot; state `(θ₁, θ₂, ω₁, ω₂)` advanced with `ode::integrate` at `h ≤ 1/240 s`; the twin starts with `Δθ₂ = 10⁻³` rad; high-energy seeded initial angles (both arms above horizontal ± variety); `GHOSTS = 96` ghost pendulums with the same Lagrangian and integrator, ghost `k` starting at twin A's state with `θ₂ += 10⁻³·(k + 1)/97`, so the fan spans exactly the gap between the twins; trails sample both lower bobs at 120 Hz and keep 360 samples (3 s) in model space; a divergence readout (`|Δθ| e-folding`) and energy drift; restart every 45 s with new seeded angles.
- **Inks**: twin A is `Ramp(Aurora)` and twin B is `Ramp(HAlpha)`, near-complementary false colours no other dynamics scene uses. **Split**: `apart` is the farthest the twins' matching bobs (upper or lower) have been apart this run, in model units, recorded with each trail sample and never shrinking within a run; `split = smoothstep((apart·dots_per_unit − 4)/(8 − 4))`. At `split = 0` a part is drawn once, as twin A, in `Text` at `255·v^0.6`: one white pendulum (closer than 4 dots the twins would share braille cells, and the twin drawn last would take them; the power lift keeps the brightness order and puts the trail head at 0.81 rather than a mid-grey 0.7). Above 0 each twin is drawn in its ramp at `v + (1 − split)(0.97 − v)`, starting from the pale near-white top every ramp shares, so white turns into two hues without a jump. Trail segments use their own sample's `apart` (old white path, then the colour split); the twins alternate which draws last per trail segment, so neither wins every shared cell. The pendulum outshines its trail: rods 0.8; bob discs 0.85 (still hued: every ramp's top is near white) with a one-dot core at 0.95; the upper bob is r 1.8 dots, the lower bob r 2.8 dots, or 3.5 dots from 40 rows up; trail values run `0.12 + 0.58·fresh²` (top 0.7). The pivot is a `Text` 230 disc of r 1, so the hinge is always visible. The ghost line runs from twin A's lower bob through every ghost's, in start order, to twin B's (98 samples, 97 links), at ramp `0.55·fade`: twin A's ramp for the first half, twin B's for the second, with a one-dot bead per ghost. A link longer than 0.35 model units (L₁ = 1) is left out. `fade = split · smoothstep((kept − 0.3)/0.4)`, where `kept` is the fraction of links joined: it fades in with the split (before that it lies under the white pendulum) and out as the line breaks up. Readout `Text` at `title::READOUT_LEVEL`.
- **Raster**: everything is a braille `Dots` lattice rasterised in `update` and `resize`; `render` only blits and writes the readout. The view leaves half a row for the lower bob's disc, so no dot reaches the readout row. The former top caption lines ("two double pendulums, released 0.001 rad apart" and the normal-mode sentence) are removed: the title already says it, and the lines crossed the pendulum.
- **Label** (M12 polish, GEN-R13): right-aligned on the last row in one of three forms, long `|dtheta| 3.1e-2 rad = 31.0x start (3.4 e-folds)   energy drift 2e-9   t 12/45s`, mid `|dtheta| 3.1e-2 (3.4 e-folds)` and short `e-folds 3.4`. The numbers carry no inner padding. Each form has a fixed width (84, 34 and 14 chars), the widest it can reach for any separation in `[0, π√2]` and any finite drift. The form is the first whose width fits `title::readout_room`, and its text is padded at the front to that width, so neither the choice nor the readout's left edge moves as the numbers change.
- **Stylised**: the colours are false colour; the white merge and its fade are a visual device. The ghost line is the true image of the start segment only where its 98 samples (97 links) resolve it: links stretched beyond 0.35 L₁ are dropped rather than drawn as chords, and the whole line fades out as most of its links are dropped, so a line stretched past its sampling dissolves rather than leaving scattered beads. The physics is unchanged. The white-to-colour split is set by on-screen separation (4 to 8 braille dots), so it comes earlier on larger terminals; the readout's |dtheta| and e-folds are the size-independent measure of divergence.
- **Acceptance**: `DP-A1` total energy drifts < 0.5 % over 20 s; `DP-A2` for small angles the two normal-mode frequencies match the linearised analytic values within 1 %; `DP-A3` from a seeded chaotic start the twins' separation (max so far — it saturates near π) grows ≥ 100× within 30 s; `DP-A4` rods keep their lengths exactly (bob positions derived from angles); `DP-A5` for seeds 0–3 at 80×24, at 1 s every drawn cell above the readout row is `Text` (one pendulum), and at 30 s `split` is 1 and both `Ramp(Aurora)` and `Ramp(HAlpha)` are drawn; `split` is 0 at 4 dots and 1 at 8; at 200×50 with the lower bobs 2 dots apart every lit cell is `Text`, and a swept trail draws exactly the dots it draws with twin B on top of twin A (twin B is not drawn); 10 dots apart both ramps light comparable cell counts (within 3:2); just past the merge a part is its ramp at 0.97, and at `split = 1` at its own value; trail top < rods < bobs < core (a compile-time assertion), and in white the same order holds with the trail head at ≥ 0.78; at 120×40 (seed 0, 30 s) at least two cells around twin B's lower bob carry its ramp at a value in [0.8, 0.85]; `DP-A6` ghost starts differ from twin A only in `θ₂`, strictly increasing inside `(θ₂A, θ₂B)`; `ghost_fade(split, kept)` is 0 when `split` is 0 or `kept ≤ 0.3`, equals `split` from `kept = 0.7`, and is monotone in `kept`; neighbours join only within 0.35 model units, and when `split` first reaches 1 (seed 1, bounded by a 45 s run) every link along A → ghosts → B is joined; at 30 s (seeds 0–3) the ghosts' lower bobs span > 0.5 model units; `DP-A7` the trail holds 360 samples = 3 s, and after a resize the first frame already shows the trail (> 100 cells lit at 120×40); `DP-A8` (GEN-R13) at 80×24, 40×12 and 200×50 over a 45 s run, no dot is lit on the readout row.

### mars_rover  (Voyages, M7, M12)
- **Intent**: a Perseverance-style rover crossing rust-red dunes under a butterscotch sky, with parallax ridges, dust devils, and a stop to drill a sample and beam data to an orbiter as the sol runs from morning through a pale Martian sunset glow and a dusty twilight to a starry night.
- **Model**: unchanged from M7.
  - Terrain: seeded fractal value noise, a pure function of world x, in 3 parallax layers.
  - Rocker–bogie suspension solved by Newton's method.
  - The sol `Timeline`: drive 18 s → deploy 3 s → drill 4 s → stow 2 s → transmit 4 s.
  - A 90 s sol at Jezero's latitude on a warped clock (hour angle piecewise linear in time), with Phobos on its real synodic period (scaled).
  - Dust particles and 0–2 dust devils.
- **Look (M12)**: the sky, Sun, Phobos and the three ground layers are one half-block Pixels image, rasterised in `update`. Each column reads sky, then far ridge, mid plain and near ground, going down.
  - The whole sky is one `Blackbody` ramp, which runs dark red, tan, white, pale blue. By day it is butterscotch (the ramp's tan stretch, about 0.34–0.42), paler toward the dusty horizon and whitening around the Sun. Its brightness follows the daylight `smooth(−14°, 8°)` of the Sun's elevation, so it fades to black through the twilight by level, and is the terminal's black below the last visible level.
  - Around a low Sun, forward scattering makes an aureole: strength `smooth(25°, 4°) · smooth(−12°, −2°)` times a Gaussian in angle from the Sun (radius 0.42 of the Sun's height range), which lifts the level to 0.565 (level 144, ~6900 K), a pale blue-white just past the ramp's white point. The ramp is lightest at its white point (level 140, 6500 K), so a bluer target would draw a white ring round a dimmer blue core; at this peak the glow is brightest at the Sun and fades outward, blending into the sky with no dither and no hard edge.
  - The Sun is a small disc at the top of `Ramp(Ember)`, white-hot, the brightest ink of every theme; drawn until it sets behind the ridge.
  - Stars come out only once the aureole has faded (`smooth(−11°, −18°)`), as braille dots with varied, twinkling levels, only in cells whose two pixels are both black sky (never in a skyline cell), and off the readout row.
  - Aerial perspective: the far ridges take the dusty air's own ramp, a muted `Blackbody` brown between the sky and the foreground, lit by the sky: at night they sink to a dim silhouette no brighter than the foreground; the mid plain is a dark rust and the near ground a bright rust (`Ramp(Ember)`); near slopes that face the Sun are a little brighter.
  - The rover, dust, dust devils, orbiter and text are drawn over the landscape. Line art and text take the landscape pixel under them as their cell background (CORE-R86), so they never punch black boxes into it. Dust over the landscape lightens the half blocks; over the sky it stays tan, never lifted past ~5000 K (level 118) toward the ramp's white and blue. The rover's inks and the wheel dust dim with the daylight (to 35 % at night) so they sit in the dusk, and dust devils, raised by the day's heating, fade out with it. The M7 rose dusk band is dropped.
- **Stylisation**: false colour. Shading moves down the Blackbody ramp, so the sky and the far ridges redden as they darken (a palette effect, not a physical one). The aureole is paler than Mars's blue one: on this ramp a blue brighter than white does not exist. A 90 s sol on a warped clock: the day runs at an easy pace, the sunset lingers and the night is brief, so the twilight is far shorter than Mars's real hour or two. Phobos's period is scaled with the sol.
- **Inks**: the sky, its aureole and the far ridges in `Blackbody`; the mid and near ground in `Ramp(Ember)`; the Sun at the top of `Ramp(Ember)`; stars in `Star` (braille); Phobos, the rover's wheels and arm in `Structure`; the rover body in `Text`; the orbiter in `Accent`; the signal in `Wave`; dust in `Particle`.
- **Acceptance**: `MR-A1` every wheel contact lies on the surface within 0.5 row; `MR-A2` terrain height is a pure, continuous function of world x; `MR-A3` the sol timeline runs in order and loops; `MR-A4` at 120×40 no sky pixel below the Sun's level is `Ramp(Ice)` (one sky ramp), at noon more than half the pixels are `Blackbody` sky and 90 % of those are at levels 70–125 (tan), at sunset (Sun < 5° up) more than 30 sky pixels are at level 128 or above (toward white), at night (Sun < −30°) stars show, stars appear only where the aureole's strength is below 0.05, the daylight is above 0.2 wherever the aureole's strength is above 0.5, in rings around the Sun the mean sky luminance (astro theme) never rises going out (± 0.01) and falls by more than half, and at 200×50 at 45, 47 and 50 s, straight up from the setting Sun, no sky pixel is brighter than the one below it (± 0.03), and at noon the far ridge is `Blackbody` and the near ground `Ramp(Ember)`, while at night the far ridge is drawn, no brighter than the near ground and under a third of its noon luminance; `MR-A5` down every column the layers never go back up the stack (sky → far → mid → near), and each column ends in near ground; `MR-A6` every non-block glyph over a landscape pixel carries a background ink (no black boxes); `MR-A7` at noon and in the afternoon the Sun's pixels are at their ramp's top level (255) and every other ramp pixel is below 245; `MR-A8` at night every star cell is a braille glyph, none on row 0, and more than 20 show at 120×40, and at 200×50 through the night every star cell's two pixels are black sky; `MR-A9` the rover's hull at night is below 0.6 of its level at noon; `MR-A10` a dust veil over a `Blackbody` sky cell lifts it no higher than level 118 (or its own level, if higher) while it still lightens `Ramp(Ember)` ground, and at 200×50 through the day no rendered `Blackbody` ink is lifted past both its sky pixel and level 118.

### voyager_journey  (Voyages, M7, M12)
- **Intent**: Voyager 2's Grand Tour, 1977–1989, then its run out of the heliosphere. Each flyby visibly flings it faster: the inset's heliocentric speed jumps at Jupiter, Saturn and Uranus and ends above the Sun's escape speed. At Neptune the craft dives over Neptune's north pole toward Triton; Neptune's pull bends it 48° south of the ecliptic and slows it. The map then pulls back until the craft crosses the heliopause into interstellar space (2018-11-05, 119 AU).
- **Model**:
  - A log-compressed heliocentric top-down map (VOY-A4): a distance `r` is drawn at `ρ = ln(1 + r/0.6 AU) / ln(1 + r_edge/0.6 AU)` of the map's radius.
  - Planets are on circular orbits with J2000 elements (Standish) and Kepler periods (VOY-A2).
  - The spacecraft flies five Kepler conics. Four are launch → Jupiter → Saturn → Uranus → Neptune, each the prograde Lambert solution (universal variables, bisection on z) from one planet's model position on its real encounter date to the next's. The fifth leaves Neptune on its encounter date at Voyager 2's excess speed today (v∞ = 14.9 km/s, ~15.4 km/s at 119 AU); its flight-path angle is fitted by bisection so the craft reaches 119 AU on 2018-11-05, which puts it at 83.6 AU at the termination-shock crossing (2007-08-30; measured 83.7 AU). All are propagated with the universal-variable Kepler solver.
  - A 1500-sample table of the path, uniform in mission day, is built once; the trail and the speed inset are drawn from it, and only the craft's own position is solved each frame.
  - The clock maps 0–54 s to launch → heliopause, with slow-downs at the encounters, over three acts: tour 43 s, outward 11 s, beyond 5 s.
  - During the outward act the map's edge eases (in log) from 34 AU to 150 AU. The termination shock (83.7 AU) and the heliopause (119 AU) are drawn as rings, each once the map holds it, brightening once the craft has crossed it, the heliopause the denser.
- **Stylisation**:
  - Planets are circular and coplanar, so the flybys are the model's geometry; Lambert speeds are within a few km/s of Voyager 2's real ones.
  - The post-Neptune dive 48° south of the ecliptic is drawn in the map plane. The model's Neptune slow-down (~4.2 km/s, 21.0 → 16.8 km/s) is about twice the real one (~2 km/s), since its Uranus–Neptune leg arrives fast.
  - The heliosphere is drawn as two Sun-centred circles at Voyager 2's own crossing distances; the real boundary is not spherical (Voyager 1 met the termination shock at 94 AU and the heliopause at 122 AU).
  - The map is turned so the outbound leg points at the roomier screen axis (left on a landscape canvas, down on a portrait one); north is still up out of the page, prograde counter-clockwise. Where the inset would overlap it, the map moves left (its rim a cell inside the left edge), shrinking only if that is not enough.
  - Distances are log-compressed (`ρ` above), so Earth through Neptune, and later the heliopause, fit on one screen. Directions and dates are true, but the orbits' spacing and the path's shape are not to scale: the rings for 1, 5.2, 9.5, 19.2 and 30 AU come out nearly evenly spaced, the 1977–89 path looks like a tight spiral, and the craft at 44 AU is drawn just outside Neptune's 30 AU ring.
  - Planet discs are sized for legibility, not to scale, and coloured in false colour.
  - The gravity assists are the jump between two conics, not an integrated flyby.
  - The Pale Blue Dot epilogue is cut (Voyager 1 took it).
- **Layers**: the Sun (a tone-mapped glow with a white-hot core), the planet discs and the craft are half-block Pixels. Each planet is centred on a pixel centre, so every disc is a symmetric stamp (one pixel, a plus, a 3 × 3 square, a rounded disc), never a tetromino, with its dark side kept visible. Jupiter is banded, Saturn ringed, each disc is lit on its sunward half, and each giant flares for 2 s as the craft swings past it. The craft is a white pixel in a small Ember glow. Stars, orbits, trail (its newest tenth at full strength and two dots wide), flyby rings, heliosphere rings and the speed inset are braille Dots. All are rasterised in `update`.
- **Text**: the row-0 readout `VOYAGER 2  1981-08-26  9.6 AU  20.5 km/s` (shortened to fit); encounter captions and outward lines on row 1, centred in the columns left of the inset; each planet's initial (`E J S U N`) beside its disc; the heliosphere rings' names (`heliopause 119 AU`, `termination shock 84 AU`, shortened or skipped to fit) beside the heliopause ring's right edge; the inset's labels (`heliocentric speed`, `40 km/s`, `escape`, `AU`, `J S U N` distance ticks). Labels keep off rows 0–1, the title row and the inset.
- **Inset**: top-right, 40×10 cells, only on canvases ≥ 110×30. It plots speed (0–42 km/s) against log distance (0.8–130 AU), with the Sun's escape speed `√(2μ/r)` dotted in. The map keeps out of the inset's cells and its labels (one column left of the plot, two rows below it): its rim keeps a cell clear of them, no star is placed there, and no ring (a flyby's flash can reach past the rim) is drawn there.
- **Captions** (`meta.captions`): `Jupiter assist`, `Saturn assist`, `Uranus assist` and `Neptune flyby` from 0.8 s before each encounter until 4.5 s after it or 1.5 mission years after it, whichever comes first (the clock races after Neptune); then, by mission date, `to the heliopause` until 2018-11-05 and `interstellar space` from then on; none on the tour's cruise legs (the title's `planets fling it on`). The row-1 lines keep the same windows: each encounter's line with its caption, the termination shock's from a year before the crossing to 1.5 years after it, the heliopause's from the crossing, on the same frame as `interstellar space`.
- **Inks**: the Sun in `Ramp(Ember)`, its core at the ramp's top; planets in `Ramp(Ice)` and `Ramp(Ember)` (kept below the Sun's core); orbits and the inset axes and escape curve in `Ramp(Ice)`; the trail, flyby rings, speed trace and the craft's glow in `Ramp(Ember)`; heliosphere rings and their labels in `Ramp(Plasma)`; the craft and stars in `Star`; text in `Text`.
- **Acceptance**: `VOY-A1` at each encounter date the spacecraft is at the planet's model position (< 1e-6 a) and within 0.5 cell on screen, and the path is continuous, outbound (within 1 % while leaving perihelion) and prograde; `VOY-A2` planet periods follow Kepler and match the real ones within 1 %; `VOY-A3` calendar conversion round-trips, and the clock is strictly monotone, prints the exact dates at the key times (including 2018-11-05) and slows at encounters; `VOY-A4` the radial map is strictly monotone at every edge and fits Neptune during the tour, and every planet is drawn in its own ramp throughout the tour (except where the craft covers it), at 80×24, 120×40 and 200×50 each planet's silhouette is symmetric across both axes through its centre pixel with every pixel at or above the last visible level, and the Sun's core is the top of its ramp, no other ramp pixel within 250; `VOY-A5` each Lambert leg conserves energy and angular momentum (1e-9) and lands on its target planet on time (< 1e-6 AU), propagation round-trips, and Lambert recovers a Hohmann transfer's speed; `VOY-A6` heliocentric speed jumps by more than 5 % at Jupiter, Saturn and Uranus, Neptune slows it by 1–5 km/s, the craft is bound before Jupiter and above escape speed after it all the way out, and it is at 14.8–16 km/s at the heliopause; `VOY-A7` the craft is at 119 ± 0.01 AU on 2018-11-05 and within 1 AU of 83.7 AU on 2007-08-30, the map edge runs monotonically from 34 to 150 AU, and at the heliopause the craft is at least 2 cells inside the frame (and above the title row) at 200×50, 120×40, 100×30, 80×24 and 40×40; `VOY-A8` the speed trace lies inside the top-right inset, well clear of the title row, there is no inset at 80×24, and at 110×30, 120×40 and 200×50 every encounter caption and the intact inset header ` heliocentric speed` share row 1; at 120×40, 110×30, 160×45 and 200×50 the map's rim, every trail point and every Sun, planet and craft pixel stay out of the inset's cells all through the loop; at 200×50 no event line is on row 1 two mission years after its event, and the heliopause line shows exactly when the mission date has reached 2018-11-05 and the caption is `interstellar space`; `VOY-A9` at 200×50 and 80×24 each planet's initial is within 5 cells right of its disc.

### moon_landing  (Voyages, M7, M12)
- **Intent**: Apollo 11's powered descent. The LM, its descent stage in gold foil, pitches over and throttles above a cratered Mare Tranquillitatis raked by an 11° Sun, with long dark shadows off every rim, black crescents under the crater walls and its own shadow running ahead. It kicks up flat sheets of dust, lights "CONTACT LIGHT", reports "The Eagle has landed" and plants the flag, while the Earth, a gibbous, hangs motionless high in the black sky.
- **Model**: unchanged from M7:
  - a 2-D point-mass LM with real Apollo numbers (`T ≤ 45.04 kN`, Isp 311 s, lunar g), integrated with RK4;
  - P64 E-guidance to a gate 8 m above the site, then P66 sink-rate descent with position hold;
  - a seeded crater field that is a pure function of x;
  - ballistic dust;
  - the `Timeline` descent 23 s → touchdown 4 s → flag 5 s → earth 4 s.

  The last beat was `earthrise` and is now `earth`. The Earth never rises at Tranquility Base (0.7° N, 23.5° E): it hangs ~66° up, fixed. It is drawn in every act at the same place, high on the side the LM flies toward. It shows the phase of 20 July 1969: a six-day waxing Moon, so a gibbous Earth, `(1 + cos α)/2 = 0.65` lit, from the Sun's side of the sky. The mid-Atlantic (~45° W, 5° S) faces the Moon (approximate), and the day side is the correct, western hemisphere: at the landing, 20:17 UTC, the subsolar point was near 123° W, 20.6° N, so the lit limb shows the Americas and the Pacific, with Africa and Europe on the night side. Drawn north up, the globe's west limb is on the left; when the scene's Sun is on the right, the globe is drawn south up (turned half a turn), so the lit limb still shows the Americas. The globe's Sun lies in the sky direction of the subsolar point seen from the sub-lunar one, tilted ~22° toward north, and the viewer sits 5° south of the equator: in July the whole Arctic is lit and the Antarctic dark.
- **Look (M12)**:
  - The regolith is a half-block Pixels image rasterised in `update`, and fills the bottom quarter (up to 12 rows).
  - The skyline is lit by the Lambert term of each slope under the 11° Sun. A single down-Sun sweep casts shadows `h / tan 11°` long; where terrain up-Sun or the LM shades it, the skyline's two-pixel surface is the darkest regolith grey (level 16), never black, so the ground's silhouette stays unbroken against the black sky. The LM's shadow is a thin sliver on that surface, running ahead of it.
  - The plain in front is evenly lit, with world-anchored rocks and pits, and darkens toward the viewer. Sixteen seeded craters (3–25 m) lie on it as ellipses, foreshortened more toward the skyline, and never over the skyline's surface. The sun-side rim's shadow is the bowl minus the bowl shifted 0.8 radii away from the Sun (the reach `depth / tan 11°` of a mature, shallow mare bowl, depth ≈ D/13): a crescent against the sun-side wall, a little over a third of the bowl. Its core (the bowl minus a 0.4-radius shift) is the terminal's black; the rest is dark grey, filled by light scattered off the sunlit far wall. The lit floor is a mid grey, the far wall facing the Sun bright, and the outer rim bright on the sun side, dim on the far slope.
  - Black is the terminal's black (an empty pixel), since even `Structure`'s lowest level renders as a grey in every theme; regolith below the last visible level (12, CORE-R66) is black.
  - The Earth is painted with `satellite_orbit`'s globe, `h/12` rows in radius (0.8–3.4), so the LM stays the focus.
  - Dust flies as braille sheets. The descent engine's hypergolic plume is nearly invisible in vacuum, so it is a faint flickering cone of Plasma dots.
  - The LM sprite, flag, astronaut and text are glyphs; they take the regolith pixel under them as background (CORE-R86). The sprite's footpad row is whole cells, so the site under the sprite, when it lies up to a cell below the pads, is raised to them: the LM stands on regolith, never over a sky-black notch.
- **Stylisation**:
  - The Earth is drawn larger than its true 1.9°.
  - The plain's craters are laid on the plain as a picture, not in perspective from the terrain profile.
  - Stars are shown, though a sunlit-surface exposure would hide them.
  - The plume colour is stylised.
  - The ALT, V/S and FUEL readout is the compressed model's, not Apollo 11's telemetry: it sinks several times faster below 100 m (≈ −8.6 m/s at 100 m, where Eagle came down at 1–3 m/s) and shows P64 well below the real handover.
  - The last minute of the descent is compressed into ≈ 21 s. P64 is flown down to 8 m (Apollo 11 switched to P66 near 150 m and flew its last ~90 s in P66); the handover is compressed with the rest of the last minute.
  - The Earth's orientation in the sky is not modelled: it is drawn north up, or south up when the Sun is on the right.
- **Inks**: regolith and its shadows in `Structure`, crater cores in the terminal's black (`Background`); the descent stage in `Sun` (gold); the ascent stage in `Text`; legs in `Structure`; the plume in `Ramp(Plasma)`; dust in `Structure`; the Earth in the globe's Ice, Aurora and Ember; stars in `Star`; captions in `Text`.
- **Acceptance**: `ML-A1` soft landing for the seeded start set (|vz| ≤ 1, |vx| ≤ 0.5 m/s, fuel ≥ 0); `ML-A2` the rocket equation holds within 0.5 %; `ML-A3` the LM never penetrates the terrain; `ML-A4` the phases, now descent → touchdown → flag → earth, run in order and loop; `ML-A5` flat ground's light is `sin 11°`, a slope facing the Sun is brighter than flat and flat brighter than one facing away, a slope steeper than the Sun facing away is dark, a 10 m block casts a shadow exactly `10 / tan 11°` long down-Sun for either approach side, and a zero sample spacing shades nothing; `ML-A6` the Earth's centre and radius are the same all through a loop, in the top 45 % of the frame, its radius at most `h/11` rows, and its lit fraction is 0.6–0.7; for either approach side the disc point under the scene's Sun lies within 40° of longitude of 123° W, and that limb is brighter than the opposite one, and the Sun stands within 8° of 20.6° N on the globe; `ML-A7` at 12 s at 120×40 the frame shows gold foil (`Sun`), more than 400 half-block regolith cells and the Earth (`Ramp(Ice)`); `ML-A8` for a Sun on either side, as a multiple of the plain's level, a plain crater is black (0) under its sun-side wall, dark but not black (0–0.5) at the shadow's edge, lit (≥ 0.7) just past its centre, bright (> 1) on its far wall and sun-side outer rim, dim (< 1) on its far outer slope, and none off it; its shadow covers 25–50 % of the bowl and the black core over 5 % and under 0.6 of the shadow; at 200×50 more than 10 pixels deep in the plain are black; and at 200×50, 120×40 and 80×24, all through a descent and touchdown, every column's skyline surface pixel is not black; and at touchdown and after, at 200×50, 120×40 and 80×24 for seeds 42, 3, 8 and 5, the rendered cell under every footpad-row glyph has regolith in its top half.

## Batch E — scientific art: fields, gravity, chemistry & biology (M10)
Design: `docs/design/M10-scientific-art-batch.md`; ADR-0010 (layered raster: `raster::Dots` braille, `raster::Pixels` half-block with `Cell.bg`), ADR-0011 (colormap roles `Seq`/`Div`), ADR-0012 (simulation lattice decoupled from the display, `grid::lattice_for`).
New core helpers (CORE-R40..R59, RND-R6/R7): `raster::{Dots, Pixels, Density}`, `grid::{resample_bilinear, resample_nearest, lattice_for, SampleMap, laplacian9}`, `ode::{verlet_step, verlet_integrate, tridiag_solve}`, `geom::{Camera, Projector}`, `ink::{seq, div, soft_clip, seq_value, div_value}`, `text::StackText`, `draw::text_mode`, `particles::{SpatialHash, InverseCdf}`, `rng::SplitMix64`, `Category::{Biology, Chemistry}`; the post-M10 audit lifted more shared code into core (CORE-R60..R70); plus the existing `draw`, `particles`, `grid`, `ode`, `timeline`/`ease` and `geom` helpers.
The `gray_scott` upgrade is specified in place in Batch C above (`CHEM-A5`..`CHEM-A8`).

### action_potential  (Physics of Life, M10, M12)
- **Intent**: a nerve impulse made visible. A stylised neuron rests in the dark: soma, branching dendrites, a long unmyelinated axon and a fan of synaptic terminals, all drawn as dim membrane outlines. A stimulus fires the soma, and a gold flame of depolarisation runs down the axon with a quiet blue after-hyperpolarisation wake behind it, while warm Na⁺ streaks fall in at its front and cool K⁺ streaks drift out behind it. It lights the terminals, which release a small puff of transmitter. An oscilloscope inset, wired to the recording electrode by a faint dotted lead, draws the classic spike waveform at the moment the pulse passes the electrode. A looping protocol shows four things in turn: a single spike, a train that slows as it runs into its own refractory wake, two spikes annihilating in collision, and the all-or-none threshold.
- **Model**: Hodgkin & Huxley (1952) squid giant axon, in the modern rest = −65 mV convention (Dayan & Abbott 2001).
  - **Cable equation**: `C_m ∂V/∂t = (a / 2R_i) ∂²V/∂x² − ḡ_Na m³h (V − E_Na) − ḡ_K n⁴ (V − E_K) − ḡ_L (V − E_L) + I_stim`, with `C_m = 1 μF/cm²`, `ḡ_Na = 120`, `ḡ_K = 36`, `ḡ_L = 0.3 mS/cm²`, `E_Na = +50`, `E_K = −77` and `E_L = −54.387 mV`.
  - **Rates** (ms⁻¹, V in mV): `α_m = 0.1(V + 40)/(1 − e^{−(V+40)/10})`, `β_m = 4 e^{−(V+65)/18}`, `α_h = 0.07 e^{−(V+65)/20}`, `β_h = 1/(1 + e^{−(V+35)/10})`, `α_n = 0.01(V + 55)/(1 − e^{−(V+55)/10})`, `β_n = 0.125 e^{−(V+65)/80}`. All are scaled by `φ = 3^{(T − 6.3)/10}` with `T = 6.3 °C`, so `φ = 1`. The removable singularities at −40 and −55 mV go through an `x/(1 − e^{−x})` helper with a series branch.
  - **Geometry**: axon radius `a = 238 μm` and `R_i = 35.4 Ω·cm` (HH 1952) give `D = a/(2 R_i C_m) = 0.336 cm²/ms`. The cable is `L = 10 cm` in `N = 200` compartments (`Δx = 0.5 mm`, ≈ 1/14 of the 7 mm resting length constant), with sealed (no-flux) ends.
  - **Sub-step (10 μs)**: first a Rush–Larsen (1978) exponential update of m, h and n, which is exact for frozen V and stays in `[0, 1]`. Then backward-Euler V with the ionic conductances frozen (Hines 1984), solved as a tridiagonal M-matrix by the Thomas algorithm into preallocated scratch. This is unconditionally stable; explicit Euler would need a step ≤ 3.7 μs and RK4 ≤ 5 μs. Against a converged fine-grid run (Crank–Nicolson, Δx = 0.05 mm, 0.5 μs: 12.31 and 18.72 m/s) the velocity error is 0.4 % at 6.3 °C and 0.9 % at 18.5 °C.
  - **Clock**: it counts integer sub-steps (`h = 0.01 ms`, through an accumulator with 1e-9 slack), so how frames chop time cannot change results. Slow motion is 4 ms of axon time per simulated second (250×).
  - **Measured physiology** (converged): propagated peak +37.9 mV; after-hyperpolarisation minimum −75.9 mV; V > −40 mV for 2.3 ms at a point (a ≈ 2.9 cm footprint); conduction velocity 12.3 m/s at 6.3 °C and 18.7 m/s at 18.5 °C (HH computed 18.8 m/s and measured 21.2 m/s); threshold ≈ 90 μA/cm² for a 0.3 ms pulse over the first 0.25 cm.
  - **Protocol**: a looping `Timeline` of 18 s (= 72 ms). `single` 3 s: a soma pulse at 0.2 s. `train` 8 s: pulses at 3.0, 5.5 and 8.0 s, 10 ms apart. `collision` 4.5 s: soma and terminal end stimulated together at 13.0 s, the neurophysiologist's collision test. `threshold` 2.5 s: a 0.5× pulse at 16.0 s. Strong pulses are 270 μA/cm² (3× threshold) for 0.3 ms over the first (or last) 0.25 cm, and the weak pulse is 45 μA/cm².
  - **Refractory timing**: every strong pulse starts ≥ 9.5 ms after the last spike passed its site. That is beyond the ≈ 6–8 ms refractory limit, so every strong pulse fires, but inside the relative refractory period, so each train spike runs slower than the last (measured 10.9, 10.1 and 9.8 m/s, against 12.3 isolated). The model state runs continuously across loops, and the second loop repeats the first to within 0.01 s. The first loop starts from rest; every later one starts 20 ms after the collision, in its supernormal phase (at 3–7 cm `h` is ≈ 0.01 above and `n` ≈ 0.006 below their resting values), so its single spike runs 0.8 % faster: 12.36 against 12.26 m/s. Moving the weak pulse (15.2–17.0 s) does not change this. Later loops repeat one another to 1e-6 m/s.
  - **Weak pulse**: it lands on the after-hyperpolarisation left by the collision and lifts the soma to ≈ −61 mV (≈ −57 mV from rest). It spreads passively ≈ 1 cm and fades.
  - **Stimulus markers**: each pulse lights a stimulus marker for 0.25 s, at a level proportional to its amplitude, so the weak kick visibly happens. The marker is `>` left of the soma, and `<` just before the terminals for the far-end pulse.
  - **Stylisation**: the soma, dendrites and terminal arbor are drawn, not simulated; the real giant axon is a syncytium grown from the stellate ganglion. The soma shows V of compartment 0, and the terminal arbor fans out the last 0.8 cm of the cable.
  - **Vesicles**: at each 0 mV up-crossing at the cable end, every bouton emits 4 vesicle dots (`ParticlePool`, capacity 20 = 5 boutons × 4). They drift 1–2 cells across the cleft and fade within 0.4 s.
- **Motion**: at t = 0 the neuron rests, showing only its outline. The stimulus marker and soma flash at 0.2 s. By 1 s the pulse is a third of the way along the axon (GEN-R9). It reaches the terminals at ≈ 2.3 s, with a bouton flash and a vesicle puff. The trace scrolls continuously (a 20 ms = 5 s window) and draws each spike as the pulse passes the electrode (compartment 100, 5 cm, ≈ 1.1 s after the stimulus). In the train, each spike leaves the soma as the previous one reaches the terminals, and each is visibly slower. In the collision beat, two pulses converge and vanish near mid-axon at ≈ 14.1 s. In the threshold beat the marker blinks and a faint soma glow spreads about a centimetre and fades within ≈ 1 s. Nothing travels, and the trace stays flat. The weak kick lands 12 ms after the collision spike left the soma, on its after-hyperpolarisation (≈ −69 mV), so its half-threshold amplitude and the residual refractoriness both hold it below threshold; AP-A5 checks that the same pulse also fails from rest. The loop is 18 s.
- **Resize**: the simulation does not depend on size, so no model state is touched. Geometry is rebuilt from normalised parameters:
  - the soma centre and radius;
  - the axon path, stored as a per-pixel table of arclength `s`, with V linearly interpolated between compartments;
  - the dendrite tree. Its topology is drawn from `ctx.rng` on the first resize only and stored in a fixed array of ≤ 40 segments, each ending at a node `(angle, ρ)`: polar about the soma centre, ρ soma radii beyond the rim, drawn at `r + k·ρ`. The tree is radial and planar. Three primaries leave the rim radially within a ±1.45 rad cone pointing left: one up (−1.15 rad), one down (+1.15) and one at ±0.55 on a random side, each jittered by ±0.08, so the stimulus marker straight left stays clear. Each primary owns the wedge of the cone between the midpoints to its neighbours. Every fork splits its wedge at the parent's angle and turns each child 22–38° off the parent's radial line (measured at the child's end), clamped inside its half-wedge. Since subtrees keep to disjoint convex wedges about the soma centre, no two branches ever cross. Radial reach by depth is 0.9, 0.7, 0.55 and 0.45 soma radii (±20 %). Primaries and their children always fork and about half the depth-2 twigs fork again. On resize each primary's subtree gets its own scale k, the largest that fits left of, above or below the soma (at most 1.3 r and 1.6× the tightest subtree's k: the room is mostly above and below, and terminal rows are coarse). The drawn depth follows the soma radius r: depth 1 below r = 4 cells, depth 2 below 8, depth 3 from 8 up, so a small neuron draws a few long branches instead of a scribble. A twig spanning under 2 cells (`max(|Δx|/2, |Δy|/4)` in dots) is dropped with its subtree; a short primary is stretched to 2 cells instead;
  - the terminal branches, the electrode and stimulus markers, and the trace panel rectangle.

  Tiers: the full neuron when w ≥ 24 and h ≥ 8; otherwise only the axon, as one Pixels row spanning the width. The axoplasm is 2, 4 or 6 pixel rows (a 2-, 3- or 4-row tube) at heights < 36, < 48 and ≥ 48 (M12). The far-end marker `<` sits just right of the boutons, clamped on screen. The trace panel appears only when w ≥ 56 and h ≥ 16, starting at row 1 and column 0.625 w, and ideally ending at row 1 + 0.27 h. The trace's after-hyperpolarisation dip lies on or just above the panel's bottom row, so that row ends at least two rows above the top bouton, with one blank row between, and the panel shrinks to fit (at least 4 rows). If 4 rows do not fit, the panel keeps its height and ends three columns left of the arbor's first column (the trunk end), provided it keeps at least 12 columns; otherwise there is no panel. It always ends at least two columns from the right edge (x1 ≤ w − 3), the boutons' margin. A dotted lead joins the electrode to the scope. It climbs from the cell above the electrode to the trace's resting baseline and then runs along the baseline's dot row to one blank cell before the `-65` tick. Where the electrode sits under the tick or the panel, the lead stops one row below them. Drawing happens in `update` and `resize` (no `RefCell`): the axon, soma and boutons go into a `raster::Pixels`, the dendrites, arbor and trace into one `raster::Dots` and the vesicles into another; `render` only blits them and writes markers and labels.
- **Inks** (M12): axoplasm, soma fill and boutons carry a signed tone. For V ≥ rest it is `x = √((V − V_rest)/105 mV)`, peaking at ≈ +0.99; the square root keeps millivolt-scale subthreshold responses visible. Below rest it is `x = −0.55·((V_rest − V)/12 mV)^1.5` (≈ −0.48 at the −75.9 mV minimum).
  - The tone is drawn on the theme's diverging map, `Role::Div`, which by definition means above/below a reference, here rest. False colour, like a voltage-dye movie: in astro amber to pale gold above rest, blue below and dark at rest; blue-red in rainbow; signed grey in mono. No other Life scene uses `Div`.
  - `x ≥ 0` is `div(x)`. `x < 0` is `div(−max(0, |x| − 0.3))`: it starts at the map's dark centre at `x = −0.3` (V ≈ −73 mV) and reaches ≈ −0.18 at the −75.9 mV minimum, a dim blue glow just behind the spike, in keeping with a ≈ 10 mV undershoot beside a ≈ 105 mV spike. The shallow tail of the after-hyperpolarisation is the plate.
  - Filled regions (axoplasm, soma fill) stay blank for `−0.3 ≤ x < 6.5/127` (+0.2 mV above rest, −8 mV below), so a resting axon reads as its two membrane lines and the half-block glyphs change as a spike passes. Outlines carry the form: `Structure` levels 60–110 for the membrane lines above and below the axon, the soma rim, dendrites and terminal branches.
  - Na⁺ (`Hot`) and K⁺ (`Cold`) match the side of the map their current drives V toward.
  - The oscilloscope trace is `Div` level 230 (≈ +0.8), an amber phosphor. The recording electrode and stimulus markers are `Accent`; the electrode's lead is `Dim` 60 and dotted (every other dot); the trace baseline and the `+40`/`-65` ticks are `Dim`, and `+40` shows only while the window holds a sample above −20 mV (it labels a spike's scale and would float alone over a flat baseline). Vesicles are `Star` at `255·(0.25 + 0.7·fade)`. The label is `Text`.
  - *Mono theme*: `Div` keeps the sign there (the negative side tops out at a mid grey, the positive at white). Na⁺ and K⁺ differ only by direction (inward fall against outward drift) and position (front against behind); that is a known limitation.
- **Ions** (M12): each frame, every trunk pixel column spawns streaks of the species whose current dominates at its compartment, `I_Na = ḡ_Na m³h(V − E_Na)` or `I_K = ḡ_K n⁴(V − E_K)` by magnitude: Na⁺ on the rising edge, K⁺ on the repolarising edge behind it, so the two form separate clusters.
  - The expected count is `(12/trunk columns)·max(0, |I| − 25 μA/cm²)·Δt_ms / Q`, with Δt the frame's simulated axon time and `Q` the charge one spike carries above the floor while that species dominates (70 nC/cm² for Na⁺, 280 for K⁺ at 6.3 °C). One spike sends about 12 streaks of each species across the whole trunk, at any width.
  - The whole part spawns. The fractional part, the side (above or below the tube) and the dot column within the cell are drawn by `hash01` coins keyed by `cell_key(column, species) ⊕ mix(step)`. `ctx.rng` is untouched, so AP-A7 still holds.
  - Na⁺ starts 7 dots outside a membrane and falls onto it within 0.35 s, brightening from 0.7 to 1.0. K⁺ starts on the membrane and drifts 7 dots out, fading from 1.0 to 0.6. Each is drawn as a 4-dot vertical stroke along its motion, head first. The pool holds 160. The resting leak is below the floor and is not shown.
  - Ions are drawn in Unicode only: ASCII's dot-count glyphs would read as the voltage glyphs `+`/`#`.
  - *Stylisation*: the streaks are symbolic, each a fixed share of the dominant current's charge; in HH both currents flow through most of the spike, and only the larger is shown. Warm Na⁺ flows in on the rising phase and cool K⁺ flows out on the falling phase; that is a species key, not the potential. Real fluxes are about 10⁴ ions/μm² per spike and change the concentrations negligibly.
- **ASCII**: in the `Pixels::blit` closure, a pixel's tone is read back from its `Div` ink (any tone in `[−0.3, 0)` reads as 0) and maps to `@` at x ≥ 113.5/127 (V ≳ +20 mV), `#` at ≥ 82.5/127 (V ≳ −20 mV), `+` at ≥ 16.5/127 (V ≳ −63 mV), `~` below −44.5/128 (V ≲ −74 mV, the deep, recent after-hyperpolarisation only), else rest. These equal the M10 Div levels 242/211/145/84. At rest the axon is `=`. Structure-only cells are `=` on the axon membrane and `o` on the boutons. The soma is drawn at cell resolution: its rim is the ellipse of radii r × r/2 cells (cells are two pixels tall), rim cells being those inside with a 4-neighbour outside, drawn `o` whatever the voltage; cells inside show the fill's voltage glyph. Dendrites, the terminal arbor and the trace are line art, one glyph per cell. Dendrites and arbor use slope glyphs (`-` `/` `\` `|`). Dendrites are drawn shallowest first, each child skipping its first cell (its parent's last), and never overwrite a drawn cell, so the parent's stroke wins every junction. An arbor cell above rest shows its voltage glyph (`+` `#` `@`), except at the junction with the trunk. Below rest an arbor cell keeps its stroke and a bouton keeps its `o`, in the `Div` ink, so the cool colour carries the after-hyperpolarisation and the fan never dissolves into `~`. The `~` of the deep wake is kept for the axon and soma fill, where the pulse pattern below depends on it. The trace uses `|` where it crosses a cell top to bottom, `/` or `\` where it rises or falls within one, and `_` or `-` for a flat run in a cell's lower or upper half; the baseline shows as `.` ticks only where the trace has left it. Vesicles use the Dots density fallback. The electrode is `v` and the stimulus markers are `>` and `<`. The lead is `:` in every row of its climb and `.` in every other column along the baseline, drawn first so that everything else overwrites it. A spike on the axon reads `==~~~~~~~~===++++#####@@@@#+++===` moving right: `~` marks only the deep, recent wake, so the resting `=` frames each pulse.
- **Captions** (M12): single `None`, so the title's own `ion channels open` shows; train `refractory slowing`; collision `spikes annihilate`; threshold `all-or-none`. The half-threshold kick fails where the 3× pulses fired: the beat shows the *none* of all-or-none against the *all* before it.
- **Label**: right-aligned on the last row with `draw::label_right` (GEN-R13), drawn only when h ≥ 3. The beat name left the readout in M12, because it is the caption now. The form depends on `title::stable_readout_room` (CORE-R83), so it never flickers with the beat and only grows with the width:
  - room ≥ 48: `Hodgkin-Huxley 1952  squid axon 6.3C  v=12.3 m/s`;
  - room ≥ 25: `HH squid 6.3C  v=12.3 m/s`;
  - room ≥ 19: `HH 6.3C  v=12.3 m/s`;
  - room ≥ 7: `HH 6.3C`;
  - none below.

  Parts are separated by two blanks, like every other readout (the ` | ` of the HUD stays the HUD's). At h = 12 the breakpoints are w 96 long, 95 mid, 63 mid, 62 short, 56 short, 55 `HH 6.3C`, 24 `HH 6.3C` and 23 none, and the form is identical at t = 0, 5, 14 and 16 s (GEN-R13 test).

  Every form with a velocity keeps the temperature: 12.3 m/s is right for 6.3 °C, not the textbook 21 m/s at 18.5 °C. `v` is measured live from the latest orthodromic spike's 0 mV crossings at 3 cm and 7 cm. The 3 cm crossing must come first, with the 7 cm crossing 1.5–6 ms later, so antidromic and colliding spikes are ignored. The readout shows `v=--.- m/s` before the first spike, then 12.3 after the first loop's single spike (12.4 in every later loop, in the collision's supernormal wake; see Refractory timing) and 10.9 → 9.8 along the train, and holds through the collision and threshold beats. It returns to `--.-` when the loop wraps, so the slowest train spike never carries into the next loop's single spike. Everything is ASCII, formatted in a stack buffer.
- **Perf**: 6.67 sub-steps × 200 compartments ≈ 1.3k compartment updates per frame (9 `exp`, ~40 flops and a Thomas sweep each). Rasterising covers ≈ 800 axon pixels, ≈ 150 soma pixels, ≤ 40 dendrite segments and ≤ 400 trace columns, then the blits. Measured ≈ 0.09 ms per `update(1/60)` + `render` at 200×50 (criterion, 2026-09-27: 89.8 μs); M12 (fastest of 5 runs per frame over 3600 frames): mean 0.087 ms, worst frame 0.096 ms. `dt = 0.25` costs 100 sub-steps, ≈ 1.5 ms. If the bench ever shows > 0.4 ms, tabulate the six rates at build time (0.1 mV steps on `[−100, +60]` mV, with linear interpolation).
- **Acceptance**: `AP-A1` with gates at steady state, `I_ion(V) = 0` has its root at `−65.0 ± 0.1 mV` (bisection on `[−80, −50]`), an unstimulated cable stays within ±0.05 mV of rest for 50 ms, and the α helpers are finite and continuous at −40 and −55 mV (`|α(V₀ ± 1e-7) − α(V₀)| < 1e-6`); `AP-A2` the propagated spike at 5 cm (strong pulse) peaks in `[+34, +42]` mV (reference +37.9), dips to a minimum in `[−77.0, −74.5]` mV (−75.9) and is back within 0.5 mV of rest 25 ms after the peak, and over one full 18 s protocol every gate stays in `[0, 1]` and every compartment's V in `[E_K − 0.5, E_Na]` (prototype −76.2 to +42.2 mV; ≈ 1 s in a debug build); `AP-A3` the conduction velocity between the 0 mV crossings at 3 cm and 7 cm is `12.3 m/s ± 3 %` at 6.3 °C with the scene discretisation (fine-grid reference 12.32, scene scheme 12.26); `AP-A4` with `φ = 3^{(18.5 − 6.3)/10}` the same solver gives `18.8 m/s ± 3 %`, HH 1952's computed velocity (scene scheme 18.55); `AP-A5` all-or-none: the scene's weak pulse (45 μA/cm²) from rest keeps V at 3 cm below −60 mV, while strong pulses at 1.5×, 3× and 6× the 90 μA/cm² threshold give peaks at 5 cm within 0.3 mV of one another; `AP-A6` refractoriness and collision: strong pulses 5 ms apart give exactly one 0 mV crossing at 7 cm, pulses 10 ms apart give two (the second slower), and simultaneous pulses at both ends give exactly one crossing at each end and at mid-axon, with max V < −60 mV 10 ms after the pulses meet (≈ 1 s in a debug build); `AP-A7` `60 × update(1/60)` and `4 × update(0.25)` leave bitwise-identical V and gate arrays (both runs execute the same 400 sub-steps), and each 18 s loop issues exactly the scripted pulses (0.2, 3.0, 5.5, 8.0 s; 13.0 s at both ends; 16.0 s weak) in order, repeating identically in the next loop; `AP-A8` over one protocol loop the live velocity readout prints exactly `12.3` after the first loop's single spike, then 10.9, 10.1 and 9.8 m/s after the three train spikes (each ± 0.3 m/s), and the collision and threshold beats leave it unchanged; the readout returns to `--.-` when the loop wraps, and the second and third loops' single spikes both print exactly `12.4`; `AP-A9` the drawn dendrites, for seeds 1–24 at 24×8, 40×12, 56×16, 80×24, 120×40 and 200×50: exactly three primaries are drawn, every drawn segment spans at least 2 cells, and no two drawn segments cross (a shared endpoint is not a crossing); `AP-A10` captions: over one 18 s protocol `caption()` follows the beats as above, and every caption is in `META.captions`; `AP-A11` palette: `tone_ink` is `Div`, positive above rest and negative below, `ink_tone(tone_ink(x))` returns x within 0.005 on [−0.48, −0.32] ∪ [0.05, 0.99], and `tone_ink(−0.3)` is `div(0)`; at 200×50, t = 1.2 s, the axoplasm holds only positive `Div` (> 20 pixels), negative `Div` (> 4) and blank pixels; the trace ink is `Div` with value > 0.5; and at 120×40, t = 5 s, the summed |div value| of the negative pixels is < 0.35 × that of the positive ones and no negative pixel reaches −0.2; `AP-A12` ions, at 200×50, seed 42: no ion at t = 0.15 s (rest); at t = 0.8, 1.2 and 1.6 s every ion lies outside the tube's pixel rows and over the trunk columns; Na⁺ moves toward the axon's axis and K⁺ away from it; each species stays at ≤ 24 streaks alive at once; the mean x of the Na⁺ streaks is ahead (toward the terminal) of the K⁺ streaks'; each ion's head cell is drawn in `Hot` or `Cold`, and both dot columns are used; the pool never saturates, and there are ≥ 10 of each species in total. The layout test pins the tube at 4 pixel rows at 120×40 and 6 at 200×50.

### aurora  (Earth & Sky, M10, M12)
- **Intent**: a subauroral night, looking north over a still lake. A quiet arc low in the north brightens suddenly and breaks into folding, rayed curtains that climb and fill the sky, green below and capped with crimson above, with a pink hem along the lower border at the substorm's peak. It then fades into softly pulsating patches. Between the curtains the sky stays black: light is drawn as coherent arcs, rayed curtains and patches, never as scattered speckle. The focal point is the lower border of the brightest curtain, which is the sharpest and brightest edge in the frame. A black spruce ridge and its rippling reflection anchor the bottom, and the sparse stars never compete. At 200×50 the shore is row 40. At scene start the arch's crest stands ≈ 18° up (≈ 12 rows above the shore) with both ends sagging into the horizon, and at the expansion peak the curtains fill rows ≈ 2–38. At 80×24 the shore is row 19 and the crest starts ≈ 6 rows above it.
- **Model**: *View*: every object lives in sky angles (azimuth α, east positive; elevation e) or in world km, projected equirectangularly onto a full-screen `Pixels` lattice (P = 2h pixel rows). The horizon row is `y_h = round(0.80·P)` with a lake below it, or `round(0.92·P)` with no lake when h < 14. The scale is `f = max(y_h/62°, W/π)` px/rad, with `x = W/2 + f·α` and `y = y_h − f·e`, so the frame is ≈ 155° × 62° at 200×50 and ≈ 130° × 62° at 80×24. *Curtains*: 1–3 thin vertical sheets aligned east–west. Sheet c lies at northward ground distance `D_c(s,t) = D0_c(t) + ξ_c(s,t)` of the along-arc coordinate s (east positive). It is sampled at M = 480 fixed points `s_j = 300 km·tan α_j`, with α_j uniform in ±85°. The displacement is `ξ = Σ_{k=1..4} w_kj·a_k sin(κ_k s + θ_k)` with λ = 900/240/70/18 km (bend/surge/fold/curl), `a_1 = 40 km` and `a_k = F(t)·σ_k/κ_k` for slope amplitudes σ = 0.5/1.3/0.8. The weight `w_kj = clamp(λ_k/(2Δs_j) − 1, 0, 1)` fades a mode out wherever it has fewer than 4 samples per wavelength, so flank folds never alias. Phases integrate as `θ_k −= κ_k c_k dt`, so mode k drifts at velocity c_k, with c = +0.3, −v_s(t), +1.2, −0.8 km/s (negative is westward; v_s is the westward-travelling surge). Speed changes therefore never make the pattern jump. Each sample lies at ground distance `d = hypot(s, D)` and azimuth `α = atan2(s, D)`. An altitude z maps to `e = atan((z − d²/2R⊕)/d)` (flat earth plus curvature drop, R⊕ = 6371 km). A straight east–west arc is therefore an arch that sags into both horizons. The sheet doubles back on screen wherever `ξ' > cot|α|`; for the fold band's slope of 1.3 that happens only beyond |α| ≈ 38°, which is how real folds read in the oblique sky. *Precipitation*: `Φ = A_c(t)·B·((1 − r) + r·ρ/ρ̄)`. B is a westward-drifting bulge `1 + b(t)·exp(−((α − α_b)/w_b)²)`. The ray field is `ρ = ⅓Σ_m max(0, cos(κ_m(s − v_m t) + φ_m))⁶` with λ_m = 23/37/61 km and `v_m = v_r(t)·{1, −0.6, 0.8}`. Its mean is ρ̄ = 5/32, so switching rays on moves precipitation into the rays without changing its average; the ray-averaged flux is `Φ̄ = A·B`. The mean energy E0(t) sets the lower border `z_b = 131 − 25·log10(E0/1 keV)` km, clamped to [85, 150]. This is a fit to the peak energy-deposition altitudes of Rees (1963). *Emission* at altitude z has three components (rays also raise the green column by up to 1.8×, since H grows inside a ray and the profile is not renormalised). Green OI 557.7 nm is `G = Φ·S·g(z − z_b)` with `g(u) = (1 − e^{−(u+2)/2})·e^{−u/H}` for u ≥ −2 km (else 0). It peaks ≈ 3 km above z_b, and `H = 22 km·(1 + 0.8 r ρ)` so that rays reach higher. Green is prompt (the O(¹S) lifetime is 0.74 s). Red OI 630.0 nm is `R = R_j·min(S, 2)·0.4·exp(1 − y − e^{−y})/(1 + e^{−(z−190)/12})` with y = (z − 240)/50 km: a Chapman production layer times collisional quenching of O(¹D) below ~200 km. O(¹D) atoms diffuse tens of km during their 110 s lifetime, so the red layer is thick: its slant gain is capped at 2, and the red is smoothed along the arc by a ≈ 4° Gaussian (three running-sum box passes per pixel row) before it is thresholded, so where folds double back the red ramps instead of standing as a wall. It is displayed at 0.27× (the photopic ratio V(630)/V(557.7)), so the diffuse fringe stays a fringe. The red source relaxes exactly as `R_j ← Φ + (R_j − Φ)·e^{−dt/τ_R}` with τ_R = 3 s. That is the 110 s radiative lifetime compressed only enough to keep the lag visible (on the 144× substorm clock it would be 0.76 s), so the red is smooth, rayless and lingers. The pink N₂ 1PG hem marks type-B aurora, where fast electrons reach heights at which O(¹S) is quenched. It is `P = Φ·S·clamp((E0 − 8)/12, 0, 1)` for z_b − 4 ≤ z ≤ z_b + 1 km, splatted for the brightest curtain only, and it always includes the layer's lowest lit pixel, so it never vanishes at small sizes. A pixel is pink only where P ≥ 0.25 G and the pink's own tone `1 − e^{−P/E_sat}` (after extinction) is ≥ 0.6; isolated hem pixels with no hem neighbour in the adjacent columns are demoted to green (keeping their level), so the hem reads as a run. The slant factor `S = min(1/|n̂·l̂|, 6)` uses the sheet normal n ∝ (−ξ', 1, 0) (east, north, up) and the line of sight l̂ to the sample's lower border. It is the path length through a thin sheet, so folds seen edge-on become bright caustic "rays". *Simplifications*: sheets and rays are vertical (field-aligned; valid for the northern sky seen from a subauroral site) and arcs are strictly east–west. The red source ignores E0 (real 630 nm weakens as precipitation hardens), and its 0.4 normalisation makes the red column ≈ 2.2× the green one, 10–45× the photometric I630/I557.7 of keV aurora, for a vivid fringe; the photopic weight 0.27 brings the displayed ratio to ≈ 0.6. *Raster*: consecutive samples are splatted as column spans into per-column layers {d, z_b, H, G/R/P amplitudes}, at most 6 per column (one crossing per curtain plus room for three more where folds double back; a seventh merges into the nearest d). A fold that doubles back gives a column several layers, and they add. Pixel row y samples each layer at `z = d·tan e_y + d²/2R⊕`. This uses a per-resize tan e row table, a 0.5-km green LUT in u with H quantised to 16 steps, and a 1-km red LUT in z. The sum is multiplied by the extinction `exp(−0.12(1/sin max(e, 2°) − 1))` and tone-mapped as `1 − e^{−E/E_sat}` (E_sat = 0.9) through a LUT. Emission fades in under the top edge over 20 % of the pixel rows (at least 3 px) by smoothstep, so a crown overhead thins into dark sky and the label stays in clear air. *Thresholds, not dither*: the theme floors lit roles at 30 % brightness, so faint light cannot be shown by level; it is left dark rather than scattered as a dither. The pulsating-patch green Q (see *Recovery*) adds to the tone but is kept apart from the curtains' green G. Where the curtains' own green tone is < 0.03 the red is dropped (below notice), so patch light never opens the crown's gate. A pixel whose red share R/(G+Q+R) is ≤ ½ is green if its tone is ≥ 0.14, at level `12 + 243·(tone − 0.14)/0.86`. A red-dominant pixel is the crown if its tone is ≥ 0.2 *and* the curtains' green tone there is ≥ 0.03, at level `12 + 98·(tone − 0.2)/0.8` in `Accent`, so every lit level starts from the core floor `ink::MIN_VISIBLE_LEVEL` = 12 (CORE-R66): the crown caps each ray and fold with crimson and so follows the curtains' envelope; diffuse red far above the green would be cut flat by the top fade (a lid) and stand as a slab over the sky. A hem pixel is `Accent` at `190 + 65·(tone − 0.14)/0.86`. Every lit run thus starts from the theme's dimmest shade of its role, each column lights contiguous runs where its emission profile crosses the threshold, and rays that reach higher end higher, so a curtain's top edge is combed into vertical streaks. Finally specks are dropped: a lit sky pixel with no lit neighbour (of 8), and a pair whose two pixels neighbour only each other, so a fading arc end or a distant arc on the horizon never crumbles into grit. *Setting*: a far-shore ridge (3-octave value noise in α, 0.8–4° high) is topped by seeded spruce spires (1.5–4° apart, 1.2–3° tall, half-width 0.28× height); above f = 60 px/rad a second, nearer forest of fewer, taller spires (2.5–6° apart, 2.5–4° tall, half-width 0.35×) replaces them so each shows triangular flanks. Below it the far spires are stretched upward (widths unchanged, so they get spikier) until a 3° spire (the height cap) stands 3 px: at 80×24 a 3° spruce is under 2 px and the comb would read as a ruled line. Silhouette pixels are never lit. A `Seq` skyglow lights every sky pixel below the glow height `max(f·8°, tallest silhouette + 1.5 px, 3 px)`, from 0.2 at the horizon falling quadratically to 0.02 at its top, solid throughout: the ramp's black end is a dark navy, so the glow stops just above it rather than being carried into it, and the ridge and spires cut a crisp black edge into it. The lake mirrors the sky about y_h at 0.45× level, with their roles: the skyglow, and each column's lit sky runs whole wherever a run's brightest pixel has level ≥ 40 (a fainter run's copy could not be dimmer than the theme's floor, while cutting the dim ends off brighter runs would leave shards), emission copies never below level 12; the ridge and spires mirror as a black band cut out of the reflected glow. Its ripple is `δx = round(0.04 d·sin(0.9d − 1.7t + 0.13x))`, where d is the number of pixel rows below the shore, so the wavefronts travel toward the viewer while the water stays still. Whole rows drop out where `sin(0.9d − 1.7t) > 0.9`, so mirrored rays read as vertical streaks cut by horizontal ripple gaps. Stars come from a seeded 1200-star catalogue in (α, e) with magnitudes. They are shown brightest-first until 2 % of sky cells hold one, so the limiting magnitude adapts to resolution. They twinkle more toward the horizon and are drawn only in cells whose two pixels are empty.
- **Motion**: a `Timeline` runs growth 12 s → onset 3 s → expansion 15 s → recovery 20 s. The 50 s loop compresses a ~2 h substorm ≈ 144× (Akasofu 1964), while folds and rays move at real km/s speeds. Keyframes are smoothstep-interpolated, except that every segment starting at the onset uses `ease::out_quad` (the sudden breakup), and every one returns to its growth-start value by the end of recovery. The scene clock starts at t = 8 s, so breakup lands 4 s into a default 15 s visit. *Growth*: two faint homogeneous arcs drift equatorward, toward the viewer (D0₁ 520 → 260 km, A₁ 0.35 → 0.4, A₂ 0.3, E0 2 → 4 keV, F 0.15, r 0.04, v_r 1 km/s), each a clear band above its threshold rather than a broken line hovering at it. *Onset*: the equatorward arc brightens 2.5× within 1.5 s (`ease::out_quad`). E0 rises to 20 keV, so the lower border drops ≈ 18 km and the pink hem appears. Rays switch on (r → 0.8, v_r → 12 km/s). *Expansion*: the bulge swells both ways. Arc 1 moves toward the zenith (D0₁ → 170 km, base ≈ 31°) while arcs 2–3 light up poleward, arc 2 at D0₁ + 150 km closing to + 110 km within 9 s, arc 3 at D0₁ + 330 km. F rises to 1.0, so folds double back in the oblique sky. The bulge surges west (α_b +10° → −40°, v_s → 6 km/s), and E0 settles to 10 keV. *Recovery*: the arcs dim and retreat poleward (D0 → 520 km; E0, F, r, v_r back to their growth values), and the red fringe outlives the green by τ_R. Six pulsating patches fade in and out: seeded one per 15° stratum across α ±45° (±3° jitter, so neighbours drifting together keep apart instead of fusing into one blob) and at e 25–45° (mid-sky, clear of the top fade), soft Gaussian 6–12° × 3–6° ellipses cut at 1.6 half-extents, with half-extents floored at min(5 px, 0.07 W) × min(3 px, P/12) (at 80×24 a 6° patch is only 4 columns, a smudge; the floor is 5 × 3 px there and at 200×50 and 2.8 × 2 px at 40×12, so a small frame's patches never swell into clouds half the sky wide), period 4–12 s, on-fraction 0.4 with 0.4 s edges, drifting east 0.5°/s, green at 0.45× the arc peak, so a patch at full pulse stays dimmer than the recovery arc. Pulsating aurora has no red cap: patch light is kept out of the crown's gate (*Thresholds*), so a patch in the lingering red dome raises no crimson hood. Activity a(t) runs 0.02 → 0.15 (end of growth) → 0.55 (end of onset) → 1.0 (t = 22 s) → 0.75 (end of expansion) → 0.02 and sets `AL = −(30 + 800a)` nT. In any 1 s window the lake ripple alone changes pixel occupancy (GEN-R9 compares glyphs), and rays and folds add more.
- **Resize**: there is no simulation lattice, so ADR-0012 bucketing does not apply. All state lives in world or sky coordinates and survives any resize untouched: fold and ray phases, red sources, clock, star catalogue, trees and patches. Resize rebuilds only the view: f, y_h, the tan e and extinction row tables, the per-column silhouette, the visible-star list, the layer scratch and `Pixels`. A resize re-frames the same sky. At 1×1 the view is sky-only and nothing panics.
- **ASCII**: every cell takes the brighter emission pixel of its pair (a hem pixel wins, so the hem stays a line); skyglow returns `None`. Sky: green is `|` (≥ 200), `!` (≥ 130), `:` (≥ 70), else `,` (so a quiet arc never reads as the dim stars `.`); the crown is `"` (≥ 60), else `'`; the hem is `=`. Lake cells are `~` (≥ 60), else `-`. After the blit, in ASCII mode only, the ridge top is drawn as one continuous `_` line (the cell holding each column's top ridge pixel), with a spire tip `^` in the cell just above it wherever a spire rises above the line (one comb however tall the stretched spires), in still-blank cells, `Dim` 70. Stars are `.`, with `*` for the brightest 10 %, and also need a still-blank cell. At 80×24 this reads as `,:!|` columns capped with `'"`, arching over a `^^_` shore and `~` water.
- **Perf** at 200×50: update ≈ 0.35 ms. Sampling costs ≈ 0.09 ms (3 × 480 samples × 4 fold sines, 3 ray cos⁶ and a red relaxation, with one exp per call), independent of terminal size; the rest is the rasterisation, which runs in `update`: projection and spans ≈ 0.08 ms; sky shading ≈ 16k px × ≤ 2 live layers of LUT reads ≈ 0.15 ms, skipping rows above the emission top; lake via per-row ripple tables ≈ 0.02 ms; silhouette. Render (blit, stars, the ASCII ridge and the label) ≈ 0.05 ms. Total ≈ 0.45 ms (measured 0.47 ms in criterion at load ≈ 14, 2026-09-27, after the patch and whole-run lake rework), with no allocation after resize. Thresholding and the speck pass cost ≈ 0.02 ms, the lake's per-column run-peak scan ≈ 0.01 ms; nothing is precomputed per process.
- **Inks** *(changed)*:
  - Green `Ramp(Aurora)` 12–255: deep teal where faint (**false colour**: the faint green mixed with blue
    N₂⁺ 427.8 nm, as photographs show it), through emerald to lime-white in the rays.
  - Red crown `Ramp(HAlpha)` **56**–155: crimson. The ramp's darkest maroon,
    next to the green, read as brown mud; the crown now starts at a visible crimson.
  - `Ramp(HAlpha)` is used here only as a crimson palette, for the O I 630.0 nm crown and the N₂ 1PG pink
    hem; no hydrogen (proton aurora) emission is modelled.
  - Pink hem `Ramp(HAlpha)` **196–212**: rose pink, deeper than before (above ≈ 225 the ramp pales to
    near-white). It now marks every curtain whose pink tone reaches **0.5** (was 0.6), so it runs longer
    along the lower borders.
  - Static check `HEM_LEVEL > RED_LEVEL_MAX + 40` holds (196 > 195). Skyglow `Seq` as before.
- **Lake** *(changed)*: each lake pixel is the mirrored sky pixel's 3 × 2 box sum (its row and the one
  above, same light only) over 6, so a run's ends soften, at **0.25** of its level (was 0.45), times a
  row gain `(1 − 0.6·depth)·(1 − 0.45·max(0, sin φ)²)`: it fades toward the lake's far edge, and ripple
  crests dim a row instead of dropping it (blank crest rows read as hard gaps). Never under
  the core floor for emission; ripple shifts as before. The lake ends two cell rows above the bottom,
  so the title's row and the row above it stay dark (GEN-R13).
- **Captions** *(new)*: `caption()` is `CAPTIONS[beat.index]`: `growth phase`, `substorm onset`,
  `substorm expansion`, `pulsating recovery`; `meta.captions` lists them.
- **Label** *(changed)*: ` AL -620 nT ` only; dropped when w < 24 or h < 10.
- **Rejected**:
  - Auroral corona: it sits near the magnetic zenith (≈ 77° up), outside the 62° north-facing frame.
  - Crown at levels 180–220 (review suggestion): it would sit within 20 levels of the hem and the two
    would merge; the crown is lifted to 155 instead, keeping the 40-level separation.
  - Star occlusion: the aurora is optically thin; stars stay drawn only in empty cells, as before.
- **Acceptance**: `AUR-A1` `lower_border_km(e0)` is strictly decreasing on [1, 60] keV, stays within [85, 150] km for any input, and gives 113.5, 106.0, 98.5 and 88.5 km (±0.1) at 5, 10, 20 and 50 keV;
