Every visible halo star is a live gravitating body or one of the three faint companions riding with it: the swarm breathes, the unresolved glow shifts with the bodies as they cross the core, and the camera's slow turn gives the halo depth through parallax. It is calm, dense and three-dimensional, the stellar counterpart to the flat `starfield` and the disk-shaped `galaxy_collision`.
- **Model**:
  - **Units and softening**: direct N-body in Hénon units (`G = M = 1`, virial radius 1, `E ≈ −1/4`), equal masses `m = 1/N`, Plummer softening `Φ_ij = −G m²/√(r² + ε²)` with `ε = 0.05` (0.085 of the Plummer scale radius `a = 3π/16`).
  - **Forces and integration**: an O(N²/2) Newton-3 symmetric pair loop, so `Σ m a = 0` to rounding. `ode::verlet_integrate` (KDK leapfrog, CORE-R44) runs with `h_max = 1/128` N-body units at a rate of `0.4` N-body units per simulated second: one force evaluation per frame at `dt = 1/60`, 13 at `dt = 0.25`. The crossing time `t_cr = 2√2` is 7.1 s.
  - **ICs**: Plummer sphere `ρ ∝ (1 + r²/a²)^{−5/2}`, sampled per Aarseth, Hénon & Wielen (1974) in `G = M = a = 1`:
    - Radius `r = (X₁^{−2/3} − 1)^{−1/2}` with mass cut `X₁ < 0.999` (so `r ≤ 38.7 a`).
    - Isotropic directions (`z = 1 − 2u`, `φ = 2πu`).
    - Speed `v = q·√2(1 + r²)^{−1/4}`, with `q` from `g(q) = q²(1 − q²)^{7/2}` by rejection under the bound 0.1 (the maximum is 0.0923).
    - COM position and velocity subtracted.
    - Positions scaled so the unsoftened `W = −1/2` (then `a ≈ 3π/16` and `r_h ≈ 0.769`).
    - The density centre moved to the origin: 4 passes that each subtract the mean position of the bodies inside the half-mass radius. The heavy tail (`r_rms ≈ 3.0 a` with the 0.999 cut) puts the COM ≈ `r_rms/√N` off the body core (0.08 units at N = 500, 0.22 at N = 64). The view and the glow are centred on the origin, so without this the core would sit off the glow and swing around it as the camera turns. It is a translation, so `W` and the dynamics are unchanged, and the momentum stays zero, so the cluster does not drift.
    - Velocities scaled so `2K = |W_ε|` (virial equilibrium in the softened potential), giving `E ≈ −0.248`.
  - **N**: the largest bucket in `{64, 96, 128, 192, 256, 384, 500}` that is `≤ max(area/5, 64)`. 80×24 gives 384, and 200×50 and anything larger gives 500 (the ADR-0012 cap).
  - **Unresolved light**: each body stands for ~10³ real stars, so 500 bodies cannot fill a braille core; their light is smoothed into a glow (see Inks) whose Plummer part uses `a_g = r_h/1.3048`, where `r_h` is the body system's 3-D half-mass radius about the origin, smoothed with `τ = 4 s` and reset at every reseed, so the glow follows the real core contraction.
  - **Luminosity tags** (visual only): the first `g_n = clamp(N/50, 3, 10)` bodies (sample order is random) are red giants, and every other body gets a colour class from a salt hash (see Inks). The dynamics stay equal-mass, so there is no mass segregation.
- **Motion**:
  - Bodies orbit in the self-consistent potential: the core crosses in about 2 s and halo stars drift slowly.
  - `geom::Camera` yaws at `2π/90 s`, with pitch `0.30 + 0.15·sin(2πt/67 s)` and perspective `distance = 10` units, so near-side stars slide against far-side ones. The camera alone moves an `r = 1.5` star ≈ 4 dots/s at 200×50.
  - A looping `Timeline`: `evolve` 120 s (48 N-body units ≈ 17 t_cr). Spitzer's `t_rh ≈ 0.138 N r_h^{3/2}/ln(0.4N)` gives 8.8 units (22 s) at N = 500; softening at ε = 0.05 cuts ln Λ to ≈ ln(r_h/ε) ≈ 2.7, so a cycle spans ≈ 3–5.5 t_rh. Then a 1.5 s `fade`, a fresh Plummer sample from `ctx.rng`, and a 1.5 s `rise`: 123 s per loop.
  - Within a cycle the core contracts slightly and the odd star escapes. Core collapse needs ≈ 15 t_rh and is never reached. The reseed keeps the halo populated and bounds the accumulated energy error.
- **Resize** (ADR-0012 analogue, with N as the cap):
  - Within the same N bucket a resize only changes the view: `scale = draw::fit_radius(size, 0.92)/2.2` rows per unit, so `R = 2.2` units (≈ 93 % of the projected mass) fills the frame, and `Dots` is resized. Pos/vel are untouched.
  - On a bucket change, shrinking keeps the first N′ bodies (an unbiased subsample). Growing appends fresh AHW samples, scaled by the same length and velocity factors as the build sample. Then `m = 1/N′`, the momentum is re-zeroed (the COM position is not re-centred: that would break GC-A6's bit-identity and jump the view; the subsample's small COM offset, ≈ `r_rms/√N′`, persists until the next reseed), and velocities are re-virialised to `2K = |W_ε|` (one O(N²) pass). The cluster never visibly resets while you drag the window.
- **Inks**:
  - **Unresolved light**: each body (weighted by its near-eye fade `f_eye`) is deposited bilinearly on a fixed 64 × 64 lattice over the model plane `[−2, 2]²` at the origin's depth (capped and size-independent, ADR-0012) and blurred by three 5-wide box passes (σ ≈ 0.15 units, a quarter of the Plummer scale radius), in units of the Plummer central surface density `1/(π a_g²)`. The glow is `g = w·g_bodies + (1 − w)·(1 + R²/a_g²)⁻²` with `w = 0.5/(1 + R²/a_g²)`, `R` measured from the light's centre (its light²-weighted centroid): the bodies supply half the core's light, so it shifts and breathes at each crossing, and the smooth Plummer profile supplies the round outskirts, where 500 bodies are too few to trace a smooth edge. A soft knee `g²/(g + 0.04)` (no pedestal cut) keeps lone halo bodies from smudging the sky; level `Tone::asinh(0.3, 0.9)` of `(knee·b)^1.25`, toned once per lattice cell per frame and interpolated, in `Ramp(Starlight)` pixels; levels below 16 dissolve (CORE-R89).
  - **Companions**: each body carries 3 faint dots (`Star` 40) at fixed hashed 3-D offsets within 0.2 model units, drawn where the glow is below level 150: the halo resolves into stars that orbit, turn (parallax) and fade with their body.
  - **Stars** (`Dots::blit_over` the glow; colour class from a salt hash, a visual tag: the dynamics stay equal-mass): 76 % turn-off and subgiant stars as one `Star` 140 dot; 17 % red-giant branch (4300–4900 K) and 7 % blue horizontal branch (9000–12 000 K, CORE-R88) as 2 × 2-dot `Blackbody` stars. The first `g_n` bodies are red giants: a `✦` in `Blackbody` 3900 K (over the glow's `cell_bg`) where the glow is below 0.25 of the centre's, an ordinary 3900 K star inside. A body is lost in the core's glare where `g ≥ 0.25·(1 + 1.5u)` (`u` its own hash), so the core has no hard rim of stars; a fade thins the stars in a fixed hashed order (a body is drawn iff its hash is below `b·f_eye(z)`).
  - `f_eye = 1 − smoothstep((z − 2.5)/(10/3 − 2.5))` fades near-eye stars out between depth `z = 2.5` and `z = 10/3`: the eye at distance 10 sits inside the far halo (`r ≤ 38.7 a ≈ 23` units), so a near-side halo star would be magnified without bound and streak across the screen like a meteor.
- **ASCII**: glow pixel pairs as `#`/`+`/`:`/`.` by the `stronger` pixel's level (none below 40), stars via the braille fallback, giants as `*`.
- **Readout** (GEN-R13): right-aligned on the title row at `READOUT_LEVEL`, `N=500 3 crossings` — the body count and the whole crossing times (`t_cr = 7.1 s`, `1 crossing` in the singular) since the last reseed, so it changes only every 7.1 s; before the first crossing the clause is left out. The longest form that fits `title::readout_room` is used (`N=500`, else nothing), written into a stack `StackText` without allocating.
- **Stylisation**: each body stands for ~10³ stars: its light is smoothed on a 0.15-unit scale and it carries three faint companion dots; the unresolved glow is half the bodies' light and half a smooth Plummer profile, so its outline stays round with only 500 bodies; the glow has no colour gradient (a globular's integrated B − V ≈ 0.7 is nearly uniform), and the Starlight ramp is neutral grey while faint.
- **Perf**: at 200×50 with N = 500 a frame costs ≈ 0.5 ms.
  - Force: 124,750 pairs × (1 sqrt + 1 div + ~20 flops) ≈ 0.25 ms scalar.
  - Projection: 500 × 4 `Camera::project` (the body and its companions).
  - Glow: 500 bilinear deposits, 3 × 2 box passes over 64², 4096 tone evaluations and a bilinear sample per pixel inside the lattice's reach.
  - `dt = 0.25` gives 13 substeps (≈ 13× the force cost, a few ms). Only the GEN-R4 contract reaches it: the runtime feeds fixed 1/60 steps (ADR-0003), and GEN-R8 covers `update(1/60)`.
  - Pos/vel/acc `Vec<f64>`s have capacity 1500 from `build`, the cluster's half-mass scratch has capacity 500, and the lattice and its blur scratch are fixed at 64². The reseed at 121.5 s refills these in place (GC-A6), so update and render do not allocate.
  - Debug note: the contract suite's 600-step 200×50 run is ≈ 75 M pair evaluations, ≈ 1.5–2.5 s unoptimised (flagged, like `molecular_dynamics`).
- **Acceptance**: `GC-A1` the AHW sampler (`G = M = a = 1`, cut 0.999): a seeded 4000-star sample has KS distance < 0.03 from the truncated Plummer mass profile `M(r) = r³/(1 + r²)^{3/2}/0.999`, median radius within 4 % of `1.3048 a`, the speed fractions `q = v/v_esc(r)` (`v_esc = √2(1 + r²)^{−1/4}`) all in `[0, 1)` with KS distance < 0.03 from the CDF of `g(q) ∝ q²(1 − q²)^{7/2}` (the regularised incomplete beta `I_{q²}(3/2, 9/2)`, integrated numerically), while exponents 5/2 and 9/2 give > 0.03, and the raw N = 1500 virial ratio `2K/|W|` in `[0.9, 1.1]`; `GC-A2` after `plummer` for seeds 1–6 and N = 64, 256, 500, the density centre (mean position inside the half-mass radius) lies within 0.03 units of the origin and `|Σ m v| < 1e-14`; for N = 256 the unsoftened `W = −1/2` within 1e-12, `2K = |W_ε|` within 1e-12, and `E = −1/4` within 0.01; `GC-A3` for N = 64 seeded, the Newton-3 pair loop equals a naive per-star O(N²) sum within 1e-12 relative, `|Σ m a| < 1e-14`, each acceleration equals `−∇Φ_ε/m` by central differences within 1e-6 relative, and two coincident stars give a finite, zero mutual force; `GC-A4` for N = 128 seeded, `ε = 0.05`, `h = 1/128`, 10 N-body units (1280 steps, ≈ 10 M pair evaluations): `|Σ m v| < 1e-14` throughout and max `|ΔE/E₀| < 5e-4` (measured ≤ 4.6e-5); `GC-A5` in the same run, `r_h(t)/r_h(0) ∈ [0.7, 1.3]` at every 0.5-unit sample with time mean in `[0.9, 1.1]`, and the time-mean `2K/|W_ε|` is in `[0.9, 1.1]`; `GC-A6` N follows the bucket rule (80×24 ⇒ 384, 200×50 ⇒ 500, 1×1 and 0×0 ⇒ 64, never > 500), a resize within a bucket leaves pos/vel bit-identical, and a bucket change keeps the first `min(N, N′)` positions bit-identical while restoring `|Σ m v| < 1e-14` and `2K = |W_ε|` within 1e-12, and the reseed at 200×50 draws a fresh N = 500 sample into pos/vel/acc/scratch without changing their capacities; `GC-A7` at 200×50 (seed 42, t = 0, Unicode): every glow pixel is `Ramp(Starlight)` (no hue gradient anywhere), > 500 are lit, and every lit pixel next to the empty sky is below level 90 (no rim); the centre pixel is ≥ 180 and the pixel at `R = a_g` is dimmer; every star cell is `Star` or `Blackbody` 3800–12 500 K (> 150 star cells, > 20 coloured); at most `g_n` giants (`g_n` = 10, 7, 3 for N = 500, 384, 64), none where the glow is ≥ 0.5 of the centre's; `f_eye` is 1 up to `z = 2.5`, strictly decreasing, and 0 from `z = 10/3`; moving every body by +0.4 model units moves the glow's level-weighted centroid by > 3 pixels (the glow is the bodies' light). At 120×40, t = 1, 10 and 30 s, the floor contour's radius from that centroid varies ≤ 1.3× over 32 rays, and along every ray the light falls from 4× the floor to nothing over ≥ 3 px. At 120×40, t = 10 s, > 40 cells hold grain alone, and every one lies within a companion's reach of a projected body; `GC-A8` over 4000 bodies the class fractions are within 3 % of 76/17/7 %, every class temperature is in its range, and the hash is a pure function of `(salt, k, stream)`; `GC-A9` the readout reads `N=384 4 crossings` at age 4.5 `t_cr` (80×24), `N=384 1 crossing` at 1.99 and `N=384` before the first crossing; at 40×12 the full form, at 30×10 `N=384`, at 8×24 nothing, never longer than `readout_room`; at 80×24 after 8 s row 23 ends with ` N=384 1 crossing ` at `READOUT_LEVEL`.

### ising_model  (States of Matter, M10, M12)
- **Intent**: the ferromagnetic phase transition as a living mosaic. A quench turns static into two colours of domain that swell, round off and straighten. Reheating melts them into a dim boil, and a slow cool through the critical point leaves clusters of every size breathing (critical opalescence). Order lights up: aligned regions glow and disorder sinks toward the background, so the eye goes to the shrinking bubbles and the straightening walls: domains read as lit pillows with dark seams. A second act (M12) scrambles the magnet into an exact 50/50 mixture and runs spin-exchange (Kawasaki) dynamics at 0.6 T_c; swaps conserve M, so the mixture un-mixes into a labyrinth of bevelled ridges that slowly ripens, and the readout says `M fixed` instead of measuring |m|. A thermometer on the label chip marks T_c.
- **Model**: 2-D Ising `H = −J Σ⟨ij⟩ sᵢsⱼ`, `sᵢ = ±1`, `J = k_B = 1`, zero field, periodic (torus) boundaries. The lattice is capped: `L = grid::lattice_for(Size(w, 2h), 16 384, 1.25)` in square pixel units, so 200×50 → 178×91 (≈ 1.1 px per spin) and 80×24 → 80×48 (below the cap the lattice is the pixel grid itself). Kinetics are single-spin Glauber heat-bath (Glauber 1963), random-sequential, with one `u64` per attempt from a scene-local SplitMix64 seeded once from `ctx.rng` at the first resize (GEN-R7). Bits 32–47 and 48–63 pick `x = (r₁₆·Lw)>>16` and `y = (r₁₆·Lh)>>16` (≤ 0.3 % non-uniform, harmless because every heat-bath move leaves the Boltzmann distribution invariant). The local field is `h = Σ_nn sⱼ ∈ {−4,−2,0,2,4}`; set `sᵢ = +1` iff the low 32 bits are `< thr[h]`, where `thr = round(p₊·2³²)` as u64 (so `p = 0, 1` are exact) and `p₊(h) = 1/(1 + e^{−2h/T})`. The 5-entry table is rebuilt once per update with T clamped to `[0.05, 100]` (NaN ⇒ T_c). This is the same model-A equilibrium, universality and coarsening class as Metropolis; it replaces checkerboard Metropolis because deterministic sublattice sweeps flip every ΔE = 0 spin at high T (a period-2 strobe) and because random-sequential updates give smooth fractional sweeps per frame at any lattice size. The rate is `max(60·N/16 384, 1)` Monte Carlo sweeps (MCS) per simulated second (N = lattice sites; one MCS per 1/60 s step at the cap), realised as `⌊acc⌋` attempts from an accumulator gaining `rate·N·dt`. Domain size relative to the lattice scales as `√(MCS/N)`, so coarsening looks the same at every terminal size. Temperature is in units of J/k_B with `T_c = 2/ln(1+√2) = 2.269 185 314` (Onsager 1944). References: `m₀(T) = (1 − sinh(2/T)⁻⁴)^{1/8}` below T_c and 0 above (Yang 1952); exact energy per spin `e(T) = −coth 2β·[1 + (2/π)·k′·K(k)]` with `k′ = 2 tanh² 2β − 1` (signed complementary modulus, computed directly to avoid cancellation), `K = π/(2·AGM(1, |k′|))`, and the product `k′·K` taken as its limit 0 when `k′ = 0`, so `e(T_c) = −√2`. T/T_c follows a looping `Timeline` of 90 s: `coarsen` 20 s at 0.60 → `reheat` 8 s, smoothstep 0.60→1.25 → `hot` 5 s at 1.25 (ξ ≈ 2–3, a boil rather than a per-frame fizz) → `cool` 15 s, `1 + 0.25(1−p)²` (eases into T_c so most of the phase sits near criticality) → `critical` 8 s at 1.00 → `quench` 2 s, smoothstep 1.00→0.60 → `spinodal` 32 s at 0.60 (the Kawasaki act, below); it is continuous at every boundary. The first resize seeds a T = ∞ state (i.i.d. ±1), so t = 0 is the textbook quench. Magnetisation `M = Σs` is tracked incrementally and recomputed on resize.
- **Motion**: within 1 s (≈ 60 MCS at the cap) static condenses into domains 10–20 spins across; coarsening follows `ℓ ∝ t^{1/2}` (Allen & Cahn 1979; Bray 1994). Over `coarsen`, about 1200 MCS grow domains to lattice scale: bubbles shrink and vanish, walls straighten, and the phase often ends in a few domains or one stripe (≈ 1/3 of torus quenches end in stripes). In `reheat`, walls roughen and thermal clusters eat the domains as T crosses T_c. `hot` is a dim boil of small clusters. In `cool`, clusters grow fractal and slow down (critical slowing, z ≈ 2.17; Nightingale & Blöte 1996). `critical` breathes at all scales up to `t^{1/z}`. `quench` leads into the Kawasaki act, and the re-quench after it into `coarsen`, so every pass of `coarsen` starts from noise.
- **Resize**: if `lattice_for` returns the same lattice, only the display maps are rebuilt (cell-centre nearest-index tables px→lattice, `Vec<u16>`, allocated in `resize`) and the spins are untouched. A different lattice (bucket change, or any change below the cap) triggers `grid::resample_nearest` into a scratch grid and a swap (ADR-0012); spins stay exactly ±1 and M is recomputed. Above the cap the lattice-to-pixel ratio is not an integer (200×50: 178×91 → 200×100 px), so about every 8th pixel column and 11th pixel row repeats a lattice line; cell-centre sampling spreads the repeats evenly, and the resulting ±1 px wall jitter is accepted (an integer ratio would halve the lattice). `resize` never draws from `ctx.rng` after the first seeding. T comes from scene time, so a resize never desyncs the schedule.
- **Spinodal act (M12)**: after the Glauber act, `PHASES` gains `("spinodal", 32 s)` at index 6, so the loop is 90 s; T/T_c is 0.60 throughout, continuous with the quench before it and the `coarsen` after it. *Scramble*: on entering `spinodal` the lattice is set to exactly ⌊N/2⌋ down spins, Fisher–Yates shuffled with the scene SplitMix64 (no extra draws from `ctx.rng`). *Kawasaki attempt* (Kawasaki 1966): each attempt draws one u64; bits 32–47 and 48–63 pick the site as for Glauber, bits 30–31 one of its four neighbours. An antiparallel pair swaps when the low 30 bits are below `thr[(ΔE + 12)/4]`, with `ΔE = 2 s_i (h_i′ − h_j′)` and fields that exclude the partner's own spin. `thr` is the heat-bath rule `p = 1/(1 + e^{ΔE/T})` stored as `round(p·2³⁰)`, the same function as the Glauber `p₊` with `h = −ΔE/2`, so the same clamps apply. Swaps conserve M, so the mixture cannot order into one colour: it un-mixes into a labyrinth that slowly ripens (`ℓ ∝ t^{1/3}`, spinodal decomposition). *Rate*: swaps run `swap_speedup(local)` times the Glauber sweep rate, ramping linearly from 1× at the start of the act to 2× at 4 s: conserved coarsening is slower than non-conserved, while a fresh mixture, where nearly every attempt is a costly antiparallel pair, de-mixes within a few sweeps anyway (3× cost frame budget for little visible gain). *Re-quench*: on leaving `spinodal` the spins are refilled in place with independent ±1 values from the scene SplitMix64, so every pass of the Glauber act starts from T = ∞, not from the labyrinth. Lattice cap, torus, Glauber kinetics and resize rules are unchanged.
- **Stylisation (stated)**: *false colour* (`Ramp(Aurora)` up, `Ramp(HAlpha)` down). The depth shading is a visual reading of domain size, not a physical field. The torus seam is not wrapped in the depth transform, so domains crossing it read slightly brighter there. The spinodal act is the binary-alloy (lattice-gas) reading of the Ising model: a real magnet's magnetisation is not conserved, and the 50/50 scramble and the re-quench after it are staged resets, not physical processes.
- **Inks** (M12, replaces the M10 `Div` table): up spins are `ramp(Aurora, 0.92·v)` and down spins `ramp(HAlpha, 0.5·v)`; down is scaled lower so the phases stay apart in Mono, where every ramp is one grey (up interiors ≥ 64 levels brighter). *Classes* (`Lattice::classify`): a *speck* is a site in a same-sign cluster of fewer than 6 spins (a flood fill that stops at 6 sites or at a site already known to be in a large cluster, so O(1) per site); a neighbour counts as aligned if it has the same spin or is a speck, so thermal specks carve no dark hollow into a domain. A speck is class 4 (`v = 0.45`, a domain's rim): a quiet fleck of its own colour, dim mint in a crimson domain and dark crimson in mint (the ramps' low ends are near black, so a lower class reads as a dead pixel). A site with a non-speck antiparallel neighbour is a wall, with class = its effective aligned count (1–3); every other site is interior. *Values*: wall classes take `v = ALIGNED[class]`, `ALIGNED = [0.06, 0.12, 0.22, 0.38, 0.45]`; interior sites take `v = 0.45 + (0.78 − 0.45)·smoothstep(min(d, p)/p)`, where d is the chamfer 2–3 distance to the nearest wall site (Borgefors 1986; 2 units per site; not wrapped), p = 20 in the Glauber act and 4 (two sites) in the Kawasaki act, whose labyrinth is only a few sites wide: there the shading saturates into a bevel instead of a flat two-tone mottle. *Kawasaki floor*: in the Kawasaki act every `v` is lifted linearly from `[0, 0.78]` onto `[0.6, 0.78]`; the labyrinth is mostly wall, and without the floor both phases sink to the same mid grey in Mono, with it up is ≥ 64 levels above down at every class. So walls are dark seams, big ordered domains glow most, and the hot phase sinks toward the background. Two ink tables (p = 20 with no floor, p = 4 with the 0.6 floor) of `2·(4 + 21)` inks are built once per scene. The chamfer runs as row passes: each row is relaxed against the finished row before it, then swept along itself (and the same backward), the same order as the per-pixel 3×3 mask, giving the exact 2–3 distance.
- **ASCII**: the `Pixels::blit` closure decodes each pixel's signed value (+v for Aurora, −v for HAlpha), takes the cell mean m and applies `draw::ramp((m/0.45 + 1)/2, [. , : + #])` with the ink of the dominant half.
- **Label**: top-left (col 1, row 0) chip `T/Tc 0.93  |m| 0.71` in `Text` 200; during `spinodal` it is `T/Tc 0.60  M fixed` (swaps pin M, so a rule is shown rather than a result). T/T_c comes from the schedule and `|m| = |M|/N` is measured live; after a quench it visibly lags the equilibrium m₀ (for example 0.71 vs 0.86 at 0.93). `put` resets bg, so the label sits on a small dark chip. It is written from a stack buffer; the `  |m| …` part is dropped when w < 24, and the whole label when w < 12 or h < 4. From `w ≥ 48` a thermometer follows: two blanks, then 13 cells covering T/T_c ∈ [0.5, 1.5], `━` (ASCII `=`) filled up to T/T_c, `─` (`-`, `Text` 70) beyond, and `┃` (`|`) at T_c in the middle, then one blank. The fill is `round((T/T_c − 0.5)·12)` cells, so T_c fills exactly half.
- **Captions** (GEN-R12): `meta.captions = ["spins align", "spins swap"]`; `caption()` is `spins swap` during `spinodal`. `meta.title` stays `Magnetic domains · spins align`.
- **Perf** (200×50, step + render, 120 s, the four M12 matter scenes in one release run, load ≈ 30 on 14 cores): mean 0.77 ms, heaviest 1-s window median 1.27 ms (criterion frame mean 0.91 ms at load 22–40). The worst phase is the Kawasaki act just after the speed-up reaches 2× (62–67 s), while the mixture is still fine-grained: a 0.73 ms margin to the 2 ms limit under load, above the ~1 ms aim; to be re-measured on a quiet machine. The costs are the swaps (∝ speed-up), classify and the chamfer. Live bytes at 500×150 are 1.54 MiB.
- **Acceptance**: `ISING-A1` for h ∈ {−4,−2,0,2,4} and T ∈ {0.5, 1, T_c, 3, 10}, `p₊(h)/p₊(−h) = e^{2h/T}` within 1e-12 relative (detailed balance against Boltzmann), `p₊(h) + p₊(−h) = 1` within 1e-15, `p₊(0) = ½` and `thr(0) = 2³¹` exactly, thresholds are non-decreasing in h, and T ∈ {0, −1, NaN, ±∞} gives probabilities in [0, 1] with no NaN; `ISING-A2` `T_c = 2.269185314` (1e-9), `m₀(2.0) = 0.911319`, `m₀(1.5) = 0.986500`, `e(2.0) = −1.745565`, `e(3.0) = −0.817310` (all 1e-6), `e(T_c) = −√2` (1e-9) and `|e(T_c ± 1e-6) + √2| < 1e-3`, m₀ = 0 for T ≥ T_c, m₀ is non-increasing on (0, T_c) and strictly decreasing on [1, T_c); `ISING-A3` on a seeded 32² torus, from a cold start at T = 2.0 after 1000 MCS the means over the next 3000 MCS (one sample per MCS) are ⟨|m|⟩ = 0.9113 ± 0.015 and ⟨e⟩ = −1.7456 ± 0.015, and from a random start at T = 3.0 they are ⟨|m|⟩ < 0.2 and ⟨e⟩ = −0.8173 ± 0.015; `ISING-A4` on a seeded 192² random start at T = 0.6·T_c the excess wall density `ρ − ρ_eq` (ρ = fraction of antiparallel bonds, `ρ_eq = (1 + e/2)/2 = 0.006678`) strictly decreases across 24, 48, 96 and 192 MCS and its least-squares log–log slope over those four points lies in [−0.62, −0.32] (Allen–Cahn −½); `ISING-A5` spins stay in {−1, +1} and the tracked M equals Σs after any sequence of updates (dt ∈ [0, 0.25]) and resizes including 1×1, a resize between two displays in the same `lattice_for` bucket at or above the cap leaves the lattice bit-identical, a bucket change makes every new site equal to its `resample_nearest` source, and 60 updates of 1/60 s perform `rate·N` attempts within ±1 with `rate = max(60·N/16 384, 1)`; `ISING-A6` T/T_c(t) is 0.60 at t = 0, 10 and 70 s, 1.25 at 30 s and 1.00 at 50 s, is periodic with period 90 s and changes by ≤ 0.01 per 1/60 s step over a whole loop; at 80×24 the wall density goes from < 0.3 (the labyrinth at 89.9 s) to > 0.4 on the first frame after 90 s (the re-quench); the label for (0.93, 0.71) is exactly `T/Tc 0.93  |m| 0.71`, is `T/Tc 0.93` for widths 12–23, and is absent below 12 columns or 4 rows; `ISING-A7` for T ∈ {0.5, 1, 0.6 T_c, 3} the swap table satisfies `p(ΔE) + p(−ΔE) = 1` within 2·2⁻³⁰ and each entry is within 2⁻³⁰ of `1/(1 + e^{ΔE/T})`, `thr[3] = 2²⁹`, and T ∈ {0, NaN, ∞} stays in `[0, 2³⁰]`; `swap_speedup` is 1 at 0 s, 1.5 at 2 s and 2 from 4 s on; a seeded 96×64 scrambled lattice has exactly N/2 down spins; under 4 × 200 MCS of swaps at 0.6 T_c, M never changes, the tracked M equals Σs, the wall density strictly decreases and ends below 0.2; a 1-wide lattice never swaps and never panics; in the scene at 80×24 the caption turns to `spins swap` at 58 s, M is then ≤ 1 in magnitude and constant through the act, and the caption returns to `spins align` on the first frame at or after 90 s; `ISING-A8` for both peaks (20 and 4) interior class values are non-decreasing in depth, every wall class is below the first interior value, and the class at the peak depth equals 0.78; two sites from a wall the Kawasaki table is brighter than the Glauber one; in both tables down interiors are HAlpha, up interiors Aurora, the up interior is ≥ 64 levels brighter and the interior peak is the same ink; in the Kawasaki table up is ≥ 64 levels above down at every class; the speck class's value is ≥ 0.2; on a 16×10 test lattice a lone flipped spin and a 2×2 speck are the speck class and their outside neighbours interior, while a 2×3 cluster (6 spins) has wall sites at depth 0; on 1×1, 1×7, 9×1 and 23×17 lattices with random wall seeds `chamfer` equals the closed-form 2–3 distance `3·min(|dx|, |dy|) + 2·(max − min)` to the nearest seed, capped at 20; the sites either side of a straight wall are class 3 with chamfer depths 0, 2, 4 moving away; the torus wraps for classification; the hot boil at 30 s is ≤ 0.65 of the mean `|signed(ink)|` at 20 s; `ISING-A9` `gauge_fill` is 6 at T_c, 1 at 0.60, 9 at 1.25, 0 for T ≤ 0.5 or NaN and 12 for T ≥ 1.5; at 80×24 the ASCII top row starts ` T/Tc 0.60  |m| 0.` and contains `  =-----|------ `; at 47 wide there is no thermometer; at 62 s (the Kawasaki act) the row starts ` T/Tc 0.60  M fixed `.

### liesegang_rings  (Reactions, M10)
- **Intent**: the rhythm of periodic precipitation. A rack of gel columns stands side by side, each with its outer electrolyte poured on top. In each gel a faint supersaturation haze sinks and, every few seconds, snaps into a bright precipitate band. Bands grow wider, brighter and farther apart with depth (Jablczynski's spacing law). Each tube keeps its own tempo: the concentrated one packs bands tight (forming its first band later and deeper), the dilute one wide and slow, so the Matalon–Packter law reads as a visual polyrhythm. Then a redissolution front wipes the bands top-down, and the second act is Liesegang's own 1896 set-up: a drop of silver nitrate in the centre of a dichromate-loaded gel dish grows concentric rings that snap into place outward from the drop, with its live spacing ratio in the header. ("Liesegang rings" names the phenomenon; in tubes it forms bands.) Both acts use `Ramp::Ember`, which borrows silver dichromate's red-brown family; lightness encodes the amount of precipitate (in the real gel the densest bands are the darkest), so this is false colour, and the yellow dichromate gel is drawn black so the bands read. The scene opens with bands already forming. The title is `Liesegang rings · form in bursts`; the captions are `bands in tubes` (rack) and `a silver drop` (dish).
- **Model**: per tube, a 1-D reaction–diffusion system with nucleation-and-growth precipitation. The ingredients are Ostwald's (1897) supersaturation theory, the nucleation threshold of Keller & Rubinow (1981) and the aggregation threshold of Chopard, Luthi & Droz (1994). The reactions are A + B → C (soluble product, idealised 1:1) and C → D (immobile precipitate), as in Liesegang's 1896 experiment of AgNO₃ diffusing into K₂Cr₂O₇-loaded gelatin to precipitate silver dichromate. The equations are `∂a/∂t = a'' − k·a·b`, `∂b/∂t = b'' − k·a·b`, `∂c/∂t = D_c·c'' + k·a·b − P`, `∂d/∂t = P`, `P = λ·max(c − c_s, 0)·[nᵢ]`. After each Euler step the per-node nucleation flag nᵢ is set irreversibly when `cᵢ ≥ c*` (homogeneous nucleation at supersaturation) or when `cᵢ ≥ c_g` and a neighbour was nucleated before the step (growth on adjacent precipitate; a Jacobi update, so the result is sweep-order-free). The step time is stored in `t_nuc[i]`. Parameters (dimensionless) are `D_a = D_b = 1`, `D_c = 0.3`, `k = 1`, `λ = 0.05`, `c_s = 0.01`, `c_g = 0.037`, `c* = 0.04`, `b₀ = 0.05`, and five presets `a₀ ∈ {0.7, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0}` (a₀/b₀ = 14–60). One unit ≈ 0.2 mm and one time unit ≈ ℓ²/D_A ≈ 27 s (D_A ≈ 1.5·10⁻⁹ m²/s), so the displayed 100 units are 2 cm grown in about 9 h, which are real Liesegang scales. The discretisation is part of the model: band positions shift about 10 % with τ while p changes by ≤ 0.02 between τ = 0.15 and 0.075. It uses a 3-point Laplacian and explicit Euler with `τ = 0.15`; positivity holds exactly since `τ(2 + k·a₀,max) = 0.75 ≤ 1` and `τ(2D_c + λ) ≤ 1`. Fields are f64 on 160-node columns, and node i sits at depth `x = i + 1` (the reservoir ghost is x = 0). The top ghost is `a = a₀` (reservoir) with no flux for b and c; the bottom ghost is `a = 0, b = b₀,c, c = 0` (semi-infinite gel; a closed bottom starves the front of b). All five tubes are always simulated, whatever is shown. Sim time is a warp of scene time s: `t_sim = T_END·(s/36 s)^{1.5}` with `T_END = 1200` through `grow`, then continuing at its final rate of 50 units/s, so `t_sim = 1500` when `hold` ends. Euler steps are taken whole from an accumulator, so the scheme always uses exactly τ. `scan_bands(d)` runs every 0.25 s: nodes with `d ≥ 5 %` of the column max form contiguous runs, each run is a band with d-weighted centroid x, width = run length and birth time `t_n = min t_nuc` over its nodes, and the first run is the junction zone, excluded from every law. Reference behaviour (a₀ = 2, t = 1500): bands at x = 8.9 (junction zone, 17 wide), 21.3, 28.3, 36.9, 48.6, 63.9, 83.6 and 106.8, ratios 1.33, 1.31, 1.32, 1.32, 1.31, 1.28 (last five mean 1.304), widths 5 → 12 (width law), and x_n/√t_n rising to 2.9 (last four mean 2.84; time law, Morse & Pierce 1903). Across the presets at t = 1400, p = 0.507, 0.406, 0.341, 0.299, 0.264, which fits `p = F + G·b₀/a₀` with F = 0.190, G = 4.41, R² = 0.999 (Matalon & Packter 1955; Antal et al. 1998). **Dish**: one axisymmetric 160-node column, the same chemistry in polar coordinates. Node i sits at radius `r_i = R_W + i + 1` with `R_W = 10`; the central well is held at `a = a₀ = 3` (the most concentrated tube preset) as the ghost at `r = R_W`. The drop is idealised as an inexhaustible reservoir (a held at a₀), as the tubes' top reservoir is; a real drop depletes, which shapes real ring spacing and count. The 3-point Laplacian gains the radial term, `f'' + f'/r ≈ (f₋ − 2f + f₊) + g·(f₊ − f₋)` with `g = 1/(2r)`: the neighbour weights `1 ∓ g` are positive and sum to 2, so the positivity bound `τ(2 + k·a₀) ≤ 1` is unchanged and diffusion conserves `Σ r·f`, the polar volume element. Tubes take the planar sweep (no radial term, no per-node division), bit-identical to M10. In the dish a band's position is its depth from the drop's edge, `x = r − R_W`, the analogue of the tube's depth below the junction; Jablczynski's law and the header ratio use that coordinate (measured from the drop's centre the same rings give ratios ≈ 1.20–1.24, still rising). Reference (t = 1500): junction zone to x ≈ 10.7, rings at x ≈ 23.7, 30.3, 39.0, 49.9, 63.3 and 80.8, ratios 1.28, 1.29, 1.28, 1.27, 1.28. **Story**: a looping `Timeline` of 6 beats, 100 s: `rack grow` 36 s, `rack hold` 6 s (bands thicken and the fronts leave the view), `rack dissolve` 8 s, then `dish grow`, `dish hold`, `dish dissolve` with the same lengths. A beat's phase is its index mod 3 and `t_sim` is the warp above of that phase. Each act starts from fresh gel with its own `b₀` jitter (every column reset); only the live act's columns step. Dissolve is a scripted redissolution with the reaction frozen, like an ammonia front complexing the silver salt to [Ag(NH₃)₂]⁺ (or Co(OH)₂ in excess NH₃). The front descends as `y_e = 120·√progress` nodes (it passes the view bottom at 69 % of the phase; in the dish it moves outward from the drop by the same `y_e`), and above it `c, d ← ·e^{−3·dt}`; a, b and the flags do not change. At each act boundary, every column resets to `(a, b, c, d, n, t_nuc) = (0, b₀,c, 0, 0, false, ∞)` with `b₀,c = b₀·(1 + 0.03ξ)`, where ξ ∈ [−1, 1] is drawn from `ctx.rng` then (ξ = 0 in the opening act). **Opening**: the first `resize` draws the opening act from `ctx.rng` and starts it part-grown: the rack 8 s into `grow` (t_sim ≈ 126, junction caps and a first band in every tube), the dish 16 s in (t_sim ≈ 356, the junction ring and a first ring). The rack steps five columns to the dish's one, so it opens earlier and both catch-ups stay within 4 500 column steps (rack 5 × 838, dish 2 370): a one-off scene-entry cost of ≈ 2–3 ms at 200×50 (release, loaded machine), accepted and bounded by a step-count test, not a clock.
- **Motion**: in the first second (at s ≈ 0.7 s) each junction zone precipitates as a dim, cloudy cap under the meniscus, and a dim-red haze starts to sink. Bands are then born 2–5 s apart, staggered across tubes (a₀ = 2: s ≈ 6, 8, 11, 15, 20, 28 s). At each birth a thin faint line appears just ahead of the haze, then brightens and thickens over about 1 s as it drains the haze, leaving a dark depletion gap between the band and the sinking haze. During `dissolve` a clear front sweeps down and erases the bands top-down; the gel rests clear for about 2.5 s before the next act.
- **Resize**: the chemistry never depends on the terminal. The five 160-node columns are the capped lattice, decoupled from the display (ADR-0012). 1-D columns have no aspect, so there is one bucket and nothing to resample; no resize after the first touches state or draws from `ctx.rng`. Only the layout is rebuilt: `n = clamp(⌊(w + 4)/24⌋, 1, 5)` visible tubes, showing presets {2}, {1,3}, {0,2,4}, {0,1,3,4} or {0..4} (always dilute → concentrated). The interior width is `clamp(round(w/(2.6n)), 1, 16)` columns with even gaps. Row 0 is the header, row 1 the liquid, and the gel ends at row `h − 2`, leaving the last row to the title (tubes run off-screen, no base). The gel's `D_px = 2·max(h − 3, 0)` pixel rows show nodes [0, V) with `V = clamp(2.4·D_px, 50, 100)` through linear box-filter weights built in `resize`: V = 100 from h = 24 (≈ 2.4 nodes/px at 80×24, ≈ 1.1 at 200×50), and short terminals zoom in on the top of the gel (V = 50 at 40×12) rather than merge bands and gaps into one striped column; the chemistry and the ratios always use the full column. A concave meniscus gives each pixel the depth `x = y − y₀ − sag·(1 − u²)`, with u ∈ [−1, 1] across the tube and sag = 0 below h = 22 (fewer than 40 gel rows, where a 1 px sag would only notch single pixels), 1 px for 22 ≤ h < 40 and 2 px from h = 40; pixels with x < 0 are liquid, so the interface and the bands curve gently. Cylinder shading is `× (0.8 + 0.2·√(1 − u²))`. Walls run over rows `1..h − 1`. The dish is centred at pixel `(w/2, h)`; its rim's inner radius is `R = max(min(h − 3, ⌊w/2⌋ − 2), 0)` pixels, the rim being the pixels with `R ≤ ρ < R + 1`. The gel shows depths `[0, V_d)` with `V_d = clamp(1.8·R − R_W, 30, 100)` at `scale = (R_W + V_d)/R` nodes per pixel of radius: finer than the tubes' 2.4, because at 2.4 the inner ring gaps are under a pixel wide and rasterise as dotted circles. A pixel at distance ρ is the drop when `ρ·scale < R_W`; otherwise its class comes from the box-filtered fields over the depths of radii `[ρ − ½, ρ + ½]`, and its ink is the mean of the inks of the two halves `[ρ − ½, ρ]` and `[ρ, ρ + ½]`: the ink curve is steep at low d, so a pixel just grazing a ring would otherwise light fully. Small dishes (80×24, R = 21) show the junction zone and the first rings only.
- **Inks**: gel pixels are `Role::Ramp(Ramp::Ember)` at the highest of three levels, times the cylinder shading. Precipitate is `(0.30 + 0.70·min(d/0.2, 1)^{0.75})·clamp(d/0.02, 0, 1)` from the box-filtered d, a continuous fade-in, so a newborn band is a thin faint line that brightens and a dissolving band fades fully out; it is not drawn below d = 0.002. Early bands are ≈ 0.6 and deep mature bands → 1.0 (pale gold). The junction zone is scaled by 0.30 (keeping its cloudy gradient; in Ember the 0.45 cap was a rust block that outweighed the bands) so the eye goes to the bands. Haze is `0.25·clamp((c/c* − 0.5)/0.5, 0, 1)` (dim oxblood, only above half the nucleation threshold, c ≥ c*/2 = 2·c_s). Clear gel is not drawn (terminal background), so depletion gaps and virgin gel are both dark. The liquid row and meniscus pixels are `Role::Wave` level 60, the glass walls and the dish rim are `Role::Structure` level 100 and the drop is `Wave` 60 (the tubes' liquid ink; the real solution is colourless), and the header is `Role::Text` (ratios 170, legend 110).
- **ASCII**: walls are `|` (Unicode `▕` left and `▏` right, hugging the gel) and liquid cells are `~`. The `Pixels::blit` closure classes each gel pixel from the fields, not the shaded ink: band iff the box-filtered `d ≥ 0.04` outside the junction zone; faint iff otherwise `d > 0.002` (faint precipitate and the whole junction zone); haze iff otherwise haze > 0 (clear gel carrying only the supersaturation haze); otherwise empty. band/band ⇒ `=`, band on top only ⇒ `-`, band on bottom only ⇒ `_`, faint/faint ⇒ `:`, one faint or any haze ⇒ `.`, empty ⇒ space, and the ink carries the level. The haze is thus a light stipple: its large sinking cloud never outweighs the `-`/`=` bands or the `:` junction cap. Wavy bands read at half-cell resolution. The 80×24 snapshots open part-grown in the rack (8 s into `grow`): three `|` tubes under a `~` row, each with a `:` junction cloud and a first `-` band over a sinking `.` haze at t = 0, and by t = 5 three to four bands per tube under the header ratios. The dish uses the same classes; the drop is `~` and the rim is the class `Glass`, `o` in `Structure` 100.
- **Label**: the header is on row 0. Centred over each tube is its live ratio of its two deepest complete bands (a band is complete once a deeper band has nucleated), for example `1.31`, blank until that exists. The legend sits at column 1 while any ratio is shown, keeping a 2-column gap before the first tube's ratio column r₀ (the walls start below the header, so nothing else can collide): `x(n+1)/x(n)` when r₀ ≥ 14 (80×24, 200×50 and 40×12), `xn+1/xn` when 10 ≤ r₀ < 14, otherwise none. During `dissolve` a tube's ratio leaves once the front `y_e` passes the end of the deeper band it measures (or the view bottom), so the numbers follow the wipe and the rest shows clear tubes under an empty header; the legend leaves with the last ratio. Numbers are dropped when the tube interior is < 4 columns, and the header when h < 6. In the dish act the header has one ratio, at column 14, right of the full legend and its 2-column gap, so it never sits on the rim's top arc; it is hidden when R < 4. Written from a stack buffer.
- **Perf**: update is ≤ 5 tubes × 160 nodes × ≤ 6 Euler sub-steps per 1/60 s (the peak comes in `hold`; 1–2 early) ≈ 4.8 k node updates ≈ 25 µs, plus a band scan of 5 × 160 nodes every 0.25 s. `dt = 0.25` costs ≤ 84 sub-steps ≈ 0.35 ms. Render at 200×50 is ≈ 7 k gel pixels ≈ 25 µs plus blit ≈ 30 µs. Total ≈ 0.1 ms, independent of terminal size; all state is fixed-size arrays, so nothing allocates after `resize`. M12: worst frame ≈ 0.32 ms and mean 0.13 ms at 200×50 (two loops, seeds 1, 2 and 42, minimum of 5 replays per frame; criterion mean 0.13 ms at load ≈ 36). The dish costs three box-filter lookups per pixel inside its bounding box.
- **Acceptance**: `LIES-A1` for any sequence of `update(dt ∈ [0, 0.25])` over two full 100 s loops, for the dish column as well as the tubes, all fields stay finite with `0 ≤ a ≤ a₀`, `0 ≤ b ≤ b₀,c`, `c ≥ 0`, `d ≥ 0`, and during grow and hold every node's d is non-decreasing and its nucleation flag and `t_nuc` never change once set; `LIES-A2` on a closed 96-node column (no flux at both ends, a = 1 on the top 40 nodes, b = b₀ everywhere), after 10⁴ Euler steps `Σ(b + c + d) = 96·b₀` and `Σ(a − b) = 40 − 96·b₀` within 1e-9 relative (A + B → C → D stoichiometry); `LIES-A3` preset a₀ = 2 on a 160-node column run to t = 1500 gives ≥ 7 bands (junction zone included) with x ≤ 112, and excluding the junction zone the last five ratios `x_{n+1}/x_n` lie within ±4 % of their mean, the mean is in [1.27, 1.35] (reference 1.304), and the deepest band is wider than the first band after the junction zone (reference 12 vs 5 nodes; spacing and width laws); `LIES-A4` in the same run `x_n/√t_n` for the four deepest bands lies within ±8 % of their mean and the mean is in [2.65, 3.0] (reference 2.84; time law); `LIES-A5` across the five presets (160 nodes, t = 1400), p = mean of the last three ratios − 1 is strictly decreasing in a₀, and the least-squares fit `p = F + G·b₀/a₀` has R² ≥ 0.98, G ∈ [3.5, 5.5] and F ∈ [0.12, 0.26] (reference 0.190, 4.41, 0.999; Matalon–Packter); `LIES-A6` the loop is 100 s; in each act the beats at 0, 36 and 42 s are grow, hold and dissolve, `t_sim` is 1200 at 36 s and 1500 at 42 s (1e-9) and strictly increases through grow and hold; from either opening act, through its dissolve, the live act's a, b and flags do not change, its view mass Σd[0, 100) never grows and ends below 2 %; the next update is in the other act with every column in its reset state (`|b₀,c/b₀ − 1| ≤ 0.03`); in both acts at s = 49.9 of the act, at 80×24 and 200×50, every pixel that is not liquid or glass is `Ink::BLANK`; `LIES-A7` two seeded scenes driven by the same update sequence across a loop boundary, one of them resized through 1×1, 7×3, 80×24 and 200×50 in between, have bit-identical states in every column, across the act boundary (no resize after the first touches the chemistry or draws from `ctx.rng`), and the visible presets follow the n(w) table (w = 20 ⇒ {2}, 80 ⇒ {0,2,4}, 200 ⇒ all five); `LIES-A8` the legend is `x(n+1)/x(n)` for r₀ ≥ 14, `xn+1/xn` for 10 ≤ r₀ < 14 and absent below, and the long form shows at 80×24, 200×50 and 40×12; at 80×24 (seed 42) the header shows the legend and three ratios at s = 41.9 (hold) and is blank at s = 49.9 (end of dissolve); a pixel with `d ≤ 0.002` and haze > 0 is class haze and any cell with haze but no faint, band or liquid pixel is `.`, while faint/faint stays `:`; and V = 100 at 80×24 and 200×50, 50 at 40×12 and 2.4·26 at 80×16, with the deepest window of 40×12 ending at node 50; `LIES-A9` / `LIES-A10` in both acts at 80×24, 40×12 and 200×50, 2 s in, every pixel's role follows its class (empty → Background, liquid → `Wave` 60, glass → `Structure` 100, others → `Ramp(Ember)`), `D_px = 2·max(h − 3, 0)`, and the rendered row `h − 1` is all blank; `LIES-A11` at 80×24 (seed 42) the rack opens with ≥ 2 bands in every visible tube and band pixels on screen, and the dish with ≥ 2 bands; over 64 seeds an unforced opening starts at `act·50 + (8 or 16)` s, both acts occur, and the catch-up takes at most 4 500 column steps; `LIES-A12` a closed 96-node radial column (a = 1 on the top 40 nodes) conserves `Σ r(b + c + d)` and `Σ r(a − b)` within 1e-9 relative over 10⁴ steps and precipitates; the tube column equals a verbatim M10 step bit for bit over 2 000 steps; the dish column at t = 1500 has ≥ 6 bands, and its last four ratios (in `x = r − R_W`) lie within ±4 % of their mean, which is in [1.25, 1.35]; `LIES-A13` at 80×24 and 200×50, R follows the formula above, `V_d` and `scale` follow it with `scale ≤ 1.8` unless `V_d` is at its floor; every glass pixel lies at `R ≤ ρ < R + 1` and every pixel with `ρ·scale < R_W` is liquid; the glass bounding box is symmetric within one pixel and lies in pixel rows `2..2(h − 1)`; the header row starts ` x(n+1)/x(n) 1.`; `caption()` is `a silver drop` in the dish and `bands in tubes` in the rack.

### molecule_viewer  (Reactions, M10, M12)
- **Intent**: a slowly tumbling ball-and-stick model of a real molecule, lit like a physical model on a dark bench. Near atoms are bright and far atoms recede. The gallery runs water, methane, ethanol, benzene, caffeine, then buckminsterfullerene. Each molecule grows out of a point, turns, and collapses back into the point before the next one grows. M12: each molecule is lit per pixel, with shaded spheres and cylinders, specular highlights, depth cueing and true occlusion; the frame fills the art area, and bond orders show (double bonds are two strands, aromatic bonds a stick plus a dashed inner strand). The formula and molar mass are a right-aligned readout on the title row.
- **Model**: static tables in the module. Each holds element, position (Å) and bonds with an order of `Single`, `Double` or `Aromatic` (1.5). Benzene uses `Aromatic`; caffeine uses Kekulé single and double bonds. Planar molecules are stored with their plane in world xy (normal along z), so yaw takes them through face-on and edge-on views. Geometries:
  - water: r(OH) 0.9572 Å, ∠HOH 104.52° (Benedict, Gailar & Plyler 1956).
  - methane: T_d, r(CH) 1.087 Å.
  - ethanol: trans (anti H–O–C–C) conformer, r(CC) 1.512, r(CO) 1.431, r(OH) 0.971 Å (NIST CCCBDB experimental); the remaining C–H bonds and angles come from CCCBDB, or are tetrahedral with r(CH) 1.09 Å.
  - benzene: D₆h, r(CC) 1.397 Å, r(CH) 1.084 Å (Herzberg 1966).
  - caffeine C8H10N4O2: the 3-D conformer of PubChem CID 2519 (24 atoms, 25 bonds, planar purine), with double bonds C2=O, C6=O, C4=C5 and C8=N9.
  - C60: generated at build, not typed in. Each of the 30 edges of an icosahedron carries two atoms at fractions `t` and `1 − t`. The icosahedron's vertices are the cyclic permutations of (0, ±1, ±φ); its edge is 2, so it is scaled by `a/2`. With `a = 2·r₅₆ + r₆₆` and `t = r₅₆/a`, the pentagon (5:6) bonds are r₅₆ = 1.458 Å and the hexagon-fusing (6:6) bonds, which lie along the old edges, are r₆₆ = 1.401 Å (Hedberg et al. 1991, gas-phase electron diffraction). Every atom then sits 3.562 Å from the centre.

  Every table is recentred on its centre of mass (H 1.008, C 12.011, N 14.007, O 15.999 u). Ball radius is 0.20 × the Bondi (1964) van der Waals radius (H 1.20, C 1.70, N 1.55, O 1.52 Å), which keeps ≥ 0.5 Å of visible stick on C60's 1.40–1.46 Å bonds, so the cage reads as rings of balls rather than a heap of blobs.

  View (M12): the camera is `geom::Camera { yaw, pitch, distance: 5·r_eff }`, with `r_eff = max(maxᵢ(|rᵢ| + r_ball,ᵢ), 1.25 Å)` so water (raw ≈ 1.16 Å) is framed but not ballooned. The art area is rows `[0, h − 1)` when `h ≥ 6` (else all rows), centred at `((w − 1)/2, (art − 1)/2)`. The scale is `s(t)·draw::fit_radius(w × art, 0.95)/(1.05·r_eff)` rows per Å, where `s(t)` is the gallery scale (Motion): a sphere of radius `r_eff` seen from `5·r_eff` projects to at most `5/√24` ≈ 1.021 of its radius, and the ≈ 3 % on top covers the pixel discs, so no orientation leaves the frame.

  Raster (M12): `update` projects the ≤ 60 atoms and paints into a z-buffered `Pixels` raster the size of the art area; each pixel keeps its surface depth (Å) and its owner (atom `i + 1` or the stick marker). `render` only blits it.
  - Balls: pixel radius `2·r_rows`, with the centre pixel always lit; surface depth `depth + r_Å·√(1 − d²)`; Lambert `I = 0.3 + 0.7·max(0, n·L)` with `L = normalise(−0.45, 0.55, 0.70)` (upper left, front); a Blinn–Phong highlight (`n·H > 0.95`) at `s²` of full brightness.
  - Depth cue: `s²·(100 + 155·ν)/255`, with `ν = (depth + r_eff)/(2·r_eff) ∈ [0, 1]`.
  - Sticks: a cylinder of radius 0.10 Å (at least 0.55 px) between the projected centres; depth is interpolated along the axis plus the cylinder's bulge. Each half is in its atom's colour at 0.8 of the ball level, with the depth cue interpolated along the bond. Double bonds are two strands of radius 0.06 Å, 0.125 Å either side of the axis on screen; aromatic bonds a full stick plus a dashed strand (3 dashes over the middle 60 %) 0.25 Å toward the view centre, inside benzene's ring in any orientation. Strands split only when a thin strand is ≥ 0.6 px in radius; below that every order is one stick. Sticks are drawn only from `s ≥ 0.5` (below it the atoms crowd the centroid).
- **Stylisation (stated)**: balls are 0.20 × the Bondi radius (a ball-and-stick convention, not space-filling). Bond-order strands are offset in the screen plane (a 2-D convention), not in the π-bond plane, so a double bond reads as two strands at any orientation. Sticks are 0.10 Å. N and O use named ramps (false colour close to CPK blue and red); H and C are `Text` greys (C at 0.6 of H), which every theme keeps neutral.
- **Motion**: everything is a closed-form function of scene time `t`.
  - The gallery is a `Timeline` of `grow` 0.6 s → `hold` 8.8 s → `shrink` 0.6 s (10 s per molecule). The molecule is `(start + timeline.cycle(t)) mod 6`, where `start` is seeded.
  - Scene time begins at the start of the first `hold`, so the first frame shows a full molecule, and successive 15 s `sim` visits show different molecules.
  - The gallery scale `s` is `ease::smoothstep(progress)` in `grow`, 1 in `hold` and `1 − smoothstep(progress)` in `shrink`. Atoms converge on the centroid and their levels dim with `s²`. The molecule and label change only at the slot boundary, where `s = 0`.
  - Yaw is `ω·τ − 0.78 rad` with `ω = 2π/10 s`, where τ is the time since the slot began, so each molecule reaches full size in a three-quarter view (≈ −0.4 rad). Pitch is `0.30 + 0.30·sin(2πt/17 s)` rad.
  - C60's equator sweeps up to ≈ 9 columns per second at 80×24 (≈ 19 at 200×50).
- **Resize**: view only; there is no simulation state (ADR-0012). The centre, scale and label tier are recomputed. Scene time, slot and orientation are kept, and the seeded `start` is drawn only on the first resize.
- **Inks** (M12): H `Text` at `255·v`, C `Text` at `255·0.6·v`, N `ramp(Ice, v)`, O `ramp(HAlpha, v)`, where v is the shaded, depth-cued brightness; bond halves take their atom's ink at 0.8 of its level.
- **ASCII**: each cell takes the brighter of its two pixels. Levels < 24 are blank; above that, `: + * # @` by level quintile, in that pixel's ink. Every atom that owns its centre pixel shows its element symbol in that cell, upper-case when `ν ≥ 0.5` and lower-case otherwise.
- **Label** (GEN-R13): `draw::label_right` on row `h − 1` (when `h ≥ 6`), in `Text` at `title::READOUT_LEVEL`: `caffeine  C8H10N4O2  194.19 g/mol`, all in ASCII, the longest tier that fits `title::readout_room` with the live caption: drop the mass, then the name, then show nothing. The Hill formula (C first, then H, then the rest alphabetically; H2O for water) and the molar mass (2 decimals) are checked against the table. While the title shows the molecule's name as its caption (`Title::fit` gives `Form::Full`, decided per molecule in `resize`), the name is left out: `C8H10N4O2  194.19 g/mol`, then `C8H10N4O2`, then nothing. The label is never clipped and switches only at the slot boundary. Row 0 is art.
- **Perf** (200×50, the same run and load as molecular_dynamics): the worst is C60, with 60 balls of ≈ 40 px and 90 sticks of ≈ 60 px checked; mean 0.06 ms, heaviest 1-s window median 0.20 ms. Live bytes at 500×150 are 2.15 MiB; no heap use in `update` or `render`.
- **Acceptance**: `MOL-A1` every table is chemically consistent: with the covalent radii of Cordero et al. (2008) (H 0.31, C 0.76, N 0.71, O 0.66 Å), bonded pairs satisfy `0.80 ≤ d/(rᵢ + rⱼ) ≤ 1.12` and every non-bonded pair has `d/(rᵢ + rⱼ) ≥ 1.35`, so the bond list equals the distance-derived connectivity; each atom's summed bond order equals its valence (H 1, C 4, N 3, O 2; aromatic = 1.5); the Hill formula derived from the table equals the label's (H2O, CH4, C2H6O, C6H6, C8H10N4O2, C60), the derived molar mass matches 18.015, 16.043, 46.069, 78.114, 194.194 and 720.66 g/mol within 0.001, and the label's 2-decimal mass is within 0.005 + 1e-9 of it; and the centre of mass is at the origin within 1e-6 Å; `MOL-A2` reference geometry: water r(OH) = 0.9572 ± 0.0005 Å and ∠HOH = 104.52 ± 0.05°; methane r(CH) = 1.087 ± 0.001 Å with all six ∠HCH = 109.47 ± 0.05°; ethanol has 9 atoms and 8 bonds with r(CC) = 1.512, r(CO) = 1.431 and r(OH) = 0.971 (each ± 0.001 Å) and an H–O–C–C dihedral of 180 ± 1°; benzene's 12 atoms are coplanar within 0.001 Å in the xy plane with r(CC) = 1.397 ± 0.001 and r(CH) = 1.084 ± 0.001 Å; caffeine's 14 heavy atoms are coplanar within 0.05 Å; C60 has 60 atoms and 90 bonds, 3 bonds and exactly one 6:6 bond per atom, 30 bonds of 1.401 ± 0.001 Å and 60 of 1.458 ± 0.001 Å, every atom at 3.562 ± 0.002 Å from the centre, and its 5:6 bonds form exactly 12 disjoint 5-cycles; `MOL-A3` the motion is rigid and fits the frame: at 500 sampled `hold` times over a 60 s cycle, the rotated coordinates (recovered from an orthographic `Camera` at scale 1 as `(x/ASPECT, −y, depth)`) keep every pairwise distance of the table within 1e-9 Å; at 80×24, 200×50, 40×12, 120×36, 60×20, 100×30, 30×10 and 160×40 every atom projects (`Some`) and every ball's pixel disc (centre ± `2·r_rows`) lies in `[0, w] × [0, 2·art]`; at 80×24 and 200×50 every molecule's balls reach ≥ 0.85 of the framing radius at some point of its hold turn; the depth-cue factor `100 + 155·ν` and the projected ball radius (for a fixed element) are strictly increasing in depth; `MOL-A4` z-buffer: for a far O and a nearer N whose balls overlap, every pixel inside both discs carries N's ink (`Ramp(Ice)`), with more than 20 such pixels; over all six molecules × 12 yaws at 200×50, each atom's centre pixel shows the atom itself in its element role, or a nearer ball, or a stick running toward the viewer, and ≥ 80 % show the atom itself; `MOL-A5` gallery timeline: from the seeded start, one 60 s cycle shows the six molecules in gallery order, 10 s each; `s` is exactly 1 throughout `hold`, never outside `[0, 1]`, and continuous (`|Δs| ≤ 0.05` per 1/60 s step); the molecule index and label change only at slot boundaries, where `s = 0`; seeds 1..=32 yield all six starting molecules (six uniform draws would cover all six only 6!/6⁶ ≈ 1.5 % of the time); no pixel exceeds `round(255·s²)`, and the bonded molecule lights more pixels than its bare balls exactly when `s ≥ 0.5`; `MOL-A6` label and fallback: the chosen label tier is the longest one that fits (empty when none fits), so the label is never clipped; at 80×24 the readout is on row 23, ends ` g/mol ` and never repeats the caption, and row 0 carries no label; in ASCII mode over 60 s the frame is pure ASCII, every atom that owns its centre pixel shows its symbol there, and `H C N O h c` all appear; `MOL-A7` bond orders and palette: on a face-on C–C pair 1.4 Å apart at 120×40 the pixel column through the bond's middle has 1 lit run for a single bond, 2 for a double bond and 1–2 for an aromatic bond; `element_ink(H, 1) = Text 255`, C is `Text` below 200, N is `Ramp(Ice)` and O is `Ramp(HAlpha)`.

### physarum  (Living Systems, M10, M12)
- **Intent**: a slime mould solving a transport problem in time-lapse. A glowing inoculum spills outward in a lace of veins, finds the scattered oat flakes, and thickens into a few pulsing tubes that link them like a rail map (Nakagaki et al. 2000; Tero et al. 2010). The network then starves and dissolves tube by tube into darkness. Fresh flakes drop onto the empty plate, and a new colony starts somewhere else.
- **Model**: the agent-based approximation of Jones (2010). The plate is the screen above the label row: `h_p = h − 1` rows when the label is drawn and `h_p = h` otherwise, so the plate's absorbing edge keeps every tube off that row and the label sits under the organism, not inside it. The chemoattractant `T` is a `Grid<f32>` on a capped square lattice `L = grid::lattice_for(Size(w, 2h_p), 20_000, 1.25)` (CORE-R48). That gives 197×98 at 200×50 and 80×46 at 80×24; below the cap the lattice equals the pixel display, so the view is exact. There are `n = max(1, round(p·lw·lh))` agents with `p = 5 %` (965 and 184), within the population range Jones explored. Each agent has a continuous position and a heading in `Z₁₆` (22.5° units) with a 16-entry `(cos, sin)` table, so no trig runs per step; turns are addition in Z₁₆ (`rotate(h, t)`, + = clockwise on screen). Its site is its rounded position. Each Jones step is two passes over a fresh Fisher–Yates shuffle of the live agents (rand's `SliceRandom::shuffle` on a `SplitMix64` drawn from `ctx.rng`). **Motor pass**: step `SS = 1` px along the heading. If the target site is on the lattice and unoccupied (particle exclusion, one agent per site), or is the agent's own site (a sub-site move, possible after a diagonal step from a non-integer position), the agent moves and deposits `depT = 5` at its new site. Otherwise it stays, deposits nothing and takes a uniformly random new heading. **Sensory pass**: sample `T` at three sensors at distance `SO = clamp(round(0.09·min(lw, lh)), 3, 9)` px (4 at 80×24, 9 at 200×50) and angles `h − SA, h, h + SA` with `SA = 22.5°`. Off-lattice sensors read −1, so walls repel. The agent then rotates by `RA = 45°` according to Jones's rule: F strictly greatest ⇒ keep the heading; F strictly least ⇒ ±RA by coin; otherwise turn toward the strictly stronger side (FL < FR ⇒ toward R, FR < FL ⇒ toward L); any remaining tie ⇒ keep. **Chemoattractant**: after both passes, `T ← (1 − δ)·mean₃ₓ₃(T)` as a separable 3-tap box filter with `δ = 0.1`. The plate edge absorbs: sites off the lattice count as 0 in the mean, so attractant leaks out there and the network sits on the plate instead of tracing the frame. **Drawn field**: a second grid `V` receives the same deposits (at inoculation and in the motor pass) and the same diffusion and decay, but no food pinning. It is what is drawn (through the display EMA `S`, see Inks); the agents sense `T` only. **Food**: `n_f = clamp(round(lw·lh/800), 4, 9)` flakes (5 at 80×24, 9 at 200×50) at seeded positions in the central 80 %. They are at least `0.25·min(lw, lh)` apart and at least `max(2·SO, 0.15·min(lw, lh))` beyond the inoculum's rim, so the colony has to spread before it touches one. Placement uses at most 200 rejection tries and accepts fewer flakes on a crowded plate, including 0. Food discs of radius 1.5 px are pinned to `T = max(T, 20)` (4·depT) during explore and consolidate, which anchors trunk lines between sources as in Tero et al. (2010). **Inoculum**: a disc of radius `√(2n/π)` px (50 % occupancy) centred at a seeded point in the central 40 %, with random headings. Each agent deposits once at seeding, and `V` is then blurred twice by the same filter with δ = 0, so the colony shows at t = 0 as a soft drop rather than a checkerboard of single deposits. **Rate**: one Jones step per `SIM_DT` (60 per simulated second), counted by the core `FixedStep(SIM_DT, 15)` (CORE-R9..R11). This is a time-lapse: the network in Tero et al. took about a day to grow. **Timeline** (48 s, loops): explore 10 s → consolidate 30 s (the physics is unchanged; the network coarsens under its own dynamics while the food holds the trunk lines) → fade 6 s (food unpinned; each agent dies at a seeded uniform time in the phase, and a dead agent stops moving and depositing and frees its site, so tubes thin and snap one by one) → rest 2 s (the trail decays to black, `0.9^120 ≈ 3·10⁻⁶`). At rest start, the next cycle's inoculum centre and food are drawn from `ctx.rng`, and the new flakes fade in. The inoculum is seeded at the cycle boundary. A step spans `[t₀, t₁]`: starvation and the Jones step are decided at `t₀`, and these two events at `t₁`, so the state already holds them when the scene time reads `t₁` and the drawing never runs ahead of the colony (PHY-A5). Every cycle differs, and each seed is deterministic.
- **Motion**: agents move 60 px/s and the trail changes every step. Within 1 s the inoculum grows filaments that reach visibly past its rim; because the rim streams outward at 60 px/s, at 80×24 the nearest flakes are touched within about a second and tied in by `+` tubes by ≈ 3 s. By 10 s the sources are joined by a web with loops. During consolidation, brightness pulses along the stable tubes as agents stream through them, and a slow stylised streaming wave runs outward along them (see Streaming), while their edges hold still (PHY-A7). The fade reads as a slow disintegration, followed by a dark plate where new flakes appear, and then a new bloom.
- **Resize** (ADR-0012): the lattice comes from `lattice_for`. If it is unchanged (same aspect bucket, with both sizes at or above the cap in both axes), only the display map and `Pixels` are rebuilt, no `ctx.rng` is consumed, and the simulation stays bit-identical. If it changes, `T` and `V` are resampled with `grid::resample_bilinear`. Agent positions are scaled by `(lw'/lw, lh'/lh)` and re-inserted into the occupancy grid in index order, and any collisions are dropped. Outside fade and rest, the population is then brought to `n' = round(p·lw'·lh')`: trimmed evenly in index order (agent i of n is kept when `⌊(i + 1)·n'/n⌋ > ⌊i·n'/n⌋`, with no randomness), or topped up with agents placed on free sites where `T` is above the mean, using at most 16 `ctx.rng` tries per agent before skipping it. Food is scaled, SO is recomputed, and the timeline is untouched. Agent, order and occupancy buffers have capacity 1000 (= p·20 000) from `build`.
- **Inks**: the drawn field `S` is `V` through a display-only EMA, `S += (V − S)·(1 − e^(−h/0.15 s))` after every step, reset to `V` at inoculation and on a lattice change. The discrete agents' per-step deposits make tube edges flip grade on almost every frame (≈ 30 % of lit ASCII cells at 80×24); the filter keeps the pulsing and the growth but stills the edges. The agents sense `T`, so no model result depends on it. `S` is tone-mapped by `u = min(1, √(S / (2.5·T_ref)))`. `T_ref` is reset at every inoculation (the first and each cycle boundary) to the bright-half mean of the new inoculum's `V`, so every bloom opens on the same soft drop, and is otherwise an EMA (τ = 1 s) of the mean `V` over sites above `V`'s global mean (the global total comes out of the diffusion pass). During the first 1.5 s of explore it follows `max(bright-half mean, 0.45·max V)` with τ = 0.25 s instead: a young inoculum is a flat stack of deposits about 2.5× its bright-half mean, which that reference alone paints as one saturated `#` disc (≈ 44 % of lit cells), and exposing for its peak leaves `#` to the hottest ≈ 10 % of the drop. It is frozen during fade and rest, so the dying network dims instead of being re-exposed. (Inheriting the old network's frozen reference instead opened every later bloom as an underexposed `.`/`:` haze with holes, ≈ 25 % fewer lit cells than the first and no `+`/`#`.) The organism is `Ramp(Ember)` (M12, replacing `Seq`), which is false colour: real *Physarum* is yellow, and the ramp ends in pale gold. No other Life scene uses Ember, so it reads differently from the `Seq` fields of gray_scott, belousov_zhabotinsky and liesegang_rings. *Tubes* (`u ≥ 0.45`, the M10 floor): `ramp(Ember, 0.36 + 0.64·t^1.6)` with `t = min(1, (u − 0.45)/0.55)`, an orange body around a pale-gold channel, so junctions and busy trunks glow; the tube floor is level `CORE_LO_LEVEL` = 92. *Halo* (`0.25 ≤ u < 0.45`): `ramp(Ember, 0.04 + 0.22·(u − 0.25)/0.20)`, the diffuse attractant around each tube in the ramp's near-black oxblood end. It softens the half-block edges without fattening the veins. `u < 0.25` stays `Ink::BLANK`, so the plate is the terminal's own dark. During the fade the frozen `T_ref` lets the dying network sink through the halo band, so it smoulders out. *Flakes*: each oat flake is a 2 × 2-pixel mote (square on screen; M10 drew a 5-pixel plus), oat beige: `Blackbody` at level 105 (≈ 4 000 K) times its fade. That is an art-directed tint, not a temperature (ADR-0015 allows `Blackbody` as art-directed colour), chosen so the food belongs to the organism's warm world instead of reading as grey UI debris. Level 105 is below the tubes' pale-gold peak, so in mono the organism outshines its food; fading the level takes a flake through the ramp's dim reds into the plate. Because the pinned `T` is not drawn, a flake has no glow until the organism reaches it. The colony's flakes fade out over the last 1 s of the fade (`105·(1 − smoothstep)`), so rest opens on an empty plate, and the next colony's fade in from 0 to 105 over rest (`105·smoothstep(progress)`); a flake at level 0 is not drawn. Label `Role::Text` 110. Agents are not drawn: the trail is the organism.
- **Streaming** (M12, stylised): while the network consolidates, tube tones pulse as `u' = max(0.45, u·(1 + 0.2·e·sin(k·r − 2πt/3 s)))`. `r` is the pixel's distance from the inoculum centre and `k = 2π/(0.3·min(w, 2h))`, so the pulses run outward along the tubes as bright swellings. The envelope `e` eases in and out over 2 s at the ends of `consolidate` and is 0 elsewhere, so the bloom and the starving network hold still. Halo pixels are not pulsed, and no pulse is computed while `e = 0`. Tones already near `u = 1` clip on the up-swing, so the busiest junctions pulse mostly downward. *Stylisation*: real shuttle streaming reverses every 1–2 minutes and is driven by peristaltic contraction. The scene is a ≈ 1000–2000× time-lapse (a network that takes 10–26 h forms in about 48 s), at which a ≈ 100 s reversal cycle would last 50–100 ms, 1.5–3 frames at 30 FPS: close to the frame rate, too fast to show honestly. The 3 s one-way outward wave is deliberately slowed and never reverses.
- **ASCII**: half-block `Pixels` (CORE-R42) over the plate, `w × 2h_p`; each display pixel samples `S` through `grid::SampleMap` (CORE-R52) and is tone-mapped in `update`; the floor test runs on `S/(2.5·T_ref) < 0.45²`, so dark pixels skip the square root. The `blit` closure works per cell: a tube pixel's tone is read back from its ink (the inverse of the tube ramp, within 0.012), so `render` only blits and the ASCII grades follow the streaming pulse; halo and food pixels count as blank; both blank ⇒ untouched; otherwise `m = max(u_top, u_bottom)` maps to `.` (< 0.60), `:` (< 0.75), `+` (< 0.90) or `#`, using the brighter pixel's ink. In ASCII, `render` then puts a single `o` (M10: `@`) in the flake's ink in each flake's cell; in Unicode a flake is its 2 × 2 mote. At 80×24 the snapshots show a soft `.`/`:`/`+` drop and five `o` at t = 0, filaments already reaching the two nearest flakes at t = 1 s, and a `.`/`:` lace with `+`/`#` trunks through the `o`s at t = 5 s.
- **Captions** (M12): explore `veins seek food`; consolidate `None`, so the title's own `trails reinforce` shows; fade `network starves`; rest `fresh oat flakes`.
- **Label**: `SA 22.5 RA 45 SO 9` (ASCII, rebuilt in `resize` when SO changes) right-aligned in the row below the plate (GEN-R13), drawn only when `w ≥ 40` and `h ≥ 12` and it fits `title::stable_readout_room` (CORE-R83), so it never comes and goes with the beat, with a blank cell either side, so it never looks glued to a tube.
- **Perf**: at 200×50 (lattice 197×98, 965 agents, 1 Jones step per update): the shuffle, the motor and sensory passes, two separable diffuse-decay passes (`T` and `V`; the `V` pass also returns its total), one more pass over `V` for the bright-half mean and peak, food pinning (< 1 µs) and the display EMA of `S` (one pass), then the `SampleMap` tone map of 200×98 pixels, all in `update`; `render` blits 9.8 k cells. Criterion on a loaded machine (load average ≈ 15–26): 0.28–0.39 ms at 200×50 (0.64 ms at load ≈ 80–99 with the 197×98 plate) and 0.71–1.05 ms at 400×100, against the < 1 ms aim (GEN-R8 is measured at 200×50). At 400×100 the 80 k-pixel tone map is ≈ 0.45 ms of that; testing the floor on the square saves ≈ 5–10 % of it. The simulation cost is independent of terminal size above the cap; the tone map and the blit scale with the display. M12 (fastest of 5 runs per frame over 3600 frames): mean 0.29 ms, worst frame 0.35 ms at 200×50 (M10: 0.32 / 0.42 ms); streaming costs one `sin` and one `hypot` per tube pixel, during consolidate only. Steady state is allocation-free.
- **Acceptance**: `PHY-A1` `steer(f, fl, fr, coin)` returns exactly 0 / ±2 (by coin) / +2 / −2 / 0 in Z₁₆ units (+ = toward FR) for the five cases of Jones's rule, listed literally and then exhaustively over all orderings of three values with ties; every entry of the 16-direction table has unit length to 1e-6; `rotate` is addition in Z₁₆ (sixteen +1 rotations return to every start, `rotate(0, −RA) = 14`, `rotate(15, RA) = 1`) and + is clockwise on screen; and an agent heading east with attractant only at its FR sensor turns by +RA in the sensory pass; `PHY-A2` `diffuse_decay`: a constant field `c` maps to `(1 − δ)c` at interior sites, `(1 − δ)c·6/9` at edge sites and `(1 − δ)c·4/9` at corners (absorbing edge; relative error 1e-6), and the returned total matches the field's sum; with δ = 0 an interior impulse spreads to exactly its 3×3 block at 1/9 each and conserves the total to 1e-6; for any seeded non-negative field, `max(out) ≤ (1 − δ)·max(in)`, no output is negative, and `min(out) ≥ (1 − δ)·min(in)` at interior sites; `PHY-A3` over a seeded 600-step run on an 80×48 lattice followed by a full fade, after every step the occupancy count equals the live agent count, no two agents share a site and every agent is on the lattice; the live count is constant outside fade, non-increasing within it and 0 at its end; `PHY-A4` network forms (thresholds from a Jones-model prototype): from a seeded inoculum on a 96×48 lattice with 5 food sources, every flake lies at least `√(2n/π) + max(2·SO, 0.15·48)` from the inoculum centre, at step 0 no site within Chebyshev distance 2 of a flake centre has trail (no flake's 3×3 disc is 8-adjacent to the inoculum); after 600 steps (10 s) the trail's coefficient of variation is ≥ 1.2, the fraction of sites above the mean is in [0.2, 0.45], and the largest 8-connected component of {T > mean} holds ≥ 85 % of those sites; the largest component of the unpinned drawn field {V > mean} holds ≥ 4 of the 5 food sources (this holds for about 90 % of seeds; seed 42 is used); as a control, the same seeded colony stepped without food pinning, judged on the same unpinned field, links ≤ 3 of the 5 sources (seed 42: 4 fed against 0), so the pinning is what ties them in; on `T` the pinning itself would hold every fed flake centre above the mean (~0.5 s in debug); `PHY-A5` on a 40×24 display (lattice 40×46) the beats are explore [0, 10) / consolidate [10, 40) / fade [40, 46) / rest [46, 48) s and repeat at 48 s, the total trail at 47.9 s is < 1e-3 of its mean over consolidate, the second cycle's food layout and inoculum centre differ from the first cycle's, and both are identical across two runs with the same seed (< 1.5 s in debug); and across the loop seams, over every step of two cycles plus 1 s (seed 42): drawn flakes are `Blackbody`; during explore and consolidate the drawn flakes are exactly the colony's own layout at `level(105)`, below `organism_ink(1)`'s level; the flake level never changes by more than 12 between consecutive steps, and the drawn layout changes (≥ 4 times) only between frames whose levels are both ≤ 12, so no seam flashes or cuts a layout at full brightness (< 2 s in debug); `PHY-A6` resizing 220×60 → 240×64 (both lattice 197×101) leaves the lattice and `T` bit-identical, and after 60 more steps `T` is bit-identical to a run that never resized; resizing 200×50 → 80×24 (197×98 → 80×46) during consolidate gives a new max ≤ the old max, a new mean within 20 % of the old, every agent on the lattice with exclusion intact, an unchanged food count and an unchanged beat, and a population trimmed to at most `round(p·80·46) = 184` and at least 90 % of it; resizing 80×24 → 200×50 during consolidate tops the population up to ≥ 90 % of `round(p·197·98)`; `PHY-A7` drawing: on the seeded 80×24 display at t = 0, no organism pixel lies within 3 pixels of a flake's centre (the pinned `T` is not drawn), the ASCII frame holds exactly one `o` per flake, at the flake's own cell, and in both glyph modes the cells at x = 0 and just after the label are blank; at 1 s samples over the first 100 s (two cycles) the bottom row holds exactly the label and nothing else in both glyph modes; in ASCII frames at 30 FPS on the seeded 80×24 display, the first frame of the second bloom (t = 48 s) has a lit-cell count within ±20 % of the t = 0 frame and a nonzero `+`/`#` share (inheriting the old reference gives ≈ 25 % fewer and no `+`/`#`), and `#` is ≤ 20 % of the lit cells in every frame of the first 1.5 s of the first and the second bloom (≈ 44 % without the bloom exposure), and over 2 s of consolidation (from t = 77 s) ≤ 15 % of lit cells change glyph from one frame to the next (≈ 30 % without the display EMA; ≈ 8 % with it) (~1 s in debug); `PHY-A8` ember glow, at 80×24 (t = 5 s) and 200×50 (t = 20 s), seed 42: every lit non-flake pixel is `Ramp(Ember)`, at a level either ≤ the halo top or ≥ the tube floor; both bands occur; and ≥ 90 % of halo pixels have a tube pixel within 3 pixels; `PHY-A9` captions: over one 48 s cycle `caption()` follows the four beats above, and every caption is in `META.captions`; `PHY-A10` shuttle streaming: `stream` is the identity at depth 0, stays within ±depth and never goes below the tube floor; `tube_tone(organism_ink(u))` returns `u` within 0.012 on [0.45, 1], and `None` for the halo; `CORE_LO_LEVEL` equals `ink::ramp(Ember, CORE_LO).level`; the envelope is off at t = 1, 9 and 43 s and on at 13 and 25 s; and with the field frozen, half a period later more than half the tube pixels change level and no halo pixel does.

### solar_surface  (Sun & Planets, M10, M12)
- **Intent**: the Sun up close as a slowly turning ball. The granulated photosphere darkens and reddens toward the limb. A few sunspot groups with dark umbrae and striated penumbrae ride differential rotation from east (left) to west (right). Cool loops, with knots of coronal rain sliding along them, stand bright above the limb as their active regions rotate into view, and cross the disc as dark filaments. The disc fills the height, and the eye goes to the loops at the limb and to the spots. The black sky around it stays empty. At 200×50 the disc is 92 cols × 46 rows with 54 empty cols on each side; at 80×24 it is 44 × 22 with 18 on each side.
- **Model**: the view is a composite: the photosphere in white light, loops and rim as in H-α. The disc is centred with radius `R = min(0.46·P, 0.36·W)` px on `Pixels` (P = 2h), with solar north up and east left. The viewer is fixed in inertial space, so rates are sidereal (from Earth the synodic rate is 0.986 °/day slower). B0, the heliographic latitude of the disc centre, is seeded in [−7.25°, 7.25°]. Each disc pixel gets an observer-frame unit normal n = (dx/R, −dy/R, √(1 − ·)) with μ = n_z, tabulated per resize. *Limb darkening*: `I(μ) = 1 − u(1 − μ)` with u = 0.6 (linear law, visible continuum). *Differential rotation*: `ω(φ) = A + B sin²φ + C sin⁴φ` with A = 14.713, B = −2.396, C = −1.787 °/day sidereal (Snodgrass & Ulrich 1990). Time runs at 0.3 day per simulated second, so the equator turns in 81.6 s and latitude 60° in 100.7 s. Every surface feature advances its longitude by exactly `ω(φ)·0.3·dt` (closed form). *Granulation*: multiplicatively weighted Worley cells from `N_g = clamp(round(4πR²/s_g²), 64, 2400)` feature points on the sphere. The spacing at disc centre is `s_g = clamp(0.085 R, 3.6, 5)` px (3.91 px at R = 46, ≈ 45× the real ≈ 1.3 Mm spacing, which would be sub-pixel), so a cell spans 2–3 terminal cells and lanes read as polygons rather than a blocky maze. Each point carries (φ, λ), a lifetime of 6–10 s and a weight w that fades in and out over 1.5 s by smoothstep; a dead point respawns uniformly on the sphere. Per frame, points with μ > 0 are projected and counting-sorted into a screen bucket grid of cell size s_g. Each disc pixel takes F1 and F2 from the sites within s_g (its 3×3 buckets), widening the query to 1.5 s_g for the few pixels whose F2 reaches s_g, with squared distances scaled by m² where `m = 1 + 1.5(1 − w)`. Newborn cells therefore grow from a point and dying ones shrink, with no popping. The pattern is `g = 0.55·smoothstep(0, 0.3 s_g, F2 − F1) + 0.45·w₁·(1 − min(F1/0.9 s_g, 1))²`, giving bright granule cores and dark intergranular lanes. It is applied as `I *= 1 + (G − 1)·gate` with the granulation factor `G = 1 + C(μ)(2g − 1)`, `C = 0.12·√μ·smoothstep(0.1, 0.2, μ)`, and `gate` the product of the spots' photosphere weights (0 inside a spot, 1 outside, see *Spots*): convection is suppressed in umbrae and penumbrae, so a spot is a clean dark ring on the mottle rather than a dot on tiles. The real rms contrast is ≈ 0.13 at 500 nm and falls toward the limb; 0.12 keeps granule cores below the ramp's clip, so the mottle is a fine texture under the spots and loops, and the smoothstep avoids a hard ring. Worley is skipped for μ < 0.1. Granules evolve on display time, because real 5–10 min lifetimes would flicker at 0.3 d/s, but they rotate with the surface, so the texture itself shows the sphere turning and shearing. *Active regions* (3): mean latitude seeded in 10–25° (Spörer), ±5° per region, either hemisphere, ~120° apart in longitude. The first region starts at −78° (just inside the east limb) with its tallest loop at h = 0.32 R, so loops show at t = 0. Each region is a bipolar pair tilted by Joy's law, γ = 0.5|φ|, with the leading spot equatorward and the spots 2δ = 13–15° apart, so the pair's blended penumbrae (out to 1.07 ρ_p each) never touch: no δ-spots, which are a few % of real groups. Penumbral radius ρ_p is 4–6° for the leading spot and 2.5–4° for the following one, which is absent 40 % of the time. The region rotates rigidly at ω(φ_AR). *Spots*: angular distance from a spot centre is β ≈ √(2(1 − n·s)) (chord, no trig), with q = β/ρ_p. The umbra is 0.22 for q ≤ 0.375 and blends by smoothstep to the penumbra over q ∈ [0.375, 0.525]. Its radius of 0.45 ρ_p gives U/(U+P) ≈ 0.20 and U/P ≈ 0.25, as observed. The penumbra is `0.70 + 0.08 cos(nψ + ψ₀)` (a little darker than the observed ≈ 0.75–0.85, so that with the granulation gated out the ring stands about one ramp step below the intergranular lanes, 0.88, at 80×24) with `n = clamp(round(2π ρ_px / 3), 6, 24)` radial filaments (one per 3 px of penumbral circumference, so they never alias below a pixel; real penumbrae hold hundreds) and blends to 1 over q ∈ [0.93, 1.07]. The umbra is displaced toward disc centre by 0.10 ρ_p sin θ in the sky plane: the Wilson depression, ≈ 2.5× the real d/ρ_p ≈ 0.04 (≈ 10× in absolute depth, since spots are drawn 2–3× oversize).
