Lifted onto the sphere it grows as 0.10 tan θ, so the centre-side penumbra narrows toward the limb and is covered below μ ≈ 0.2 (≈ 0.08 at the real depth). Faculae brighten outside the spots as `I *= 1 + 0.35(1 − μ)·exp(−(β/3ρ_p)²)`, which shows only near the limb, as observed. Each spot acts only within its own facular cap `cos β ≥ cos 6.5 ρ_p` (where the Gaussian is < 1 %), and faculae never brighten a spot pixel, whatever the spot order. The only per-pixel trig is ψ inside penumbrae. Small discs floor the drawn spot size: each spot is drawn at `ρ_eff = max(ρ_p, min(2.0 px/R, sep/2.2))`, with sep the pair's angular separation, so at 80×24 (R ≈ 22 px, where a 2.5° following spot is ≈ 1 px in penumbral radius and its umbra under half a pixel) every spot is ≥ 2 px with an umbra ≥ 0.9 px and reads as umbra and penumbra. A floored pair's penumbrae still never touch: at 80×24 2δ ≥ 13° > 1.07·(6° + 5.2°), and on smaller discs, where both spots may reach sep/2.2, 2·1.07/2.2 < 1. At 200×50 the floor (2.49°) is under every ρ_p, so no spot is enlarged. This is a display exaggeration like the granule spacing, and it applies to the umbra, filaments, Wilson shift and faculae alike. *Loops*: each region has 3 nested field lines of a horizontal point dipole buried at depth `d_b = 2δ` (in R) below its midpoint, so B_z peaks under the spots. A field line is `p(θ') = c_d + L sin²θ'(cos θ' â + sin θ' ẑ)` with `L = d_b + h`; the apex heights are `h_max·{1, 0.72, 0.48}` (floored at 0.04 R) with `h_max` = 0.20–0.32 R (first region 0.32 R): 139–223 Mm, ≈ 1–1.5× the tallest observed cool H-α and coronal-rain loops, a stated display exaggeration like the granules and the Wilson shift, so the arcade is the focal point at 80×24 (≈ 7 px) and still fits the empty columns beside the disc. The three lines lie in one vertical plane through the region midpoint, turned 45–65° (either sense) from the spot axis: a sheared arcade over the inversion line. That is a display compromise, less sheared than real filament channels (≳ 70°), so the plane stays inclined to the line of sight at either limb and the loops show as nested arches rather than edge-on spikes. Loops with an apex below 2.5 px are not drawn, and on discs with R < 30 px (80×24 is 22) the middle arch is dropped so the outer and inner arches keep a black gap. Footpoints (|p| = R) are found by bisection at spawn. The 56 samples between them are stored in region-local coordinates and rotated each frame through an orthographic `geom::Camera`. Each loop carries 2 knots of coronal rain (Gaussian width 0.05 in σ) on display time (like the granules). A knot condenses within 0.1 σ of the apex and falls down one leg, its distance `a = |σ − ½|` from the apex growing as `ȧ = (0.3 + 1.4a)/T` (T = 5–9 s; closed form `rain_fall`, exact over any step split): slow near the top, accelerating down the leg. It fades in after condensing and out before the footpoint, over 0.05 σ, then condenses again. A sample is *Emission* if its projected radius is ≥ R, and *Filament* if it lies inside the disc with z ≥ 0. Otherwise the sample is *Hidden*. A Filament sample standing higher than 0.35 of its loop's apex darkens its pixel idempotently to `min(level, seq(0.12 + 0.78·0.6·I_disc))`, except inside a spot (photosphere weight < ½), so the thread bridges a group's spots without slashing across their penumbrae: the low legs would trace a ring around the spots, while the elevated arches overlap as one short dark thread along the loop plane, still well above the umbrae (0.22). Cool H-α loops are thus bright against the sky and absorb against the disc. A region that has reached +150° and is fully hidden (all spots μ < 0, no Emission or Filament sample) respawns 0–60° further on with new parameters, so nothing pops on the visible disc. *Chromosphere*: a 1 px rim at R < r ≤ R + 0.8, plus spicules of height `s_h·max(0, ½sin(n₁ϑ + 1.3t + φ₁) + ½sin(n₂ϑ − 0.9t + φ₂))` px with `s_h = min(0.035 R, 1.6)` (real spicules stand ≈ 0.014 R☉; fixed in pixels they would break a small disc's limb into stray pixels and notches). Here n₁ = round(2πR/3) and n₂ = round(2πR/2) are whole numbers of lobes (so the pattern closes around the limb), and ϑ is the limb angle.
- **Motion**: granules boil with 6–10 s lives while the surface turns at ≈ 3.5 px/s at disc centre (200×50). Features foreshorten toward the limb, so the sphere reads in 3-D. An equatorial spot crosses the disc in ≈ 41 s while high-latitude features visibly lag. Loops rise over the east limb, cross the disc as dark filaments and set over the west limb. Rain knots condense near the loop tops and fall, accelerating, down one leg, and spicules flicker. The label's day counter ticks 0.3 per second. For GEN-R9, rim, spicule and knot pixels change half-block occupancy within 1 s; interior colour changes alone do not change `to_lines` in Unicode mode.
- **Resize**: there is no simulation lattice. Heliographic state survives any resize untouched: regions, knots, granule (φ, λ, age), clock and B0. Resize rebuilds only the view: disc normals, the rim annulus list (pixel, r, ϑ), the bucket grid, the spicule lobe counts and `Pixels`. The granule pool changes only when N_g moves by > 10 % (hysteresis). It is then truncated, or topped up with points that fade in (capacity 2400 is reserved at build). For R < 4 px, granulation and loops are skipped, and nothing panics at 1×1.
- **ASCII**: the `Pixels::blit` closure averages the lit `Seq` levels of the pair. On the disc (cell centre inside R) the glyph is `#` for ≥ 0.92, `*` for ≥ 0.84, `+` for ≥ 0.72, `=` for ≥ 0.58, `:` for ≥ 0.46, `.` for ≥ 0.36, and otherwise a written space. Granules read as `#*` mottling, the limb as `:.`, a spot as a `=` ring around a blank umbra, and filaments one step darker. Off the disc the cell takes the brighter pixel: a knot core (≥ 0.60 + 0.9·0.28 ≈ 0.85) is `o`; a cell holding a pixel within 1 px of the limb is the one-cell rim `'`; a loop (≥ the rim/loop midpoint 0.575) is `.`; anything dimmer, including spicule-only cells, stays blank so loops stand clear of the rim. Empty cells stay untouched.
- **Label**: `day 12.4  Teff 5772 K` (`Text` at `title::READOUT_LEVEL`, CORE-R76), right-aligned on the last row (GEN-R13). The elapsed days (0.3·t, one decimal) are written into a stack buffer, and 5772 K is the IAU nominal effective temperature. The Teff part is dropped first when it would reach the title (`title::readout_room`), then the whole label; there is none when w < 30 or h < 10.
- **Perf** at 200×50: update ≈ 0.37 ms. Ages and longitudes cost ≈ 0.02 ms (1739 granules, ω precomputed per point; respawns; 3 regions and 18 knots); the rest is the rasterisation, which runs in `update`: projecting and bucketing ~870 visible granules ≈ 0.05 ms; disc ≈ 6.6k px × ~12 weighted-distance candidates ≈ 0.25 ms; spots and faculae ≈ 0.03 ms (3 dot products per pixel with early reject); loops (504 samples) and rim ≈ 0.02 ms. Render (blit and label) ≈ 0.03 ms. Total ≈ 0.4 ms (measured 0.42 ms in criterion at load ≈ 14, 2026-09-27), bounded at any size by the 2400-point cap, with no allocation after resize.
- **Inks** *(changed)*:
  - White-light photosphere, spots and faculae: `Ramp(Ember)` at `v = 0.08 + 0.74·I^1.25`. Granules are
    gold (v ≈ 0.91), intergranular lanes ≈ 20 % darker, the limb orange-red, penumbrae scarlet and umbrae
    oxblood (v < 0.2). **False colour**: the real disc is near-white. The power spreads the ramp's
    crowded pale end so the granulation keeps its contrast.
  - Every H-α feature (rim, spicules, loops, rain knots) in `Ramp(HAlpha)` with the same values.
  - Filament darkening stays photospheric (`Ember`). Only the arcade's tallest loop
    absorbs, so the filament is a 1 px thread; the nested arches under it thickened it into a bar.
- **Arcade twist** *(new)*: each field line is bowed out of the arcade's vertical plane by
  `0.1·half·sin(π·a/half)` (`a` along the arcade, `half` its half-span): one leg one way, the other the
  other. Seen from above, the arcade and its filament form a gentle S, as sigmoidal filament channels
  do, instead of a straight rod. **Stylised**: the twist is a fixed shape, not a flux-rope model.
- **Rim** *(changed)*: the rim's level follows the spicule wave, `0.55·(0.9 + 0.1·wave)`; a rim pixel
  is dropped where the wave is below −0.35 (under 120 columns) or −0.6 (from 120 columns), so
  the chromosphere reads as travelling fuzz rather than a drawn outline at every size.
  - ASCII glyphs are chosen by the pair's mean intensity, with the old thresholds.
- **Spectrograph inset** *(new)*:
  - **Slit**: the pixel column through the disc centre. Marked only by jaws, a thin dark
    hairline (`│`, `Dim` 0, over the photosphere's colour; Unicode only) in the first two cells inside
    the limb at the top and at the bottom, like the notches of a slit-jaw image; a hairline across the
    whole disc read as a render seam. (The disc fills the height at 200×50, so the jaws cannot sit
    outside it.)
  - **Axes**: 400–700 nm across, blue left, red right; one cell row per slit position, from the disc's
    top to its bottom.
  - **Colour**: every cell's background is `Phase` at `0.78·(700 − λ)/300` turns, a continuous band from
    red at 700 nm to violet at 400 nm. **False colour**: `Phase` is the only hue loop, and it is
    isoluminant, so it carries no intensity.
  - **Lines**: G 430.8, H-γ 434.0, H-β 486.1, Mg b 518.4, Fe E 527.0, Na D 589.3 and H-α 656.3 nm, each a
    thin dark glyph (`Dim` 0) on its nearest column; G and H-γ share one at this resolution. **Widths
    exaggerated**: a column is ≈ 7.5 nm at 40 cells, some 20–70× the real line widths.
  - **Doppler**: where the granulation under the slit is > 2 % brighter than its limb-darkened mean
    (rising gas) the lines shift blueward (`▏`), where > 2 % darker (sinking) redward (`▕`), else `│`.
    A shifted row keeps its shift until the excess falls inside ±1 % (hysteresis), so a
    row near the threshold does not flicker between glyphs.
    **Stylised**: a real granular shift is ~10⁻³ nm, far below one column.
  - **Umbra**: a slit row whose intensity is < 0.45 is a dark stripe (`▓`, about a quarter of the
    continuum) with heavy lines (`┃`). Rows off the disc stay empty.
  - **Size and placement**: `clamp(w/5, 24, 40)` × `clamp(h/6, 4, 8)` cells at the top right, from cell
    row 1 with a one-cell right margin; kept only if every pixel of it is ≥ 1.35 R from the disc centre
    (clearing the loops, ≤ 1.32 R) and it ends ≥ 2 px above the last row. In practice it shows at 200×50
    and wider.
  - **Label**: ` slit spectrum 400-700nm, false colour ` on row 0 (` slit spectrum 400-700nm ` if the
    inset is too narrow), at `READOUT_LEVEL`.
  - **ASCII**: continuum `=`, lines `|`, umbra `-`; no jaws.
- **Acceptance**: `SUN-A1` `limb_intensity(μ)` is 1 at μ = 1, 0.4 at μ = 0 and 0.7 at μ = 0.5, and strictly increasing; in a 200×50 render at t = 0 with regions off (test hook), the mean `Seq` level of disc pixels with μ > 0.8 exceeds that of pixels with μ < 0.3 by ≥ 60 levels (≈ 84 expected); `SUN-A2` `omega_deg_per_day(φ)` is 14.713 at 0°, 14.002 ± 0.001 at ±30° and 11.911 ± 0.001 at ±60°, even in φ and strictly decreasing in |φ|; the equatorial period is 81.56 ± 0.01 s at 0.3 d/s; over 10 s of updates split three ways (40 × 0.25, 600 × 1/60, mixed), a region and a granule that do not respawn in the window each advance by ω(φ)·3 days within 1e-9°; `SUN-A3` `spot_intensity(q, ψ)` is 0.22 for q ≤ 0.375, has azimuthal mean 0.70 ± 0.005 on [0.525, 0.93] with |modulation| ≤ 0.08, and is 1 for q ≥ 1.07; its azimuthal mean is non-decreasing in q, crosses the umbra–penumbra midpoint 0.46 at q = 0.45 ± 0.005 (U/(U+P) = 0.2025) and the penumbra–photosphere midpoint 0.85 at q = 1.00 ± 0.005; `wilson_offset(ρ_p, θ)` points toward disc centre with magnitude 0.10 ρ_p sin θ (1e-9); the filament count is `clamp(round(2π ρ_px/3), 6, 24)` and the azimuthal mean stays 0.70 ± 0.005 for every count in [6, 24], and a rendered 200×50 spot's filaments stand ≥ 2 px apart at mid-penumbra unless held at 6; faculae never brighten a spot pixel (and the result is independent of spot order), and a spot changes no pixel outside its facular cap `cos(6.5 ρ_p)`; a pixel within q ≤ 0.9 of a spot shades identically under any granulation factor; over seeds 0–199 at 40×12, 80×24 and 200×50, every shown spot is drawn at its own `ρ_eff` (above), which at 200×50 is its own ρ_p and at 80×24 gives an umbra of ≥ 0.85 px radius, and every pair with both spots present is strictly more than 1.07(ρ_eff,lead + ρ_eff,follow) apart; `SUN-A4` every loop sample satisfies `|p − c_d| = L sin²θ'` (relative 1e-9) in its vertical plane; both footpoints lie on the photosphere (||p| − R| < 1e-6 R) and every interior sample lies above it; a field line's apex height is L − d_b (1e-9 R); the two footpoints lie on opposite sides of the region midpoint along â; each region's 3 loops lie in one vertical plane through the region midpoint (1e-9), turned 45–65° from the spot axis, with strictly decreasing apex heights and footpoint spans; at 80×24 the first region's tallest apex is ≥ 7 px, every drawn loop clears 2.5 px, the middle arch is dropped and the outer and inner arches stay ≥ 2 px apart, and loop pixels clear of the limb lie in [rim + 0.03, 0.60 + 0.28 + 0.01] with the loop body under the penumbra; in ASCII at 80×24 off-disc cells are only `'` (on the one-cell rim), `.`, `o` or blank, with ≥ 8 loop cells clear of the rim; rain: `rain_fall` composes over split steps (1e-12) and moves a knot > 2.5× faster at a = 0.4 than at the apex, a condensing knot starts within 0.1 σ of the apex at weight 0, and a falling knot moves monotonically toward its footpoint, where its weight is 0; `SUN-A5` `classify(p)` returns Emission iff the projected radius is ≥ R, Filament iff the point is inside the disc with z ≥ 0, and Hidden otherwise; in a 200×50 render with loops on versus off (test hook), off-disc pixels differ only within 1 px of a projected Emission sample, and no on-disc pixel is brighter with loops on; over 80 s at 200×50 (seeds 11 and 42), every on-disc pixel darkened by loops lies within 1 px of a Filament sample standing higher than 0.35 of its loop's apex and outside every spot (photosphere weight ≥ ½), and some filament crosses the disc; `SUN-A6` over 2000 seeded spawns the two spots of a region are 13–15° apart and more than 1.07(ρ_lead + ρ_follow) apart; at 80×24 over 1200 × `update(0.25)` with no renders (< 2 s in debug), exactly 3 regions exist at every step, all with |latitude| in [5°, 30°]; at t = 0 the first region is at −78° with ≥ 1 Emission sample east of the disc; every respawn happens at relative longitude ≥ 150° with all spots μ < 0 and no Emission or Filament sample; `SUN-A7` after warm-up at 200×50 (R = 46 px) `granule_spacing` is 3.6 px at R = 10, 3.91 at R = 46 and 5 at R = 200, and the pool holds exactly N_g = 1739 points, never grows, and every lifetime is in [6, 10] s; with regions off, the rms of I/limb_intensity(μ) about its mean (from `Seq` levels mapped back to I) is in [0.06, 0.18] for μ > 0.8 and < 0.6× that value for 0.15 < μ < 0.3; `granule_pattern` is < 0.26 whenever F2 − F1 ≤ 0.1 s_g and F1 ≥ 0.45 s_g (w₁ ≤ 1), and ≥ 0.55 whenever F2 − F1 ≥ 0.3 s_g; the bucketed Worley query returns exact F1/F2 within its 1.5 s_g reach (a site two buckets over is found) and F2 = the reach for a lone site. *M12*: `SUN-A1..A7`: unchanged (the SUN-A4 loop-body bound now reads "below the quiet disc's lanes, intensity 0.8", since the photosphere mapping changed), except:; `SUN-A4` *(changed)*: the arcade test reads "every sample lies within 1.05·0.1·half of the vertical plane through the region centre, its offset the negative of its mirror sample's (an S), and each loop bows by more than half the twist"; `SUN-A8` *(changed)*: no inset at 80×24, one at 200×50; every inset pixel ≥ 1.35 R from the centre and the inset above row h − 2; each line's nearest column is a line column (6 or 7 in all); on the middle row every cell has a `Phase` background and exactly the line columns carry a thin dark line; red end < 0.1 turn, violet end > 0.7 turn; an umbra row (I = 0.2) renders `▓`/`┃` while a rising row below it renders `▏` lines; an off-disc row is empty; the slit's column carries exactly four jaw cells, `│` in `Dim` 0 over `Ember`, two consecutive at each end, the first and last just inside the limb. `doppler_shift` sets a shift beyond ±2 %, holds it down to ±1 %, flips it past the opposite threshold, and changes the glyph once, not every frame, under a ±0.4 % wobble about +2 %; `SUN-A9` *(new)*: `photosphere_value(1.1) − photosphere_value(0.9)` ≥ 15 % of the former; the limb (I = 0.45) lies in [0.25, 0.45) and an umbra (I = 0.22) below 0.2; `photosphere_intensity` inverts it.

### strange_attractors  (Forces & Waves, M10, M12)
- **Intent**: a slowly turning gallery of six classic chaotic flows: Lorenz's butterfly, Aizawa's shell, Thomas's labyrinth, Chen's two wings (Chen & Ueta 1999), Halvorsen's propeller and Rössler's band. Each attractor threads in its own false-colour palette, so the gallery changes colour as it changes shape. Each is drawn by a swarm of particles leaving short glowing threads on the braille lattice, so the flow itself builds the shape instead of a plotted curve. Every 12 s the swarm flows into the next attractor, with no cut and no fade to black. Each attractor is framed to its own projected extent, so it fills about 0.88 of the height (or of the width, if that is tighter). Each is also seen from its own best elevation: Halvorsen from above its 3-fold axis, so the three blades read, with the frame following the blades so the figure stays centred as they turn. Threads are continuous at every size and the particle count grows only linearly with the lattice, so 200×50 gains resolution but not clutter. The eye goes first to the bright dense cores (the Lorenz wing eyes, the Aizawa tube), then follows the threads outward. A single corner label is the only text.
- **Model**: model time in *tu*. Gallery: Lorenz (1963) `ẋ = σ(y−x), ẏ = x(ρ−z) − y, ż = xy − βz`, σ = 10, ρ = 28, β = 8/3. Aizawa (1982, Langford-type torus flow) `ẋ = (z−b)x − dy, ẏ = dx + (z−b)y, ż = c + az − z³/3 − (x²+y²)(1+ez) + fzx³`, (a,b,c,d,e,f) = (0.95, 0.7, 0.6, 3.5, 0.25, 0.1). Thomas (1999) `ẋ = sin y − bx` (cyclic), b = 0.18 (λ₁ ≈ 0.038). The popular b = 0.19 is a limit cycle, and at the often-quoted b = 0.1998 and 0.208186 orbits stay on one of several coexisting off-centre attractors. Halvorsen (Sprott 2003) `ẋ = −ax − 4y − 4z − y²` (cyclic), a = 1.27 (λ₁ ≈ 0.82); the popular a = 1.89 is periodic (λ₁ ≈ 0). Rössler (1976) `ẋ = −y − z, ẏ = x + ay, ż = b + z(x − c)`, a = b = 0.2, c = 5.7 (λ₁ ≈ 0.071). Starts are fixed in-basin constants, never random: Lorenz (1, 1, 1), Aizawa (0.1, 0, 0), Thomas (0.1, 0, 0), Halvorsen (−1, 0, 0), Rössler (1, 1, 0). Aizawa has a coexisting non-chaotic attractor: from (0.2, 0.1, 0.5), λ₁ ≈ 0 and the z extent is only 82 % of the chaotic one. Each particle is advanced in f64 with `ode::integrate` (RK4) at `h_max` = 0.01 / 0.02 / 0.1 / 0.005 / 0.02 tu, at a model rate `r_k` = 0.33 / 0.69 / 6.5 / 0.31 / 1.6 tu per simulated second. These rates are measured so the mean normalised speed is 1.2 box half-widths/s for every attractor (the peak is ≈ 9/s in Rössler's fold). That is ≤ 2 substeps per particle per 1/60 s. Normalisation: `x̃ = Q_k(x − c_k)/s_k`, where `c_k` is the reference box centre and `s_k` its largest half-extent: Lorenz (0, 0, 24.5)/26.0, Aizawa (0, 0, 0.74)/1.49, Thomas 0/4.2, Halvorsen −3.54·(1,1,1)/9.8, Rössler (1.17, −1.47, 11.36)/11.35. World position `p = x̃/ρ_k` with `ρ_k = max|x̃|` = 1.24 / 1.02 / 1.13 / 1.32 / 1.38, so `|p| ≤ 1` and the perspective stays bounded. `Q_k` maps native z (Lorenz, Aizawa, Rössler) or the (1,1,1) axis (Thomas, Halvorsen) onto the camera's up axis +y, so yaw spins the last two about their 3-fold axis. Ensemble: N = 1024 particles per attractor, sampled from one scout trajectory. The scout starts at the fixed start plus a seeded 10⁻³ Gaussian jitter, burns in for 40 s·r_k, and takes a sample every 0.15 s·r_k. Samples are stored at bit-reversed indices, so every power-of-two prefix is spread along the scout. Two slots hold the current and next ensemble. The next slot is filled during the first 2 s of each hold (≤ 400 scout steps per update, with any remainder finished at the morph boundary); a scout that escapes restarts from the fixed start offset by 10⁻³·i on every axis at its i-th restart, burn-in included, so no transient sample is kept; after 8 restarts it fills the rest of its slot by repeating the samples it has (the start if none), so filling always terminates (unreachable with today's in-basin starts, but `run` is called with an unbounded budget). Morph: at morph start each prefix is put in pairing order: 8 equal-count azimuth sectors about the vertical axis, each cut into 4 equal-count height bands, each band ordered by cylindrical radius (in place with `sort_unstable_by`, ties broken by index). Equal counts keep sector j of one attractor paired with sector j of the other however unevenly their mass is spread (Lorenz's wings). The next slot's prefix is then permuted onto the current slot's indices, so the points on screen are never re-indexed, and point i pairs a_i ↔ b_i. Both slots integrate during the morph, so the pairs decorrelate within about a second, and a plain lerp of two uncorrelated clouds is narrower (its spread falls to `√((1−w)² + w²)` ≈ 0.71 at w = ½): the swarm knotted to 0.65–0.83 of its height mid-morph. So the displayed point is `p_i,j = m_j + g_j·(lerp(a_i,j, b_i,j, w) − m_j)` per world axis j, with `w = smoothstep(s)`, `m_j = lerp(mean a_j, mean b_j, w)`, and `g_j` clamped to [1, √2] so that the blend's spread `(⟨|p_j − m_j|^16⟩)^{1/16}` equals the lerp of the two ensembles' spreads. The factor is measured after every morph step (O(N_eff), allocation-free), and the endpoints w = 0 and 1 are exact. A variance match (power 2) overshot: the extremes decorrelate last (pairs at the top of both stay there while the bulk mixes) and a blend has thinner tails than the shapes it averages, so Aizawa → Thomas spilled past the frame (80×24: 5.6 edge-row cells per morph frame, against 0.07 in holds, and 0.7 at power 16). One gain for all axes turns uncorrelated extremes into corners the shapes never have (4.8), and an order statistic jumped 0.3 world units in one update when a fast particle overtook. At s = 1 the next slot becomes current. Blow-up guard: a particle that is non-finite or has `|x̃|∞ > 3` is replaced by a clone of particle `(i + N_eff/2) mod N_eff` of the simulated prefix, or, if that has escaped too, of the next finite particle (the fixed start only if all have escaped, since it lies off the attractor, e.g. at Lorenz's origin saddle), plus a seeded 10⁻⁶·s_k jitter, so the clone separates by chaos instead of shadowing its source forever. Camera: `geom::Camera` with yaw `2πt/36 s`, pitch `p_k + 0.10 sin(2πt/29 s)` with `p_k` = 0.20 / 0.48 / 0.55 / 1.35 / 0.50 (Halvorsen from nearly above its 3-fold axis, so the three blades read; Aizawa, the seed-42 opening, from far enough above that its shell's loops open into rings around the tube instead of a tangle, which at 0.35 made it the least legible member at 80×24), and distance 4.5. Framing uses each attractor's 99.5th-percentile projected box over the camera loop (yaw every 5°, pitch p_k ± 0.10), perspective included, in world units with screen y down: `Frame_k = (x, top, bottom, swing)` with `|x|/ASPECT ≤ x` and `top ≤ y − swing·cos 3ψ ≤ bottom` at camera yaw ψ: Lorenz (0.90, −0.66, 0.66, 0), Aizawa (1.01, −0.82, 0.94, 0), Thomas (0.87, −1.01, 0.98, 0), Halvorsen (0.94, −0.80, 0.92, 0.12), Rössler (0.65, −0.74, 1.02, 0). The swing follows Halvorsen's blades: seen from above they turn like a triangle, and every 120° of yaw a tip points up, then down, swinging the box midpoint by ±0.17 half-heights while the centroid stays put. A fixed centre left 4 blank rows on one side and none on the other within one hold at 80×24 (9 of 49 at 200×50), and following it also narrows the frame from 1.89 to 1.72. The others swing ≤ 0.06 half-heights with no 3-fold harmonic. The stage is the rows above the label (all rows when it is hidden). The fit is `scale_k = min(0.88·rows/(bottom − top), 0.92·w/(2·ASPECT·x))` rows per world unit, and the frame's midpoint `(top + bottom)/2 + swing·cos 3ψ` sits on the stage's middle row, `rows/2`. Rare excursions beyond the 99.5 % box may be clipped at the frame edge. During a morph `p_k` and the frame midpoint are lerped by w and `scale_k` log-lerped. The horizontal centre is `w/2`, and dot coordinates are cell coordinates × (2, 4). Density: `Grid<f32>` at `Dots::dot_size()`. Each update multiplies it by `e^{−dt/τ}` (τ = 0.1 s), then deposits `w = (0.6 + 0.4·near)·(1 − 0.2·sin πw_morph)` (near = (depth + 1)/2; the second factor dims a morph lightly at its middle, where the lerped velocity already shortens and fades the blended threads) on every dot of the segment from each particle's previous projected dot to its current one (`geom::line`, start dot excluded). A segment longer than 16 dots deposits only its end dot; this happens after a morph start, a blow-up replacement, a resize or a large dt. A dot is lit when `D ≥ D_min = 0.08`, so threads are ≈ 0.25 s of flow long and unbroken at any size. Because thread length in dots grows with the lattice, the prefix grows only linearly: `N_eff = clamp(pow2_floor(6·min(h, w/2)), 64, 1024)`, which is 128 at 80×24 and 256 at 200×50 and keeps coverage near 30 % of the silhouette. Chaos reference: Lorenz λ₁ = 0.9056 (Viswanath 1998; Sprott 2003), with Σλ = −(σ + 1 + β) = −41/3.
- **Model (Chen, M12)**: `ẋ = a(y − x)`, `ẏ = (c − a)x − xz + cy`, `ż = xy − bz`, with a = 35, b = 3, c = 28; it is odd in (x, y), and its divergence is `c − a − b = −10`. Start (−10, 0, 37); `h_max` 0.004 tu; rate 0.229 tu/s (mean normalised speed 1.20 half-widths/s). Normalisation: centre (0, 0, 28.5), half 31.5, using the median scout box of seeds 0–7, (1.45, 1.63, 1.13), because a long orbit's box is set by rare outer loops. ρ 1.22; `Up::Z`; pitch 0.20. Frame (0.86, −0.49, 0.60, 0), measured by the ATT-A4 procedure; Chen alone is drawn at `zoom` 0.85 of its fitted frame, since its rarest outer loops reach 1.38× the frame. λ₁ = 2.06 from the fixed start (h 0.004, 50 tu burn-in, T = 400); the literature value is ≈ 2.03.
- **Motion**: particles stream at ≈ 1.2 half-widths/s and the camera turns 10°/s, so the frame changes from the first step (GEN-R9). The Timeline is `hold` 8 s plus `morph` 4 s per attractor, in the order Lorenz → Aizawa → Thomas → Chen → Halvorsen → Rössler, a 72 s loop. The camera reads the gallery's one clock, which starts at `12 s × first`, so each member's hold is seen from the same yaws whatever the seeded first member. The yaw is `2π·t/36 s + offset`, the offset lerped across a morph: 240° by default and 268° for the two-wing flows, Lorenz and Chen. 240° is two thirds of a turn: for the 3-fold flows (Thomas, Halvorsen) the views are unchanged. Holds start every 120° of yaw, so Aizawa's hold sweeps 0°–80° (it is nearly axisymmetric about its up axis, so only the tube's side changes) and Rössler's 120°–200° (the disc seen obliquely with the fold spike rising off its near side). Lorenz's and Chen's projected width peaks near 125°–130° of yaw (wings side by side) and drops to a third near 45° (a narrow V, one wing behind the other); an offset of 88° keeps the 80° a hold sweeps ≥ 70 % open. Both flows are odd in (x, y) about their up axis, so the view at ψ + 180° is the view at ψ, and 268° shows the same wings. 268° is chosen over 88° because it sits 28° from the default instead of 152°: a morph beside a wing flow changes the 10 °/s turn rate by at most about 1× (the rate stays within −0.5 to +20.5 °/s), where 88° whipped the camera at up to 67 °/s and turned it backwards at up to 47 °/s. The run starts at the beginning of a seeded attractor's hold, so every 15 s scene visit (the default `interval`) includes a morph. During a morph the cloud flows as one body, keeping its size: the old silhouette dissolves while the new one condenses, framing and elevation glide to the new attractor's, and the label switches at s = 0.5.
- **Resize**: no simulation state depends on the display (ADR-0012 needs no lattice here). Ensembles, timeline, time and camera phase are kept. A resize reallocates only the density grid and `Dots` (zeroed), recomputes centre, scale and `N_eff`, and forgets each particle's previous dot. It then rebuilds the threads by replaying each particle's flow backwards (the reversed field, integrated forwards, weight, stretch and camera held) over the lifetime of a unit deposit, `τ·ln(1/D_min)` ≈ 0.253 s = 16 updates of 1/60 s, depositing each replayed segment with the decay it would have had since. The first frame after a resize therefore already shows whole threads, at ≥ 0.85 of the following hold's mean lit cells (measured 0.93; seeds 0–23 ≥ 0.90). The reversed flow expands volumes, but over (16/60)·r_k tu (0.08–1.7 tu; stiff Lorenz only 0.09) it amplifies rounding by at most ≈ e^{|λ₃|·span} ≈ 10× (Rössler); a backward orbit that leaves the box stops its replay. A resize during a morph defers the `N_eff` change to the next hold, so the pairing stays intact. Zero-area sizes skip projection and plotting, and the fit is clamped to a positive minimum so the log-lerped scale never becomes NaN.
- **Inks**: with `u = ln(1 + D/D_min)/ln(1 + 4/D_min)` clamped to [0, 1], threads are `ink::ramp(palette, f + (0.95 − f)·u^1.6)` in the shown attractor's palette, which switches with the label at the morph's s = 0.5: Lorenz `Ramp(Ember)`, Aizawa `Ramp(Ice)`, Thomas `Ramp(Aurora)`, Chen `Ramp(HAlpha)`, Halvorsen `Ramp(Plasma)`, Rössler `Ramp(Ferroin)`. The floor lifts the sparsest strands into the hue (Chen's HAlpha reads crimson, not wine); the gamma keeps the bulk saturated, and only the densest cores approach the near-white top, so the figures glow at their cores without washing every palette to the same grey. The floor `f` is 0.26, and 0.34 for the deep crimson, violet and wine ramps (Hα, Plasma, Ferroin), whose sparse strands at equal OKLab lightness read darker on a black terminal than orange, cyan or green. A cell takes its brightest dot (CORE-R41). Label `Text` at `title::READOUT_LEVEL`. Named ramps are false colour.
- **ASCII**: `Dots` fallback by dot count (`.`, `:`, `+`, `#`). At 80×24 (Lorenz, t ≈ 1 s) the figure spans ≈ 30 columns × 18 rows: one wing face-on as nested `+`/`#` loops, the other nearly edge-on as a `#` blade, which turns into the classic two-lobed view as the camera swings. Halvorsen seen from above is a three-bladed `+#` triangle. Rössler is a `:` band with one `+` fold. The label uses its ASCII variant.
- **Label**: the attractor's name is `caption()` (GEN-R12), so the title reads `Strange attractor · Lorenz`. Only its parameter set is drawn, right-aligned on row h−1 (GEN-R13), static strings only (GEN-R11): `σ=10 ρ=28 β=8/3` (ASCII `s=10 r=28 b=8/3`), `b=0.18` (Thomas), `a=1.27` (Halvorsen), `a=.2 b=.2 c=5.7` (Rössler), `a=35 b=3 c=28` (Chen), and none for Aizawa: its six (41 characters) do not fit, and quoting two of six would read as its only parameters. Every set is restrained (≤ 16 characters), Unicode and ASCII variants have the same length, and a set is shown whole within `title::readout_room` or not at all. It is dropped when `w < 12` or `h < 8`; while the label can show, row h−1 takes no dots.
- **Stylised**: the palettes are false colour. Chen's rarest outer loops (≈ 1 % of points) can still leave the frame and are clipped at the edge while out.
- **Considered and not added** (M12): Dadras (a..e = 3, 2.7, 1.7, 2, 9; λ₁ 0.386): its wedge tips flow at 0.29 world units per 1/60 s, against the ATT-A6 continuity bound of 0.2, its silhouette spans only 0.62–0.69 of the stage rows (ATT-A8 needs 0.72), and scout radii vary 0.88–1.25 by seed. Sprott 2014 (a = 2.07, b = 1.79): λ₁ ≈ 0.008 over 600 tu, below the ATT-A2 bound of 0.015: weak chaos beside invariant tori, not an honest strange-attractor beat. Dequan Li with the quoted parameters is a limit cycle. A harmonograph act: a damped Lissajous figure is not a strange attractor, so the title would overclaim. A separate long-exposure glow layer: the camera turns 10°/s, so any exposure long enough to read as glow smears the figure, and the log-compressed thread density already renders dense cores brightest.
- **Perf**: estimated at 200×50 (N_eff = 256) for `update(1/60)` + `render`. Integration: 256 × ≤ 2 RK4 steps (×2 during a morph), ≈ 0.02 ms. Projection and segment deposits ≈ 0.02 ms. A fused decay, threshold and `Dots::set` pass over 80 k dots ≈ 0.08 ms. Scout ≤ 400 steps ≈ 0.01 ms. Blit of 10 k cells ≈ 0.03 ms. The morph stretch is three O(N_eff) passes over both slots per update. Total ≈ 0.2 ms (measured 0.24 ms per hold update and 0.27 ms per morph update at 200×50 on a loaded machine). The worst case is dt = 0.25 during a Thomas morph (17 substeps × 2 slots), ≈ 0.3 ms at N_eff 256 and ≈ 1 ms at N_eff 1024 (terminals ≥ 342×171). The first resize fills the current slot synchronously (≤ 13 k RK4 steps). Allocation-free after warm-up: particle, previous-dot, pairing and stretch buffers are sized at build, the density grid and `Dots` in resize, the Timeline in the first resize, and sorting uses `sort_unstable_by` (the stable sort allocates).
- **Acceptance**: `ATT-A1` the tangent-vector (Benettin 1980) estimate of Lorenz λ₁ (analytic Jacobian, RK4 h = 0.005, 20 tu burn-in, T = 400 tu, mean over the starts (1,1,1), (−3,2,20) and (5,−5,30)) lies in [0.86, 0.95] (reference 0.9056; measured 0.899–0.912); `ATT-A2` from its fixed start every gallery member has λ₁ > 0.015 (h = h_max,k except Lorenz 0.005; burn-in 50 tu, Rössler 100 and Thomas 200; T = 400 for Lorenz, Chen and Halvorsen, 600 for Aizawa, 1500 for Rössler and Thomas; measured 0.91 / 0.089 / 0.039 / 0.83 / 0.071, Chen 2.06), while the periodic controls Halvorsen a = 1.89 (T = 400) and Thomas b = 0.19 (T = 1500) from the same starts give |λ₁| < 0.005 (measured −0.003 to 0.002; ≈ 1 s in a debug build, the slowest test here); `ATT-A3` field fidelity: Lorenz vanishes at C± = (±√72, ±√72, 27) (1e-12); analytic Jacobians match central differences at 64 seeded points (1e-6 relative), and their traces equal the analytic divergences −41/3, 2(z−b) + a − z² − e(x²+y²) + fx³, −3b, −3a, a + x − c and Chen's c − a − b = −10; Lorenz is exactly odd in (x, y), and Thomas and Halvorsen are exactly cyclic, f(Cx) = C f(x); `ATT-A4` for seeds 0–7, each 1024-point scout ensemble has max|x̃|∞ ≤ 1.05, per-axis extents ≥ 0.9× the reference box (Lorenz 0.8×: a 1024 × 0.05 tu = 51 tu scout rarely reaches the outer spiral; minimum 0.825 over seeds 0–7, 0.806 over 0–63) (Lorenz (1.44, 1.95, 1.70), Aizawa (2.00, 2.00, 1.49), Thomas and Halvorsen 2.0 each, Rössler (1.81, 1.64, 2.00), Chen its median scout box (1.45, 1.63, 1.13)) and a max radius in [0.90, 1.03]·ρ_k (Chen [0.85, 1.03]: heavy tail, measured 0.86–1.03); as a negative control, Aizawa's inner attractor (from (0.2, 0.1, 0.5), h = 0.02, 100 tu burn-in, 500 tu) has a z extent in [0.7, 0.9)× the box (measured 1.226 = 0.82 × 1.49) while the seed-0 ensemble reaches ≥ 0.9×; the rates give a mean normalised speed |f|·r_k/s_k in [1.1, 1.3] half-widths/s for every attractor after the scout burn-in (100 tu, Thomas 1000; measured 1.20–1.22) and a Rössler peak in [8, 10] (measured 9.06); over the camera loop (yaw every 5°, pitch p_k ± 0.10 in 5 steps) the 99.5th-percentile projected box, with y taken about the swinging midpoint, lies within Frame_k, some pose of some seed reaches ≥ 0.97 of it on every side, and no point lies beyond 1.2× Frame_k (Chen 1.4×: measured 1.38, clipped at the edge while out); `ATT-A5` a 64-particle `Gallery` stepped over two full loops at dt = 0.25 keeps every displayed point finite with |p| ≤ 1.5, and an injected NaN particle is replaced within one update by a point within 10⁻⁵ of its source, or of the next finite particle when its source has escaped too, and a scout that escapes takes no sample before a fresh burn-in, while one whose every orbit escapes (a never-finite field) still returns from an unbounded `run` with a full slot of finite points; `ATT-A6` the Timeline order is Lorenz → Aizawa → Thomas → Chen → Halvorsen → Rössler with 8 s holds, 4 s morphs and a 72 s loop; for every start and display prefix (64, 256) the pairing matches the two ensembles rank for rank in (sector, band, radius) order, and the display equals slot A exactly at s = 0 and slot B exactly at s = 1; across a full loop at dt = 1/60 (64 particles, ≈ 1 s in debug) no displayed point moves more than 0.2 world units in one step (measured flow maximum ≈ 0.11 at Rössler's fold; a hard cut moves typical points ≈ 0.7), and at every morph step each axis's displayed spread is `lerp(σ_a, σ_b, w)` clamped to [1, √2]× the plain blend's (1e-9); `ATT-A7` the parameter counts are (3, 6, 1, 3, 1, 3) in gallery order; only the parameter set is drawn, right-aligned, pure ASCII in ASCII mode with equal-length variants, ≤ 16 characters, all or none; the name is `caption()`; Lorenz's set is dropped at 35 columns and shown at 36 (h = 12), and the label is absent when `w < 12` or `h < 8`; `ATT-A8` composition: rendered at 80×24 (seed 42, Lorenz hold, t = 1 s) the lit cells span ≥ 14 rows and ≥ 24 columns and none lies on row h−1; at 40×12 no dot lies on row h−1 over a loop; with the label hidden (80×7, 11×24) the figure is framed in all rows and reaches the last; at every size in {1×1, 3×2, 80×24, 200×50} over one loop at dt = 0.25 no plotted dot has a non-finite coordinate, and at 0×24, 80×0 and 0×0 the view's scale stays finite and positive and its centre finite; the resize replay spans exactly the 16 updates a unit deposit stays lit. Over one loop (4320 updates, six holds and six morphs) of the real scene at 80×24 (seed 42), with no dot on row h−1: frame 0 lights ≥ 0.85 of the first hold's mean lit cells; every hold's silhouette spans ≥ 0.72 of the stage rows and ≥ 0.8 on average; Aizawa lights ≤ 2× the other five's mean; at s = 0.5 each morph lights ≥ 0.55 of the smaller neighbouring hold's cells and spans ≥ 0.85 of its rows (a plain lerp gave 0.65–0.83; seeds 0–7 at 80×24 and 200×50 give ≥ 0.88, except Halvorsen → Rössler at some yaws, 0.75, where the camera swings from above the propeller to beside the band); and through the Halvorsen hold the blank rows above and below the figure differ by ≤ 2 (4 with a fixed centre); `ATT-A9` consecutive gallery members, and all six, have distinct palettes; at 80×24, 0.5 s into each hold, every drawn cell above the label row is in the shown attractor's palette; the lit threshold `D_min` maps to ≥ 0.3, `D_top/8` to ≤ 0.75, and `D_top` (and anything denser) to 0.95; zero density maps to 0.34 for Hα, Plasma and Ferroin and 0.26 for the others; `ATT-A10` from a 60 000-step RK4 orbit, the projected width of Lorenz and of Chen at each of 9 yaws across its hold is ≥ 0.7 of its widest over all yaws (the camera reads the gallery's one clock, so this holds whatever the seeded first member), and over a whole 72 s loop the camera's yaw rate stays within [−1, 25] °/s (base 10 °/s), so opening the wings never whips or reverses the camera at a morph.

### three_body  (Forces & Waves, M10, M12)
- **Intent**: three equal suns dancing some of the rare *periodic* solutions of the three-body problem: the figure-8, where all three chase each other along one curve; Lagrange's equilateral triangle spinning and breathing; Broucke's A1 and R4; and Šuvakov & Dmitrašinović's moth I. Each glowing head draws its own comet trail, exactly one period long, so the orbit is sketched once and then keeps refreshing. The loop is a small gallery of 250 years of celestial mechanics, and it is honest about chaos: each orbit restarts from its published initial conditions long before any instability can show.
- **Model**: planar Newtonian three-body problem, `G = m₁ = m₂ = m₃ = 1`, `r̈ᵢ = Σ_{j≠i} (rⱼ − rᵢ)/|rⱼ − rᵢ|³`, unsoftened. The symmetric (Newton 3) pair loop conserves `P` and `L` to rounding. KDK leapfrog (`ode::verlet_step`, CORE-R44) runs on a fixed per-orbit substep lattice `hᵢ = Tᵢ/(480·qᵢ)`, `qᵢ = ⌈4096·Tᵢ/480⌉` (q = 54, 48, 54, 47, 128, so `hᵢ ≤ 1/4096`), and one trail sample is pushed every `qᵢ` substeps. Each update takes `⌊τ·kᵢ/hᵢ⌋ − n_done` whole substeps, where `τ` is the time since the current `run` started, so the state is a pure function of the substep count and does not depend on the frame rate. `verlet_integrate` is not used because it chooses its own equal substeps. State is `pos, vel, acc: [f64; 6]`. The orbits (ICs COM-centred with `P = 0`) are below; `k` is model time units per simulated second and `n` is the number of periods shown:
  - **figure-8** (Moore 1993; Chenciner & Montgomery 2000; ICs by Simó): `r₁ = −r₂ = (0.97000436, −0.24308753)`, `r₃ = 0`, `v₃ = (−0.93240737, −0.86473146)`, `v₁ = v₂ = −v₃/2`, `T = 6.32591398`, `E = −1.287142`, `L = 0`; KAM-stable (Simó 2002); `k = 1`, `n = 3`.
  - **Lagrange** (1772), homographic elliptic variant with `e = 0.3`: the bodies start at apocentre at `90° + 120°·i` on the unit circle, with perpendicular velocity `v_a = √(μ(1−e))` and `μ = Gm/√3` (the net pull on each body is `μ/r²` toward the COM). Each body therefore follows a Kepler ellipse about the COM with `a = 1/1.3`, `r_p = 0.538462`, `T = 2π√(a³√3) = 5.578856`, `E = −1.125833`, `L = 1.907172`. Routh's stability condition `27(m₁m₂ + m₂m₃ + m₃m₁) < (Σm)²` fails for equal masses (81 > 9), so the configuration is linearly unstable (Gascheau 1843, Routh 1875); in the circular case the growth rate is `ω/√2`, ×85 per period. `k = 1`, `n = 2`.
  - **Broucke A1** (Broucke 1975; Hénon 1976; ICs from the Šuvakov–Dmitrašinović gallery): `x = (−0.9892620043, 2.2096177241, −1.2203557197)`, `y = 0`, `v_x = 0`, `v_y = (1.9169244185, 0.1910268738, −2.1079512924)`, `T = 6.283247` (refined from the published 6.283213, see below), `E = −0.854131`, `L = 1.098206`; `k = 1`, `n = 2`.
  - **Broucke R4**: `x = (0.8733047091, −0.6254030288, −0.2479016803)`, `v_y = (1.0107764436, −1.6833533458, 0.6725769022)`, `T = 5.396075` (refined from the published 5.396), `E = −2.054282`, `L = 1.768757`; `k = 1`, `n = 2`.
  - **Refined periods**: for A1, R4 and moth I, `T` is the period that minimises the return error of the quoted ICs on the orbit's own leapfrog lattice (TB-A3), so each trail closes on itself. The tabulated periods are rounded, and they do not match the truncated ICs: at the published values the lattice return error is 1.1e-4 (A1), 2.5e-4 (R4) and 3.6e-4 (moth). For A1 and R4 that error is a phase lag. For moth I it is mostly leapfrog truncation at the 0.079 close approach, so refining `T` gains only ≈ 30 % (2.6e-4), while `h = 1/16384` with `T` at its `h → 0` minimum brings it to 2.3e-5. The `h → 0` return minima are 6.283186, 5.396072 and 14.894290. The lattice values differ from these by the leapfrog phase error, which is largest at the close approaches of A1 and moth.
  - **moth I** (Šuvakov & Dmitrašinović 2013, PRL 110 114301, I.B.1): `r₁ = (−1, 0)`, `r₂ = (1, 0)`, `r₃ = 0`, `v₁ = v₂ = (0.464445, 0.396060)`, `v₃ = −2v₁`, `T = 14.894089` (refined from the published 14.8939), `E = −1.382282`, `L = 0`, closest approach 0.079; `k = 1.5`, `n = 1`.
  - **Excluded**: butterfly I, yin-yang and goggles pass within 0.01–0.02 of a collision and would need a regularised integrator (at `h = 1/4096`, butterfly I loses 2 % of its energy per period).
- **Motion**:
  - A looping `Timeline` of five `run` phases (`nᵢTᵢ/kᵢ` s), each followed by a 1.2 s `fade`, totalling `Σ nᵢTᵢ/kᵢ + 6.0 ≈ 69.42 s`: fig-8 18.98 s, Lagrange 11.16 s, A1 12.57 s, R4 10.79 s, moth 9.93 s.
  - At every `run` start the state is reset bitwise to that orbit's ICs and the trails are cleared, so the unstable orbits never visibly diverge. Lagrange's side deviation is 2·10⁻¹¹ after its 2 shown periods but 2·10⁻³ after 6.
  - The bodies move from `t = 0`: at 80×24 the fig-8 heads travel 8–22 rows/s.
  - Each body has a `Trail<(f64, f64)>` of 480 model-space samples, one every `qᵢ` substeps, so the trail is exactly one period long and closes on itself. The exception is moth I (`n = 1`): its one period folds into a knot, so only its newest 25–45 % is shown, depending on the view scale (see Inks).
  - During `fade` the physics continues while trails, heads and triangle dim by `1 − u³` over its 1.2 s: the orbit holds bright and drops late. Each run fades in over 0.3 s from 0.35, so no run opens on a blank frame; the gallery is below a quarter brightness only for the fade's last 0.13 s.
- **Resize**: there is no simulation lattice (ADR-0012 does not apply). Physics and trails live in model units, so a resize only recomputes the view and resizes `Dots`. The scale in rows per unit is `s = max(0.25, 0.9·min((w/2 − 1)/(ASPECT·x_half), (h/2 − 1)/y_half))` for a constant per-orbit bounding box, centred on the box centre. The boxes are the measured extents over run plus fade, padded by ≥ 0.5 %: fig-8 ±1.09 × ±0.36, Lagrange x ±0.97 / y [−0.87, 1.01], A1 ±2.22 × ±0.57, R4 ±0.88 × ±0.88, moth ±1.13 × ±1.15. The scale changes only at `run` starts, where the fade and the empty trails hide the jump.
- **Inks**:
  - Bodies: each body has its own ramp, a false-colour triad: body 1 `Ramp(Ember)`, body 2 `Ramp(Ice)`, body 3 `Ramp(Plasma)` (orange, cyan, violet; Aurora and Hα are `double_pendulum`'s). Trail samples keep the level curve below, drawn as ramp value `min(level/255, 0.7)` (× the brightness factor), so the comet tapers from the head's hue rather than from white.
  - Comet tails: the newest `COMET = 0.15` of a period is thickened by dot discs (CORE-R82) whose radius tapers linearly from 2.0 dots at the head to 0.
  - Heads: a disc of r 2.3 dots (3.0 from 40 rows up) at ramp `0.7 × head_level/255`, the saturated middle of each ramp (every ramp's top is near white, so pale heads could not be told apart), with a one-dot core at `0.95 × head_level/255`. The core keeps the head brightest, also in mono.
  - Brightness: every ink an orbit draws (heads, trails, triangle) is multiplied by `lerp(0.35, 1, smoothstep(min(τ/0.3, 1)))·fade`, so each run fades in over its first 0.3 s from 0.35 instead of switching on or opening dark (visual only; the physics is untouched). `head_level` is 255 times that factor. Trail samples fade with age fraction `f` as `40 + 160·(1 − f)^1.5`. The moth's trail spans `s = clamp(0.45·scale/8, 0.25, 0.45)` of its period, where `scale` is the view's rows per unit: 0.45 at 80×24 (scale 8.6) and above, 0.25 at 40×12. It tapers to nothing as `160·(1 − f/s)^1.5 + 40·(1 − f/s)`, and samples below level 12 are dropped (from `f ≈ 0.89·s`). So its old loops dissolve, and a small view shows one clean stroke rather than a knot. A braille cell keeps the ink of its brightest dot, so on the shared fig-8 curve the most recent pass sets the colour, which makes the `T/3` phase shift of the choreography visible.
  - Lagrange adds a faint triangle joining the bodies (`Dim` 80 times the fade factor, so it rises with its bodies and never shows before them). It lights one dot per cell, and only in cells no trail has lit, so it never thickens a trail and in ASCII reads as a clean dotted line of `.`.
  - The label is `Text` 150. No `Seq`/`Div`, because there is no field.
- **ASCII**: trails use the `Dots` count fallback (`.` `:` `+` `#`) and read as dotted curves. In Unicode the heads are discs on the lattice; in ASCII they are drawn as `@` after the blit, in the body's ramp at `0.7 × head_level/255` (the same saturated band as the discs, so colour terminals still tell them apart). The label is pure ASCII in both modes. The 80×24 snapshots show the label and three `@` at t = 0, short dotted arcs with three `@` (1 s) and a mostly drawn figure-8 (5 s).
- **Stylised**: the colours are false colour, and the comet tails are thickened for visibility; the trail length is still exactly the published period fraction.
- **Label**: top-left (row 0, column 1; the HUD owns the last row), static per orbit. Full forms: `figure-8  Moore 1993`, `Lagrange triangle  1772`, `Broucke A1  1975`, `Broucke R4  1975`, `moth I  Suvakov 2013`. Short forms: `figure-8`, `Lagrange`, `Broucke A1`, `Broucke R4`, `moth I`. The full form is used when it fits in `w − 2`, else the short form when it fits, else no label. While the title shows the orbit's name as its caption (`Title::fit` gives `Form::Full`, decided per orbit in `resize`), the label is only the attribution, so the name is not shown twice: `Moore 1993`, `1772`, `1975`, `1975`, `Suvakov 2013`.
- **Perf**: at 200×50 and `dt = 1/60` a frame runs 68 leapfrog substeps (103 for moth) of 3 pair forces (≈ 2 µs). It rasterises 3 × 479 trail segments on the dot lattice (≈ 6k dot sets, ≈ 15 µs), then clears and blits `Dots` (≈ 25 µs): ≈ 0.05 ms in total. `dt = 0.25` costs at most 1548 substeps (moth; ≈ 30 µs), well under `MAX_STEPS`. Nothing allocates after `build`: trails are allocated in `build`, `Dots` in `resize`, and the labels are `&'static str`.
- **Acceptance**: `TB-A1` figure-8 from the Simó ICs on its lattice (`q = 54`): after exactly one period (25 920 substeps) every position is back within 1e-5 of its IC (measured 1.3e-7), max `|ΔE/E|` < 1e-6 over 3 periods (measured 3.5e-8), `E₀ = −1.287142 ± 1e-6` and `|L| < 1e-12`; `TB-A2` for every orbit `|Σv| < 1e-9` at the ICs, `E₀` and `L₀` match the table within 1e-6, `P` and `L` are conserved to < 1e-12 over run plus fade (measured ≤ 6e-14), and max `|ΔE/E|` is < 1e-5 for fig-8/Lagrange/A1/R4 (measured ≤ 5.9e-6) and < 1e-3 for moth I (measured 2.6e-4); `TB-A3` every orbit is back at its IC positions after each shown period within a per-orbit tolerance: fig-8 2e-6, Lagrange 1e-6, A1 8e-5, R4 2e-5, moth 3e-4 (measured 8.3e-7, 3.0e-7, 4.9e-5, 6.0e-6, 2.6e-4), the published periods 6.283213, 5.396 and 14.8939 fail the A1, R4 and moth tolerances (measured 1.1e-4, 2.5e-4, 3.6e-4; the moth by only ≈ 20 %), and nudging the refined A1, R4 and moth periods by ±2e-5 makes the return error larger, Lagrange's `T` equals `2π√(a³√3)` within 1e-12, and the minimum pair distance over each run is ≥ 0.07 (measured 0.079 for moth, ≥ 0.23 otherwise); `TB-A4` Lagrange e = 0.3: over the 2 shown periods `max side / min side − 1 < 1e-8` (measured 2e-11) and body 1's distance from the COM stays in `[0.538462, 1]` ± 1e-6; integrating on to 6 periods, the side deviation grows by a factor in `[30, 300]` per period between periods 3 and 6 (measured 99), which is why every loop restarts; `TB-A5` the timeline total equals `Σ nᵢTᵢ/kᵢ + 5·1.2` (≈ 69.42 s) within 1e-9 and loops, every `run` start restores the ICs bitwise with empty trails, 70 s driven with `dt = 1/60` and with `dt = 0.25` have taken the same number of substeps and hold bitwise-identical positions at every whole second, and a single `update(0.25)` takes ≤ 1548 substeps; `TB-A6` at 80×24 and 200×50 every position of every orbit's run plus fade lies inside its bounding box and projects inside the canvas with ≥ 1 cell margin and off rows 0 and `h − 1`, and the label is ASCII-only, starts at column 1, and is the full form if that fits in `w − 2`, else the short form if that fits, else absent; `TB-A7` (visual) the trail span is 1 for every orbit but moth, whose span is 0.45 at 80×24 and 200×50, 0.25 at 40×12, strictly between at 60×18, and non-decreasing with size; a full-period trail follows `40 + 160·(1 − f)^1.5`, the moth's ramp starts at 200, decreases strictly and is below level 12 from `f = 0.45` on; at the end of the moth's run (80×24) every lit dot lies within 6 dots of a trail sample younger than 0.45 period; head level is `255·0.35` at `τ = 0`, increasing, 255 from `τ = 0.3 s` and `255·fade` in the fade, so all three heads are drawn on a run's first frame (and the scene's); the triangle level is `80·0.35` at `τ = 0`, increasing, 80 from 0.3 s and `80·fade` in the fade, and it adds ≥ 20 dots by 0.3 s; at 0.6 of the fade the orbit is still at > 0.75, and over the fade plus the next fade-in the ink multiplier is below 0.25 for < 0.15 s; at 1, 4 and 9 s into the Lagrange run the triangle adds at most one dot per cell, none to a cell a trail has lit, and ≥ 20 dots in all; `TB-A8` `comet_radius` is 2.0 at the head, strictly decreasing, and 0 from `f = 0.15`; at 80×24 (2 s in) every head has ≥ 24 lit dots within 6 dots; at 5 s all three ramps are drawn; at 120×40 (3 s, seed 0), in the 3 × 3 cells around each head, at least two cells carry the body's own ramp at a value ≤ 0.75 and at most one (the core) above it; in ASCII the three `@` carry a body ramp at a value ≤ 0.75.

### vortex_street  (Forces & Waves, M10, M12)
- **Intent**: flow past a cylinder at Re ≈ 90–150 (≈ 125–140 on the reference lattices) sheds a von Kármán vortex street. That is the regime of real 2-D laminar shedding: onset is at Re ≈ 47, and 3-D mode A appears above Re ≈ 190. A calm inflow comes in from the left and meets a solid round cylinder 15 % of the way across (never closer than 1.65 D to the inlet). Two thin shear layers peel off its top and bottom, flap, and roll up into alternating eddies, clockwise (cyan) in the upper row and counter-clockwise (orange) in the lower row, drifting right at ≈ 0.85 U. The eye lands on the cylinder and then follows the wake. Only the vorticity field, the cylinder and one corner label are drawn; the irrotational flow is the terminal's own background. At 80×24 the cylinder is ≈ 7 columns × 3½ rows centred near column 11.3 and row 11.5, with 3–4 separate eddies in view in a band across the middle rows. At 200×50 the framing is the same, and the eddies have soft graded cores and the shear layers read as fine filaments.
- **Model**: D2Q9 lattice Boltzmann (Qian, d'Humières & Lallemand 1992) in lattice units (Δx = Δt = 1). Streaming is `f_i(x + c_i, t + 1) = f_i*(x, t)`, with equilibrium `f_i^eq = w_i ρ (1 + 3 c_i·u + 4.5 (c_i·u)² − 1.5 u²)`, weights `w = 4/9, 1/9 (×4), 1/36 (×4)` and `c_s² = 1/3`. **Collision**: two-relaxation-time (TRT; Ginzburg, Verhaeghe & d'Humières 2008), `f_i* = f_i − ω⁺(f_i⁺ − f_i^{eq+}) − ω⁻(f_i⁻ − f_i^{eq−})`, where `f_i^± = (f_i ± f_ī)/2` pairs opposite directions, `ω⁺ = 1/τ`, `ν = (τ − ½)/3`, and `ω⁻ = 1/τ⁻` with magic parameter `Λ = (τ − ½)(τ⁻ − ½) = 1/4`. BGK (Bhatnagar, Gross & Krook 1954) is the special case `τ⁻ = τ`, i.e. TRT with `Λ = (τ − ½)² ≈ 9·10⁻⁴` at τ = 0.53; the stable velocity range collapses as Λ → 0 and BGK blows up in the under-resolved shear layers, whereas Λ = ¼ is Ginzburg's stability-optimal choice (Λ = 3/16 would instead place the bounce-back wall exactly halfway for Poiseuille flow). TRT has the same ν(τ) and a similar cost. The kernel is a fused pull-scheme stream+collide in f32 over SoA planes. Populations are stored as `f_i − w_i` (Lehmann et al. 2022) so the O(Ma²) deviations keep their precision, and ρ, uₓ, u_y are written out as a by-product. **Lattice**: the lattice does not depend on terminal size. The display's square-pixel aspect `A = w/(2h)` is clamped to [1, 4] and widened by the hidden sponge (`A/0.9`). The lattice is the capped shape of exactly that snapped aspect, `grid::lattice_for_aspect(1.12^k, MAX_NODES = 10 240)` (CORE-R69), whatever the display's size, so every terminal in one aspect bucket runs identical physics: 150×68 at 200×50 and 134×76 at 80×24. The view shows lattice columns 1 … W_vis − 1 with `W_vis = W − ⌈0.1 W⌉`, which hides the forced inlet column and the sponge; it is sampled into the `Pixels` by a `grid::SampleMap`. For display aspects in [1, 4] its aspect is within 7 % of the display's, so the cylinder stays round; outside that range the view is stretched. **Geometry**: `D = clamp(round(min(0.14 H, W_vis/9)), 6, 16)` (blockage ≤ 0.14; the W_vis/9 bound only binds on square displays), centre `(x_c, y_c) = (max(round(0.15 W_vis), round(1.65 D)), (H − 1)/2)`, staircase mask. The `1.65 D` floor keeps a calm run-up of ≥ 1.15 D between the inlet and the cylinder's front on every display: at aspect 1 (80×40, 100×50, a vertical split of 200×50; lattice 107×95) 15 % alone gave D = 13 and x_c = 14, the front only ≈ 4 nodes past the inlet sponge, and the inflow filled with a persistent haze; the floor gives D = 11, x_c = 18, as at 80×24. 80×24 and 200×50 are unchanged. `(H − 1)/2` is the domain's exact middle, so the mask and the drawn disc are symmetric about it (the probe row is `round(y_c)`). y is periodic, so the domain is a column of cylinders H ≈ 7 D apart; the visible street is ≈ 12 D long, room for 3–4 shed eddies after the formation region (with D = 0.19 H the view held ≈ 8.6 D × 5.3 D, one street wavelength, and the eddies stretched into full-height banners). The inlet (column 0) is set every step to `f^eq(ρ_B, u_pot)` of the undisturbed potential flow past the cylinder and its images at y ± H (the inlet is only ≈ 2 D upstream, so a uniform inlet would shear the deflected stream and shed a vorticity sheet into the calm inflow), with its Bernoulli density `ρ_B = 1 + 1.5 (U² − |u_pot|²)`, the same as the start state, so the inlet forces no pressure mismatch (with ρ = 1 it was ≈ 0.004 too low on the centre line, where the flow 1.65 D upstream has slowed to ≈ 0.91 U; harmless, but inconsistent). Columns 1–3 get a ramped (≤ 0.25) relaxation toward each node's own equilibrium, which raises the viscosity there without imposing a velocity and damps the τ = 0.53 even-mode checkerboard the forced inlet column would otherwise feed. The outlet (column W − 1) copies column W − 2. Over the hidden last ⌈0.1 W⌉ columns a sponge (Israeli & Orszag 1981) adds relaxation toward the uniform stream `f^eq(1, (U(t), 0))`, with strength ramping quadratically from 0 to 0.25. The cylinder wall uses halfway bounce-back (Ladd 1994). **Parameters**: `τ = 0.53` (ν = 0.01) and `U₀ = 0.125` on every lattice, so `Re = U₀ D/ν = 12.5 D`. The node cap keeps D ≤ 12 (D ≥ 13 would need H ≥ 90 and W ≥ 125, over 10 240 nodes), so Re ≤ 150. The inflow Mach number is `U₀√3 = 0.217` (0.234 at the breath's peak), but the shoulders reach |u| ≈ 1.7 U (local Ma ≈ 0.4) and ρ swings by ≈ ±6 % (see Watchdog): the flow is weakly compressible, and its shedding frequency is validated against the literature by VOR-A5. The reference lattices give D = 10, Re = 125 (200×50) and D = 11, Re = 137.5 (80×24 and the square 107×95 lattice); narrow displays reach Re = 150 (D ≥ 12), and the widest bucket (D = 7) gives 87.5. **Breathing inlet**: the inlet speed is `U(t) = U₀·breath(t)`, `breath(t) = 1 + 0.08 sin(2π t/47 s)`, in the scene's own simulated time (lattice steps / 270); the inlet profile and the outlet sponge's target follow it, set once per `update` (quasi-static: < 0.1 % U per frame). Shedding frequency and eddy spacing drift visibly over a long visit, Re stays within [0.92, 1.08]·Re₀ ⊂ (47, 190), and the peak Ma is 0.234. **Start**: the flow starts from an analytic street, so a street exists at t = 0. The background is potential flow past the cylinder, `u = U(1 − R²(x² − y²)/r⁴, −2R² x y/r⁴)` with R = D/2, which is rational and irrotational and avoids an impulsive jolt at the wall. On top of it sit two rows of Kaufmann–Scully vortices, `v_θ = Γ r/(2π(r² + r_c²))` (rational, so no libm), with `Γ = −1.8 U D` in the upper row (clockwise) and `+1.8 U D` in the lower row, and `r_c = 0.35 D`. The streamwise spacing is `a = 4.3 D` (≈ 0.85 U/f at St ≈ 0.2) and the row gap is `b = 0.281 a` (von Kármán's stable ratio). Each vortex is summed with its periodic images at y ± H. The rows run from `x_c + 1.5 D` + a seeded offset in `[0, a/2)` to the outlet, with a seeded leading row (together every phase in `[0, a)`, always with an eddy in the near wake). A zero-net-circulation start-up pair (Kelvin) is added: a bound circulation 1.5 U D on the cylinder and the opposite eddy at (0.9 D, ±0.3 D) on the leading row's side, so the wake sheds at full amplitude within ≈ 4 s instead of growing its asymmetry for ≈ 10 s. Both are windowed toward the periodic seam, where the truncated image sums would otherwise shed vorticity sheets, and toward the inlet, which imposes the undisturbed flow; a window `w` multiplying an irrotational field creates vorticity `w′(x)·v` wherever it varies, so each has its own. The street's smoothstep eases in from the inlet to `x_c + D/2`. The pair's is 1 from `x_c − D` on and eases in from the inlet ahead of it, where the pair's field is small: under the street's window, steepest just in front of the cylinder where the bound vortex is strong, it left a vorticity sheet that took ≈ 120 steps to wash through. Initially ρ follows Bernoulli, `ρ = 1 + 1.5 (U² − |u|²)` (δp = ½(U² − |u|²), δρ = δp/c_s²), which is exact in the irrotational flow, plus each Scully core's cyclostrophic excess `δp_k = −Γ_k² r_c²/(8π²(r_k² + r_c²)²)` (street vortices with their images and the start-up eddy, times the square of their window; zero for the point bound vortex), because Bernoulli does not hold across the rotational cores: it gives a core pressure that climbs back to p∞ at the centre, where the cyclostrophic `−Γ²/(8π²(r² + r_c²))` is lowest (≈ 0.016 in ρ at D = 10, ≈ 70 % of 1.5 U²). So the start carries the pressure its velocity field implies instead of launching an acoustic pulse; u = 0 and ρ = 1 in the solid, and `f = f^eq(ρ, u)`. The first `resize` then pre-rolls 160 lattice steps (≈ x_c/U, one flush of the run-up; ≈ 1.6 M lattice updates, a one-off 12–20 ms on a release scene switch), so the first frame is past the slip sheet the potential flow leaves on the no-slip staircase, the start-up pressure pulse and the inlet's adjustment (with 40 steps the cylinder wore a speckled halo and a thin vorticity sheet crossed the calm inflow for ≈ 0.3 s). **Watchdog**: once per simulated second, any non-finite value or ρ outside [0.7, 1.3] re-initialises the flow from the analytic street. It never fires in normal operation (VOR-A5, VOR-A6, VOR-A7: 45 s at five displays and at the D = 12, Re₀ = 150 lattice; 150 s runs at every extreme lattice gave ρ ∈ [0.941, 1.037] and |u|max ≤ 1.70 U).
- **Motion**: `STEPS_PER_S = 270` lattice steps per simulated second: 4.5 per 60 Hz frame through a fractional accumulator, and at most 68 per `update` at dt = 0.25. The shedding period `P ≈ D/(St U)` is ≈ 400–450 steps at the reference lattices (St ≈ 0.20–0.21 on the free stream), or ≈ 1.5–1.7 s. Eddies advect ≈ 25–29 lattice nodes/s (≈ 0.85 U), which is ≈ 40 columns/s at 200×50 and ≈ 18 columns/s at 80×24, so motion is obvious within 1 s. Without forcing the flow would be an exact limit cycle (frames 25 s apart nearly identical); the 47 s breathing inlet makes the shedding period and eddy spacing drift by ±8 %, so a long visit evolves. It loops forever without a Timeline, and each seed opens on a different street phase.
- **Resize**: `lattice_size(display)` decides everything. If the lattice is unchanged (same aspect bucket), only the view mapping and `Pixels` are rebuilt and the populations stay bit-identical. On a bucket change, ρ, uₓ and u_y are resampled onto the new lattice with `grid::resample_bilinear` (ADR-0012); every lattice runs at the same U₀, so the moments need no rescaling. Solid nodes of the new cylinder get `u = 0`, `FlowParams` are recomputed, `f = f^eq(ρ, u)` is rebuilt and the probe history is cleared. Non-equilibrium populations are never resampled. The first resize initialises the flow from the analytic street and pre-rolls it. Every buffer (lattice planes, vorticity grid, `SampleMap`, `Pixels`) is sized here. The view is refreshed at the end of both `resize` and `update`, so `render` only blits and writes the label.
- **Inks**: vorticity `ω = ∂ₓu_y − ∂_y uₓ` (y up on screen) is taken by central differences on the visible lattice and normalised as `x = ω D/(2.5 U)`. It is soft-clipped with a rational tanh-like squash, `s = clamp(x(27 + x²)/(27 + 9x²), −1, 1)` (`ink::soft_clip`), which is odd, monotone and exactly 1 at |x| = 3, so no transcendental function runs per pixel. The vorticity grid is resampled bilinearly to pixel size, and each pixel gets `ink::div(s)` (`Role::Div`): CW (upper row, s < 0) is cyan and CCW (lower row) is orange. Pixels with |s| < 0.06 stay `Ink::BLANK`. The Scully core peak `ω_max = Γ/(π r_c²) ≈ 4.7 U/D` maps to |s| ≈ 0.975, so eddy cores are graded while the thin shear layers saturate. **Hysteresis** (as in Canny's 1986 edge detector): a 4-connected group of drawn `Div` pixels stays only if one of its pixels reaches |s| ≥ 0.12, the ASCII blank band, so a faint structure shows in both glyph modes or in neither. The faint fringes of eddies and shear layers hang on to their cores, while isolated threshold-level specks with nothing behind them are blanked: start-up transients (a detached 4-pixel strip below the cylinder at 80×24, t = 0.2 s; a dotted diagonal trailing the upper shear layer at 200×50, t = 1 s) and staircase noise, ≈ 1–2 groups per frame at any time, ≈ 0.2 % of the drawn pixels. Dropping every faint pixel not next to a strong one would instead strip ≈ 11 % of the drawn pixels at 200×50, the soft edges of the eddies. It is a flood fill from the seeds over the pixel buffer, with scratch buffers sized in `resize`. The fluid is drawn first, the hysteresis runs on it, and the cylinder goes on top; pixels centred inside the rim are not fluid, so the wall's own shear never seeds a group. Cylinder pixels are those whose centre, mapped into lattice coordinates, lies within D/2 of (x_c, y_c), so the outline is round rather than the staircase. The rim is anti-aliased by coverage `c = clamp(½ − d, 0, 1)`, with `d` the signed distance of the pixel centre outside the rim in pixel widths along the radius: `Role::Structure` level 200 where c = 1 and `120 + 80c` otherwise, i.e. 160–199 for the rim pixels whose centre is inside, and 120–159 for pixels partly covered from outside, which show the wall only where the fluid is undrawn, so they never hide the boundary layer (at 80×24 a hard 7×6-pixel disc read as a rounded square). On the upstream half (dx < 0), weak vorticity (|s| < 0.2) is not drawn within 1 node of the rim, where the staircase's corners stick out of the round mask, nor, when its sign is opposite to the boundary layer's (CW above, CCW below), within 0.6 D of the rim, where the steps' odd-even noise is washed along the wall: at Re ≲ 190 the only vorticity there is the attached boundary layer, far stronger, and opposite-signed secondary vorticity only forms behind separation. These removed single-pixel brown and navy specks at the cylinder's upstream shoulders. The label is `Role::Text` level 150.
- **ASCII**: the `Pixels::blit` closure decides per cell. A cell whose two halves are both cylinder, `Structure` at level ≥ 160 (centre inside the rim), gives `@`; so does a cell with one such half whose column centre lies within 0.35 D of x_c (the disc's top and bottom caps, columns computed in `resize`). Elsewhere a single cylinder half, and anywhere an anti-aliased outside-edge half (< 160), falls through to the other half's `Div` value, so the shoulders show the boundary layer instead of a one-pixel sliver. At 80×24 the disc is then four rows of 5, 7, 7 and 5 `@`, round at 2:1 cells; with both halves required it was two rows of 7–8, a 2:1 bar in square units inside the wake. (At 40×12 the disc is only ≈ 3.7 × 1.7 cells and stays a 2×2 block.) `s` is the mean of the `Div` halves: |s| < 0.12 is left blank, |s| < 0.25 gives `.`, s < 0 gives `x` (|s| < 0.6) or `X`, and s > 0 gives `o` or `O`, each with the averaged `Div` ink (the Unicode threshold stays 0.06; the wider blank band keeps decaying far-wake eddies from reading as clouds of dots). A cell with both halves empty is left untouched. Snapshots show a symmetric, round `@` disc, an `x/X` upper row and an `o/O` lower row alternating downstream on a blank field.
- **Label**: `Re≈140` (ASCII `Re~140`, the current Re = Re₀·breath(t) rounded to the nearest 5) at column 1 of row 0, above the calm inflow. `FlowParams::re` is `U₀D/ν` rounded to 1e-6: ν = (0.53 − 0.5)/3 comes out slightly above 0.01 in f64, so Re₀ = 137.4999… rounded down to `Re≈135` at t = 0 and flipped to `Re≈140` within 0.5 s as the breath rose; now the first number read is 140. At width ≥ 40, `  St≈0.18` (ASCII `St~0.18`) is appended once ≥ 3 upward zero-crossings of u_y, with ±0.02 U hysteresis, have been recorded at the probe `(x_c + 2.5 D, round(y_c))`. The probe keeps the last ≤ 5 crossings (a core `Trail`), and `St = D/(U·P̄)`, where P̄ is the mean of their ≤ 4 intervals and U the inlet speed U₀·breath at the middle of that window: the breath drifts by up to ≈ 3 % over the ≈ 6 s window, so the speed at the latest crossing would bias the reading by as much. St is based on the free-stream U, like Re. With y periodic at blockage D/H ≈ 0.15 the fluid passes a row of cylinders, the gap speed is U·H/(H − D) ≈ 1.17 U, and that St reads above the unconfined value: over t = 20–140 s the reference lattices average 0.207 (80×24, Re₀ 137.5) and 0.200 (200×50, Re₀ 125), ranging ±6 % with the breathing inlet. The label shows St on the gap speed, `St·(1 − D/H)`, 0.177 and 0.170. This is a heuristic confinement correction, calibrated empirically, not an established blockage correction: Maskell- or Allen–Vincenti-type corrections for a walled channel at this blockage typically give a smaller factor (≈ 0.9 against 0.855), and a y-periodic row of in-phase cylinders is not a walled channel. On these lattices it matches Williamson's unconfined St(137.5) ≈ 0.179 and St(125) ≈ 0.175 to within ≈ 3 %, and gives 0.148 against 0.149 at Re 75 (VOR-A5), so a viewer comparing it with the literature reads about the right number instead of one ≈ 15 % high. There is no label below 16 columns or 6 rows, and it is never clipped. It is written through a stack buffer.
- **Perf**: 10 200 nodes × 4.5 steps ≈ 46 k lattice updates per frame. The interior uses a branch-free row-sliced kernel as in `gs_step`; only the cylinder's bounding rows and the inlet, outlet and sponge columns take the special paths. At 60–100 MLUPS single-threaded that estimate is 0.45–0.75 ms, plus vorticity ≈ 0.02 ms, resampling to 20 k pixels ≈ 0.08 ms, ink mapping ≈ 0.04 ms and the blit ≈ 0.05 ms. Measured (`cargo bench`, frame = update + render): 0.39 ms at 200×50, 0.76 ms at 400×100 (the view grows with the display; the lattice does not), i.e. the kernel runs at ≈ 150 MLUPS. The hysteresis pass adds ≈ 0.6 M retired instructions per frame at 200×50 (+10 %), mostly the per-pixel mark written while shading; reading the pixels back through `Pixels::get` instead cost ≈ 0.1 ms. The fastest frame measured 0.36 ms against 0.33 ms without the pass (M10 polish, same machine, under heavy load). 80×24 costs about the same as 200×50 because the lattice does not depend on display size (GEN-R8 is 5 ms). `render` and steady `update` do not allocate. Every `update(1/60)` costs ≈ 46 k lattice updates at any size, including 1×1; the debug suites stay fast because `sciart-generators` builds at opt-level 1 in dev.
- **Acceptance**: `VOR-A1` `feq(ρ, u)` for 100 seeded states (ρ ∈ [0.9, 1.1], |u| ≤ 0.2) has moments Σf = ρ, Σf c = ρu and Σf c_α c_β = ρ(δ_αβ/3 + u_α u_β) to 1e-6, and `feq(1, 0) = w`; `collide(f, ω⁺, ω⁻)` conserves ρ and ρu to 1e-6, scales the even non-equilibrium part by (1 − ω⁺) and the odd part by (1 − ω⁻) to 1e-4 of each part plus 5e-8 (perturbations ±2e-2·w_i; f32 round-off is ≈ 1e-8), while an ω⁻ off by 1e-3 fails in ≥ 95 of 100 states, equals BGK when ω⁻ = ω⁺, and `tau_minus(0.53, 1/4)` = 8.8333 ± 1e-4; `VOR-A2` on a fully periodic 32×16 lattice a uniform state (ρ = 1, u = (0.1, 0)) stays uniform (all nodes bit-identical to each other) with ρ and u within 1e-6 of the start after 1 000 steps, and with a D = 6 cylinder at (10, 8) (halfway bounce-back, fully periodic, no inlet, outlet or sponge, u₀ = (0.05, 0)) the total mass Σρ (summed in f64) is conserved to 1e-5 relative over 300 steps; `VOR-A3` a shear wave uₓ = 0.01 sin(2πy/64) on a periodic 4×64 lattice decays as exp(−ν k² t), with ν fitted by Fourier projection between steps 100 and 2 100 at τ = 0.53 and between steps 100 and 1 100 at τ = 0.8, within 2 % of (τ − ½)/3 (validates the τ → ν → Re chain under TRT); `VOR-A4` for every display with w ∈ 1..=480 (step 7) and h ∈ 1..=160 (step 3), `lattice_size` gives between 9 500 and 10 240 nodes, displays whose clamped, sponge-widened aspects round to the same power of 1.12 map to one lattice, and for display aspects in [1, 4] the visible lattice aspect is within 7 % of the display's; `flow_params` gives ω⁺ = 1/0.53, U₀ = 0.125 (Ma = 0.217), D = clamp(round(min(0.14 H, W_vis/9)), 6, 16) with D ≤ 12 and D/H ≤ 0.15, Re = U₀ D/ν ∈ [87, 150], x_c = max(round(0.15 W_vis), round(1.65 D)) with the front x_c − D/2 ≥ 1.1 D, and y_c = (H − 1)/2, and 200×50, 80×24 and 100×50 give exactly the 150×68 (D = 10, x_c = 20, Re = 125), 134×76 (D = 11, x_c = 18, Re = 137.5) and 107×95 (D = 11, x_c = 18, Re = 137.5) lattices; `VOR-A5` on a 96×36 test lattice (`flow_params` gives D = 6, U = 0.125, Re = 75) started from the seeded analytic street, after 1 000 warm-up steps the next 1 400 steps give a probe Strouhal number in [0.165, 0.195], ±8 % around the gap-speed heuristic 0.149/(1 − D/H) = 0.149/(1 − 1/6) ≈ 0.179 from Williamson's (1988) unconfined St(75) ≈ 0.149 (measured 0.177) with u_y amplitude > 0.1 U, time-mean ω < 0 over the upper near wake and > 0 over the lower one (x_c … x_c + 2D, above and below y_c), ρ ∈ [0.9, 1.1] and |u| ≤ 2.5 U from the first step on, and no watchdog reinit (≈ 8.3 M lattice updates, the one slow test, ≈ 2–4 s in debug); `VOR-A6` resizing 200×50 → 190×48 → 200×50 (the lattice stays 150×68) leaves every population bit-identical, and 200×50 → 100×50 (150×68 → 107×95, D 10 → 11, U₀ unchanged) resamples with mean ρ within 2e-3 of before and the fluid-mean uₓ/U within 3 % of before, no non-finite value, and after 200 further steps ρ ∈ [0.9, 1.1] with no watchdog reinit; `VOR-A7` a seed-42 80×24 ASCII frame at t = 0.5 s is pure ASCII, its `@` cells form one 4-connected blob whose centroid is within 1.5 columns of column 11.3 and within 0.5 rows of row 11.5, mirrored rows (y, 23 − y) hold the same number of `@`, the `@` rows are at least four and the outermost two are each ≥ 2 shorter than the widest (round, not a bar; measured 5, 7, 7, 5), columns 0–5 of rows 1–23 are blank, both `x|X` and `o|O` appear right of the blob with more `x|X` than `o|O` above row 11.5 and more `o|O` than `x|X` below it, and a Unicode frame at the same time uses only `Div`, `Structure`, `Text` and `Background` inks; in the developed flow at 80×24 (seeds 42 and 7), in five frames spanning one shedding period from t = 20 s (every 0.4 s), there are same-sign eddies (4-connected `x|X` or `o|O` components of ≥ 4 cells) of both signs whose centroids lie right of the display column of x_c + 3D in every frame, and ≥ 3 of them in at least one (the view right of x_c + 3D holds ≈ 1.5 wavelengths and the newest eddy stays joined to the shear layer for part of each period, so the count depends on the phase); the calm inflow stays calm from the first frame on: at 100×50 (seeds 3 and 42), 100×40, 80×24 and 200×50 (seed 42), at t = 0, 0.1, 0.2, 0.3, 1, 20, 30 and 45 s, no `Div` pixel whose centre lies left of x_c − 0.6 D outside the band |y − y_c| ≤ 0.6 D, no `Div` pixel on the upstream shoulders (dx < 0, |dy| > D/4, within 1.1 D of the centre) with the sign opposite to the boundary layer's, and no faint island anywhere (every 4-connected group of drawn `Div` pixels holds one with |s| ≥ 0.12), and no watchdog reinit in any of those runs nor in 45 s at 13×5 (lattice 119×85, D = 12, Re₀ = 150, the highest Re); the cylinder's pixels are exactly those whose centre lies within D/2, at `Structure` 160–200 with graded rim pixels, pixels partly covered from outside are `Structure` 120–159 or `Div`, and an outside-edge half never makes `@`, and a single inside half makes `@` only in the cap columns (within 0.35 D of x_c); the hysteresis pass keeps a faint fringe 4-connected to a pixel with |s| ≥ 0.12, blanks a faint group without one (touching a seed only diagonally, or only the cylinder, does not count), never changes another role, and reuses its scratch buffers; `VOR-A8` `breath(0) = 1`, its extremes are 1.08 and 0.92 at a quarter and three quarters of 47 s, and it is 47 s periodic; for every display of VOR-A4, 0.92·Re₀ > 47, 1.08·Re₀ < 190 and 1.08·U₀√3 ≤ 0.234; in a 200×50 scene after 1.5 s the centre-row inlet velocity is breath(t) times its t = 0 value and the outlet sponge's target is U₀·breath(t), both within 1e-3, with no watchdog reinit; at t = 0 every inlet row carries the potential flow's velocity and its Bernoulli density `1 + 1.5 (U₀² − |u_pot|²)`, within 1e-6; the label reads Re₀·breath(t) rounded to 5: `flow_params` gives Re₀ = 137.5 exactly at 80×24, the label reads Re≈140 at t = 0 and at t = 0.5 s and Re≈150 at t = 10 s, when it also shows St on the gap speed, St·(1 − D/H), in [0.16, 0.19]; `strouhal` reads nothing before 3 crossings, uses the last ≤ 4 periods, and divides by the inlet speed at the middle of their window (VOR-A5 uses the same function). M12 dye: `VOR-A9` in a uniform lattice flow u = (0.1, −0.03) a particle moves exactly n·u in n steps and y wraps, and in the scene (20 s, 200×50) no particle is ever inside the cylinder or upstream of the ports; `VOR-A10` the pool never fills over 20 s at 200×50 (so releases never stall) and resizing keeps its capacity and sizes the per-pixel dye buffer, a watchdog restart empties it and keeps its capacity, after 20 s at 200×50 more than 2 000 particles are live with more than 500 on each side of the centre line and more than 100 dye pixels lie right of `x_c + 3D`, and a bucket change keeps every particle's position in diameters relative to the cylinder (1e-9); `VOR-A11` the Unicode ink of a pixel equals the vorticity pixel wherever there is no dye, every dye pixel is `Ramp(Plasma)` at level ≥ 130, never over the cylinder or a blank pixel, and at least `255·|s|` of the vorticity under it, the ASCII frame equals the vorticity-only frame, and Unicode frames use only `Div`, `Structure`, `Text`, `Background` and `Ramp(Plasma)`; `VOR-A12` `dye_units` is 16 at release, never rises, and is 1 from 3 s on, and after 20 s at 200×50 the mean dye level between x_c + 0.5D and x_c + 2D exceeds that right of x_c + 7D by > 20.
- **Dye (M12)**: as in the dye-streak photographs of the street (Taneda; Van Dyke, *An Album of Fluid Motion*), dye seeps from one port on each shoulder, rides the shear layers and winds into every eddy core as thin pale spiral filaments over the soft vorticity clouds. Dye is a passive, non-diffusing tracer: particles in a `ParticlePool` of capacity `2 · 270 · 12 = 6480` (sized once), advected by the bilinear lattice velocity with the midpoint rule once per `update` over its n lattice steps (field frozen). Ports at ±80° from the front stagnation point, 1 node outside the rim (just ahead of separation), each releasing one particle per lattice step, carried along its first path for the rest of the update; particles leaving the visible columns or entering the cylinder are dropped; on a bucket change they keep `(x − x_c)/D`, `(y − y_c)/D`. Dye starts at the first frame, so the streaks grow out of the cylinder over the first seconds. Stylisation: these are streaklines (where fluid has been), not vorticity; real dye diffuses slightly; more ports fill the cores solid and hide the spirals. Each particle carries its release step; its weight falls linearly from 1 at release to 1/16 at 3 s and stays there (`dye_units`, counted in sixteenths; VOR-A12), so fresh streaks near the shoulders are bright and old filaments in the far wake dim, which gives the dye depth (art). Each frame the weights are summed per pixel and the sums become `Ramp(Plasma)` levels in place (a u8 per pixel, sized in `resize`; false colour), `130 + 125·Tone::asinh(knee 0.25, white 12)(sum)/255`, raised to at least the magnitude of the vorticity under it (`255·|s|`), so in a grey theme dye never reads darker than a bright core. A pixel keeps no dye where there is none, where it is cylinder, or where no vorticity cloud is drawn: tracers that drift out of the wake into calm water would only be confetti (stylisation: the dye is shown only inside the wake). The pool is cleared when the watchdog restarts the flow. `render` blits the vorticity `Pixels` as before, then, in Unicode, re-draws only the dyed cells through `raster::half_block` (CORE-R42), dye over vorticity; ASCII is the vorticity frame, unchanged. No second `Pixels` buffer, which keeps the scene under the TEST-R7 ceiling at 500×150. Measured worst frame (step + render, 200×50, per-frame minimum over 5 runs of a 50 s visit): 0.63 ms.
